# Part II — SUB

**Decomposition under Finer Resolution**

<a id="chapter-18-the-provisionally-compressed-object"></a>

## 18. The Provisionally Compressed Object

Chapter 18 begins Part II — SUB by defining what must be declared before any finer reconstruction can count as a STRATA decomposition. Foundations established the common object model, analytical coordinates, operation grammar, continuity requirements, Admissibility Band, Shared Transformation Record, and non-equivalence controls. Part I — PATH then formed and tested temporally extended objects without granting them truth priority, target-function status, or immunity from later pressure. SUB now turns to a different problem: when may an occurrence or composite that has so far been treated as one analytical unit be responsibly opened under finer resolution?

The governing question is:

> What may legitimately be treated as a compressed object, and what warrants opening it?

This question precedes the `DECOMPOSE` operation. It does not ask what the finer components already are, which target granularity should be selected, or what result a decomposition will produce. Those duties belong to Chapter 19 and Chapter 20. Chapter 18 first establishes a bounded source object, its provisional status, its source-side typing, and the distinction between what is already known and what remains unresolved.

Sections 18.1–18.4 establish the Part-II purpose, provisional elementarity, the compressed source object, and the operator-type/occurrence boundary. Sections 18.5–18.8 then distinguish necessary compression from claim-relevant insufficiency and define bounded reasons to decompose or not to decompose. Sections 18.9–18.10 complete the source-entry architecture through preservation without immunization, the Minimal Source Declaration, Stop, Non-Capture, and the controlled handoff to Chapters 19 and 20.

The integrated Chapter-18 result is therefore a controlled **source-entry architecture**, not a `DECOMPOSE` occurrence.

```text
Chapter 18 source candidate
≠ target granularity
≠ discovered component set
≠ completed DECOMPOSE record
≠ source-function result
```

<a id="18-1-purpose-of-sub"></a>

### 18.1 Purpose of SUB

SUB specifies controlled reconstruction under finer resolution. Its concern is not detail as such, but whether a currently compressed occurrence or composite can be opened while preserving the same reference object, declaring the changed analytical coordinates, retaining source trace, and testing a claim-relevant difference.

SUB therefore addresses questions such as:

- Which internal distinctions have been omitted by the current representation?
- Are those distinctions source-supported or merely hypothesized?
- Would their inclusion alter a warranted reconstruction of roles, costs, alternatives, temporal order, asymmetry, binding, expectation, or another praxis-relevant relation?
- Can the same source reference still be maintained under the proposed opening?
- Does the current/coarser analytical function remain confirmed, become refined, become internally differentiated, become only partially supportable, fail, or remain underdetermined?
- Where must further resolution stop because it has no purchase, exceeds the sources, substitutes another object, or creates untraceable abstraction?

These questions define a discipline of opening. They do not establish a general preference for small units, microscopic description, maximal data density, or presumed hidden depth.

```text
finer reconstruction
≠ deeper reality

additional distinction
≠ additional praxis finding automatically

more data
≠ SUB entry
```

#### SUB is a Part, not a fourth operation

SUB is one of the four Parts of STRATA. It contains concepts, source-object criteria, granularity relations, decomposition procedures, resolution outcomes, boundary tests, cases, and a local audit. It is not itself an operation and does not expand the closed operation inventory.

```text
SUB
≠ DECOMPOSE
```

`DECOMPOSE` remains one of exactly three STRATA operations. Chapter 4 supplies its generic identity:

```text
compressed occurrence or composite
→ finer reconstruction of the same reference object
```

Chapter 20 will supply the complete SUB-specific procedure. Chapter 18 does not execute that procedure. It determines whether an identifiable occurrence or composite can even be declared as a bounded source candidate for it.

```text
SUB source declaration
≠ DECOMPOSE occurrence
```

This separation matters because ordinary description can become more detailed without constituting a transformation. A source archive may grow, a transcript may be transcribed more precisely, or a visualization may expose more marks while the analytical object and its distinction set remain unchanged. Conversely, a legitimate source candidate can be identified even when the correct later decision is not to decompose it.

#### SUB begins from a source-side insufficiency question

The Part-II entry question is not:

> What smaller things must this object really be made of?

It is:

> Is the current treatment of this occurrence or composite as one unit sufficient for the present claim, and is there a source-supported reason to test a finer reconstruction of the same reference object?

A bounded SUB entry therefore requires, at minimum:

- an identifiable source reference;
- an occurrence or composite rather than an operator type;
- a declared source-side typing;
- a source Frame, granularity, relative level, and bounded temporal scope where material;
- a current/coarser function or claim under test;
- a distinction between known and unresolved internal structure;
- a plausible source route for any proposed opening;
- an expected praxeological difference rather than general curiosity;
- uncertainty and claim limits;
- preserved Stop and Non-Capture capability.

The current/coarser function need not already be a strong or exclusive function claim. It may be a bounded occurrence typing, a composite claim, a Path or Trajectory claim, a documented event or Non-Event function, or another analytical role currently carried by the source object. It may be provisional, contested, partially supported, or underdetermined. Chapter 18 keeps it visible as a later test target; it does not guarantee its survival.

```text
source function retained for testing
≠ source function guaranteed
```

#### PATH supplies possible source objects, not decomposition permission

Part I provisionally locked Paths, Trajectories, branch structures, Non-Event structures, and `COMPOSE` records. Those objects may now become source candidates, but their PATH status does not authorize finer reconstruction automatically.

```text
provisionally locked PATH object
≠ automatically decomposable object
```

A Path may already be sufficiently resolved for its current claim. A Trajectory may lack sources for an internal reconstruction. A failed PATH claim may remain failed rather than being rescued by asking for more detail. A different periodization, graph, Frame, granularity, or operation creates a new testable claim; it does not erase the prior result.

The controlled handoff is:

```text
PATH object and source trace
→ Chapter 18 source-candidate declaration
→ Chapter 19 granularity relation
→ Chapter 20 DECOMPOSE occurrence, if warranted
```

No step inherits truth, success, or authority from the previous one.

<a id="18-2-provisional-elementarity"></a>

### 18.2 Provisional Elementarity

**Provisional elementarity** is the analytical status of an object treated as undivided for a declared Frame, granularity, relative level, temporal scope, claim, source access, and present analytical use. It means that the current reconstruction stops its internal distinctions at that point. It does not mean that STRATA has established an ultimate constituent, an indivisible entity, or a final level of reality.

The anti-ontological formulation is strict:

```text
STRATA authorizes no claim of absolute elementarity.
```

An object can be provisionally elementary because:

- the current claim does not require finer differentiation;
- the available sources do not support finer differentiation;
- the finer distinctions currently available do not alter a praxis-relevant reconstruction;
- the object must remain bounded to preserve comparison or relation visibility;
- further opening would exceed calibration or claim precision;
- the present analysis has reached a justified Optional Stop.

These are analytical conditions. None proves that the object has no internal structure outside the current reconstruction.

```text
elementary-for-this-claim
≠ elementarily constituted in reality

not opened here
≠ undecomposable in every later analysis
```

#### Elementarity is coordinate- and claim-relative

The same reference object may be treated as provisionally elementary in one analysis and as compressed in another without contradiction. The relevant question is which claim is being tested under which coordinates and sources.

Consider the documented review arrangement introduced in Chapter 1. For a bounded claim that the session used an agenda to distinguish admissible from inadmissible decision matters, the arrangement may be treated as one Frame-typed occurrence. The agenda, role list, and transcript may already provide sufficient support for that claim. A finer reconstruction is not required merely because more procedural detail exists.

For a different claim—such as whether admission, role allocation, and approval capacity distribute costs and decision access asymmetrically—the same reference object may require a finer distinction set. This does not show that the earlier Frame claim was false or shallow. It shows that the later question demands another source-bounded test.

```text
same reference object
+ different warranted question
→ different provisional-elementarity status may be legitimate
```

#### C18-ELEM-01 — stable Frame occurrence retained as provisionally elementary

The source bundle contains:

- a circulated agenda;
- a role list;
- a transcript showing that only agenda-listed matters were admitted for decision.

The current claim is limited:

```text
the documented review arrangement functioned as a Frame occurrence
for admissible decision matters during the recorded session.
```

The source supports the claim without requiring a finer reconstruction of every utterance, administrative step, role history, or institutional dependency. The occurrence is therefore treated as provisionally elementary for this claim and source set.

The bounded status is not:

```text
the arrangement has no internal structure
```

It is:

```text
the current Frame-occurrence claim does not yet require
or justify a finer reconstruction.
```

If a later source-supported question concerns unequal approval capacity, repeated gatekeeping practices, internal delays, or competing procedural paths, the status may be reopened. Reopening would require a new declared question and source route. The mere possibility of further detail is insufficient.

#### Reopening is not automatic after pressure or counterexample

A counterexample can pressure provisional elementarity, but it does not always warrant decomposition. The pressure may concern another Frame, another temporal period, another source object, or a rival occurrence typing. It may show that the current claim requires reduction without showing which finer structure should replace it. It may also be unsupported.

```text
counterpressure
≠ decomposition warrant automatically
```

Provisional elementarity is withdrawn responsibly only where a traceable finer difference is expected to change the current reconstruction and the sources can support testing that difference. Otherwise the correct route may be to retain the coarse object, reduce the claim, suspend judgment, or stop.

<a id="18-3-the-compressed-object"></a>

### 18.3 The Compressed Object

A **compressed object** is an occurrence or composite treated as one analytical unit while the current representation leaves some internal distinctions, relations, or temporal structures unrepresented or unresolved.

Compression is neutral at the point of definition. It may be deliberate, necessary, source-forced, claim-relative, inherited from an earlier valid `COMPOSE`, or retained because finer distinctions have no demonstrated purchase. A compressed object is not thereby defective, false, homogeneous, or simple.

```text
compressed object
≠ simple object
≠ erroneous object
≠ internally homogeneous object
```

The definition also avoids a hidden-truth claim. To say that internal structure remains unresolved is not to claim that a specific concealed component architecture already exists and merely awaits discovery.

```text
unresolved internal structure
≠ hidden microstructure asserted as fact
```

A responsible source declaration distinguishes at least three domains:

1. **Known source-side structure** — distinctions directly supported by the current materials.
2. **Currently unrepresented or unresolved structure** — bounded categories of possible internal relevance that have not yet been reconstructed.
3. **Unsupported content** — components or relations that the analysis may not populate by inference from the coarse label alone.

The second domain is not a license to fill the third.

#### Object category, source typing, and current function remain distinct

A compressed source candidate must not collapse three different declarations:

```text
object category
≠ source-side operator typing
≠ current/coarser analytical function
```

A source object may be categorized as a composite structure while carrying several operator-typed occurrences. A Path may be typed through temporal and asymmetry occurrences while its current function is simply to support a bounded historical-order claim. A structured Non-Event may remain one occurrence category even though its expectation, window, positive sub-events, and residue later require finer reconstruction.

The singular field `origin_type` in a minimal record view must therefore not be read as proof that an object possesses exactly one true operator type. It declares the source-side typing relevant to the present transformation question. Compatible or rival typings remain visible where supported.

```text
one declared origin type for this question
≠ exclusive true type of the reference object
```

#### Eligible compressed source categories

Without yet executing `DECOMPOSE`, Chapter 18 permits the following categories to become source candidates:

- concrete operator-typed occurrences;
- event-like objects and structured Non-Event occurrences;
- transitions and transition clusters;
- institutional or relational arrangements reconstructed as bounded occurrences or composites;
- Paths and Trajectories whose source identity and current function remain declared;
- composites produced by a prior `COMPOSE` occurrence;
- origin-typed objects carrying a bounded contextual function from an earlier `PROJECT_AS` record, provided the new source declaration states which retained object is being opened and does not decompose the target function as if it were a primitive.

Eligibility is not admissibility. A candidate can still be too weakly sourced, wrongly framed, source-indifferent, already sufficient at its present resolution, or impossible to retain as the same reference object.

#### Known and unresolved structure in the review arrangement

Return again to the review arrangement. At the current source resolution, the following is known:

- an agenda bounded admissible decision matters;
- a role list identified formal participation positions;
- the transcript records admission and exclusion decisions during the session.

The current representation may leave unresolved:

- whether the admission boundary was reproduced by one rule or several interacting practices;
- whether role allocation and final approval capacity materially altered the Frame claim;
- whether a repeated practice, rather than the single documented session, carried the relevant stability;
- whether a rival Asymmetry typing bears equal or greater load for another declared question.

These are unresolved categories, not discovered components. Chapter 18 may name the questions. It may not claim their answers.

```text
bounded unresolved question
≠ populated internal model
```

#### C18-COMPOSITE-01 — a prior composite as source candidate

Suppose a prior `COMPOSE` record formed a review-governance arrangement from source-supported agenda, approval-allocation, and routing occurrences. The resulting composite may later become a compressed source object if a new claim asks how its constitutive relations distribute decision access or reproduce stability.

The later source declaration must retain:

- the same composite reference;
- the prior Formation Rule and source lineage;
- the existing five-part Loss;
- the fact that the original composition was selective and non-invertible;
- the current composite function under test.

It may not assume:

```text
DECOMPOSE(COMPOSE(X)) = X
```

The later opening is a new transformation occurrence. It cannot restore information marked excluded, uncertain, or irrecoverable merely because the composite once had finer sources. The composite is eligible as a source candidate; exact inverse recovery is not.

#### C18-PATH-01 — a Path as a compressed source candidate

A provisionally locked Path may be treated as one unit for a claim about the actually traversed order of configurations and transitions. A later question may ask whether one apparently continuous transition should be reconstructed through source-supported intermediate configurations or parallel subpaths.

Chapter 18 can declare the Path as a compressed source candidate only if:

- the same historical reference and temporal scope remain identifiable;
- the original Path selection and Loss remain visible;
- the proposed opening concerns claim-relevant internal structure rather than an attempt to rescue a failed Path claim;
- Chapter 19 still declares the granularity relation;
- Chapter 20 still performs the separate `DECOMPOSE` test.

```text
Path available as source
≠ finer Path already established
```

#### C18-TRAJ-01 — a Trajectory as a compressed source candidate

A Trajectory may compress years of sedimented change, persistent residue, altered action corridors, binding shifts, and differentiated asymmetry. It can therefore be a legitimate source candidate where the current Trajectory claim depends on internal periods or carriers that remain unresolved.

The source declaration must preserve the Trajectory's:

- historical reference;
- periodization and competing-construction status;
- Source–Result Dependency;
- current historical-load claim;
- Loss and source uncertainty.

A finer reconstruction may later support, revise, differentiate, or reject the Trajectory claim. It may not assume that every long or internally varied Trajectory should be decomposed, or that finer periodization has higher truth.

```text
historically rich object
≠ automatic demand for finer resolution
```

#### C18-LAMBDA-01 — a structured Non-Event as source candidate

A documented expected review that did not occur within its warranted window may be reconstructed as one `Λ` occurrence. The current representation may compress reminders, acknowledgements, rescheduling acts, responsibility handoffs, and repeated non-decisions.

A later opening must preserve the governing expectation, Frame, window, and non-realization. Positive sub-events cannot be substituted for the expected event, and missing information cannot be converted into `Λ` content.

```text
finer positive sub-events
≠ realization of the governing expected event
```

Chapter 18 may declare the Non-Event occurrence as a compressed candidate. It does not yet determine which sub-events or relations are constitutive, whether they alter the `Λ` typing, or whether the sources support a finer reconstruction.

#### C18-OMEGA-01 — distributed asymmetry as source candidate

A coarse institutional arrangement may be typed as an `Ω` occurrence because decision capacity, exposure, reversibility, or exit burden is distributed unequally. The apparent asymmetry may be concentrated in one role, distributed across several local relations, partially offset, temporally variable, or insufficiently supported.

Chapter 18 can identify distributed asymmetry as a reason the coarse occurrence may require later opening, but it cannot infer local carriers from the `Ω` label itself.

```text
coarse Asymmetry typing
≠ proof of a specific internal distribution
```

The source candidate remains bounded to the evidence. Chapter 20 must later reconstruct components and relations jointly; a list of local differences without their relation to the coarse `Ω` claim would not suffice.

<a id="18-4-operator-type-versus-decomposable-occurrence"></a>

### 18.4 Operator Type versus Decomposable Occurrence

The type boundary established in Chapter 1 remains absolute within STRATA operations:

```text
DECOMPOSE opens occurrences and composites.
DECOMPOSE never opens Δ–Ψ operator types.
```

An operator type is a canonical function in PMS Base. Its sign, name, definition, ordering, and dependencies belong to the Base grammar. It is not an empirical aggregate whose internal pieces can be discovered by increasing resolution.

A concrete occurrence is different. It is a reference-bound, source-bound, Frame-bound, coordinate-bound, and claim-bound reconstruction that may be treated as provisionally compressed. Its internal carriers, relations, temporal structure, or competing typings may be tested under finer resolution if the later SUB burdens are met.

```text
Frame operator type
≠ documented Frame-typed occurrence

Asymmetry operator type
≠ documented Asymmetry-typed occurrence

Non-Event operator type
≠ documented Non-Event occurrence
```

#### Type critique is not prohibited; operation confusion is

The non-decomposability of operator types does not make PMS Base immune from theoretical criticism. The minimality, ordering, dependency structure, explanatory adequacy, or empirical productivity of an operator may be challenged at the level of PMS theory and versioned Base revision.

What is prohibited is misclassifying that theoretical critique as a STRATA `DECOMPOSE` occurrence.

```text
critique or revision of PMS Base
≠ DECOMPOSE(operator type)
```

STRATA cannot change the Base by representing `□`, `Ω`, or `Θ` as composites of newly invented suboperators. Such a move would alter the grammar and exceed STRATA authority.

#### C18-TYPE-01 — attempted decomposition of the Frame operator type

An analyst proposes:

```text
Frame = access + boundary + role + norm
```

and calls the list a decomposition of `□`.

The move fails before granularity analysis. Access rules, boundaries, roles, and norms may be concrete structures that carry a particular Frame-typed occurrence. They are not thereby constituents of the Frame operator type.

The correct distinction is:

```text
which source-supported practices carry this Frame occurrence?
→ possible later DECOMPOSE question

what smaller operators constitute □ itself?
→ not a STRATA DECOMPOSE question
```

Continuation as a STRATA decomposition is prohibited. The failure does not settle whether the proposed concepts are theoretically useful elsewhere; it only protects the operation and authority boundary.

#### Occurrence eligibility does not establish decomposition warrant

A concrete operator-typed occurrence can be a source candidate, but candidacy alone does not pass the Relevance Floor, source ceiling, reference-continuity test, or later operation procedure.

```text
operator-typed occurrence
→ eligible source category

eligible source category
≠ admissible DECOMPOSE occurrence
```

The occurrence may already be sufficiently reconstructed. Sources may not support internal relations. The finer distinction may answer another question or substitute another object. A rival typing may be more appropriate than decomposition. These possibilities remain open.

#### C18-OCC-01 — Frame-typed occurrence admitted as a bounded candidate

The documented review arrangement is retained as the reference object. Its relevant source-side typing is declared as a Frame occurrence for the current question. The available material supports the agenda boundary, role list, and admission practice. It also leaves bounded questions about how those structures jointly reproduce the decision boundary.

The Chapter-18 result is only:

```text
the review arrangement is an eligible compressed occurrence candidate
for a later source-supported decomposition test.
```

It is not:

```text
the internal components have been discovered;
the target granularity is selected;
the Frame claim is confirmed;
the Asymmetry claim is rejected;
or DECOMPOSE is admissible already.
```

The same reference object may also support a compatible or rival Asymmetry typing. Chapter 18 does not force one exclusive origin type where the sources support multiple functions. The later decomposition question must state which source-side claim is under test and preserve the other supported typing as alternative or counterpressure.

#### WP1 completion boundary

Sections 18.1–18.4 establish:

- SUB as the Part governing controlled finer reconstruction, distinct from `DECOMPOSE` as an operation;
- the Part-II entry requirement of an identifiable, source-bounded occurrence or composite and a claim-relevant opening question;
- provisional elementarity as an analytical status without ontological indivisibility;
- the compressed object as a neutral source category rather than a simple, false, or already decomposed object;
- the separation of object category, source-side typing, and current/coarser function;
- the separation of known, unresolved, and unsupported internal content;
- eligibility of occurrences, composites, Non-Events, Paths, and Trajectories without inherited decomposition permission;
- the permanent STRATA prohibition on decomposing Δ–Ψ operator types;
- the possibility of multiple or rival occurrence typings without Base revision;
- the protected ownership of Chapter 19 granularity mechanics and Chapter 20 operation procedure.

The eight WP1 Pressure Duties are represented in canonical prose. They remain methodological pressure tests and later integrated-case seeds. They are not standalone `03_cases/*` artifacts, empirical findings, completed transformation records, training data, or application outputs.

The chapter-production result remains:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

This classifies the bounded state of the canonical WP1 production. It does not classify a `DECOMPOSE` occurrence, because none has been executed.

The controlled handoff is:

```text
WP1 source-candidate architecture
→ WP2 necessity and insufficiency of compression
→ reasons to decompose and reasons not to decompose
```

WP1 completion therefore does not establish source insufficiency, target granularity, component identity, component relations, resolution gain, source-function outcome, Chapter-18 completion, SUB lock, or additional authority.

<a id="18-5-why-compression-is-necessary"></a>

### 18.5 Why Compression Is Necessary

Compression is a necessary condition of bounded reconstruction. Every analysis selects a reference object, distinction set, temporal scope, source basis, and claim boundary. It therefore leaves some available detail outside the active representation, holds some relations together as one unit, and stops internal differentiation at a declared point. This remains true even where no explicit `COMPOSE` operation has been performed.

```text
analytical compression
≠ COMPOSE automatically
```

`COMPOSE` is a declared STRATA operation that forms a new composite analytical object from many or sequential source structures. Compression is broader and more basic as a representational condition: a transcript can be treated as one occurrence, a transition can be represented without every intermediate moment, and a prior composite can be retained as one source object for a later question. Chapter 18 does not add compression as a fourth operation. It asks whether the present compressed treatment is warranted for the current claim.

Compression can be necessary because it preserves structures that would become harder to reconstruct under indiscriminate atomization. A bounded representation may keep visible:

- the reference identity of the object;
- the relation among roles, events, Non-Events, or transitions;
- a temporal order that would be obscured by isolated data points;
- a comparable unit across cases or periods;
- the current/coarser function under test;
- uncertainty that should not be filled with speculative detail;
- a claim ceiling matched to the actual source precision;
- the point at which further analysis should stop.

The relevant contrast is not between a compressed falsehood and an uncompressed truth. It is between different representations with different burdens, losses, and analytical uses.

```text
compression
≠ error

compression
≠ concealment automatically

less internal detail
≠ weaker reconstruction automatically
```

#### Compression as relation preservation

A coarse representation can preserve a relation more effectively than a list of fragments. A review arrangement may be represented as one Frame-typed occurrence because the bounded claim concerns how an agenda distinguishes admissible decision matters from matters routed elsewhere. If the analysis immediately separates every utterance, access event, document edit, role movement, and timing variation, the governing boundary may become less—not more—legible.

This does not mean that the local occurrences are unreal or irrelevant. It means that relevance is claim-relative. The coarse unit preserves the relation currently being tested, while finer distinctions remain outside the active representation unless they are needed.

```text
preserved relation at coarse granularity
≠ denial of internal variation
```

The same principle applies to temporal objects. A Path needs selective compression to remain a Path rather than a complete chronology. A Trajectory needs periodization to retain historical load rather than becoming an unbounded archive. A structured Non-Event needs a bounded expectation and window rather than every surrounding positive occurrence. A composite needs selection and formation rules rather than a flat inventory of all source material.

#### Compression as uncertainty discipline

Compression can also prevent unsupported completion. When sources establish that a review boundary existed but do not establish how each role reproduced it, the responsible coarse representation can retain the Frame occurrence and mark the internal reproduction relation as unresolved. Premature opening could populate a plausible but unsupported mechanism.

```text
coarse but source-bounded
may be stronger than
fine but source-invented
```

This is especially important where missing source material could be mistaken for a structured Non-Event, where distributed asymmetry could be inferred from one visible local difference, or where a Trajectory could be divided into periods selected only by the later result. Compression can keep the claim within the available trace.

#### Compression as comparison and calibration control

Comparison requires units that are sufficiently stable across the compared cases. If one review arrangement is represented at the level of institutional rules while another is represented at the level of individual utterances, apparent differences may arise from granularity mismatch rather than from the source objects. A justified coarse representation can therefore preserve comparability until a declared granularity relation makes finer comparison possible.

Calibration can also worsen with unnecessary detail. More distinctions create more classification decisions, more uncertain boundaries, and more opportunities for source drift. Where those decisions add no praxeological discrimination, they reduce reliability without increasing warrant.

#### C18-COMP-01 — necessary coarse compression

A documented review arrangement contains an agenda, a role list, a sequence of admissions, several comments, and a final routing decision. The current claim is limited: the arrangement functioned as a Frame occurrence that separated matters eligible for decision from matters deferred to another process.

The agenda boundary, role allocation, and routing record support that claim. Utterance-level coding, second-by-second timing, and reconstruction of every preparatory exchange would add detail, but none is required to establish the bounded Frame claim. Treating the arrangement as one occurrence preserves the relation among admission, decision scope, and routing while keeping unresolved internal reproduction questions visible.

The correct Chapter-18 result is:

```text
the coarse representation is necessary and sufficient
for the present bounded claim.
```

It is not:

```text
the arrangement has no internal structure;
the occurrence can never be opened;
or the Frame typing is immune from later pressure.
```

Compression here is an analytical achievement: it keeps the reference, relation, source precision, and claim ceiling aligned.

#### Compression and the five-part Loss discipline

Even outside an explicit `COMPOSE` record, the logic of Loss remains relevant. A compressed source declaration should be able to distinguish, at least provisionally:

```yaml
loss:
  preserved:
  compressed:
  excluded:
  uncertain:
  irrecoverable:
```

Chapter 18 does not yet complete a `DECOMPOSE` Loss record. It requires awareness that the current source object already has a representational history. Some internal material may be preserved in source links, some summarized, some outside scope, some uncertain, and some no longer recoverable. A later opening cannot assume that all prior detail remains available.

Necessary compression therefore establishes a positive baseline. SUB does not begin from a presumption that the source object is defective. It begins from a question of adequacy:

> Does the current unit carry the current claim with sufficient source trace and praxeological discrimination?

Only if a bounded reason places that adequacy under pressure does decomposition become a live possibility.

<a id="18-6-why-compression-can-become-insufficient"></a>

### 18.6 Why Compression Can Become Insufficient

Compression becomes insufficient when the current representation prevents a warranted reconstruction of a claim-relevant difference that the sources can potentially support. The decisive condition is not that more detail exists. It is that an unresolved internal distinction may alter the current claim, reference relation, burden distribution, temporal reconstruction, source-function status, or relevant alternatives.

```text
available finer detail
≠ insufficient compression

source-supported claim-relevant difference
→ legitimate insufficiency pressure
```

Insufficiency is therefore relational. It exists between:

- a bounded source object;
- a current/coarser claim or function;
- an unresolved distinction;
- an available source route;
- and an expected change in reconstruction.

Without all five, “the object is complex” remains a description of possible richness, not a reason to open it.

#### Signals of possible insufficiency

A compressed representation may require pressure when:

- a coarse function is asserted but its source-supported carriers cannot be identified sufficiently for the claim;
- incompatible internal findings are presented as one homogeneous occurrence;
- an internal temporal order is constitutive but disappears in the coarse representation;
- distributed costs, access, exposure, or exit burdens are collapsed into a single undifferentiated `Ω` label;
- repeated practices disappear behind a stable macro-description;
- a transition is asserted although the relation between source and target configurations remains unexplained at the current resolution;
- competing internal Paths would change the meaning of the same endpoint;
- a counterfactual change in a plausible internal relation would alter the coarse function, but the relation remains unresolved;
- a source-supported counterpressure specifically burdens the coarse typing.

These are signals, not automatic findings. Each must still be connected to the sources and to a discriminating claim difference.

#### Carrier pressure without hidden-carrier presumption

A stable function may appear analytically under-carried. For example, a review arrangement may be described as consistently excluding certain matters, while the coarse sources do not yet show whether the exclusion is reproduced by the agenda, chairing practice, role permissions, routing conventions, repeated non-decisions, or some combination.

The responsible formulation is:

```text
the current representation does not yet identify
which source-supported relations carry the bounded function claim.
```

It is not:

```text
hidden carriers certainly exist in the hypothesized form.
```

Chapter 18 may state the insufficiency question. Chapter 19 must later define the finer distinction relation, and Chapter 20 must reconstruct components and relations if the source route warrants it.

#### Internal heterogeneity and coarse-function pressure

A compressed object can remain one reference object while containing source-supported internal differences. Those differences matter where the coarse representation converts heterogeneity into a claim of uniformity.

Examples include:

- one role having formal access but no practical decision capacity;
- a Path containing two periods with different continuation conditions;
- a Non-Event window containing repeated acknowledgements but no governing expected event;
- a composite whose apparent stability depends on different local practices at different times;
- a Trajectory whose historical-load claim changes if a supposed continuous period contains a source-supported rupture.

The issue is not that heterogeneous objects must always be decomposed. The issue is whether the current claim depends on suppressing a difference that the sources make material.

#### C18-INSUFF-01 — a compressed claim-relevant distinction

The same review arrangement is now used for a stronger claim: decision access was evenly available to all listed roles because the formal agenda applied to everyone.

The source bundle, however, contains a role rule showing that only two roles could place an item on the decision agenda, while the others could comment only after admission. This distinction is internal to the same arrangement, directly source-supported, and relevant to the claim about equal decision access.

The coarse representation becomes insufficient because it treats shared participation as equivalent to shared agenda-setting capacity. A finer reconstruction could change:

- the `Ω` typing of access and burden;
- the relation between formal participation and effective decision capacity;
- the warranted scope of the equality claim;
- the interpretation of repeated deferrals.

Chapter 18 may therefore record a legitimate reason to test finer reconstruction. It still does not declare the full component model, select the target granularity, or decide the eventual `DECOMPOSE` output.

```text
specific source-supported distinction
+ material pressure on the coarse claim
→ decomposition warrant may be testable
```

#### Insufficiency does not guarantee successful decomposition

Even a well-founded pressure point may later produce:

- a confirmed coarse function with better source trace;
- a refined or differentiated function;
- partial preservation;
- rejection of the coarse function;
- a resolution-neutral result;
- source overreach;
- mandatory stop;
- or Non-Capture.

Those are later outcomes. Chapter 18 identifies why the current compression may be inadequate; it does not predetermine what finer reconstruction will show.

```text
compression under legitimate pressure
≠ failed source function already
≠ successful DECOMPOSE already
```

<a id="18-7-reasons-to-decompose"></a>

### 18.7 Reasons to Decompose

A reason to decompose is a bounded proposal to test whether a finer reconstruction of the same reference object changes a warranted praxis claim. It must be formulated before components are treated as established and before a target granularity is selected.

A complete Chapter-18 reason contains six elements:

1. **coarse claim under pressure** — which current typing, relation, function, or temporal claim is being tested;
2. **expected distinction** — which unresolved internal difference may matter;
3. **source route** — which materials could support that distinction and its relations;
4. **praxeological gain condition** — what change would count as added discrimination;
5. **neutrality condition** — what result would leave the coarse reconstruction materially unchanged;
6. **stop condition** — where source, relevance, reference, or claim limits prohibit continuation.

```text
reason to decompose
= claim-relative expected difference
+ source route
+ gain condition
+ neutrality condition
+ stop condition
```

A reason is not a prediction that the proposed internal model is true. It is a disciplined statement of why the model is worth testing and how failure to gain would be recognized.

#### Legitimate reason families

Legitimate reasons include, but are not limited to:

- **unclear formation of a stable function:** the coarse function is source-supported at the aggregate level, but the practices or relations carrying it remain materially unresolved;
- **contradictory internal findings:** source-supported internal occurrences pull against a homogeneous coarse typing;
- **distributed asymmetry:** the coarse `Ω` claim depends on role, access, exposure, cost, timing, or exit relations that may be differently distributed;
- **claim-relevant internal temporality:** order, duration, delay, repetition, or rupture inside the source object may change its function;
- **insufficient transition explanation:** a coarse transition omits intermediate configurations or relations required to connect source and target;
- **competing internal Paths:** more than one source-supported route through the same object would alter historical interpretation or current alternatives;
- **specific failure of a coarse claim:** a counterpressure identifies a traceable internal reason why the current claim may be too broad or wrongly typed;
- **source-function test:** finer reconstruction is needed to determine whether the current function is confirmed, refined, differentiated, partially preserved, rejected, or underdetermined.

Each reason must remain tied to the same reference object. If the proposed analysis instead constructs a new temporal object, changes Frame without preserving the original relation, or assigns a contextual target function, another operation or chapter boundary is involved.

#### Expected praxeological difference

The expected difference must concern praxis structure rather than descriptive abundance. It may involve:

- changed action corridors;
- different access or exit conditions;
- altered cost, burden, or exposure allocation;
- role differentiation;
- a different temporal order or branch structure;
- a revised expectation or Non-Event account;
- different binding or responsibility relations;
- a changed source-function claim;
- newly visible Loss or uncertainty.

The expected difference need not imply intervention or practical manipulability. A distinction can have praxeological purchase by changing the warranted reconstruction of an irreversible, inaccessible, or non-controllable structure.

#### Source route and relation burden

A source route must identify more than additional documents. It must indicate how the materials could support the proposed internal distinctions and their relation to the coarse object.

```text
more sources
≠ finer reconstruction automatically

component evidence without relation evidence
≠ complete DECOMPOSE support
```

For the review arrangement, an agenda version history may support changes in admission categories; a role policy may support differentiated access; meeting records may support repeated deferral; a routing log may support continuation outside the review. None alone establishes how these elements jointly carry the coarse Frame or Asymmetry claim. The relation burden remains explicit.

#### Counterfactual sensitivity as a reason test

A proposed distinction is stronger where a relevant change in it would alter the coarse reconstruction. For example:

> If all roles had equal agenda-setting capacity rather than comment-only access, would the current Asymmetry claim change?

If no plausible source-side change in the proposed distinction would affect the claim, the distinction may lack praxeological purchase. If the claim changes regardless of every relevant source variation, it may be overelastic.

Counterfactual sensitivity does not decide empirical truth. It tests whether the proposed finer distinction is constitutive enough to justify inquiry.

#### C18-COUNTER-01 — counterexample without a finer source route

An analyst presents one meeting in which a non-chair role successfully introduced an agenda item and concludes that the coarse access claim must be decomposed into a detailed model of every participant's influence.

The occurrence is genuine counterpressure. It may require reduction, qualification, or rejection of a universal claim that only chairs can introduce items. But no source route has yet been provided for reconstructing the full internal influence structure across the review arrangement. The single counterexample does not establish which components, relations, time span, or target granularity would form a valid decomposition.

The correct response is:

```text
counterexample burdens the coarse claim
≠ counterexample automatically warrants DECOMPOSE
```

The coarse claim may need immediate reduction while the finer reconstruction remains unsupported. A failed claim cannot be protected by changing granularity, and a counterexample cannot manufacture a source route that does not exist.

#### Reasons remain proposals until Chapter 20

At the end of Section 18.7, a valid reason establishes only that a later decomposition test may be warranted. It does not establish:

- the final distinction set;
- component identities;
- component relations;
- target granularity;
- the preservation result;
- the Output Class;
- or increased authority.

```text
well-formed reason to decompose
≠ admissible DECOMPOSE result
```

<a id="18-8-reasons-not-to-decompose"></a>

### 18.8 Reasons Not to Decompose

Not decomposing can be the correct positive decision. STRATA does not require every eligible occurrence or composite to be opened, and it does not treat a retained coarse representation as an analytical failure.

A no-decomposition decision means:

```text
given the current reference, Frame, granularity, sources, claim,
and expected difference, finer reconstruction is not presently warranted.
```

It does not mean that the object is simple, absolutely indivisible, permanently closed, or immune from a later claim with changed grounds.

#### Five principal no-decomposition routes

1. **Coarse sufficiency**  
   The current representation already supports the bounded claim with adequate source trace and discrimination. Finer detail would not improve the claim being made.

2. **No praxeological purchase**  
   Additional distinctions may be describable, but they do not change roles, costs, alternatives, temporal relations, source-function status, Loss, or another warranted praxis dimension.

3. **Source insufficiency**  
   The available materials do not support the proposed internal distinctions or their constitutive relations. Continuing would populate an internal model beyond the source ceiling.

4. **Claim irrelevance**  
   The proposed internal structure may matter for another question, but not for the bounded claim currently under review.

5. **Calibration loss**  
   Additional resolution would reduce comparability, classification stability, or source precision without compensating praxeological gain.

Further reasons include operation mismatch, reference substitution, repeated neutral refinement without changed grounds, and fragmentation without functional relation.

#### Coarse sufficiency is claim-bounded

A source object may be sufficiently reconstructed for one claim while remaining open for another. The review arrangement can be sufficient as a Frame occurrence for agenda-boundary analysis and insufficient for a claim about equal decision access. These results are compatible because the claim boundaries differ.

```text
sufficient for claim A
≠ sufficient for every claim
```

A no-decomposition decision must therefore name the claim it protects from unnecessary expansion. It may not convert local sufficiency into permanent closure.

#### C18-NODEC-01 — coarse reconstruction already sufficient

The review arrangement is again tested only for whether an explicit agenda separated decision matters from matters routed elsewhere. The agenda, minutes, and routing record support the boundary. A proposed finer coding of speaking time, sentence form, and seating order would not alter that limited Frame claim, and no source-backed argument connects those details to the boundary under test.

The correct decision is:

```text
no decomposition presently warranted for this claim.
```

This decision preserves the coarse representation, source precision, and Optional Stop capability. It does not establish `resolution_neutral`, because no `DECOMPOSE` occurrence has been executed and no finer reconstruction has been compared. Nor does it create an eleventh Output Class.

```text
no-decomposition entry decision
≠ resolution_neutral result
≠ permanent undecomposability
```

#### C18-DETAIL-01 — detail appetite below the Relevance Floor

An analyst requests utterance-by-utterance grammatical coding of the same review solely because the transcript permits it. No expected change is stated for the Frame claim, access relation, temporal order, cost distribution, or source-function status. The additional distinctions would produce a denser description but no identifiable praxeological purchase.

The proposal falls below the Relevance Floor:

```text
resolution without praxeological purchase
```

The correct response is not to reward detail volume with analytical status. The current coarse representation remains preferable for the claim. A later request could reopen the question only by providing a new bounded claim and a traceable expected difference.

#### C18-SOURCE-01 — unsupported internal reconstruction

A summary record states that some matters were repeatedly deferred, but the underlying agenda versions, role rules, and routing logs are unavailable. An analyst proposes reconstructing a detailed internal sequence of who blocked which item and how the responsibility handoff occurred.

The coarse source supports a bounded repeated-deferral claim. It does not support the proposed role-specific component and relation model. Continuing would cross the source ceiling and could turn missing information into positive occurrence or Non-Event content.

The correct Chapter-18 decision is to withhold decomposition. Depending on the later governance record, the route may require a source-bounded claim, Mandatory Stop on the unsupported internal claim, or Non-Capture if no stable source object or route can be maintained. Section 18.8 does not pre-assign that later class; it preserves the stop boundary.

```text
source insufficiency
cannot be compensated by
formal detail, plausibility, or model elegance
```

#### Operation mismatch as a no-decomposition reason

A proposed “opening” may actually perform another analytical move:

- adding earlier and later transitions may construct a new Path through `COMPOSE`;
- changing the institutional question to a participant-experience question may change Frame;
- treating the review arrangement as a higher-level gate in another process may require `PROJECT_AS`;
- comparing two alternative periodizations may require rival compositions rather than one decomposition.

In these cases, rejecting `DECOMPOSE` does not reject analysis. It preserves operation integrity and requires the new move to be declared and tested on its own terms.

#### Calibration loss and infinite regress

A decomposition request can become self-defeating when every component is immediately treated as another compressed object requiring further opening, without a new claim-relevant difference. STRATA contains no universal smallest unit and no obligation to continue recursively.

```text
recursive possibility
≠ recursive necessity
```

Further resolution should stop when:

- the current claim is already carried;
- added distinctions become neutral;
- relations become less traceable than fragments;
- source precision is exceeded;
- comparison becomes unstable;
- or the analysis loses the reference object it was meant to reconstruct.

The full Optional Stop, Mandatory Stop, and Non-Capture integration belongs to Sections 18.9–18.10 and later LIMITS. WP2 establishes that reasons not to decompose are constitutive of SUB entry discipline rather than exceptions to it.

#### WP2 completion boundary

Sections 18.5–18.8 establish:

- compression as a necessary representational condition distinct from `COMPOSE` as an operation;
- coarse representation as capable of preserving relations, uncertainty, comparability, temporal orientation, and claim precision;
- insufficiency only where a source-supported internal distinction may change a warranted praxis reconstruction;
- carrier pressure without asserting hidden carriers as fact;
- a six-part reason-to-decompose structure with gain, neutrality, and stop conditions;
- counterexamples as pressure on claims without automatic decomposition warrant;
- coarse sufficiency, no purchase, source insufficiency, claim irrelevance, and calibration loss as legitimate no-decomposition routes;
- no-decomposition as a positive bounded entry decision rather than `resolution_neutral`, permanent closure, or a new Output Class;
- operation mismatch and recursive detail appetite as reasons to withhold `DECOMPOSE`;
- continued ownership of target granularity by Chapter 19, operation execution by Chapter 20, resolution classification by Chapter 25, and full preservation/Stop/Non-Capture integration by WP3.

The six WP2 Pressure Duties are now executed in canonical prose:

```text
necessary compression
insufficient compression
below-floor detail appetite
coarse sufficiency
source ceiling
counterexample without source route
```

No target granularity has been selected, no component set has been declared, no `DECOMPOSE` occurrence has been executed, and no standalone case or test artifact has been produced.

<a id="18-9-preservation-requirement"></a>

### 18.9 Preservation Requirement

A valid SUB entry cannot replace its source object with an unconnected inventory of finer details. The reference object that motivated the opening must remain reconstructible across the later granularity change, and the current/coarser analytical function must remain visible as a test target. Otherwise, the analysis has not decomposed the same object; it has substituted another object, changed the question without declaration, or fragmented the source beyond functional continuity.

The preservation requirement is therefore relational rather than additive:

```text
preserve the source object
≠ copy every source detail

identify finer material
≠ preserve the source relation
```

Preservation requires the later reconstruction to explain, with source-bounded support:

- how the proposed finer structures relate to one another;
- how those relations connect to the declared source reference;
- which structures carry, modify, contest, or fail to carry the current/coarser function;
- which source-side identity conditions remain stable;
- which changes are products of finer resolution rather than a new Frame, new object, new operation, or new target function;
- which Loss and uncertainty survive the opening;
- and why the result may still be discussed as a reconstruction of the same historical or analytical object.

Chapter 18 establishes this burden before components are identified. Chapter 19 will declare the source-to-target granularity relation. Chapter 20 will test components, component relations, source support, and source-function effect. The preservation requirement does not infer those results in advance.

#### Reference continuity is not nominal continuity

Reusing the same name is insufficient. A finer account of “the review arrangement,” “the delayed review,” or “the trajectory” preserves reference only where the source lineage and constitutive relations remain traceable. If the analysis begins with one institutional review and ends with a general model of organizational voice, the label may remain similar while the reference object has changed.

Reference continuity may be carried by:

- stable document or record lineage;
- a declared temporal and institutional boundary;
- identifiable occurrence identity;
- a preserved event, Non-Event, transition, Path, or Trajectory reference;
- explicit relation between coarse and finer representations;
- preserved uncertainty about disputed identity conditions.

It is not carried by semantic resemblance alone.

```text
same label
≠ same reference object

related topic
≠ preserved reference identity
```

A lawful decomposition may reveal that the source boundary was too broad, too narrow, or internally unstable. In that case, the revision must be declared. Preservation does not prohibit boundary correction; it prohibits silent substitution.

#### Functional continuity is a test relation

The current/coarser function is the analytical role or claim presently carried by the source object. It may be a Frame-typed occurrence, an Asymmetry claim, a bounded Non-Event structure, a Path, a Trajectory, or another source-side function. Its preservation means that the later finer reconstruction remains answerable to it.

The finer reconstruction must ask:

- Do the source-supported finer relations carry the coarse function?
- Do they carry it only under a narrower claim?
- Do distinct internal structures carry different parts of it?
- Does internal heterogeneity revise or defeat the coarse function?
- Does available evidence leave the relation underdetermined?

The possible later effects include:

```text
confirmed
refined
internally differentiated
partially preserved
rejected
underdetermined
```

These are possible source-function effects, not Chapter-18 findings and not additional canonical Output Classes. Chapter 20 owns their operational determination and Chapter 25 owns later resolution classification.

```text
source function retained as test target
≠ source function guaranteed as result
```

#### C18-PRES-01 — preservation without immunization

The review arrangement is declared as a Frame-typed source occurrence because the circulated agenda and routing practice bounded which matters could be decided in the review. A later finer reconstruction examines role-specific admission, agenda-setting capacity, repeated deferral, and routing outside the session.

A preservation-compliant reconstruction must retain:

- the same documented review arrangement;
- the agenda-boundary claim under test;
- the relevant source lineage and temporal scope;
- the relation between admission practices and the coarse Frame occurrence;
- uncertainty where records do not identify how every matter was routed.

It may then find that the coarse Frame claim is supported, narrowed, internally differentiated, or unsupported. Preservation does not require a favorable result. It requires that the finer account remain capable of testing the original claim rather than abandoning it.

```text
preserve the question and reference
≠ preserve the answer
```

If the finer record shows that agenda boundaries were routinely bypassed, the coarse Frame claim may require reduction or rejection. Calling that result “loss of preservation” would be incorrect. Preservation has succeeded if the same source object and claim were genuinely tested and the adverse result remains traceable.

#### Source preservation versus source immunization

Source preservation becomes immunization where the coarse function is treated as true by definition and every finer finding is forced to support it. Typical immunizing moves include:

- redefining all contrary detail as irrelevant because it conflicts with the source typing;
- selecting only components that reproduce the expected coarse function;
- treating internal heterogeneity as noise without a claim-relative test;
- changing the target granularity whenever a finer result burdens the claim;
- converting a failed function into a new label while suppressing the failure;
- appealing to reference continuity to block revision;
- using Non-Capture to protect an unsupported coarse claim.

These moves violate anti-immunization and the rule:

```text
new transformation = new testable claim
```

A failed source claim remains failed unless separately retested under new grounds. A changed Frame, granularity, level, composition, or contextual function does not erase it.

#### C18-FUNC-01 — uncertain source function remains uncertain

The review sources clearly document a recurring boundary between agenda items and routed matters, but they do not establish whether the arrangement consistently functioned as an Asymmetry structure across all roles. Some records show unequal agenda-setting capacity; others leave informal influence and exceptions unresolved.

The source declaration therefore records:

```text
current Frame function: bounded and supported
current Asymmetry function: provisional / underdetermined
```

A later decomposition may test the Asymmetry claim. Chapter 18 must not collapse the two functions, select one exclusive true typing, or upgrade the uncertain function because finer analysis is planned.

```text
function under test
≠ function already established

multiple source-side typings
≠ one fused origin type
```

The uncertainty is part of the source object and must survive into Chapter 19 and Chapter 20. Formal completeness cannot convert it into positive structure.

#### Preservation includes Loss and prior failure

The source object may already carry representational Loss from selection, compression, earlier `COMPOSE`, missing records, or disputed periodization. A later `DECOMPOSE` cannot promise inverse restoration.

At source entry, the analysis must preserve awareness of:

```yaml
loss:
  preserved:
  compressed:
  excluded:
  uncertain:
  irrecoverable:
```

Chapter 18 does not complete a new operation-level Loss record, but it must not erase inherited Loss. In particular:

- `DECOMPOSE(COMPOSE(X)) ≠ X` remains in force;
- irrecoverable material cannot be reconstructed by formal elaboration;
- excluded or uncertain source structure cannot silently become discovered components;
- prior failed or reduced claims remain part of the audit lineage;
- finer resolution may create additional compression, exclusion, uncertainty, or irrecoverability of its own.

Preservation is therefore compatible with revision, failure, incomplete recovery, and Non-Capture. It is incompatible with lineage erasure.

<a id="18-10-minimal-source-declaration"></a>

### 18.10 Minimal Source Declaration

Before Chapter 19 specifies a granularity relation and before Chapter 20 executes `DECOMPOSE`, the source candidate must be declared in a form sufficient to preserve reference, type integrity, current function, known structure, uncertainty, and the reason for or against opening.

The minimal Chapter-18 source declaration is:

```yaml
compressed_object:
reference:
origin_type:
source_frame:
source_granularity:
source_level:
current_function:
known_internal_structure:
unresolved_internal_structure:
decomposition_reason:
```

This is a source-entry declaration, not a complete Shared Transformation Record and not a substitute for the Chapter-7 fields. Temporal scope, source basis, claim scope, uncertainty, Loss, governance, and other required declarations remain inherited from the common record architecture where material.

The minimal declaration does not contain:

- target granularity;
- target level;
- discovered components;
- component relations;
- a selected source-function effect;
- a `DECOMPOSE` result;
- a target function;
- or a canonical Output Class for an unexecuted operation.

```text
minimal source declaration
≠ minimal DECOMPOSE record
```

#### Field semantics

**`compressed_object`** identifies the source-side object category and local object identifier. It may name an operator-typed occurrence, Event, Non-Event, Transition, Path, Trajectory, institutional arrangement, or prior composite. It must not name an operator type as the decomposable object.

**`reference`** identifies the historical, documentary, institutional, relational, or otherwise source-bound referent that must remain traceable. A topic label is insufficient where occurrence identity matters.

**`origin_type`** declares the source-side typing relevant to the current question. It does not require one exclusive true type and does not authorize retrospective retyping. Compatible, rival, or uncertain typings must remain visible where material.

**`source_frame`** declares the contextual boundary within which the source object and claim are legible. A later Frame change must be separately marked and cannot masquerade as finer granularity.

**`source_granularity`** declares the current distinction density and resolution. It is the source coordinate for Chapter 19, not a truth rank.

**`source_level`** declares the object's relative position in the current composition relation. It is relational and local, not an ontological layer.

**`current_function`** records the current/coarser analytical function or claim under test, including whether it is bounded, provisional, contested, partially supported, or underdetermined. It is not guaranteed to survive decomposition.

**`known_internal_structure`** records only source-supported internal distinctions already warranted at source entry. It must not be populated with conjectured components merely because they are plausible.

**`unresolved_internal_structure`** records bounded open questions, uncertain relations, inaccessible areas, rival internal models, or distinctions that the current representation does not settle. It does not assert hidden truth.

**`decomposition_reason`** records the claim-relative reason to test a finer reconstruction, including the expected praxeological difference and source route established in Section 18.7. Where the correct decision is not to decompose, it records that entry decision and its grounds rather than fabricating a positive warrant.

#### A bounded source declaration

For the review arrangement, a compliant minimal declaration may read:

```yaml
compressed_object: documented_review_arrangement
reference: review_session_and_its_agenda_routing_record
origin_type:
  primary: Frame-typed occurrence
  additional_status:
    Asymmetry-typed occurrence: provisional
source_frame: bounded institutional review procedure
source_granularity: agenda-boundary and role-category resolution
source_level: one review arrangement relative to its recorded practices
current_function:
  Frame claim: bounded and supported
  Asymmetry claim: underdetermined
known_internal_structure:
  - circulated agenda categories
  - named role categories
  - documented routing outside the session
unresolved_internal_structure:
  - relation between role and agenda-setting capacity
  - repeated deferral across agenda versions
  - scope of informal exceptions
decomposition_reason:
  status: candidate warrant
  expected_difference: possible revision of access, burden, and decision-corridor claims
  source_route: agenda versions, role rules, minutes, and routing records
```

The example does not select a target granularity, identify final components, or establish a decomposition result.

#### Stop at source entry

Chapter 18 supports both Optional Stop and Mandatory Stop before operation execution.

**Optional Stop** is appropriate where:

- the bounded coarse claim is already sufficiently supported;
- additional distinctions lack expected praxeological purchase;
- a finer reconstruction would reduce calibration or comparability;
- the current question does not require internal opening;
- or further analysis would merely repeat a neutral detail request without changed grounds.

**Mandatory Stop** is required where continuation would:

- attempt to decompose a Δ–Ψ operator type;
- infer hidden components without source support;
- cross the source or claim ceiling;
- substitute another reference object without declaration;
- convert missing information into Event or Non-Event structure;
- erase prior failure or inherited Loss;
- assign a target function or authority through the source declaration;
- or continue an operation whose type integrity cannot be maintained.

Stop is a positive boundary result. It is not evidence that the source object is simple, finally understood, or permanently closed.

#### C18-STOP-01 — source-bound Stop before DECOMPOSE

The review summary supports a bounded claim that several matters were deferred. The analyst proposes a role-by-role causal reconstruction, but the agenda histories and routing records needed to establish the internal relation are unavailable.

The coarse deferral claim may remain. The proposed internal claim may not proceed.

```text
bounded source claim retained
+ unsupported component/relation claim stopped
```

If the finer claim merely lacks current support while a coherent source object remains, Mandatory Stop applies to that proposed continuation. The Stop does not become a finding about who caused the deferral, and it does not invalidate every bounded coarse claim.

#### Non-Capture at source entry

Non-Capture differs from Stop. Stop governs whether a proposed analytical continuation is admissible or necessary. Non-Capture records that, after bounded and source-disciplined testing, no single adequate reconstruction can presently capture the object or relation without destructive closure.

Chapter 18 must preserve Non-Capture where:

- the source reference cannot be stabilized sufficiently for the intended claim;
- rival internal models remain materially incompatible and source-indecidable;
- the available record cannot determine whether one or several objects are being compressed;
- essential relation structure is inaccessible rather than merely omitted;
- any proposed opening would erase decisive heterogeneity;
- or a single source function cannot be warranted without forcing contradictory traces into one account.

Non-Capture must not protect a weak claim. If the coarse claim has already failed, the failure remains. `non_capture` may describe the unresolved object or relation only where genuine bounded non-capture is established.

```text
failed claim
≠ rescued by non_capture

unresolved source object
≠ permission to retain the strongest coarse label
```

#### C18-NC-01 — rival internal models without forced closure

Two source-supported reconstructions of the review arrangement remain possible. One treats repeated routing outside the session as an intended procedural division. The other treats the same pattern as an emergent exclusion produced by unequal agenda-setting access. The available records support parts of both accounts but do not resolve the relation between formal routing rules and informal exceptions.

Chapter 18 may preserve the review arrangement as a bounded source reference and record both models as unresolved. It may not select one as hidden truth, fuse them into a stronger label, or retain an unsupported universal Asymmetry claim.

Where later source-bounded tests cannot discriminate between the models without erasing their material difference, `non_capture` remains available. The admissible response may still include narrower claims shared by both reconstructions. Non-Capture concerns the unresolved stronger reconstruction; it does not prohibit all analysis and does not upgrade the source.

#### Handoff to Chapters 19 and 20

A Chapter-18 declaration hands forward a bounded package:

```text
identified source reference
+ source-side typing
+ source Frame, granularity, and relative level
+ current/coarser function under test
+ known and unresolved internal structure
+ reason to decompose or not decompose
+ source and claim limits
+ inherited Loss
+ Stop and Non-Capture availability
```

Chapter 19 receives this package and determines what a declared change in granularity would mean. It owns:

- source and target granularity relation;
- added distinction set;
- Frame preservation or declared change;
- relative downward movement;
- functional component versus fragment;
- comparability and mismatch.

Chapter 20 receives the source declaration and granularity relation and owns:

- the precise decomposition question;
- component identification;
- component relations;
- internal temporality;
- source support;
- source-function effect;
- operation-level Loss;
- admissibility and result.

Neither chapter inherits success from Chapter 18. A well-formed source declaration may lead to no decomposition, claim reduction, Stop, failed transformation, Non-Capture, or an admissible finer reconstruction.

```text
Chapter 18 source readiness
≠ Chapter 19 granularity validity
≠ Chapter 20 DECOMPOSE success
```

<a id="chapter-18-completion-boundary"></a>

#### Chapter 18 completion boundary

The integrated WP4 audit confirms Chapter 18 as canonically complete and provisionally locked because:

1. operator types remain non-decomposable;
2. source candidates are limited to occurrences and composites;
3. provisional elementarity remains coordinate-, claim-, source-, and use-relative;
4. compression is separated from error and from `COMPOSE`;
5. reasons to decompose are separated from curiosity, detail appetite, and automatic counterexample response;
6. reasons not to decompose remain legitimate;
7. the source object, reference, origin typing, Frame, granularity, relative level, current function, known structure, unresolved structure, and reason are declarable;
8. source preservation remains a test relation rather than source immunization;
9. inherited Loss, uncertainty, prior failure, Stop, and Non-Capture remain visible;
10. target granularity, component relations, source-function effect, and operation result remain owned by Chapters 19 and 20.

All eighteen Chapter-18 Pressure Duties are now represented exactly once in the canonical prose. They remain chapter-local tests and later Integrated Cases and Audit seeds. No new standalone case, YAML record, Case Index entry, schema, Smoke Record, or test artifact is created during this block-production step.

The integrated Chapter-18 result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

Chapter 18 is therefore **provisionally locked**. The class names the chapter-level methodological result under the still-open SUB Part and later corpus-wide audits. It is not the result of a `DECOMPOSE` occurrence, does not select a target granularity or source-function effect, and does not provisionally lock Part II.

Reopening requires a concrete conflict involving PMS Base, Structure or Contract ownership, operator-type integrity, source-reference continuity, Chapter-19/20 boundaries, the closed operation or Output-Class inventories, an assigned Pressure Duty, a Reference or Formal-Model binding, or a later integrated dependency. Preference for more detail, a different internal model, a stronger coarse label, cleaner notation, or premature operation execution is insufficient.

The controlled continuation is:

```text
Chapter 18 provisional lock
→ Chapter 19 Preparation Gate
→ Chapter 19 — granularity relation
→ Chapter 20 — DECOMPOSE procedure
```

Chapter 18 adds no operation, no PMS primitive, no eleventh Output Class, no truth priority for finer resolution, and no additional application authority.


<a id="chapter-19-granularity-change-and-the-logic-of-decomposition"></a>

## 19. Granularity Change and the Logic of Decomposition

Chapter 18 established the bounded source-entry architecture of SUB. It identified what may be treated as a provisionally compressed occurrence or composite, distinguished necessary compression from claim-relevant insufficiency, preserved the current/coarser function as a test target rather than a protected result, and withheld target granularity, component discovery, component relations, and the `DECOMPOSE` result. Chapter 19 now supplies the coordinate relation required before Chapter 20 can execute the operation.

The governing question is:

> What exactly changes when a source object is reconstructed at finer resolution?

The answer cannot be given by saying only that the analysis becomes “more detailed,” moves “inside” the object, or descends from “macro” to “micro.” Such expressions may be convenient shorthand, but they do not yet identify a transformation relation. A controlled granularity claim must state which distinctions were compressed at the source position, which distinctions are proposed at the target position, along which comparison dimension the target is finer, whether the Frame and reference object remain stable, and what bounded praxeological difference the changed resolution is expected to test.

Chapter 19 therefore defines the **source-to-target granularity relation**. Its object is neither the coarse source object by itself nor the later finer reconstruction by itself. It is the declared analytical relation between two resolutions of a bounded reference object.

```text
Chapter 18
→ declares the compressed source object

Chapter 19
→ declares the source-to-target granularity relation

Chapter 20
→ executes and evaluates DECOMPOSE
```

Sections 19.1–19.4 establish granularity change, relative downward movement, and stable-Frame/changed-Frame separation. Sections 19.5–19.7 then define distinction-set change, local and distributed candidate structure, and functional-component versus fragment criteria. Sections 19.8–19.11 complete the coordinate architecture through comparability, mismatch, the Lower Granularity Question, the exact Minimal Granularity Relation, Loss, claim ceiling, and the controlled Chapter-20 handoff.

The integrated Chapter-19 result is therefore a declared **source-to-target granularity relation**, not a completed finer reconstruction or `DECOMPOSE` occurrence.

```text
granularity relation declared
≠ target components discovered
≠ DECOMPOSE executed
≠ finer claim established
```

<a id="19-1-granularity-change"></a>

### 19.1 Granularity Change

A **granularity change** is a declared change in the distinction set and resolution used to reconstruct and test a bounded source reference. It changes which analytically relevant differences can be represented as separate, related, temporally ordered, distributed, or internally heterogeneous structures. It does not necessarily change the source object, Frame, relative level, temporal scope, or target function, and it does not by itself constitute a completed `DECOMPOSE` occurrence.

The minimal direction is:

```text
coarser source representation
→ finer target representation
```

This direction is meaningful only relative to a stated comparison dimension. “Finer” may mean, for example:
