- the Lower Granularity Question with Stop, Non-Capture, and non-compensation;
- and the exact eight-field Minimal Granularity Relation with Shared Record mapping.

They do not establish:

- actual components or component relations;
- a completed finer reconstruction;
- source-function effect;
- `DECOMPOSE` admissibility or result;
- a Chapter-25 resolution classification;
- a RETYPE target function;
- a new operation, Rule, Output Class, schema, scale, or authority;
- or SUB provisional lock.

The integrated Chapter-19 result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

Chapter 19 is therefore **provisionally locked**. The class names the chapter-level methodological result under the still-open SUB Part and later corpus-wide audits. It is not a `DECOMPOSE` result, does not establish actual components or component relations, does not select a source-function effect or Chapter-25 resolution outcome, and does not increase claim or application authority.

Reopening requires a concrete conflict involving PMS Base, Structure or Contract ownership, the separation of Frame/granularity/relative level, source-reference continuity, functional-component criteria, comparability or contradiction handling, the exact Minimal Granularity Relation, Chapter-20/25/26 ownership, a Pressure Duty, a Reference or Formal-Model binding, or a later integrated dependency. Preference for more detail, a universal micro/macro scale, cleaner notation, automatic semantic translation, or premature operation execution is insufficient.

The controlled continuation is:

```text
Chapter 19 provisional lock
→ Chapter 20 Preparation Gate
→ Chapter 20 — DECOMPOSE: Conditions, Procedure, and Preservation Requirements
```

<a id="chapter-20-decompose-conditions-procedure-and-preservation-requirements"></a>

## 20. DECOMPOSE: Conditions, Procedure, and Preservation Requirements

Chapters 18 and 19 established the two declarations that must exist before a SUB operation can be attempted. Chapter 18 identified a provisionally compressed occurrence or composite as a bounded source object. Chapter 19 declared the proposed relation between source and target granularities without treating the finer position as truer, deeper, or already populated by supported components. Chapter 20 now specifies the first complete `DECOMPOSE` procedure within STRATA.

The governing question is:

> Under what conditions may a compressed occurrence or composite be reconstructed through finer relational structures?

The answer cannot be reduced to a request for more detail. `DECOMPOSE` is a controlled transformation occurrence. It must preserve the source object as a traceable reference and test target while changing the resolution at which its internal structures and relations are reconstructed. The operation may succeed, fail, stop, remain unresolved, or reveal that the prior coarse function was only partially warranted. None of these outcomes follows from the decision to look more closely.

Sections 20.1–20.4 establish the exact operation definition, conjunctive preconditions, independently identifiable source object, and precise decomposition question. Sections 20.5–20.8 then operationalize expected difference and no-gain pressure, source-support roles and precision ceilings, actual component identification, relational reconstruction, and internal temporality where the claim depends on temporal order. Sections 20.9–20.13 complete the generic procedure through source-function testing, four-axis result separation, controlled Output-Class mapping, non-invertibility, Failure/Stop/Non-Capture separation, canonical Loss, the complete sixteen-field operation view, and the Chapter-21 handoff.

The integrated Chapter-20 result is therefore a complete **generic `DECOMPOSE` procedure**. It does not complete occurrence-family theory, the Chapter-25 resolution taxonomy, the Chapter-26 operation-boundary system, or the SUB Part.

```text
Chapter 18 source declaration
+ Chapter 19 granularity relation
≠ DECOMPOSE execution automatically
≠ supported components automatically
≠ operation success automatically
```

<a id="20-1-definition"></a>

### 20.1 Definition

`DECOMPOSE` is the controlled reconstruction of a provisionally compressed source object as a relational organization of finer structures under declared granularity, while retaining the same bounded source reference as the object of reconstruction and testing.

The operation has the directional form:

```text
compressed occurrence or composite
→ finer reconstruction of the same reference object
```

Each part of the definition is restrictive.

**Controlled** means that the operation is governed by an explicit question, declared coordinates, source limits, preservation duties, Loss, Stop, Non-Capture, and a bounded claim ceiling. A reconstruction does not become `DECOMPOSE` merely because it is detailed, sophisticated, formally represented, or internally coherent.

**Provisionally compressed source object** means an occurrence or composite that has already been identified independently of the proposed finer reconstruction and treated as one analytical unit for a bounded Frame, granularity, temporal scope, source basis, and claim. The source object is not invented retrospectively from whatever components an analyst prefers to list.

**Relational organization** means that finer structures must be reconstructed together with the dependencies, sequences, feedbacks, thresholds, substitutions, asymmetries, reproduction conditions, or other relations through which they bear on the source object. A list of parts is not a completed decomposition.

**Finer structures** means source-supported distinctions at a declared target granularity. Finer does not mean ontologically smaller, causally deeper, more fundamental, more complete, or more authoritative. The changed resolution may concern roles, temporal phases, access relations, recurrent practices, linked Non-Events, distributed burdens, or other relations rather than spatial fragments.

**Same bounded source reference** means that the finer reconstruction remains traceably attributable to the declared source object. Historical or case identity, source Frame or marked Frame transition, temporal scope, origin-type visibility, and unresolved structure must remain readable. Repeating the same label is insufficient.

**Object of reconstruction and testing** means that the prior coarse function or claim remains visible but is not immunized. Later finer evidence may confirm, refine, internally differentiate, partially preserve, reject, or leave it underdetermined.

```text
source preservation
≠ source-function preservation

same label
≠ same reference object

finer organization
≠ final constituents
```

#### C20-DEF-01 — valid relational definition

A documented review arrangement has so far been reconstructed as one Frame-typed occurrence: an agenda and chairing procedure bounded which objections could enter the decision process. The available transcript, agenda, minutes, and routing records also permit a proposed finer distinction among objection entry, interruption, qualification, repair proposal, closure placement, and later follow-up.

A valid `DECOMPOSE` question does not ask for a richer narrative of the meeting. It asks whether those source-supported interactional and procedural structures, in their relations, reproduce, qualify, or disturb the coarse Frame claim. The target is not a pile of utterances. It is a finer relational reconstruction of the same review occurrence.

The distinction is:

```text
more quotations + more narrative
→ possibly better description

source-supported finer units + reconstructed relations
+ same bounded reference + claim test
→ candidate DECOMPOSE occurrence
```

The candidate remains only a candidate at this stage. Section 20.1 fixes operation identity; it does not certify that the finer units are supported, that the relations are adequate, or that the transformation is admissible.

#### DECOMPOSE is not generic description

Description can expand while the operative distinction set remains unchanged. An analyst may add dates, quotations, biographies, diagrams, examples, or contextual prose without opening the source object under a new resolution. Such additions can improve legibility and still fail to constitute a transformation.

`DECOMPOSE` requires a change in how the same source object is analytically partitioned or relationally differentiated. The change must be tied to a warranted claim that could be altered, qualified, or left unchanged by the finer reconstruction.

```text
added volume
≠ changed granularity

changed granularity
≠ completed DECOMPOSE automatically
```

#### C20-DESC-01 — description confusion

Suppose the review analysis is extended with ten additional quotations, a longer chronology, and participant biographies. The objection, discussion, and decision remain treated as three undifferentiated units, and no new relation is tested. The result may be informative, but it is not `DECOMPOSE`.

A second analysis distinguishes interruption from qualification, immediate closure from later routing, and promised review from expiry of the review window. It specifies how these distinctions could change the bounded reconstruction of objection uptake and procedural closure. This establishes a coherent decomposition proposal, although source support and actual relations remain to be tested later.

The pressure case blocks a common inflation:

```text
more source material
≠ finer reconstruction by itself
```

#### DECOMPOSE is not atomization

The operation does not presume that an object is reducible to independent smallest pieces. Many relevant structures are distributed, relational, or temporally extended. A coarse Asymmetry occurrence may be carried by access rules, timing, role authority, exposure, and exit costs that are not separable atoms. A compressed Non-Event may consist of linked expectation-window failures rather than one hidden object. A Trajectory may be opened into already traversed transitions and sedimented relations without claiming ultimate constituents.

`DECOMPOSE` therefore reconstructs a finer organization. It does not discover the final material from which praxis is made.

#### DECOMPOSE does not act on operator types

The Δ–Ψ operator grammar remains unchanged. Operator signs, names, types, order, and dependencies are not empirical aggregates that SUB may open.

The invalid question is:

> What is □, Α, or Ω made of?

The valid occurrence-level question is:

> Which source-supported practices and relations produce or maintain this concrete Frame-, Attractor-, or Asymmetry-typed occurrence?

```text
operator type
≠ decomposable occurrence
```

#### C20-TYPE-01 — operator-type decomposition error

An analyst proposes to decompose `□` into agenda rules, speaking rights, and closure procedures. This is inadmissible because `□` is the canonical Frame operator type, not the documented arrangement under examination.

The corrected source statement is:

```text
source object:
  this documented review arrangement
source-side typing:
  Frame-typed occurrence
question:
  which supported practices and relations reproduce its bounded Frame function?
```

Agenda rules, speaking rights, and closure procedures may later qualify as components of the concrete occurrence. They cannot be entered as constituents of the operator type. The correction preserves PMS Base and leaves the occurrence typing open to confirmation, revision, or rejection.

<a id="20-2-preconditions"></a>

### 20.2 Preconditions

A `DECOMPOSE` occurrence may be executed only when all required preconditions are satisfied. The conditions are conjunctive and non-compensatory. Strong source identity cannot compensate for absent target granularity. A precise question cannot compensate for missing source access. Extensive detail cannot compensate for loss of reference continuity. A complete record cannot compensate for lack of praxeological purchase.

The minimum preconditions are:

1. **Identifiable source object** — an occurrence or composite exists independently of the proposed components.
2. **Bounded insufficiency question** — the current compression is under a specific claim-relevant pressure rather than a general preference for detail.
3. **Expected PraxisPurchase** — at least one proposed finer distinction could change a warranted reconstruction of roles, costs, alternatives, expectations, temporal order, asymmetry, binding, or another declared praxis dimension.
4. **Source route** — there is a plausible and bounded route to materials capable of supporting or refusing the proposed distinctions.
5. **Declared granularity change** — source granularity, proposed target granularity, and the added distinction family are explicit.
6. **Frame and reference status** — Frame preservation or change and source-reference continuity are declared rather than assumed.
7. **Source-function test target** — the current/coarser function or claim is retained as an explicit object of later evaluation, including uncertainty or absence of a stable function.
8. **Loss and Stop readiness** — preserved, compressed, excluded, uncertain, and irrecoverable structure can be recorded, and continuation can stop where relevance, source, reference, claim, or operation boundaries fail.

These are entry conditions for attempting the operation. They are not evidence that actual components exist, that proposed relations are correct, or that the final transformation will be admissible.

```text
preconditions satisfied
≠ components supported
≠ relations reconstructed
≠ source function confirmed
≠ admissible transformation automatically
```

#### Preconditions consume Chapters 18 and 19 without inheriting their success

Chapter 18 supplies a Minimal Source Declaration. Chapter 19 supplies a Minimal Granularity Relation. Chapter 20 must restate and test those declarations rather than treating them as self-validating.

A source candidate may turn out to be too weakly identified. A proposed target granularity may not be supportable. A Frame may change more substantially than expected. A finer distinction may produce no claim-relevant difference. The source route may expose inaccessible or missing intermediate structure. Each possibility remains open.

```text
source readiness
≠ operation warrant automatically

granularity proposal
≠ supported finer model
```

#### C20-PRE-01 — failed precondition

A Trajectory has been provisionally identified from documented transitions, and Chapter 19 proposes opening it into finer phases. The available sources, however, contain only the initial and final records. Intermediate transitions, branch points, and relevant Non-Events are inaccessible. The proposed target granularity is intelligible, but the source route cannot support the distinctions on which the decomposition would depend.

The analyst may not compensate by inferring likely phases from the coarse Trajectory label. The correct result at this stage is that the operation cannot yet be executed as proposed.

Possible bounded responses include:

- reformulating the question to a weaker source-supported distinction;
- seeking additional sources;
- recording an Optional Stop where further work is unnecessary for the current claim;
- applying `mandatory_stop` where continuation would require invented structure or exceed the Source Ceiling;
- later recording `non_capture` where the internal organization remains boundedly unavailable without preserving the stronger Trajectory claim.

No canonical class is selected merely from the phrase “failed precondition.” The tested claim and route determine the later mapping.

#### Non-compensation

The preconditions belong to different control dimensions. They cannot be averaged.

```text
high detail
+ elegant model
+ complete YAML
≠ source support

strong source support
+ no claim-relevant difference
≠ warranted DECOMPOSE automatically

plausible finer relations
+ changed reference object
≠ same-source decomposition
```

This non-compensation rule protects the Admissibility Band. Below the Relevance Floor, additional resolution lacks PraxisPurchase. Above the Source or Traceability Ceiling, an internally coherent reconstruction lacks traceable load. Between those boundaries, Type Integrity, Reference Continuity, Functional Continuity, Contextual Boundedness, and Counterfactual Sensitivity remain separately required.

#### Preconditions and Stop

Stop is available before, during, and after the operation. Before execution, Stop can be appropriate when:

- the current coarse reconstruction already carries the bounded claim;
- the proposed distinction cannot alter a warranted reconstruction;
- the source route is exhausted or inaccessible;
- the same-reference burden cannot be met;
- the question actually requires a new `COMPOSE` or `PROJECT_AS` operation;
- continuation would decompose an operator type;
- the target granularity would exceed claim or calibration precision.

An Optional Stop records that further resolution is unnecessary. A Mandatory Stop records that continuation would be inadmissible. Neither is a failure to analyze.

<a id="20-3-source-object"></a>

### 20.3 Source Object

Every `DECOMPOSE` occurrence begins from an explicit source object. The source must be identifiable before its proposed components are accepted. Otherwise the operation risks constructing an object retrospectively from a preferred parts list and then claiming to have discovered its internal structure.

The source object must be declared regarding:

- reference or case identity;
- object category;
- origin type or relevant source-side typing;
- source Frame;
- source granularity;
- source relative level;
- temporal scope where material;
- current/coarser function or claim under examination;
- known internal structure;
- unresolved internal structure;
- decomposition reason;
- source basis and uncertainty.

The Chapter-18 Minimal Source Declaration remains the minimum source-side core:

```yaml
compressed_object:
reference:
origin_type:
source_frame:
source_granularity:
source_level:
current_function:
known_internal_structure:
unresolved_internal_structure:
decomposition_reason:
```

Chapter 20 does not silently replace this declaration. It restates it in the operation record and links it to temporal scope, source basis, uncertainty, and the Chapter-19 granularity relation.

#### Independent source identity

The source object must exist as an analytically and historically traceable object before the proposed decomposition. This does not require ontological independence. It requires record independence from the target parts list.

A documented review occurrence can be identified through agenda, transcript, minutes, date, participants, and procedural scope before deciding whether interruption, qualification, repair, and closure are components. A Trajectory can be identified through its locked Path record and temporal boundary before deciding which internal transitions carry its sedimentation claim.

Invalid source construction takes the reverse form:

```text
preferred components selected first
→ convenient object label assembled later
→ alleged decomposition of that label
```

That is not a finer reconstruction of an independently declared source object.

#### Object category, origin typing, and function remain distinct

The source declaration must not collapse three different questions:

```text
what kind of analytical object is this?
which PMS occurrence typing is relevant?
which current/coarser function or claim is under examination?
```

For example:

```yaml
object_category: composite review occurrence
origin_type: Frame-typed occurrence with a possible Asymmetry-typed relation
current_function: bounded objection-admission and closure function under test
```

The object category is not the operator typing. The operator typing is not the source function. Multiple or rival occurrence typings may remain visible. `origin_type` does not demand one exclusive true label where the sources support competing analyses.

#### The current function may be weak, disputed, or absent

A complete-looking record must not fabricate a stable coarse function. The current function may be:

- provisionally supported;
- contested;
- partially specified;
- one among rival functions;
- underdetermined;
- or absent where the source object has so far been identified only as an occurrence or composite.

Chapter 20 retains whatever bounded claim actually exists. Later finer evidence may alter it, but the operation cannot test a function that was invented solely for procedural symmetry.

```text
current function field completed
≠ current function established
```

#### Known versus unresolved internal structure

Known internal structure records source-supported distinctions already available at source entry. Unresolved internal structure records what the current representation does not yet distinguish or cannot yet support. Neither field authorizes hidden-depth language.

```text
unresolved internal structure
≠ hidden true components
```

The unresolved field may contain several statuses:

- a distinction is source-accessible but not yet reconstructed;
- a distinction is plausible but unsupported;
- a source segment is missing;
- competing internal models remain open;
- the current claim does not require the distinction;
- the structure may not be stably reconstructible at all.

#### C20-SRC-01 — explicit source object

The review case enters Chapter 20 with the following bounded source declaration:

```yaml
compressed_object: documented review arrangement treated as one composite occurrence
reference: review R-17 on the declared date, linked to agenda, transcript, minutes, and routing record
origin_type: Frame-typed occurrence; possible Asymmetry-typed relations remain secondary hypotheses
source_frame: decision-coordination Frame concerning objection entry, uptake, and closure
source_granularity: one review occurrence at decision-sequence resolution
source_level: source-side composite relative to proposed interactional and procedural units
current_function: bounded objection-admission and closure function under test
known_internal_structure: agenda, named roles, recorded objection, chair response, closure statement, later routing entry
unresolved_internal_structure: relation among interruption, qualification, repair, closure, follow-up assignment, and expiry of the revisit window
decomposition_reason: test whether the coarse closure claim compresses a source-supported unresolved objection and distributed authority relation
```

The declaration protects the independent source object and keeps its function testable. It does not claim that every proposed unit is an actual component, that the possible Asymmetry typing is established, or that the coarse function will survive.

#### Same-reference burden

The target reconstruction must remain attributable to the source object. Reference continuity requires:

- the same bounded historical or case process;
- the same temporal scope or an explicitly justified internal refinement;
- traceable source lineage;
- preserved origin-type visibility;
- a declared Frame relation;
- and visible uncertainty, exclusion, and inaccessible structure.

If the target selects a different period, a different institutional object, or a different Path, the analysis may require a new source declaration and possibly a new operation. Reusing the original label cannot preserve identity by itself.

<a id="20-4-decomposition-question"></a>

### 20.4 Decomposition Question

Every `DECOMPOSE` occurrence requires one precise leading question. The question is the procedural hinge between the source declaration and the later component/relation reconstruction. It must be narrow enough to guide source selection and Stop, but open enough to permit confirmation, revision, rejection, underdetermination, or no gain.

A complete decomposition question identifies:

1. **the source object** being opened;
2. **the proposed finer distinction family**;
3. **the current claim or function** that may change;
4. **the source route** capable of supporting or refusing the inquiry;
5. **the no-gain condition** under which the finer distinction leaves the warranted reconstruction unchanged;
6. **the Stop condition** under which continuation becomes unnecessary or inadmissible.

The question must not contain its preferred result. It may state what could change; it may not presume that hidden components will prove the coarse claim.

A useful canonical form is:

> Which source-supported finer structures and relations within **[source object]**, under **[target granularity]**, would confirm, qualify, disturb, reject, or leave underdetermined **[current bounded claim/function]**, and what would count as no gain or require Stop?

This is a template, not mandatory wording. The six burdens are mandatory.

#### Valid question families

For an operator-typed occurrence:

> Which source-supported practices and relations reproduce or disturb this concrete Frame-typed occurrence, and would their reconstruction alter the bounded Frame claim?

For an Asymmetry-typed occurrence:

> Which role, access, exposure, timing, and cost relations carry or offset the declared Asymmetry, and what source change would weaken the coarse typing?

For a compressed Non-Event:

> Which expectation-window failures, linked delays, responsibility handoffs, and missed repair opportunities form the declared Non-Event structure, and when would the available sources fail to distinguish it from missing information?

For a Path or Trajectory:

> Which already traversed transitions, branch closures, persistent residues, and temporal relations support the declared Path or Trajectory claim, and would an alternative selection require a new `COMPOSE` occurrence instead?

These questions point toward later Chapters 21–24 without performing their family-specific work here.

#### Invalid or incomplete questions

The following formulations are insufficient:

- “What is inside it?”
- “Give more detail.”
- “What are the real micro-causes?”
- “What is the operator made of?”
- “Which hidden components prove the coarse claim?”
- “What target function does this object perform elsewhere?”

The first two lack a bounded distinction and claim relation. The third presupposes causal and truth depth. The fourth attempts operator-type decomposition. The fifth immunizes the source function. The sixth belongs to `PROJECT_AS`.

```text
thematic curiosity
≠ decomposition question

question containing preferred conclusion
≠ open reconstruction test
```

#### Source route belongs inside the question architecture

A decomposition question is not operationally complete if no materials could answer it. The source route need not already prove the finer model, but it must identify where support, refusal, uncertainty, or inaccessible structure can be assessed.

For the review case, the route may include:

- transcript segments around objection entry and closure;
- agenda and procedural rules;
- minutes and approval history;
- routing records for promised follow-up;
- later documentation of implementation or review;
- uncertainty where recordings or intermediate records are absent.

The route constrains semantic precision. It prevents the question from becoming an invitation to invent an internally satisfying model from the source label.

#### No-gain and Stop are built into the question

A precise question must specify how the operation can fail to add a warranted praxis distinction.

For the review case, no gain may occur if the finer interactional and procedural units are source-supported but do not alter the bounded reconstruction of objection uptake, closure, role access, temporal residue, or later reachability. This keeps `resolution_neutral` available later without preclassifying the result in Chapter 20 WP1.

Stop may be required if:

- the proposed units cannot be distinguished by the sources;
- the Frame or source object changes beyond the declared relation;
- the inquiry shifts into a new Path construction;
- the question seeks a target function;
- finer semantic precision exceeds source precision;
- or the additional distinction has no plausible claim-relevant purchase.

#### C20-Q-01 — precise decomposition question

For the review occurrence, the complete leading question is:

> Which transcript-supported interactional units and procedure-supported role, authority, repair, closure, and follow-up relations within review R-17 would change whether the documented objection is reconstructed as integrated, deferred but unresolved, or closed under distributed access and authority asymmetry? The source route is the agenda, transcript, minutes, routing record, and later review documentation. No gain occurs if the finer distinctions leave the warranted decision-integration and asymmetry reconstruction unchanged. Stop is required if the units or relations exceed source precision, substitute another review process, require a new Path composition, or seek a contextual target function.

The question does not assert that distributed authority asymmetry exists. It names one bounded possibility that later source and relation reconstruction may support, weaken, reject, or leave underdetermined.

#### WP1 boundary

Sections 20.1–20.4 establish operation identity, conjunctive preconditions, the source-object burden, and the question architecture. They do not yet establish:

- the expected additional difference in operational detail;
- source-support categories or precision status;
- actual components;
- component relations;
- internal temporality;
- source-function effect;
- local operation result;
- prior claim disposition;
- canonical Output Class;
- operation-level Loss;
- Non-Invertibility result;
- failure classification;
- or the completed `DECOMPOSE` Record.

```text
WP1 complete
≠ Chapter 20 complete
≠ DECOMPOSE executed
≠ components established
≠ relations reconstructed
≠ source-function effect selected
≠ Output Class mapped
```

<a id="20-5-expected-additional-difference"></a>

### 20.5 Expected Additional Difference

The decomposition question identifies what may be opened. The expected additional difference states what the proposed finer reconstruction must be capable of changing. This declaration is required before component identification because otherwise any newly visible detail can be retrospectively presented as a successful gain.

An expected difference is not a prediction that the finer reconstruction will confirm a preferred interpretation. It is a bounded statement of **discriminative possibility**:

> If the proposed finer distinctions and relations are source-supported, which warranted reconstruction could become more precise, weaker, internally divided, rejected, or unchanged?

The expected difference must connect four elements:

1. **the distinction family** to be added;
2. **the current bounded claim or function** placed under pressure;
3. **the praxeological dimension** on which a change would matter;
4. **the observable no-gain condition** under which the operation adds resolution but no additional praxis finding.

```text
expected difference
≠ promised discovery
≠ preferred conclusion
≠ automatic PraxisPurchase
```

#### Difference must be claim-relative

A finer distinction is not valuable merely because it is available. It must be able to alter at least one warranted feature of the source reconstruction, for example:

- the available or closed action corridors;
- role access or role exclusion;
- the distribution of cost, exposure, or timing burden;
- the presence or persistence of a Non-Event;
- the sequence or duration of internal transitions;
- the conditions under which a Frame is reproduced or disturbed;
- the support for a coarse Attractor, Asymmetry, Path, Trajectory, or Binding claim;
- the location of uncertainty or irrecoverable loss.

The expected difference therefore does not ask whether more distinctions can be made. It asks whether those distinctions could change the warranted praxis reconstruction.

```text
more distinguishable detail
≠ expected additional difference
```

#### Expected gain and no-gain remain symmetrical possibilities

The expected-difference declaration must include a no-gain counterpart. This prevents the operation from becoming self-confirming.

For review R-17, the proposed finer distinction family includes:

- interactional units around objection entry, interruption, qualification, response, closure, and repair;
- procedural units concerning agenda position, speaking access, decision authority, follow-up routing, and revisit expiry;
- temporal relations among the recorded objection, closure statement, promised follow-up, later routing, and absence of reopening.

The expected additional difference is not “more detail about the meeting.” It is:

> The finer reconstruction may change whether the documented objection is warrantedly represented as integrated, deferred but unresolved, or closed under distributed access and authority asymmetry, and may change which roles carried the costs and continuation possibilities of closure.

No gain occurs if the source-supported finer units and relations leave the warranted reconstruction of objection uptake, closure, role access, temporal residue, and later reachability unchanged.

This symmetry is essential:

```text
possible gain
+ explicit no-gain condition
→ testable decomposition pressure

possible gain
without no-gain condition
→ confirmation bias risk
```

#### Expected difference does not select an output

At this stage, the operation has not established:

- that the finer distinctions are source-supported;
- that they form actual components;
- that the components stand in constitutive relations;
- that the current function survives or fails;
- that the operation is admissible;
- or that any canonical Output Class applies.

The expected difference is an entry burden, not a result.

#### C20-DIFF-01 — bounded expected difference

For review R-17, the expected difference is recorded as follows:

```yaml
expected_difference:
  distinction_family:
    - interactional units of objection entry, response, closure, and repair
    - procedural role, authority, routing, and revisit-window relations
    - internal temporal order among objection, closure, follow-up, and non-reopening
  current_claim_under_pressure: the review occurrence performed a bounded objection-admission and closure function
  praxeological_dimensions:
    - role access
    - authority asymmetry
    - temporal residue
    - continuation and repair corridors
  possible_change: the coarse function may be confirmed, refined, internally differentiated, partially preserved, rejected, or left underdetermined
  no_gain_condition: supported finer distinctions leave the warranted reconstruction of uptake, closure, role access, residue, and later reachability unchanged
```

The declaration opens several possible effects without selecting one. It also makes a later resolution-neutral result possible without classifying the operation in advance.

#### Counterfactual pressure at the expected-difference stage

A useful pre-operation check is:

> Would a relevant change in the proposed finer source structure alter the current bounded claim?

If the answer is “no” regardless of what the finer evidence shows, the proposed operation is overelastic. The coarse claim has been insulated from source variation, and the expected difference is not genuine.

If the answer is “yes, but only because any detail can be redescribed as support,” the operation is equally defective. Counterfactual sensitivity requires specified source changes that would strengthen, weaken, split, or leave unchanged the bounded claim.

The expected-difference stage therefore prepares, but does not complete, the later source-function and admissibility tests.

<a id="20-6-source-support"></a>

### 20.6 Source Support

A finer reconstruction may proceed only as far as the available sources can carry its distinctions and relations. Source support is not a general statement that the source object is documented. It is a role-sensitive account of what each material can support, what must be reconstructed indirectly, what remains uncertain, and where the operation reaches an inaccessible or unsupported boundary.

The governing ceiling is:

```text
semantic precision
≤ source precision
```

This ceiling applies separately to:

- component identity;
- component boundaries;
- component relations;
- temporal order and duration;
- operator-typed occurrence claims;
- source-function effects;
- and any claim about necessity, sufficiency, causality, or reproduction.

A transcript may support a sequence of utterances while not supporting unrecorded motives. Minutes may support an official closure classification while not preserving the interaction that produced it. A routing record may support later assignment while not proving that the original objection was integrated. Different sources can support different parts of the reconstruction without becoming interchangeable.

#### Source-support roles

Chapter 20 uses the following open support roles. They are descriptive roles, not a new closed enum or scoring system.

**Direct support**

The relevant component or relation is explicitly represented in a source whose provenance and scope are adequate for the claim. Direct support does not eliminate interpretive judgment, but the source-to-claim path is short and inspectable.

**Indirect reconstruction**

The component or relation is inferred from multiple source traces whose conjunction supports a bounded reconstruction. The inference rule and uncertainty must remain visible.

**Uncertain attribution**

A source trace exists, but its assignment to a specific role, component, sequence position, or function remains contested or ambiguous.

**Missing intermediate structure**

The source records endpoints or selected moments while omitting a potentially claim-relevant transition, handoff, or interval.

**Rival internal model**

More than one source-compatible organization of the finer material remains available. A preferred model cannot be treated as established merely because it is more coherent or convenient.

**Inaccessible area**

Relevant material may exist, but it is unavailable, irretrievable, restricted, destroyed, or outside the legitimate source scope.

**Unsupported inference**

The proposed component or relation exceeds what the declared sources can warrant. It must not be upgraded through formal completeness or narrative plausibility.

```text
source exists
≠ source supports this distinction

multiple sources
≠ convergent support automatically

coherent internal model
≠ supported internal model
```

#### Source roles must remain claim-specific

The same source can be direct for one claim and insufficient for another.

For review R-17:

- the transcript directly supports the order of recorded objection, response, interruption, closure statement, and selected repair language;
- the agenda and procedure document directly support declared speaking order and formal authority positions;
- the minutes directly support the official closure representation;
- the routing record directly supports later assignment of follow-up;
- the relation between interactional closure and later non-reopening may require indirect reconstruction;
- the intention of the chair, the subjective uptake of participants, and unrecorded side communication remain unsupported or inaccessible unless separate sources exist.

This role separation prevents one well-documented layer from lending authority to another.

#### C20-SUP-01 — source-support matrix

The review occurrence receives the following bounded support map:

```yaml
source_support:
  direct_support:
    - transcript timestamps for objection entry, interruption, response, closure, and repair attempt
    - agenda and procedure rules for declared speaking access and decision authority
    - minutes for official closure classification
    - routing record for later follow-up assignment
  indirect_reconstruction:
    - relation between closure wording, follow-up routing, and later absence of reopening
  uncertain_attribution:
    - whether a qualification was treated as procedural clarification or substantive objection uptake
  missing_intermediate_structure:
    - undocumented exchange between meeting closure and creation of the routing entry
  rival_internal_models:
    - objection integrated through deferred routing
    - objection procedurally closed while substantively unresolved
  inaccessible_areas:
    - unrecorded side communication and unavailable draft minutes
  unsupported_inferences:
    - participant motives
    - subjective agreement
    - causal intent behind closure timing
```

The matrix does not decide between the rival models. It identifies the support structure within which later component and relation claims may be tested.

#### C20-OVER-01 — semantic overreach

An analyst reads the official minutes, which state that the objection was “addressed,” and records:

> The objection was cognitively integrated by all participants and therefore no residual Non-Event remained.

The minutes directly support the official closure label. They do not support participant cognition, universal uptake, or the absence of residue. The claim exceeds semantic precision and must be reduced.

```text
official closure label
≠ universal integration
≠ absence of residual structure
```

The correction retains the weaker finding:

> The official record represents the objection as addressed; transcript, routing, and later reopening evidence must be examined separately to determine whether the bounded integration claim is supportable.

#### C20-MODEL-01 — rival internal models

The same source set permits two internally coherent reconstructions:

1. the objection was integrated through a legitimate deferred follow-up mechanism;
2. the objection was procedurally displaced into follow-up while the decision Frame closed around it.

Neither coherence nor symmetry between the models decides the issue. The operation must identify which source changes would distinguish them and may remain underdetermined if those changes are unavailable.

```text
rival source-compatible models
≠ forced selection
≠ automatic non_capture
```

Rivality is first a source and model condition. Its later canonical classification depends on the operation claim, the remaining evidence, Stop, and whether any weaker findings remain supportable.

#### Source ceiling and Stop

Mandatory Stop applies where continued component or relation claims would require:

- inaccessible evidence presented as known;
- unsupported motive or causal inference;
- a precision level not represented by the source;
- a changed source object concealed as internal reconstruction;
- or an attempt to repair a failed claim through speculative finer structure.

Optional Stop applies where available sources are sufficient for the current bounded claim and additional source work would not change a warranted praxis distinction.

Non-Capture remains available where the internal structure is genuinely unresolved and cannot be responsibly reduced to one model. It does not preserve a stronger source claim that has already failed.

<a id="20-7-component-identification"></a>

### 20.7 Component Identification

A component is not any detail found inside the source description. It is a source-supported finer structure that stands in a specified relation to the same source object and bears on the current bounded claim or function.

A component candidate from Chapter 19 becomes an established component only when five burdens are met:

1. **Reference relation** — it belongs to the same bounded source object or process;
2. **Source route** — its identity and boundary are supported at the declared precision;
3. **Function/claim relation** — it can carry, disturb, limit, reproduce, or test the current bounded claim;
4. **Praxeological relevance** — its inclusion changes or could change a warranted praxis distinction;
5. **Relational placement** — it can be located within at least one relevant relation to other components or the source object.

```text
component candidate
+ five burdens satisfied
→ established component within this reconstruction

interesting fragment
without claim relation or relational placement
→ not an established component
```

Establishment remains local to the declared Frame, granularity, source scope, and claim. It is not a claim that the component is an ultimate constituent.

#### Permissible component forms

Components may include:

- operator-typed occurrences;
- local configurations;
- subevents;
- Non-Events;
- transitions;
- role positions;
- access, resource, exposure, or cost relations treated as bounded relational units;
- subpaths already contained within the same source Path;
- repeated reproduction practices;
- distributed handoffs or procedural sequences;
- threshold conditions;
- or bounded composite substructures.

A component need not be spatially small, individually owned, or continuously present. A distributed routing sequence can be a component of a review occurrence if it belongs to the same bounded process and carries a claim-relevant function.

#### Carrying, disturbing, and replaceable components

A **carrying component** supports or reproduces the current coarse function.

A **disturbing component** introduces source-supported pressure against the coarse function, reveals heterogeneity, or shows that the function is only partially warranted.

A **replaceable component** participates in the source structure but is not shown to be uniquely necessary. Another component or route could perform an equivalent local role under the bounded claim.

These roles are neither exhaustive nor mutually permanent. One component can carry one part of a function and disturb another. The role must be stated relative to a particular claim.

```text
carrying
≠ necessary

disturbing
≠ external noise

replaceable
≠ irrelevant
```

#### C20-COMP-01 — established component burden

In review R-17, the following are established as components at the declared target granularity:

- the recorded objection-entry unit;
- the chair-response and qualification unit;
- the closure-declaration unit;
- the formal follow-up-routing unit;
- the bounded revisit window established by procedure and later record;
- the non-reopening of the issue within that window, treated as a Non-Event only relative to the documented follow-up expectation.

Each component has a source route, belongs to the same review process, bears on the closure/integration claim, and can be placed in the later relation model.

By contrast, participant discomfort, hidden agreement, and informal influence are not established components because the declared source set does not support their identity or boundaries.

#### C20-DISTURB-01 — disturbing component

The transcript records a substantive qualification after the official response but before closure. The minutes omit that qualification and record the objection as addressed.

The qualification is a disturbing component because it is source-supported, belongs to the same review occurrence, bears directly on whether integration occurred, and changes the relation between official closure and unresolved content.

It must not be excluded merely because it weakens the coarse function.

```text
component disturbs source function
≠ component excluded

source preservation
≠ function preservation
```

#### C20-REPL-01 — replaceable component

The follow-up routing entry participates in the review’s continuation structure, but the sources do not show that this particular routing mechanism was uniquely necessary. Another documented repair route could have carried the same bounded continuation function.

The component is relevant and established, but necessity and sufficiency remain withheld.

```text
participation
≠ necessity
≠ sufficiency
≠ causal priority
```

#### Component exclusion remains explicit

A finer reconstruction also records proposed elements that fail component status. They may be:

- unsupported;
- outside the source object;
- below the Relevance Floor;
- redundant at the target granularity;
- not relationally placeable;
- or part of a rival model not presently distinguishable.

Exclusion does not mean universal irrelevance. It means the element does not qualify as a component for this bounded `DECOMPOSE` occurrence.

<a id="20-8-relation-identification"></a>

### 20.8 Relation Identification

`DECOMPOSE` does not complete when a set of finer components has been named. The operation must reconstruct how the supported components are organized within the same source object and how that organization bears on the current bounded claim.

```text
component list
≠ relational decomposition
```

A relation claim requires:

- identified endpoints or participating structures;
- a declared relation type or bounded description;
- source support adequate to that relation;
- temporal scope where relevant;
- directionality or symmetry status where relevant;
- uncertainty and rival relations;
- and a claim about why the relation matters to the source reconstruction.

#### Relevant relation families

Depending on the source object, relevant relations may include:

- dependency;
- temporal sequence;
- overlap or concurrency;
- feedback;
- threshold;
- substitution;
- inhibition or blockage;
- mutual reinforcement;
- asymmetric load distribution;
- access and authority routing;
- reproduction conditions;
- branch relation;
- persistence relation;
- or relation between positive subevents and a bounded Non-Event.

These descriptions do not create new STRATA operations or PMS operators. They specify local organization within a `DECOMPOSE` occurrence.

#### C20-LIST-01 — parts-list failure

An analyst identifies seven review details—objection, interruption, response, closure, minutes, routing entry, and later silence—and declares the review decomposed.

The list is insufficient. It does not state:

- which items are components rather than documents or traces;
- how they relate;
- what temporal order matters;
- which role or authority relation carries closure;
- whether later silence is a Non-Event or merely missing information;
- or how any relation changes the bounded source claim.

The operation remains incomplete.

```text
more named items
≠ reconstructed internal organization
```

#### C20-REL-01 — relational reconstruction

For review R-17, the supported relation model includes:

1. the objection-entry unit precedes and activates a chair-response unit;
2. a substantive qualification follows the response but precedes formal closure;
3. closure authority is asymmetrically located in the chair role under the declared procedure;
4. the closure declaration routes unresolved material into a follow-up mechanism rather than reopening the current decision sequence;
5. the follow-up-routing entry creates a bounded expectation of later handling;
6. non-reopening within the documented revisit window forms a source-supported Non-Event relative to that expectation;
7. the combination of closure authority, deferred routing, and non-reopening alters later continuation possibilities.

The model is relational rather than merely chronological because it identifies authority, routing, expectation, blockage, and persistence relations that bear on the current objection-integration claim.

#### Relation claims must not inflate causality

A source-supported sequence does not by itself establish causality. A dependency in the record may be procedural, logical, or reconstruction-specific rather than empirically causal.

```text
A precedes B
≠ A caused B

A is required by procedure for B
≠ A is the only sufficient cause of B
```

Causal claims require independent source and claim support. Chapter 20 does not borrow causal authority from relational clarity.

#### Internal temporality is conditionally mandatory

Internal temporality must be reconstructed whenever the finer claim depends on:

- sequence;
- duration;
- delay;
- persistence;
- repetition;
- transition;
- phase;
- branch closure;
- Path or Trajectory relation;
- sedimentation;
- irreversibility;
- or an expectation window relevant to a Non-Event.

Internal temporality may be represented as:

- exact order where sources support it;
- partial order where only some precedence relations are known;
- bounded intervals;
- overlapping phases;
- uncertain sequence;
- or explicitly inaccessible temporal structure.

The operation must not invent a complete chronology merely to obtain a tidy model.

#### C20-TEMP-01 — internal temporality

The review occurrence requires internal temporality because the claim depends on whether qualification occurred before closure, whether follow-up was created after closure, and whether reopening failed within a bounded expected window.

The supported temporal model is:

```yaml
internal_temporality:
  t1: objection entered during the declared review interval
  t2: response issued
  t3: substantive qualification recorded after response and before closure
  t4: closure declared
  t5: follow-up routing created after closure; exact intermediate exchange missing
  t6: documented revisit window remained open
  t7: no reopening recorded before window expiry
  uncertainty:
    - exact timing of the undocumented handoff between t4 and t5
    - whether all participants received the same follow-up notice
```

The missing handoff does not erase the supported order. It also prevents stronger claims about the mechanism connecting closure and routing.

#### Same-source Path boundary

A Path or subpath may be opened only where it remains part of the same source object and selection frame. If the analyst selects different transitions, introduces new branch points, changes the temporal boundary, or forms a new trajectory, the work may require a separate `COMPOSE` occurrence.

```text
opening internal transitions of the same declared Path
≠ selecting and forming a different Path
```

This boundary is fully adjudicated later in Chapter 26. Chapter 20 preserves it procedurally.

#### WP2 boundary

Sections 20.5–20.8 establish:

- the expected additional difference and no-gain condition;
- role-sensitive source-support architecture and precision ceiling;
- actual component-identification burdens;
- carrying, disturbing, and replaceable component roles;
- explicit component exclusions;
- relation-identification burdens;
- parts-list failure;
- conditional internal temporality;
- and the same-source/new-Path boundary.

They do not yet select:

- source-function effect;
- local operation result;
- prior source-claim disposition;
- canonical Output Class;
- complete operation-level Loss;
- Non-Invertibility disposition;
- failure classification;
- final Stop or Non-Capture result;
- or the completed `DECOMPOSE` Record.

```text
WP2 complete
≠ Chapter 20 complete
≠ source-function effect selected
≠ operation result selected
≠ canonical Output Class mapped
≠ DECOMPOSE Record complete
```

<a id="20-9-preservation-of-source-function"></a>

### 20.9 Preservation of Source Function

The source or coarser function remains an explicit test object throughout `DECOMPOSE`. It is not a protected answer. Preservation therefore means that the operation keeps visible:

- which coarser function was originally attributed;
- which source reference carried that attribution;
- which finer components and relations now bear on it;
- which evidence supports or weakens it;
- which uncertainties remain;
- and how the current operation changes the warranted claim.

```text
preserve the source-function question
≠ preserve the source-function answer
```

A finer reconstruction can affect the source function in several ways. Chapter 20 uses the following controlled source-function effects:

```text
confirmed
refined
internally_differentiated
partially_preserved
rejected
underdetermined
```

These values are neither an ordinal scale nor canonical Output Classes. They describe what the finer reconstruction does to the function attributed to the source object.

#### Confirmed

The finer reconstruction identifies source-supported components and relations that carry the coarser function without revealing a material contradiction or required narrowing. Confirmation does not increase authority. It shows only that the coarser function remains warranted under the declared finer test.

#### Refined

The coarser function remains warranted, but its mechanism, internal boundary, temporal form, or component dependence becomes more precise. Refinement changes the description without converting the source object into a new type.

#### Internally differentiated

The coarser function is not uniformly carried. Different components, phases, roles, or relations support different local effects. The whole-object claim may remain usable only if its internal heterogeneity is made explicit.

#### Partially preserved

A separable portion of the original function survives while another portion fails, becomes unsupported, or exceeds the declared scope. Partial preservation requires claim segmentation rather than averaging incompatible local results.

#### Rejected

The finer reconstruction is source-supported and relationally coherent, but it defeats the coarser functional attribution. Rejection of the prior source function is not failure of the operation. It can be evidence that `DECOMPOSE` performed its discriminative task successfully.

#### Underdetermined

The finer reconstruction establishes some components or relations but does not support a stable decision about the source function. Underdetermination may result from missing intermediates, unstable component boundaries, rival internal models, inaccessible temporal structure, or insufficiently discriminating sources.

```text
source-function effect
≠ operation result
≠ prior claim disposition
≠ canonical Output Class
```

#### C20-FUNC-01 — confirmed and refined source function

Consider a documented scheduling arrangement previously typed as a Frame occurrence because it defines who may enter a review interval, which materials count as admissible, and when the interval closes.

At finer granularity, the sources show:

- an eligibility check before admission;
- a stable agenda-registration sequence;
- a role-specific moderation step;
- and a closure rule consistently applied across the sampled intervals.

The finer reconstruction confirms the bounded Frame function. It also refines it: the function is not carried by one isolated rule but by a repeated relation among eligibility, registration, moderation, and closure.

The result does not show that the Frame is normatively justified, universally stable, causally sufficient, or more authoritative because its internal carriers are visible.

```text
confirmed or refined source function
≠ higher truth
≠ stronger authority
```

#### C20-REJECT-01 — successful decomposition rejects the coarse claim

For review R-17, the coarser function under test is:

> The review integrated the entered objection before closure.

WP2 established a source-supported relational model in which:

- the objection is entered;
- a response is issued;
- a substantive qualification follows the response;
- closure authority remains located in the chair role;
- unresolved material is routed to later handling;
- and no reopening occurs within the documented revisit window.

This decomposition is relationally successful. It preserves the review as the same reference object, identifies supported components, reconstructs authority and routing relations, and maintains uncertainty about the undocumented handoff.

The finer evidence nevertheless rejects the coarse integration claim. The sources support official closure and deferred routing; they do not support completed integration of the qualification before closure.

```text
successful DECOMPOSE
+ rejected source function
→ operation may remain admissible
```

The prior claim must remain visible as failed or withdrawn. It cannot be rescued by relabelling closure as integration at a finer level.

#### C20-UNDER-01 — underdetermined source function

A second review record contains the entered objection, the formal response, and a later note that the issue was “handled.” The intermediate transcript is missing, the follow-up route is inaccessible, and two source-compatible models remain:

1. the objection was materially integrated before closure;
2. the objection was procedurally closed and deferred without integration.

The available sources support some component boundaries and the existence of a later handling stage. They do not discriminate the relation between qualification, closure, and later handling.

The source-function effect is therefore `underdetermined`. The operation must not retain the stronger integration claim merely because rejection is not proven. It must also not reject the claim merely because the record is incomplete.

```text
not confirmed
≠ rejected automatically

not rejected
≠ confirmed automatically
```

If a narrower claim remains usable—such as “the record documents a transition from objection entry to later handling”—that distinct claim may be retained under its own record and class. The original integration claim remains unresolved.

#### Four-axis result separation

Every completed `DECOMPOSE` occurrence must separate at least four axes:

1. **Local operation result** — what finer reconstruction was achieved;
2. **Source-function effect** — what happened to the coarser function under test;
3. **Prior source-claim disposition** — whether the prior claim is maintained, withdrawn, failed, or superseded without erasure;
4. **Canonical Output Class** — the governance result for the current tested transformation claim.

#### C20-AXES-01 — four-axis result declaration

For review R-17, the axes are:

```yaml
result_axes:
  local_operation_result:
    value: supported_relational_decomposition
    scope: documented objection-response-closure-follow-up sequence
  source_function_effect:
    value: rejected
    tested_function: objection integrated before closure
  prior_source_claim_disposition:
    value: failed
    claim: the review integrated the entered objection before closure
  canonical_output_class:
    value: admissible_with_bounded_claim
    retained_claim: the documented review process is decomposable into a supported authority-routing structure that shows closure and deferred handling, not demonstrated integration
```

The canonical class applies to the current decomposition claim, not to the rejected prior source-function claim. The material narrowing from “integration” to a documented closure-and-routing structure is explicit and tested. Nothing in this result authorizes a claim about participant motives, institutional legitimacy, causal sufficiency, or all review occurrences.

<a id="20-10-decomposition-output"></a>

### 20.10 Decomposition Output

A `DECOMPOSE` output consists of more than a component list and more than a verdict on the source function. It includes:

- the bounded finer reconstruction;
- its component and relation model;
- source-support and uncertainty declarations;
- internal temporality where relevant;
- source-function effect;
- prior source-claim disposition;
- canonical five-part Loss;
- Stop and Non-Capture status;
- and one canonical Output Class for the current tested claim.

Local result descriptions remain open and descriptive. They may include:

- supported relational decomposition;
- revised source function;
- heterogeneous source object;
- competing internal models;
- valid no-gain decomposition;
- insufficient source support;
- failed decomposition;
- or bounded non-capture.

These descriptions do not constitute a second class inventory.

```text
local result label
≠ canonical Output Class
```

#### Mapping discipline

A local output maps to a canonical class only after the applicable checks are complete.

| Local situation | Possible canonical treatment | Prohibited shortcut |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Fully supported bounded decomposition without material extra restriction | `admissible` | supported model = truth |
| Operation passes only after a material scope narrowing | `admissible_with_bounded_claim` | any contextual boundary = bounded-claim class |
| Usable decomposition with material unresolved source, temporal, calibration, or rival-model limits | `admissible_but_provisional` | uncertainty automatically protects the claim |
| Valid source-supported finer test without changed warranted reconstruction | `resolution_neutral` | no new finding = failed transformation |
| Separable subclaims or components receive different admissibility results | `partially_admissible` | average incompatible results into one class |
| Initial decomposition claim must be narrowed and retested | `claim_reduction_required` | retain the unrevised overclaim |
| Continuation would cross a binding boundary | `mandatory_stop` | continue because more detail is available |
| Operation identity or required procedure fails | `failed_transformation` | preserve the operation label because some details were found |
| No stable source-supported finer reconstruction is available | `non_capture` | use non-capture to shield a stronger failed claim |

`analogy_only` is not a successful `DECOMPOSE` output. If the supposed source relation is merely structural resemblance across different reference objects, the candidate has left same-reference decomposition. The analogical claim may be legitimate, but it must be recorded as such rather than routed through `DECOMPOSE`.

#### C20-NEUTRAL-01 — valid no-gain decomposition

A procedural Frame occurrence is opened from rule-level resolution into separate admission, registration, moderation, and closure components. Direct records support the components and their order. The finer reconstruction shows no material heterogeneity, no changed action corridor, no newly visible asymmetry, no altered source-function boundary, and no additional Loss relevant to the tested claim.

The operation is valid. The added distinctions do not change the warranted reconstruction.

```text
valid source-supported finer test
+ no praxeological resolution gain
→ resolution_neutral
```

This result does not prove that no finer distinction could ever matter. It does not automatically require a Stop. Further decomposition becomes unwarranted only if continued differentiation lacks a new source-supported relevance claim, merely repeats the neutral result, or crosses another binding boundary.

#### Distinct claims require distinct records

A single decomposition can affect several claims differently. For example:

- the claim that closure occurred may be confirmed;
- the claim that integration occurred may be rejected;
- the claim that every participant received the follow-up notice may remain underdetermined.

These are distinct claims. They cannot be compressed into one ambiguous class assignment. Each materially distinct claim requires its own record segment or linked record, while the operation-level source trace remains shared where appropriate.

```text
one source object
≠ one undifferentiated claim
```

<a id="20-11-non-invertibility"></a>

### 20.11 Non-Invertibility

`DECOMPOSE` is not the inverse of `COMPOSE`.

```text
DECOMPOSE(COMPOSE(X))
≠ X
```

The inequality persists even when both operations are admissible. A prior composition may have:

- selected only some source elements;
- compressed local variation;
- excluded rival paths;
- erased exact intermediate states;
- rendered component boundaries inseparable;
- or produced irrecoverable loss.

A later decomposition begins from the composed object and the sources currently available. It uses a new question, a new target granularity, and a new selection of relevant distinctions. It can reconstruct lineage and internal organization. It cannot recreate information that was never retained or is no longer recoverable.

The reverse direction is also non-identical:

```text
COMPOSE(DECOMPOSE(X))
≠ X
```

A new composition after decomposition introduces another selection and formation rule. It produces a new analytical object, not a transparent restoration of the source representation.

#### C20-NINV-01 — failed restoration claim

Suppose review R-17 had first been composed into the coarse label:

> objection handled and review closed.

The composition compressed the substantive qualification, deferred routing, notice uncertainty, and bounded non-reopening window.

The Chapter-20 decomposition can recover some of these structures because transcript, minutes, procedure, and follow-up records remain available. It cannot recover:

- undocumented exchanges during the handoff;
- exact participant uptake;
- unrecorded alternatives considered before closure;
- or a complete contemporaneous possibility space.

The resulting finer reconstruction is therefore not the original uncompressed review reality. It is a new, source-bounded reconstruction with explicit preserved, compressed, excluded, uncertain, and irrecoverable elements.

```text
reconstructed lineage
≠ restored prior information state
```

#### DECOMPOSE versus new PATH

Opening internal transitions of the same declared Path is permissible where reference, selection frame, temporal boundary, and path identity remain stable. If the analyst selects different transitions, adds new branch points, changes the temporal boundary, or forms a different historical object, the work is no longer merely opening the same Path.

#### C20-PATH-01 — operation switch to COMPOSE

An analyst begins with the review R-17 process but adds two earlier preparatory meetings, a later appeal, and an alternative branch that was not part of the declared source Path. The analyst then orders these elements into a new development and calls the result a decomposition of R-17.

This is not the same-source finer reconstruction specified in Chapters 18–20. The new selection and formation create a different Path object. A separate `COMPOSE` occurrence is required.

```text
new temporal selection + new formation rule
→ new COMPOSE claim
≠ DECOMPOSE of the original Path automatically
```

The earlier `DECOMPOSE` result remains recorded. The operation switch does not erase it.

#### DECOMPOSE versus PROJECT_AS

A finer reconstruction may later support a contextual function claim, but it does not itself assign that function.

#### C20-PROJ-01 — target function requires a separate record

After decomposing R-17, an analyst claims that the review trajectory functions as a Frame for later organizational dissent.

The source trace may support testing that proposition, but the proposition introduces:

- a target context;
- a contextual target function;
- a validity scope;
- and a new counterfactual relation.

It is therefore a separate `PROJECT_AS` occurrence. It must preserve the review’s origin type and cannot be inserted into the `DECOMPOSE` result as though finer reconstruction automatically produced a Frame function.

```text
finer source trace
≠ target function
```

<a id="20-12-decomposition-failure"></a>

### 20.12 Decomposition Failure

`DECOMPOSE` fails as a transformation when the operation claim cannot satisfy the defining procedural burdens. Failure may occur when:

- no stable source object is independently identifiable;
- the source is a Δ–Ψ operator type rather than an occurrence or composite;
- description is presented as changed granularity;
- proposed components lack source support;
- components are listed without reconstructed relations;
- the source object disappears into fragments;
- internal temporality is required but invented or omitted;
- source precision is exceeded by finer terminology;
- a new Path is silently substituted;
- a target function is silently assigned;
- the source function is no longer evaluable;
- canonical Loss is not disclosed;
- or a binding Stop condition is ignored.

Failure of the operation claim does not erase every weaker finding. Supported observations, source limitations, excluded components, and unresolved structures remain available under appropriately reduced claims.

#### C20-FAIL-01 — failed transformation with weaker findings preserved

An analyst proposes to decompose an alleged “informal alignment mechanism” inside a review. The available record supports only that three participants spoke in sequence and later voted similarly. It does not support:

- a bounded mechanism;
- participant coordination;
- component boundaries;
- dependency relations;
- shared intent;
- or a stable relation to the declared source function.

The analyst nevertheless names “signal exchange,” “coalition formation,” and “binding reinforcement” as components.

The `DECOMPOSE` claim fails because plausible terminology replaces source-supported components and relations. The canonical operation result may map to `failed_transformation`.

Weaker findings remain:

- a documented speaking order;
- a later voting pattern;
- and insufficient evidence for the proposed mechanism.

```text
failed transformation
≠ no observations exist
≠ stronger model protected
```

#### Mandatory Stop

Mandatory Stop applies when continuation itself would be inadmissible, not merely unproductive. Trigger families include:

- source precision no longer carries finer distinctions;
- reference continuity is lost;
- further detail falls below the Relevance Floor after a valid neutral result;
- continued decomposition merely displaces an objection to a finer level;
- claim or authority ceiling would be crossed;
- or the analyst must switch operations without declaring a new record.

#### C20-STOP-01 — binding continuation boundary

After a valid decomposition has established the review’s documented authority-routing sequence, an analyst seeks still finer claims about private motives, unrecorded emotional reactions, and hidden coalition structure. No declared source route supports these distinctions.

Further analysis is not merely provisional. The requested continuation would exceed the Source Ceiling and change the object from documented praxis structure to speculative person-level inference.

The correct result is `mandatory_stop` for that continuation claim.

```text
available interpretive vocabulary
≠ admissible continuation
```

The supported decomposition already obtained remains recorded. Stop does not retroactively invalidate it.

#### Non-Capture

Non-Capture applies when the source object and problem remain legitimate, but no stable, adequately supported finer reconstruction can be retained. It may arise when:

- rival internal models remain equally viable;
- every proposed component boundary is unstable;
- the relevant internal temporal order is inaccessible;
- or any finer model would require speculative completion that destroys source coherence.

#### C20-NC-01 — unresolved internal model without claim rescue

A review record establishes that an objection was entered, closure occurred, and a later change was implemented. The intervening materials are unavailable. Two incompatible internal reconstructions remain equally compatible with the sources:

1. the objection was integrated during an inaccessible intermediate process;
2. the later change arose independently after procedural closure.

Neither model can be preferred without invented intermediates. No stable finer reconstruction is retainable.

The operation may end as `non_capture`.

This result does not preserve the stronger claim that integration occurred. It preserves only:

- the source object;
- the known endpoints;
- the inaccessible interval;
- the rival models;
- and the inability to establish a finer relation.

```text
non_capture
≠ both models true
≠ stronger source claim retained
```

#### Failure, Stop, and Non-Capture remain distinct

```text
failed_transformation
→ the attempted operation claim does not satisfy DECOMPOSE

mandatory_stop
→ continuation is prohibited at the current boundary

non_capture
→ no stable supported finer reconstruction can be retained
```

A record may contain a successful earlier segment and a stopped later continuation. It may also preserve weaker findings after operation failure. Distinct claims and stages must remain separated.

<a id="20-13-decompose-record"></a>

### 20.13 DECOMPOSE Record

The operation-specific view for Chapter 20 is:

```yaml
decompose:
  source_object:
  origin_type:
  source_frame:
  source_granularity:
  target_granularity:
  decomposition_question:
  expected_difference:
  components:
  component_relations:
  internal_temporality:
  source_support:
  preserved_function:
  revised_function:
  unresolved_structure:
  loss:
  admissibility_result:
```

This sixteen-field view does not replace the Shared Transformation Record. It organizes the Chapter-20 fields that must be carried into their authoritative shared locations or controlled `extensions`.

#### Record mapping

| DECOMPOSE view | Shared Record owner |
| --- | --- |
| source object, type, Frame, granularity | `source` |
| target granularity and distinction change | `target` plus controlled granularity extension |
| question, expected difference, selection discipline | `operation` and `claim` |
| components, relations, internal temporality, support | controlled operation-specific `extensions` and evidence declarations |
| preserved/revised source function | `result.operation_specific_result.source_function_effect` and linked claim disposition |
| unresolved structure | uncertainty, alternatives, capture statement, and controlled extension |
| canonical five-part Loss | `loss` |
| admissibility result | `result.output_class` with routing and Stop/Non-Capture declarations |

A complete record must keep local result, source-function effect, prior claim disposition, resolution result, support status, capture statement, and canonical class separate.

#### C20-RECORD-01 — complete bounded record for review R-17

```yaml
decompose:
  source_object:
    reference: review_R17_documented_process
    object_category: operator_typed_composite_occurrence
    current_claim: review_R17_integrated_the_entered_objection_before_closure
  origin_type:
    primary: Frame_typed_occurrence
    additional_relevant_typing:
      - Asymmetry_typed_occurrence
      - Non_Event_bearing_composite
  source_frame:
    value: documented_objection_response_closure_and_follow_up_process
    preserved: true
  source_granularity:
    value: review_as_single_closed_handling_unit
  target_granularity:
    value: objection_response_qualification_closure_routing_and_revisit_relations
  decomposition_question:
    value: Which source-supported components and relations carried or disturbed the claim that the objection was integrated before closure?
    no_gain_condition: If finer distinctions show only the same supported integration function without material heterogeneity, classify the resolution test as neutral.
    stop_condition: Stop before motive, hidden-coordination, or participant-state claims unsupported by the declared sources.
  expected_difference:
    - distinguish official closure from substantive integration
    - test whether deferred routing preserved unresolved content
    - identify whether non_reopening alters the source-function claim
  components:
    established:
      - objection_entry_unit
      - chair_response_and_qualification_unit
      - closure_declaration_unit
      - follow_up_routing_unit
      - bounded_revisit_window
      - non_reopening_within_expected_window
    excluded:
      - hidden_participant_agreement
      - undocumented_motive_structure
      - informal_coalition_mechanism
  component_relations:
    - objection precedes and activates response
    - substantive qualification follows response and precedes closure
```
