```yaml
    - closure authority is asymmetrically located in the chair role
    - unresolved material is routed to later handling rather than integrated before closure
    - routing creates a bounded expectation of later treatment
    - non_reopening within the expected window preserves unresolved structure and changes continuation possibilities
  internal_temporality:
    order: partial_but_sufficient_for_the_tested_claim
    uncertainty:
      - exact undocumented handoff between closure and follow_up routing
      - whether all participants received identical notice
  source_support:
    direct_support:
      - transcript for objection response qualification and closure order
      - procedure for closure authority and revisit window
      - follow_up record for routing
      - later record for non_reopening within the window
    indirect_reconstruction:
      - relation between deferred routing and unresolved continuation
    uncertain_attribution:
      - participant uptake of follow_up notice
    missing_intermediate_structure:
      - undocumented handoff after closure
    rival_internal_models:
      - closure_as_integration
      - closure_with_deferred_unresolved_content
    inaccessible_areas:
      - private motives and unrecorded discussion
    unsupported_inferences:
      - hidden coalition
      - universal participant agreement
  preserved_function:
    value: official_closure_and_follow_up_routing_confirmed
  revised_function:
    source_function_effect: rejected
    rejected_claim: objection_integrated_before_closure
    retained_claim: documented_closure_and_deferred_handling_without_demonstrated_integration
    prior_source_claim_disposition: failed
  unresolved_structure:
    - exact mechanism of the closure_to_follow_up handoff
    - participant uptake of the routing notice
  loss:
    preserved:
      - source reference and origin typing
      - documented component order
      - authority routing
      - qualification before closure
      - bounded expectation and non_reopening
    compressed:
      - transcript wording not required for the bounded relational claim
    excluded:
      - person level motives
      - unsupported coalition hypotheses
      - other review occurrences
    uncertain:
      - undocumented handoff
      - notice distribution and uptake
    irrecoverable:
      - unrecorded alternatives considered during the closed interval
      - undocumented contemporaneous participant interpretations
  admissibility_result:
    local_operation_result: supported_relational_decomposition
    resolution_test_result: resolution_gain
    source_function_effect: rejected
    prior_source_claim_disposition: failed
    canonical_output_class: admissible_with_bounded_claim
    retained_scope: documented procedural structure of review_R17 only
    authority_inheritance: prohibited
```

This record is complete enough to expose the operation’s source, question, components, relations, temporal structure, support, source-function effect, Loss, and governance result. It does not prove empirical causality, institutional legitimacy, participant intention, or universal recurrence.

<a id="chapter-20-completion-boundary"></a>

#### Chapter 20 completion boundary

The integrated WP4 audit confirms Chapter 20 as canonically complete and provisionally locked because Sections 20.1–20.13 establish the complete generic `DECOMPOSE` procedure:

```text
source entry
→ granularity relation
→ operation question
→ expected difference and no-gain condition
→ source-support mapping
→ component identification
→ relation reconstruction
→ internal temporality where relevant
→ source-function effect
→ result-axis separation
→ canonical Loss
→ Output Class mapping
→ Failure / Stop / Non-Capture
→ complete DECOMPOSE Record
```

All twenty-eight Chapter-20 Pressure Duties are operationalized exactly once in the canonical prose. The integrated Chapter-20 result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

Chapter 20 is therefore **provisionally locked**. The class names the chapter-level methodological result under the still-open SUB Part and later corpus-wide audits. It does not imply that every attempted decomposition is admissible, does not elevate the review-R17 example into a general empirical finding, and does not increase claim or application authority.

```text
Chapter 20 provisionally locked
≠ family-specific occurrence decomposition complete
≠ Chapter 25 resolution taxonomy complete
≠ SUB provisionally locked
```

Reopening requires a concrete conflict involving PMS Base, Structure or Contract ownership, source-reference continuity, the distinction between components and relations, source-support ceilings, source-function/result-axis separation, Non-Invertibility, Loss, Failure/Stop/Non-Capture routing, the sixteen-field operation view, a Pressure Duty, a Reference or Formal-Model binding, or a later integrated dependency. Preference for more detail, a cleaner internal model, automatic class mapping, stronger restoration language, or premature family-specific extension is insufficient.

#### Chapter-21 handoff

The controlled continuation is:

```text
Chapter 20 provisional lock
→ Chapter 21 Preparation Gate
→ Chapter 21 — Decomposing Operator-Typed Occurrences
```

Chapter 20 supplies Chapter 21 with:

- a complete generic operation procedure;
- source and granularity declarations;
- component and relation burdens;
- source-support and uncertainty architecture;
- conditional internal temporality;
- source-function effects;
- four-axis result separation;
- Loss, Failure, Stop, and Non-Capture controls;
- and the complete operation-specific Record.

Chapter 21 owns the family-specific decomposition of Frame-, Attractor-, Asymmetry-, Impulse-, and Binding-typed occurrences. It may apply the procedure but may not redefine it.

```text
complete generic DECOMPOSE procedure
→ family-specific application

complete generic procedure
≠ completed occurrence-family theory
```

<a id="chapter-21-decomposing-operator-typed-occurrences"></a>

## 21. Decomposing Operator-Typed Occurrences

Chapter 21 applies the provisionally locked generic `DECOMPOSE` procedure to concrete source occurrences that have been typed through PMS operators. It does not reopen the definition of `DECOMPOSE`, does not revise the Δ–Ψ grammar, and does not treat operator types as empirical aggregates. Its subject is the occurrence-level question: which source-supported practices, relations, temporalities, Non-Events, roles, costs, thresholds, commitments, or other conditions produce, maintain, disturb, or fail to sustain one bounded operator-typed occurrence?

Sections 21.1–21.4 establish the general occurrence/type boundary and operationalize the Frame family through formation, maintenance, stability, internal variation, substitution, and counterevidence. Sections 21.5–21.9 apply the same generic procedure to Attractor- and Asymmetry-typed occurrences, including recurrence, friction, dynamic course forms, directional gradients, coordination, and offset. Sections 21.10–21.12 complete the comparative architecture through Impulse and Binding families, source-function effects, family-specific Failure, Mandatory Stop, Non-Capture, and the Chapter-22 handoff.

The integrated Chapter-21 result is therefore a complete **occurrence-family application of `DECOMPOSE` across five representative operator-typed families**. The families are not exhaustive, do not form a new enum or hierarchy, and do not modify the PMS operator grammar. Across every family, the operator type remains unchanged while the concrete source typing remains testable and revisable.

```text
operator type
≠ operator occurrence

operator identity preserved
≠ occurrence typing immunized

production conditions of an occurrence
≠ constituents of the operator type
```

<a id="21-1-general-rule"></a>

### 21.1 General Rule

SUB opens concrete operator-typed occurrences, not the PMS operators through which those occurrences are typed. The operation therefore starts from an independently identifiable source object and a bounded source-side typing claim. It asks how that occurrence is produced, maintained, disturbed, or limited under a declared finer granularity. It does not ask what the operator sign, name, type, or canonical function is materially “made of.”

The invalid question is:

> What is `□` made of?

The valid occurrence-level question is:

> Which source-supported practices, relations, temporalities, exclusions, Non-Events, and role structures produce or maintain this concrete Frame-typed occurrence?

The same distinction applies to every family:

```text
what is Α made of?
→ prohibited operator-type decomposition

which repeated configurations and friction relations
maintain this concrete Attractor-typed occurrence?
→ valid occurrence-level question candidate
```

The difference is not merely grammatical. An operator type is part of the unchanged PMS Base grammar. An operator occurrence is a bounded analytical typing of one historical or reconstructed source object. The first is not an empirical aggregate. The second may have source-supported production and maintenance conditions, internal variation, temporal phases, distributed work, disturbing components, or rival typings.

A Chapter-21 decomposition must therefore keep at least four declarations separate:

```text
object category
operator typing under test
current coarse function
source-function effect after decomposition
```

For example, the documented review arrangement used in Chapters 18–20 is an occurrence or composite as object category. It has been typed, for a bounded claim, as Frame-structured. Its current coarse function is to regulate which objections enter, remain inside, or leave the operative decision process. A later source-function effect may confirm, refine, internally differentiate, partially preserve, reject, or leave that bounded Frame typing underdetermined. None of these declarations changes the canonical meaning of `□`.

#### C21-RULE-01 — occurrence decomposition versus operator-type decomposition

An analyst lists agenda control, chair authority, objection-entry rules, closure declarations, and follow-up routing and concludes that these are the “parts of `□`.” The analysis fails before substantive component assessment because it assigns empirical constituents to the Frame operator type.

The corrected formulation is:

```yaml
source_object:
  reference: documented review occurrence R-17
  object_category: bounded procedural occurrence
  source_operator_typing: Frame-typed occurrence
  coarse_function_under_test: regulation of objection admission and procedural closure
  question: which source-supported practices and relations produce, maintain, qualify, or disturb this occurrence-level Frame function?
```

Agenda control, chair authority, entry rules, closure declarations, and routing may qualify as components or production conditions of this occurrence. They do not become constituents of `□`. If finer evidence later rejects the Frame typing, the result concerns this source claim only.

```text
rejected Frame typing of R-17
≠ rejection or decomposition of □
```

#### The generic procedure is reused, not re-derived

Chapter 20 already owns source identity, granularity declaration, decomposition question, expected difference, source-support mapping, component and relation burdens, conditional internal temporality, source-function effects, Loss, Failure, Stop, Non-Capture, and Output Class mapping. Chapter 21 changes the family-specific question and evidence burden. It does not create a separate operation for each operator family.

The recurring family questions are:

1. What is the concrete source occurrence?
2. Which operator typing is under test?
3. What bounded coarse function is associated with that typing?
4. Which finer structures are source-supported?
5. Which relations and temporalities organize them?
6. Which internal variations, substitutions, or counterevidence matter?
7. What source-function effect remains warranted?

These questions do not form a numerical scale or an operator hierarchy. A Frame occurrence is not “more structural” than an Impulse occurrence, and a Binding occurrence is not “higher” than an Asymmetry occurrence. Each family specifies a different source-side typing pressure within the same operation grammar.

#### Type preservation without typing immunity

Protecting operator identity does not protect the analytical typing of the occurrence. A source occurrence may have been coarsely typed too strongly, too exclusively, or through the wrong operator profile. Finer evidence may show that the occurrence is only partially Frame-structured, that Asymmetry carries more of its organization, that the supposed Attractor lacks sufficient recurrence, or that the claimed Binding rests on unsupported continuity assumptions.

```text
operator type preserved
+ occurrence typing rejected
→ Type Integrity retained
```

The source question and lineage remain available for testing. The earlier answer does not acquire immunity merely because it uses a canonical operator name.

#### Multiple typings remain possible

One source object may support more than one compatible or rival occurrence typing. A review arrangement can be Frame-typed because it regulates relevance and admission; Asymmetry-typed because access, timing, exposure, and closure authority are unequally distributed; and Binding-typed if repeated records and revisit commitments generate durable obligations. These claims may concern different functions of the same occurrence or compete over which typing carries the central explanatory load.

Chapter 21 must not collapse layered typing into an exclusive essence. Nor may it use multiplicity to avoid testing each bounded claim.

```text
multiple possible typings
≠ all typings equally supported
≠ one true essence required
```

The family analysis therefore segments claims. A Frame decomposition tests the bounded Frame function. A separate Asymmetry decomposition tests a bounded asymmetry claim. Where later composition or target-function projection is required, separate operation records remain necessary.

<a id="21-2-frame-typed-occurrence"></a>

### 21.2 Frame-Typed Occurrence

A Frame-typed occurrence is a concrete source object currently reconstructed as performing a bounded relevance, admission, exclusion, containment, or scope-setting function. The occurrence may be a meeting arrangement, institutional review procedure, recurring intake practice, access protocol, interactional episode, or another source-bounded configuration. Its Frame typing is not established by the presence of any one formal rule. It depends on how source-supported structures organize what can enter, remain operative, be deferred, be excluded, or count as outside the relevant reconstruction.

The family-specific decomposition question is:

> Which source-supported practices, relations, exclusions, roles, temporal routines, Non-Events, bindings, and stabilized interpretations produce or maintain this concrete Frame-typed occurrence?

A concrete Frame occurrence may be carried through:

- repeated selection practices;
- admission and exclusion rules;
- agenda and scope control;
- role allocation;
- access conditions;
- speaking or objection-entry procedures;
- temporal windows and deadlines;
- closure and reopening rules;
- recurring Non-Events;
- institutional commitments;
- stabilized interpretive cues;
- distributed reproduction work.

This list is open and occurrence-bound. It is not a catalogue of universal constituents of `□`. Each candidate condition requires a source route and a relation to the bounded Frame claim.

#### C21-FRAME-01 — source-supported Frame production and maintenance

For review occurrence R-17, the available agenda, transcript, minutes, closure notice, and follow-up record support a finer reconstruction with the following bounded structures:

```yaml
occurrence_family:
  source_operator_typing: Frame-typed occurrence
  coarse_function_under_test: bounded admission, qualification, closure, and possible revisit of objections
  source_supported_conditions:
    - agenda-defined issue boundary
    - chair-controlled objection entry
    - qualification requirement before substantive uptake
    - closure declaration after bounded discussion
    - separate follow-up route for unresolved material
    - time-limited revisit window
    - repeated non-reopening after window expiry
  relations_expected_to_matter:
    - selection
    - authority placement
    - temporal ordering
    - routing
    - expectation formation
    - non-reopening
```

The materials support the existence and order of these structures at different levels of precision. They do not establish participant motives, universal institutional practice, or the legitimacy of the procedure. They support a bounded occurrence-level claim that the review arrangement was maintained through linked admission, qualification, closure, routing, and temporal-window practices.

The Frame typing remains under test because these same materials also show disturbance: the objection entered the procedure, was qualified, and was routed rather than simply excluded. A decomposition must therefore reconstruct the boundary as internally differentiated rather than merely restating that the meeting “had a Frame.”

#### Frame occurrence versus formal container

A formal document, agenda, room, role title, or rule is not automatically a Frame occurrence. Such elements may contribute to framing, but the occurrence-level claim concerns their operative relation. An agenda that is ignored may fail to bound relevance. A nominal revisit window that cannot be used may function differently from an effective reopening route. A chair title without control over admission may not carry the claimed boundary.

```text
formal container
≠ operative Frame occurrence automatically

written rule
≠ reproduced relevance boundary automatically
```

A Frame occurrence must therefore be reconstructed through actual or source-supported production and maintenance, not inferred from labels alone.

#### Frame typing and source precision

The semantic precision of the Frame claim cannot exceed the precision of the sources. If the record supports agenda order and official closure but not informal preselection, the decomposition may reconstruct the documented procedural Frame while withholding a broader claim about all relevant access. If notice distribution is uncertain, the analysis must not treat the revisit window as equally available to every role.

A bounded Frame decomposition can therefore retain differentiated support:

```text
directly supported procedural boundary
+ uncertain informal access conditions
+ inaccessible participant interpretation
```

This is not a defect to be hidden. It is the actual source topology of the occurrence.

#### C21-MULTI-01 — multiple and rival typings of one source object

R-17 supports at least two distinct bounded questions. The first asks whether agenda, entry, closure, routing, and non-reopening maintain a Frame function. The second asks whether chair authority, timing, notice access, and unequal capacity to activate follow-up constitute an Asymmetry occurrence.

The Frame analysis may not absorb the Asymmetry claim merely because both concern the same event. Nor may the presence of Asymmetry invalidate the Frame claim automatically.

```text
same source object
+ Frame-typed claim
+ Asymmetry-typed claim
→ segmented tests
```

The two typings may be compatible, or finer evidence may show that the Frame description understates the asymmetry that carries the operative boundary. That comparison belongs to separately declared claims and later family analysis. The source object does not acquire one exclusive operator essence.

<a id="21-3-frame-formation"></a>

### 21.3 Frame Formation

Frame formation concerns the source-supported production and maintenance conditions through which an occurrence comes to perform a bounded Frame function. It is distinct from the coarse function itself.

```text
how a Frame occurrence is produced and maintained
≠ what bounded Frame function it performs
```

The function may be stated coarsely as regulating relevance, admission, exclusion, scope, or closure. Formation analysis asks which practices, relations, temporalities, roles, Non-Events, and commitments make that function operative in this occurrence.

The analysis should test at least five dimensions.

**Selection and admission.** Which structures determine what can enter? This may include agenda categories, submission routes, speaking order, evidentiary requirements, authorization, or implicit recognition practices.

**Exclusion and deferral.** What is treated as outside, premature, insufficiently qualified, or routed elsewhere? Deferral can preserve an issue, neutralize it, or transform its later accessibility. These effects must not be collapsed.

**Role and authority placement.** Which positions can set, revise, suspend, or close the boundary? Authority may be concentrated, distributed, formally assigned, informally exercised, or partly contested.

**Temporal organization.** Which windows, deadlines, sequences, repetitions, or delays make the boundary effective? A Frame can be reproduced through timing even when no explicit exclusion occurs.

**Non-Events and maintenance.** Which expected reopening, response, escalation, or repair fails to occur, and how does that non-occurrence stabilize the boundary? Missing information alone is not a Non-Event; the expectation basis and window must be source-supported.

#### C21-FORM-01 — formation conditions versus coarse function

In R-17, the coarse Frame function is that objections can enter a bounded review process, be qualified and discussed, and then be closed or routed to a later channel. The formation conditions are more heterogeneous:

1. the agenda defines the initial issue scope;
2. chair authority controls objection entry and turn allocation;
3. qualification requirements determine whether an objection receives substantive uptake;
4. a closure declaration ends the immediate review phase;
5. unresolved material is transferred to a separate follow-up route;
6. the revisit window limits how long reopening remains institutionally available;
7. repeated non-reopening after expiry stabilizes closure.

The function is not identical to any one condition. Nor are the conditions interchangeable by default. Their relation produces the bounded occurrence-level Frame.

```text
agenda rule alone
≠ complete Frame occurrence

closure declaration alone
≠ complete Frame occurrence

linked admission + qualification + closure + routing + temporal window
→ source-supported formation model candidate
```

This distinction prevents circular analysis. The Frame typing cannot be “explained” by merely repeating that the occurrence bounded relevance. The decomposition must show how the boundary is reproduced and where it is disturbed.

#### Reproduction is not necessarily centralized

Frame maintenance may be distributed across multiple roles and artifacts. One role sets the agenda, another administers access, another records closure, and a later office controls reopening. Participants may also reproduce the Frame by orienting their contributions to accepted categories or by not contesting a closure marker. Such distributed work does not automatically create a higher-level composite claim; it specifies the occurrence-level relations through which the same bounded Frame is sustained.

The analysis must preserve unequal contributions. “The group maintained the Frame” may be too coarse where one role possesses closure authority and others merely adapt to it. Distributed maintenance does not imply symmetric responsibility or equal capacity.

#### Non-Events in Frame formation

A Frame may be maintained by relevant non-occurrences: an expected response is not issued, a promised review is not reopened, an excluded category never receives an admission route, or an exception procedure remains unused. These Non-Events require their own expectation basis, source window, affected alternatives, and uncertainty.

For R-17, repeated non-reopening after the documented revisit window is relevant because the follow-up route created an expectation of possible reconsideration. The expiry and subsequent absence of reopening contribute to closure. The analysis must not infer intention, deliberate suppression, or guilt from this structure.

#### C21-PERSON-01 — occurrence analysis without person inference

The chair’s role in objection entry and closure is structurally relevant. It does not authorize the claim that the chair is “a controlling person,” possesses a Frame-oriented personality, intended exclusion, lacks openness, or bears a moral rank. Likewise, participants’ adaptation to agenda categories does not prove passivity, compliance traits, or psychological acceptance.

The permitted claim is occurrence-bound:

```text
within R-17,
chair authority carried specified admission and closure relations
```

The prohibited conversion is:

```text
occurrence-level role function
→ person property, motive, diagnosis, maturity rank, or moral identity
```

This boundary is not merely ethical caution. It is Type Integrity. Chapter 21 reconstructs praxis structures in one bounded occurrence; it does not infer enduring person types from operator-typed configurations.

<a id="21-4-frame-stability-and-internal-variation"></a>

### 21.4 Frame Stability and Internal Variation

A stable coarse Frame function does not require an internally homogeneous occurrence. Frame stability concerns whether a bounded relevance, admission, exclusion, or scope-setting function remains reconstructable across relevant internal variation. Homogeneity concerns whether components, roles, practices, and local effects are alike. These are different questions.

```text
stable Frame function
≠ internally homogeneous structure

internal variation
≠ automatic Frame failure
```

A Frame occurrence may remain stable while:

- roles change occupants;
- admission practices vary locally;
- participants contest the boundary;
- exclusion attempts partly fail;
- maintenance work is unequally distributed;
- counter-practices create exceptions;
- one component is substituted;
- some material remains unresolved outside the main route.

Conversely, apparent stability may be overstated where variation changes the operative boundary itself. The decomposition must therefore test which differences are compatible with the coarse function and which materially pressure it.

#### C21-HOMO-01 — stable function with internal variation and conflict

R-17 does not display a single homogeneous boundary practice. The objection enters, is interrupted, receives qualification, generates a repair proposal, is followed by closure, and remains partially represented in a follow-up route. Some structures admit material; others narrow, redirect, or terminate its operative status. Participants contest the adequacy of closure, and the later route preserves only a bounded possibility of reconsideration.

Despite this variation, a coarse Frame function may still be retained: the occurrence regulates how objections move from entry to qualification, closure, and possible revisit. The function is stable only in this bounded sense. It does not justify the stronger claim that every component excludes, every participant shares the same boundary, or all internal phases perform one identical function.

```text
heterogeneous production conditions
+ traceable admission/closure relation
→ possible stable bounded Frame function
```

The Frame analysis becomes stronger by preserving conflict rather than translating every local structure into Frame-like behavior.

#### C21-SUBST-01 — component substitution without automatic type failure

Suppose a later review occurrence uses an automated submission queue rather than chair-controlled verbal entry, while agenda scope, qualification criteria, closure authority, follow-up routing, and revisit limits remain functionally comparable. The entry component has changed, but the bounded Frame function may remain.

This does not establish that components are freely interchangeable. The substitution test asks:

- does the replacement preserve the same reference-relevant admission relation?
- does it redistribute access, timing, exposure, or authority?
- does it alter the relevant Non-Events?
- does the coarse function survive only after claim reduction?

```text
changed component
≠ changed occurrence type automatically

same coarse function
≠ same internal configuration
```

Component substitution therefore pressures one-to-one models of occurrence typing. A Frame function may be multiply realized, but “multiple realization” is itself a bounded claim requiring comparable source support and Loss disclosure.

#### C21-COUNTER-01 — finer evidence pressures the Frame typing

The R-17 decomposition also contains counterevidence. If the follow-up route is inaccessible in practice, notice is unevenly distributed, or closure authority prevents any operative reopening, the earlier claim that objections remain inside a bounded review Frame may be too strong. The finer reconstruction may show that the arrangement performs admission only temporarily and functions primarily as closure or displacement after qualification.

Possible source-function effects remain open:

```text
confirmed
refined
internally_differentiated
partially_preserved
rejected
underdetermined
```

WP1 does not select a final effect. It establishes why the Frame typing is genuinely testable. Finer evidence must be able to alter the claim; otherwise the decomposition has been constructed as an immunity device.

```text
all finer evidence translated into support for Frame typing
→ failed counterevidence discipline
```

A material disturbance may refine the typing, reduce its scope, or reject it. Rejection of the occurrence typing does not invalidate `□`, and retention of the typing does not validate every proposed internal model.

#### Internal variation and Stop

Further Frame decomposition should stop when:

- additional variation no longer changes a warranted occurrence claim;
- sources cannot distinguish competing production models;
- the analysis begins assigning motives or person properties;
- the question shifts to a new composite object;
- a target function is introduced without `PROJECT_AS`;
- operator definitions are being treated as decomposable content.

An Optional Stop records sufficient resolution. A Mandatory Stop prevents operation drift, source overreach, person inference, or operator redefinition. Non-Capture remains available where no stable source-supported formation model can be retained; it does not restore the original Frame typing by default.

#### Chapter 21 WP1 completion boundary

Sections 21.1–21.4 establish the operator-type/occurrence boundary and operationalize the Frame family through production and maintenance conditions, formation/function separation, stable coarse function with internal variation, component substitution, counterevidence, multiple typings, and the person-inference prohibition.

All eight WP1 Pressure Duties are represented exactly once. The local result remains:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

WP1 does not yet operationalize Attractor-, Asymmetry-, Impulse-, or Binding-typed occurrences, complete the Chapter-21 source-function analysis, select a canonical chapter-level Output Class beyond the local production status, create standalone case or Record artifacts, or provisionally lock Chapter 21.

```text
WP1 complete
≠ Chapter 21 complete
≠ operator-family theory complete
≠ occurrence typing automatically confirmed
≠ Chapter 21 provisionally locked
≠ SUB provisionally locked
```

The next controlled step is:

```text
Chapter 21 WP1 complete
→ Chapter 21 WP2, §§21.5–21.9
→ Attractor- and Asymmetry-typed occurrences
```

<a id="21-5-attractor-typed-occurrence"></a>

### 21.5 Attractor-Typed Occurrence

A concrete Attractor-typed occurrence is a bounded source object for which one configuration, continuation, or transition form has become recurrently easier to enter, sustain, recognize, or resume than relevant alternatives. `DECOMPOSE` does not open `Α` as an operator type. It reconstructs the source-supported repetition, friction relations, expectations, roles, costs, Non-Events, thresholds, and exit conditions through which this one occurrence is maintained.

The family question is therefore:

> Which source-supported configurations and relations make this concrete continuation recurrently easier, more familiar, more visible, or more readily coordinated than relevant alternatives?

The question is stronger than asking what happens repeatedly. Repetition is part of the possible source load, but it is not sufficient. A bounded Attractor occurrence requires a relation between recurrence and the practical field of continuation. At least one relevant configuration must become differentially accessible, friction-reduced, expected, role-supported, or costly to leave. The claim must remain sensitive to changes in those conditions.

```text
repetition
≠ Attractor occurrence automatically

frequent event
≠ stabilized continuation field automatically

Attractor operator preserved
≠ Attractor typing immunized
```

The source object may be a single temporally extended occurrence, a bounded recurrent procedure, or a previously identified composite. Its reference boundary must be declared before family-specific component selection. A series of superficially similar scenes cannot be gathered after the fact and called one Attractor occurrence merely because they fit the same narrative. If the analyst must first select several independent occurrences and form a new object, that formation requires `COMPOSE` before the result can become a `DECOMPOSE` source.

For illustration, consider a bounded review-series example supported by dated agendas, routing logs, closure notices, follow-up requests, and reopening records. Across the declared interval, objections are repeatedly admitted through the same qualification channel, routed away from the operative decision after closure, and reconsidered only if a narrow revisit condition is activated within a limited window. The source question is not whether “the organization resists objections” or whether any participant possesses an avoidant disposition. It is whether the documented route has become the recurrently easier continuation relative to reopening inside the operative review.

The candidate Attractor typing is carried only if the sources support more than recurrence. Relevant finer structures may include:

- repeated availability of the qualification-and-routing sequence;
- lower procedural friction for closure plus later routing than for operative reopening;
- stable expectations about where objections will be placed after closure;
- repeated occupation of coordinating roles;
- bounded revisit windows that expire without activation;
- documentation practices that preserve the routed continuation;
- increasing coordination cost for returning an objection to the operative decision;
- absence or delay of alternative routes that would otherwise remain available.

These structures are occurrence-level production and maintenance conditions. They are not constituents of `Α`, and they do not establish a general organizational Attractor beyond the declared source series.

#### C21-ATTR-01 — source-supported Attractor reproduction conditions

In the bounded review-series example, the same routing continuation appears across the declared interval. The record supports repeated closure notices, repeated placement of unresolved objections into the follow-up channel, and repeated reliance on a narrow revisit window. Participants can activate the familiar route with fewer procedural steps than operative reopening, while reopening requires additional authorization and coordination.

A bounded Attractor claim is warranted only to the extent that the sources support the differential relation:

```text
familiar routed continuation
+ lower local procedural friction
+ stabilized expectation
+ higher reopening coordination cost
→ candidate Attractor-typed occurrence
```

The claim remains limited. It does not establish inevitability, preference, intention, legitimacy, efficiency, or a person-level tendency. It also does not establish that every review follows the route. The occurrence may tolerate exceptions while remaining recurrently biased, provided the exceptions and their practical effects are preserved.

#### C21-REPEAT-01 — repetition without sufficient Attractor load

Suppose the same review form is used five times, but each use is independently required by a current rule, both reopening and follow-up remain equally accessible, participants possess no stable expectation about the continuation, and the sequence leaves no increasing friction, role stabilization, or alternative restriction. The source supports repetition, but not a differential continuation field.

The correct result is not to force an Attractor typing from frequency alone. A weaker recurrence description remains available:

```text
repeated form
+ no supported friction difference
+ no expectation stabilization
+ no altered alternative accessibility
→ recurrence retained
→ Attractor typing not established
```

This counterpressure is essential. Otherwise any repeated event would become an Attractor occurrence, and `Α` would lose discrimination. Finer resolution may therefore reject or leave underdetermined the occurrence typing while preserving the documented repetition.

<a id="21-6-attractor-reproduction"></a>

### 21.6 Attractor Reproduction

Attractor reproduction concerns the relations through which a recurrent bias is renewed. It is not a claim that the occurrence reproduces itself autonomously. The reconstruction must identify source-supported carriers and show how they alter entry, continuation, interruption, or exit relative to relevant alternatives.

A complete family-specific reconstruction should test at least six questions:

1. **Repeated object:** What configuration or transition form recurs?
2. **Friction relation:** Which continuation requires fewer steps, permissions, resources, explanations, or coordination acts?
3. **Expectation relation:** Which continuation becomes anticipated by relevant roles?
4. **Alternative relation:** Which alternatives become less visible, more delayed, or more costly?
5. **Maintenance relation:** Which roles, records, routines, Non-Events, or thresholds renew the bias?
6. **Counterfactual relation:** Would a relevant change in those carriers alter the occurrence typing?

The components may be distributed. One role may initiate the familiar route, a record may preserve it, a time limit may close alternatives, and repeated Non-Activation may stabilize the expectation that reopening will not occur. No single component need be necessary or sufficient. The occurrence-level Attractor may be multiply maintained, partially redundant, or vulnerable to substitution.

```text
maintenance carrier
≠ sole cause

lower friction
≠ moral preference

stable expectation
≠ psychological belief diagnosis

restricted alternative
≠ impossible alternative
```

#### Repetition and local friction

“Lower friction” is relational and claim-bound. It may mean fewer procedural steps, lower coordination cost, greater role familiarity, easier document reuse, quicker authorization, or reduced uncertainty. It must not become a universal quantitative metric unless the sources support one. The chapter may compare declared burdens; it may not manufacture a score from qualitative material.

In the review-series example, follow-up routing may be lower-friction because the closure notice already contains the route, the responsible role is assigned, the template exists, and no renewed decision quorum is required. Operative reopening may remain possible but require a new agenda slot, renewed authorization, participant availability, and redistribution of already closed materials. These relations can support a bounded Attractor typing without implying that the route is desirable or normatively justified.

#### Expectation stabilization and Non-Events

Expectation stabilization requires an identifiable expectation frame. Repeated absence of reopening is not automatically an Attractor carrier. It becomes relevant where the procedure creates a bounded reopening possibility and later records show repeated non-activation or expiration of that possibility. The Non-Event may then contribute to the recurrent continuation by reducing the practical salience of reopening and stabilizing the follow-up route.

```text
missing information
≠ Non-Event

repeated non-reopening
+ warranted reopening expectation
+ altered continuation field
→ possible Attractor carrier
```

The analysis must preserve uncertainty about why the window was not activated. It may reconstruct the structural effect of expiration without assigning motive, indifference, strategic intent, or guilt.

#### Thresholds and exit costs

Attractor pressure may change discontinuously. A deadline, quorum requirement, accumulated documentation burden, or repeated role handoff may create a threshold after which the familiar continuation becomes markedly easier than reversal. Such a threshold must be source-supported. It is not inferred merely because the later path appears stable.

Exit cost is likewise bounded. It may refer to additional coordination, delay, formal authorization, resource expenditure, or exposure. It does not mean that exit is impossible or that an actor is psychologically unable to leave the pattern. The source record must distinguish available but costly alternatives from alternatives that were never available.

#### Reproduction without determinism

An Attractor occurrence biases continuation; it does not determine it. Counter-practices, new rules, role substitution, unexpected events, or activated alternatives may interrupt the pattern. A strong family reconstruction therefore preserves both reproduction and interruption conditions.

A useful component test asks:

> If the familiar template, role assignment, reopening threshold, or routing record changed, would the continuation remain recurrently easier in the same warranted sense?

If the answer is no, the altered component has counterfactual load. If the answer is yes across many changes, the typing may be overelastic or supported by other carriers. The reconstruction should identify which interpretation the sources permit rather than protecting the label against revision.

<a id="21-7-dynamic-attractor-occurrence"></a>

### 21.7 Dynamic Attractor Occurrence

An Attractor-typed occurrence may stabilize a recurrent transition form rather than one static configuration. The object of decomposition is then a bounded cycle or recurrent course-form whose internal transitions and reproduction conditions remain source-supported.

A representative dynamic form may be written:

```text
approach
→ intensification
→ binding expectation
→ withdrawal
→ reframing
→ renewed approach
```

This notation is not itself evidence. The chapter must identify the source reference, transition markers, relevant temporal order, repeated relation, interruption conditions, and practical difference between the recurrent form and alternatives. A selected dramatic sequence does not become a dynamic Attractor merely because it can be narrated as a cycle.

#### C21-DYN-01 — recurrent transition form without static-state reduction

Consider a bounded coordination series in which a proposal repeatedly enters exploratory discussion, gains provisional commitment, encounters an unresolved allocation condition, is moved into a later frame, and then returns in revised form. The sources support several iterations of the transition form, but no single stable state persists across the whole interval.

A static description such as “the proposal remains deferred” would lose the recurring movement. A dynamic Attractor reconstruction may instead identify:

- the trigger for renewed approach;
- the intensification threshold;
- the provisional commitment marker;
- the unresolved condition producing withdrawal;
- the reframing practice enabling renewed entry;
- the lower friction of repeating this course-form relative to final adoption or abandonment.

The claim remains occurrence-bound:

```text
recurrently stabilized transition form
≠ teleological trajectory
≠ inevitable cycle
≠ person pattern
```

The decomposition may show that one transition carries most of the stabilizing load, that later iterations differ, or that the course-form fragments under finer resolution. The Attractor typing must remain revisable.

#### Dynamic occurrence versus Path and Trajectory

A dynamic Attractor occurrence can contain temporal order, but temporal order alone does not make it a Path or Trajectory. PATH asks how configurations and transitions form a realized historical object. Chapter 21 asks which finer relations reproduce a concrete Attractor-typed occurrence. The same source material may support both questions only through separated claims and records.

If the analyst selects several cycles, orders them into a new historical development, identifies branch points, and forms a new composite path object, a separate `COMPOSE` occurrence is required. The decomposition of one already identified dynamic occurrence cannot silently perform that formation.

#### C21-RETYPE-01 — dynamic Attractor, COMPOSE, and PROJECT_AS ownership

An analyst reconstructs the internal transition cycle of the bounded coordination series and then states that the cycle “is an Attractor for the entire organization” and “functions as the Frame through which later proposals are interpreted.” Three claims have been collapsed:

1. occurrence-level `DECOMPOSE` of the bounded dynamic Attractor;
2. possible `COMPOSE` of several occurrences into a broader organizational object;
3. possible `PROJECT_AS` of an origin-typed object as a bounded target function.

The Chapter-21 result may establish only the first claim. A broader composite requires declared source selection and formation. A target function requires origin-type preservation, target context, Source Trace, Counterfactual Sensitivity, and its own record.

```text
dynamic Attractor-typed occurrence
≠ organizational Attractor composite automatically
≠ projected Attractor-function automatically
≠ projected Frame-function automatically
```

This boundary also protects against label substitution. Recurrent internal transitions do not authorize a new PMS primitive or a cross-context function merely because the word “Attractor” already appears in the source typing.

<a id="21-8-asymmetry-typed-occurrence"></a>

### 21.8 Asymmetry-Typed Occurrence

A concrete Asymmetry-typed occurrence is a bounded source object in which relevant positions differ directionally with respect to access, resource, exposure, burden, information, timing, capacity, role authority, responsibility capacity, or exit condition. `DECOMPOSE` reconstructs the source-supported local gradients and their relations. It does not decompose `Ω`, infer moral rank, or assume that every difference forms one coordinated asymmetry.

The family question is:

> Which source-supported differences constitute this concrete Asymmetry-typed occurrence, along which dimensions, between which positions, during which interval, and with what practical effects?

The declaration must identify the comparator. “Unequal” has no sufficient analytical meaning without stating who or what is compared, along which dimension, under which Frame, and for which claim. Different dimensions may point in different directions. One role may possess greater decision authority while another possesses greater information, greater exposure, or lower exit cost.

```text
difference
≠ Asymmetry automatically

one directional gradient
≠ total superiority

role authority
≠ person worth

structural burden
≠ guilt automatically
```

The R-17 review occurrence already supports a bounded Asymmetry question distinct from its Frame typing. Agenda entry, chair interruption authority, closure authority, notice access, timing, capacity to activate follow-up, and exposure to unresolved consequences are not evenly distributed. Chapter 21 may now open that occurrence-level typing without collapsing it into the Frame claim.

#### C21-ASYM-01 — source-supported finer structure of one Asymmetry occurrence

For R-17, the declared source bundle supports several local gradients:

- the chair can interrupt and declare closure; ordinary participants cannot;
- one office controls final approval; other participants can recommend or object;
- formal notice reaches recorded roles directly, while informal or peripheral participants have less certain access;
- activating the follow-up route requires procedural knowledge and action within a bounded window;
- the unresolved objection imposes different temporal and coordination burdens on the approving office, the objecting participant, and affected downstream roles;
- exit from the operative review is immediate for the approving office after closure, while participants seeking reconsideration must carry additional follow-up work.

These distinctions support a bounded Asymmetry occurrence only where the relations are source-traceable. They do not establish a single total ranking among persons or roles. Some gradients may be independent, some may reinforce one another, and some may remain underdetermined.

A family-specific record should preserve at least:

```yaml
asymmetry_occurrence:
  compared_positions:
  dimensions:
  directional_gradients:
  temporal_scope:
  practical_effects:
  source_support:
  offsetting_gradients:
  unresolved_dimensions:
```

This is a controlled view, not a new schema. The Shared Transformation Record and existing extensions remain authoritative.

#### Source-function pressure

Finer evidence may confirm that R-17 contains a bounded procedural Asymmetry while rejecting stronger claims such as “all power lies with one actor” or “every participant bears the same kind of disadvantage.” It may also show that the Frame function and Asymmetry function are carried by overlapping but non-identical components.

The chapter does not yet select the final Source-Function Effect. It preserves the open possibilities:

```text
confirmed
refined
internally_differentiated
partially_preserved
rejected
underdetermined
```

A rejected Asymmetry typing concerns the bounded occurrence claim, not `Ω`. A retained typing does not authorize diagnosis, sanction, legitimacy judgment, or automatic action recommendation.

<a id="21-9-distributed-asymmetry"></a>

### 21.9 Distributed Asymmetry

A coarse Asymmetry occurrence may be distributed across many local gradients. Distribution can occur across roles, procedures, resources, records, locations, phases, or intervals. The analytical burden is not met by listing unequal positions. The decomposition must test whether the local gradients belong to the same reference object and whether they align, reinforce, offset, alternate, or remain uncoordinated.

```text
many local asymmetries
≠ one coordinated Asymmetry occurrence automatically

spatial distribution
≠ macro level automatically

temporal persistence
≠ common direction automatically
```

A distributed occurrence is established only where the sources support a relation among the gradients. Possible coordination relations include:

- common direction toward the same role position;
- sequential reinforcement across phases;
- shared dependency on one access rule;
- cumulative burden imposed by several independent requirements;
- substitution, where one gradient weakens while another preserves the practical effect;
- threshold interaction, where several modest differences jointly alter reachability or exit.

The relation may remain partial. A source object can contain a bounded Asymmetry on one dimension while local gradients offset on another.

#### C21-DISTR-01 — distributed local gradients with tested coordination

In R-17, chair authority, final approval authority, notice access, time-window knowledge, and follow-up activation capacity are distributed across different roles and records. The gradients are not located in one person or one rule. A coordinated occurrence claim is supported only if the sources show that these differences converge on the same practical relation: unequal capacity to keep an objection operative after formal closure.

The decomposition therefore tests:

- whether each gradient affects that same continuation;
- whether the gradients operate during compatible intervals;
- whether one gradient merely correlates with the others;
- whether a changed notice rule would alter follow-up accessibility;
- whether a changed approval rule would redistribute closure authority;
- whether participants with procedural knowledge can offset limited formal authority.

If these relations are supported, the occurrence may be internally distributed yet analytically coherent. If not, the coarse Asymmetry label must be segmented or reduced.

#### C21-OFFSET-01 — local asymmetries partly offset rather than reinforce

Suppose ordinary participants lack final approval authority but possess equal agenda access, direct notice, a low-friction appeal route, and an independent capacity to trigger reconsideration. The approval gradient remains real, while the access and exit gradients partly offset its practical effect.

The correct reconstruction does not sum all differences into one increasing severity measure. It records dimension-specific directions:

```text
approval authority gradient
→ favors approving office

agenda and notice access
→ approximately equal

independent reconsideration trigger
→ offsets some closure asymmetry
```

The bounded Asymmetry claim may remain, but it must be refined. Offset is neither symmetry nor erasure. Likewise, persistence of one gradient does not prove dominance across all dimensions.

#### C21-MACRO-01 — local differences without a warranted macro-asymmetry

An analyst collects unequal speaking times from one meeting, different resource allocations from another unit, and unequal appeal knowledge from a third process. The examples are real, but the analyst has not established a common reference object, compatible temporal scope, shared roles, or a coordination relation. Calling the collection “the institutional Asymmetry” silently composes a new macro-object.

The valid results are limited:

- several local Asymmetry candidates may be retained;
- their comparability may remain partial;
- a broader composition may be proposed as a new testable claim;
- Non-Capture may remain where no stable coordination model can be supported.

```text
local gradients from several objects
+ no declared formation rule
→ no macro-Asymmetry object yet
```

A separate `COMPOSE` occurrence is required to select and relate those sources into a new composite. Chapter 21 cannot gain macro authority by treating distribution as already composed.

#### Distributed Asymmetry and operation boundaries

The same source object may support several separated operations:

```text
DECOMPOSE
→ open one already identified Asymmetry occurrence

COMPOSE
→ form a new composite from several local occurrences

PROJECT_AS
→ assign one origin-typed object a bounded target function
```

No operation inherits success from another. A valid R-17 decomposition does not validate a broader institutional composition. A valid composition does not establish a projected Frame-, Attractor-, or governance function. Each transformation is a new testable claim with its own Loss, Source Trace, Counterfactual Sensitivity, Stop, and Non-Capture conditions.

#### WP2 source-function and output boundary

Sections 21.5–21.9 establish Attractor- and Asymmetry-family burdens, but they do not select the final Chapter-21 Source-Function Effects or canonical Output Classes. Family-specific findings may later be confirmed, refined, internally differentiated, partially preserved, rejected, or left underdetermined. Their operation results and classes remain claim-segmented.

Further analysis must stop where:

- recurrence cannot be connected to differential continuation;
- local friction or cost relations exceed source precision;
- a dynamic course-form is converted into a target function;
- distributed gradients lack a common reference or coordination relation;
- person, motive, legitimacy, or moral rank is inferred;
- a new macro-object is formed without `COMPOSE`;
- a contextual function is assigned without `PROJECT_AS`.

WP3 retains ownership of the Impulse and Binding families, explicit application of source-function effects across Chapter 21, Failed Operator-Occurrence Decomposition, Mandatory Stop, Non-Capture, the completion boundary, and Chapter-22 handoff.

#### Chapter 21 WP2 completion boundary

Sections 21.5–21.9 operationalize Attractor and Asymmetry occurrence families through recurrence, friction, expectation, Non-Events, thresholds, dynamic transition forms, dimension-specific gradients, distributed coordination, offset, and macro-claim restraint. All eight WP2 Pressure Duties are represented exactly once.

The local result remains:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

```text
WP2 complete
≠ Chapter 21 complete
≠ final source-function effect selected
≠ macro-Asymmetry composed
≠ target function projected
≠ Chapter 21 provisionally locked
≠ SUB provisionally locked
```

The next controlled step is Chapter 21 WP3, §§21.10–21.12: Impulse-Typed Occurrence, Binding-Typed Occurrence, Failed Operator-Occurrence Decomposition, complete source-function/failure/Stop/Non-Capture logic, and the Chapter-22 handoff.

<a id="21-10-impulse-typed-occurrence"></a>

### 21.10 Impulse-Typed Occurrence

A concrete Impulse-typed occurrence is a bounded source object in which a directional activation becomes analytically legible under declared differences, Frames, temporal conditions, Non-Events, and available continuation corridors. `DECOMPOSE` may reconstruct how that occurrence is activated, directed, intensified, attenuated, delayed, or stabilized. It does not decompose `∇`, infer an inner motive, or convert structural activation into a psychological property of a person.

The family question is:

> Which source-supported differences, Frames, Non-Events, temporal relations, thresholds, and continuation conditions produce or modulate this concrete Impulse-typed occurrence?

An Impulse occurrence is not established merely because an action follows an event. The decomposition must preserve the source reference and show why the directional activation belongs to the same bounded occurrence rather than to a retrospectively invented motive or a newly composed Path. Relevant source traces can include:

- a declared discrepancy or unmet condition;
- a recorded activation request;
- a deadline or threshold after which action becomes available or costly;
- a Frame that channels activation toward one option rather than another;
- a repeated Non-Event that increases pressure without itself becoming a positive action;
- a procedural trigger;
- an interruption or attenuation condition;
- and an observable shift in the reachable continuation set.

```text
activation structure
≠ inner motive
≠ desire diagnosis
≠ person trait

formation history of one Impulse occurrence
≠ decomposition of ∇
```

The origin typing remains revisable. Finer evidence may show that the coarse “Impulse” label captured a genuine activation, that it over-compressed several distinct activations, that the directional relation was imposed by the analyst, or that the source object is better reconstructed through Frame, Non-Event, Attractor, or Asymmetry typing. Rejection of the occurrence typing does not alter the PMS operator.

#### C21-IMP-01 — temporal formation of one Impulse occurrence

Consider a bounded review sequence in which a documented discrepancy is entered, the approving office requests clarification, no clarification arrives within the first review window, and a later procedural deadline activates an escalation route. The coarse description “the objection triggered escalation” compresses several temporally ordered structures:

1. a difference between the submitted record and the required condition;
2. a Frame that makes this difference procedurally relevant;
3. an initial clarification request;
4. a source-supported Non-Event during the first response window;
5. a deadline that changes the reachable continuation set;
6. a formal escalation trigger;
7. and a bounded route toward reconsideration.

A valid Impulse-occurrence decomposition may identify the deadline-sensitive activation without claiming that the objector, chair, or approving office “wanted escalation.” It may also show that the activation was distributed: the discrepancy supplied directional pressure, the Frame selected the operative route, and the Non-Event intensified the pressure across time.

The relevant temporal chain can be represented as a source-bound analytical view:

```yaml
impulse_occurrence:
  source_reference: R-17 escalation segment
  activating_difference: unresolved record-condition discrepancy
  directing_frame: bounded review and reconsideration procedure
  amplifying_non_event: clarification not received in the first response window
  threshold: expiry of the declared response window
  directional_activation: escalation route becomes operative
  attenuation_conditions:
    - clarification received before threshold
    - discrepancy withdrawn or reclassified
  unresolved:
    - relative contribution of deadline pressure and role authority
```

This view is not a new schema. It does not prove causality beyond the supported procedural relation, and it does not turn `Δ`, `□`, `Λ`, or `Θ` into constituents of `∇`. It reconstructs the finer conditions of one occurrence typed through Impulse.

#### C21-MOTIVE-01 — structural activation without motive inference

Suppose the record shows that a participant submitted a request immediately after the escalation threshold. The timing supports a relation between the threshold and the action. It does not establish why the participant acted, what they felt, whether they were anxious, strategic, angry, dutiful, or opportunistic, or whether the action expresses a stable disposition.

The admissible claim remains structural:

```text
threshold crossed
+ route available
+ action follows within declared interval
→ bounded activation relation may be reconstructed
```

The inadmissible expansion is:

```text
bounded activation relation
→ inferred motive
→ person type
→ moral or psychological judgment
```

Where source materials contain explicit self-reports, those reports may be recorded as source claims with their own uncertainty. They do not authorize STRATA to diagnose an underlying motive. Where no motive evidence exists, the decomposition must stop at the structural activation relation.

#### Impulse occurrence and stable course form

An Impulse occurrence may enter a recurrent or stable course form, but that transition does not automatically make the source object an Attractor, Path, or Trajectory. The analyst must separate:

- the activation conditions of this occurrence;
- any repeated activation pattern across several occurrences;
- any newly composed historical object;
- and any contextual target function.

If several activation episodes are selected and related into a broader sequence, `COMPOSE` may be required. If the origin-typed Impulse occurrence is later claimed to function as a Frame-, Attractor-, or governance condition in another context, `PROJECT_AS` is required. Chapter 21 owns neither transformation merely because the occurrence has a temporal formation structure.

<a id="21-11-binding-typed-occurrence"></a>

### 21.11 Binding-Typed Occurrence

A concrete Binding-typed occurrence is a bounded source object in which commitments, rules, records, continuity expectations, role relations, and breach conditions constrain future praxis. `DECOMPOSE` may reconstruct how the occurrence is produced, maintained, distributed, contested, weakened, or partially broken. It does not decompose `Ψ`, infer a permanent identity, certify loyalty, measure moral worth, or treat a binding occurrence as a property of a person.

The family question is:

> Which source-supported commitments, rules, records, continuity expectations, role relations, and breach costs carry this concrete Binding-typed occurrence, and how are those loads distributed?

Relevant finer structures can include:

- explicit promises or approvals;
- institutional rules and role mandates;
- signed or shared records;
- repeated confirmations;
- continuity expectations;
- renewal or withdrawal procedures;
- monitoring and notification practices;
- dependencies created by prior commitments;
- and practical costs of suspension, breach, exit, or revision.

```text
Binding-typed occurrence
≠ person identity
≠ moral rank
≠ permanent commitment
≠ proof of voluntary endorsement
```

A binding occurrence may be internally heterogeneous. One role may carry formal commitment authority, another may carry implementation work, another may bear breach exposure, and another may have a low-cost exit. The coarse function can remain recognizable while the finer reconstruction shows unequal, conditional, or contradictory participation.

#### C21-BIND-01 — binding through commitments, rules, records, continuity, and breach cost

Consider a bounded implementation arrangement following a review decision. The coarse claim states that “the participating units are bound to the approved plan.” Finer sources show:

- a formal approval record;
- role-specific implementation assignments;
- a shared timeline;
- required progress notices;
- a revision procedure;
- dependencies created by prior resource allocation;
- and different costs for suspension or withdrawal.

These elements can support a concrete Binding-typed occurrence when their relations are traceable. The binding is not carried by the approval sentence alone. It is reproduced through the record, role assignments, continuity expectations, repeated notices, and the costs produced by dependencies over time.

A controlled occurrence view may state:

```yaml
binding_occurrence:
  source_reference: R-17 implementation arrangement
  formal_commitments:
    - approved implementation record
    - role-specific assignments
  continuity_carriers:
    - shared timeline
    - required progress notices
  revision_conditions:
    - bounded change procedure
  breach_or_exit_costs:
    - reallocation work
    - delayed dependent tasks
    - additional approval burden
  unequal_loads:
    - formal authority concentrated in approving office
    - implementation load distributed across assigned units
  unresolved:
    - degree of practical exit available to peripheral roles
```

This representation does not establish that every participant consented in the same way, that all roles are equally bound, or that the arrangement is legitimate. It identifies the occurrence-level structures carrying the bounded binding claim.

#### C21-UNEQUAL-01 — internally unequal or contradictory Binding structure

Suppose the approving office can revise the timeline unilaterally, implementation units must absorb the resulting work, and peripheral roles receive notice only after revision. At the same time, the formal record describes the arrangement as a shared commitment.

The finer reconstruction may show:

- common continuity expectations;
- unequal revision authority;
- unequal implementation burden;
- unequal exit or objection conditions;
- and contradiction between the shared-language Frame and the distribution of binding load.

The correct result is not to deny all binding merely because it is unequal, nor to preserve the coarse “shared commitment” claim unchanged. The occurrence may remain Binding-typed while its source function is internally differentiated or only partially preserved.

```text
shared record
≠ equal binding load

formal commitment
≠ equal revision authority

unequal Binding occurrence
≠ person blame automatically
```

Finer evidence can also show that the apparent binding is too weak or fragmented to sustain the original typing. If required notices are absent, role assignments are non-operative, revision conditions are undefined, and exit carries no practical consequence, the coarse Binding label may need reduction or rejection. The operator `Ψ` remains unchanged.

#### C21-TYPEFX-01 — explicit source-function effect on one occurrence typing

For the R-17 implementation arrangement, the source-supported result is bounded as follows:

```yaml
occurrence_result:
  occurrence_family: Binding-typed occurrence
  local_operation_result: supported_relational_decomposition
  source_function_effect: internally_differentiated
  prior_source_claim_disposition: reduced
  canonical_output_class: admissible_with_bounded_claim
```

The source-function effect applies to the coarse claim that all participating units are bound through one homogeneous shared commitment. The finer reconstruction supports a binding occurrence, but shows that formal authority, implementation load, revision capacity, and breach exposure are distributed unequally. The original homogeneous claim is therefore reduced rather than preserved unchanged.

The four result axes remain separate:

```text
supported relational decomposition
≠ homogeneous Binding confirmed
≠ prior claim preserved
≠ unrestricted admissibility
```

This local result does not classify a person, validate the arrangement normatively, or establish a general Binding taxonomy. It demonstrates how Chapter 20’s result architecture applies to one operator-typed occurrence family.

<a id="21-12-failed-operator-occurrence-decomposition"></a>

### 21.12 Failed Operator-Occurrence Decomposition

Operator-occurrence decomposition fails when the analyst no longer reconstructs a bounded occurrence under finer source-supported relations, but instead redefines the operator type, forces all details to fit the coarse label, substitutes psychological or moral claims for structural evidence, loses the source reference, or drifts into another operation without declaration.

The primary failure forms are:

1. **Operator materialization** — treating `□`, `Α`, `Ω`, `∇`, or `Ψ` as aggregates made from observed components.
2. **Type forcing** — interpreting every finer feature as evidence for the original occurrence typing and disabling counterevidence.
3. **Function–formation collapse** — treating a coarse function description as if it already explained the conditions producing it.
4. **Source overreach** — assigning components, relations, temporal order, motive, or causal necessity beyond source precision.
5. **Reference drift** — replacing the original occurrence with a different event, Path, composite, person, institution, or context.
6. **Operation drift** — silently forming a new composite or assigning a target function while claiming only `DECOMPOSE`.
7. **Person conversion** — turning role relations, activation, recurrence, asymmetry, or binding into a person type, diagnosis, or rank.
8. **Immunization** — preserving the original occurrence typing regardless of finer counterevidence.

#### C21-FAIL-01 — type forcing and missing support

An analyst begins with the claim that a review occurrence is Attractor-typed. The sources show two similar meetings, but do not establish lower friction, expectation stabilization, restricted alternatives, thresholds, or changed exit costs. Rather than reduce the claim, the analyst redescribes every repeated agenda item as an “Attractor component” and treats missing evidence as hidden stabilization.

The transformation fails because:

- repetition has been substituted for differential continuation;
- unsupported relations have been entered as components;
- the coarse typing has been immunized;
- and the occurrence has been forced to fit `Α`.

The retained weaker finding may be:

```text
source-supported recurrence across two meetings
```

The failed transformation is:

```text
unsupported Attractor-occurrence decomposition
```

Where these claims are separated, the recurrence finding may remain admissible while the decomposition maps to `failed_transformation` or requires `claim_reduction_required`, depending on the exact claim segment. Failure of the operation does not erase weaker source-supported observations.

#### C21-STOP-01 — Mandatory Stop before operator redefinition, person inference, or operation drift

Mandatory Stop applies when continuation would require any of the following:

- redefining a Δ–Ψ operator through empirical parts;
- inferring motive, diagnosis, character, maturity, loyalty, guilt, or worth from occurrence structure;
- creating a broader composite without `COMPOSE`;
- assigning a contextual function without `PROJECT_AS`;
- claiming components or relations beyond the Source Ceiling;
- preserving an occurrence typing by excluding effective counterevidence;
- or treating formal model consistency as substantive validation.

Stop is a positive governance result. It prevents the current transformation from acquiring authority it has not earned.

```text
mandatory_stop
≠ operator invalid
≠ source object unreal
≠ person judgment
≠ all weaker findings erased
```

A new transformation can be proposed only as a new testable claim with its own source, operation, loss, and boundary record. Frame, granularity, relative level, or function change cannot repair the stopped claim retroactively.

#### C21-NC-01 — Non-Capture without rescue of the original typing

Non-Capture applies where the sources support an independently identifiable occurrence and relevant internal variation, but no stable operator-occurrence model can be reconstructed without destroying decisive heterogeneity or inventing unsupported relations.

For example, a record bundle may show commitments, repeated revisions, conflicting role expectations, informal exceptions, and uneven breach consequences, while no stable relation among them supports one Binding occurrence, several separated Binding occurrences, or a rival Frame/Asymmetry model. The result can be:

```text
non_capture
```

This does not mean that all proposed typings are equally valid. It does not preserve the original Binding claim by default. The original typing may remain rejected, reduced, or underdetermined while the finer object resists stable capture.

```text
non_capture
≠ original typing rescued
≠ evidence absence
≠ theory immunity
```

Non-Capture records the limit of the current transformation. It remains compatible with later source improvement or a differently framed new claim, but grants no authority to either.

<a id="chapter-21-completion-boundary"></a>

#### Chapter 21 family comparison and completion boundary

Across the five occurrence families, Chapter 21 establishes one shared logic:

| Occurrence family | Typical finer burdens | Central failure pressure |
|---|---|---|
| Frame | selection, exclusion, roles, access, routines, Non-Events | stable function treated as homogeneity |
| Attractor | recurrence, friction, expectation, alternatives, thresholds, exit | repetition treated as sufficient stabilization |
| Asymmetry | comparators, dimensions, gradients, coordination, offsets | local differences silently composed into total rank |
| Impulse | activating difference, directing Frame, Non-Event, threshold, attenuation | structural activation converted into motive |
| Binding | commitments, rules, records, continuity, revision, breach cost | binding converted into person identity or moral rank |

The family logic remains occurrence-specific. It does not create a closed enum, exhaustive operator catalogue, automatic typing system, or priority order among operators. Other occurrence types may be opened later through the same generic procedure where the Contract and sources require it.

All family outcomes remain claim-segmented through the Chapter-20 axes:

```text
local operation result
≠ source-function effect
≠ prior source-claim disposition
≠ canonical Output Class
```

The integrated WP4 audit confirms all ten Chapter-21 completion tests, the family-comparison architecture, operator-type integrity, result-axis separation, and the person/authority boundary. The Chapter-21 result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

Chapter 21 is therefore **provisionally locked**. The class names the chapter-level methodological result under the still-open SUB Part and later corpus-wide audits. It does not confirm every occurrence typing, make the five families exhaustive, authorize person inference, compose a new macro-object, assign a contextual target function, or increase claim or application authority.

All twenty-four Chapter-21 Pressure Duties occur exactly once in the canonical prose. No standalone case, Transformation Record, Smoke Record, appendix expansion, schema artifact, new operation, Rule, Output Class, audit stage, occurrence-family enum, person type, or authority mechanism has been produced.

```text
Chapter 21 provisionally locked
≠ occurrence families exhaustive
≠ Chapter 22 composite theory complete
≠ person typing authorized
≠ SUB provisionally locked
```

Reopening requires a concrete conflict involving PMS Base, Structure or Contract ownership, operator-type/occurrence integrity, source reference, occurrence-family evidence, stable-function/internal-variation logic, family comparison, source-function/result-axis separation, person or authority boundaries, Failure/Stop/Non-Capture, a Pressure Duty, a Reference or Formal-Model binding, or a later integrated dependency. Preference for another family example, more detail, a closed family enum, automatic substantive typing, person scoring, stronger causal language, or premature composite/projection theory is insufficient.

#### Chapter-22 handoff

The controlled continuation is:

```text
Chapter 21 provisional lock
→ Chapter 22 Preparation Gate
→ Chapter 22 — Decomposing Composite Structures
```

Chapter 21 supplies Chapter 22 with a developed distinction between:

- operator type;
- operator-typed occurrence;
- occurrence-level production and maintenance conditions;
- internal variation and component substitution;
- source-function revision;
- and family-specific failure.

Chapter 22 changes the source-object problem. A composite already contains multiple selected elements and relations. Its decomposition must therefore reconstruct not only occurrence-level conditions, but also the internal composition map, selection history, dominant and peripheral components, coherence relations, compression, and inherited loss.

```text
operator-occurrence decomposition
→ production and maintenance conditions of one occurrence

composite decomposition
→ internal composition, selection, relations, coherence, and loss
```

No Chapter-21 family result automatically establishes a composite. Where several occurrences are selected into one new object, `COMPOSE` remains separately required. Where an origin-typed occurrence is assigned a bounded contextual function, `PROJECT_AS` remains separately required.

The next controlled step is the Chapter-22 Preparation Gate for **Decomposing Composite Structures**.

<a id="chapter-22-decomposing-composite-structures"></a>

## 22. Decomposing Composite Structures

Chapter 22 applies the provisionally locked generic `DECOMPOSE` procedure to source objects whose bounded identity already depends on more than one constituent and at least one constitutive relation among them.
