Its subject is not any collection of materials, labels, operator typings, or local occurrences. Its subject is an independently warranted source composite whose internal organization can be opened at finer granularity while the same bounded reference object and the same composite-function question remain reconstructible.

A composite enters SUB already selective. It may have been produced by a recorded `COMPOSE` occurrence, inherited from a controlled prior analysis, or independently warranted through source-supported constituents, boundaries, relations, and formation limits. In every case, its decomposition begins after selection and compression have already occurred. Chapter 22 therefore cannot promise restoration of a pre-composition information state. It reconstructs the current composite more finely and keeps inherited compression, exclusion, uncertainty, and irrecoverability visible alongside any additional Loss introduced by the present operation.

Sections 22.1–22.4 establish the composite source threshold, composition lineage and inherited Loss, relational internal composition maps, five claim-bound component roles, and bounded operator weighting without ontology, score, or PMS-dependency revision. Sections 22.5–22.8 extend the same source-bound reconstruction through modulating profiles, distributed function, redundancy, substitution, qualitative thresholds, internal conflict, and explicit `DECOMPOSE`/`COMPOSE`/`PROJECT_AS` boundaries. Sections 22.9–22.11 complete the architecture through composite stability, decomposition without fragmentation, four result axes, Failure, Mandatory Stop, Non-Capture, the eighteen-field Record view, and the Chapter-23 handoff.

The integrated Chapter-22 result is therefore a complete **relational `DECOMPOSE` procedure for already-composite source objects**. It preserves the source composite as the object under test while exposing constituent relations, inherited and new Loss, distributed load, repair, compensation, conflict, and stability. It does not invert `COMPOSE`, turn component roles or profiles into types, calculate operator strength, create a new macro-object, assign a contextual target function, or infer person properties, legitimacy, sanction, or authority.

```text
source composite
→ constituents + boundary + constitutive relation + bounded function

composite decomposition
→ finer relational reconstruction of the same source composite

composite decomposition
≠ archive sorting
≠ parts enumeration
≠ inversion of COMPOSE
≠ new macro-object construction
```

<a id="22-1-composite-structure-as-sub-object"></a>

### 22.1 Composite Structure as SUB Object

A Chapter-22 source object must be composite before the present `DECOMPOSE` occurrence begins. The operation may test how the composite is internally organized, which components carry or alter its function, and whether the coarse composite claim survives finer reconstruction. It may not manufacture composite identity merely because several documents, events, roles, or operator typings are available.

The minimum source-entry burden is conjunctive:

1. more than one identifiable constituent;
2. a declared boundary around the source object;
3. at least one source-supported constitutive relation;
4. a bounded composite identity or function under test;
5. internal traceability sufficient to challenge the composite claim;
6. known selection, compression, exclusion, uncertainty, and irrecoverability limits;
7. a Same-Reference route from the coarse composite into the proposed finer map.

No single item compensates for another. A richly documented bundle without a constitutive relation is not yet a composite. A familiar institutional label without an independently bounded source object does not create reference continuity. Several operator-typed occurrences do not become one composite merely because they occur in the same organization, document set, or time interval.

```text
multiple constituents
+ no warranted composite boundary
→ no Chapter-22 source object yet

shared label
+ changed relation map
+ changed boundary
→ reference continuity not established
```

#### C22-OBJECT-01 — independently warranted composite source entry

Suppose an analyst collects meeting minutes, approval records, follow-up messages, and policy extracts and names the collection “the review system.” That name does not establish a Chapter-22 object. The materials become evidence for a composite only if the analyst can identify a bounded arrangement whose constituents and relations jointly support one testable function.

For the bounded review-governance anchor, the entry claim can be stated as follows:

```yaml
source_composite_entry:
  reference: review-governance arrangement R-17C
  constituent_A: agenda-and-admission Frame occurrence
  constituent_B: approval-allocation Asymmetry occurrence
  constituent_C: recurrent routing Attractor occurrence
  declared_boundary: documented review cycle from issue admission through closure and bounded reconsideration
  constitutive_relations:
    - admission channels which objections reach the approval gate
    - approval allocation determines which admitted objections can alter closure
    - recurrent routing stabilizes post-closure continuation
  bounded_function_under_test: select, route, authorize, and stabilize review decisions
```

The source object is not “all review-related material.” It is the bounded arrangement formed by the declared constituents and relations within the specified review cycle. Documents outside that boundary may remain relevant sources, but they do not enter the composite merely by topic association.

#### Source bundle versus source composite

A source bundle is an evidence container. It may contain heterogeneous records, duplicate accounts, unused details, later commentary, and materials related only by archive practice. A source composite is an analytical reference object whose constituents and relations support one bounded identity or function claim.

```text
source bundle
≠ source composite

archive relation
≠ constitutive composite relation
```

`DECOMPOSE` acts on the composite object. It may traverse the source bundle to test the object, but it may not substitute the archive's filing structure for the composite's internal relation structure. A folder, database, dossier, chronology, or corpus can be highly useful without itself being the object under decomposition.

#### Composite continuity under component change

Composite identity need not require every current carrier to remain unchanged. A communication channel may shift from email to a case-management portal while the relation between objection routing and reconsideration remains supported. A committee chair may be replaced while the admission and closure authority relation persists. Such substitutions can preserve the same bounded composite if its constitutive relation and function remain reconstructible.

The reverse is equally important. Retaining the same institutional or procedural label does not preserve composite identity when the boundary, constituent lineage, relation map, or function under test has materially changed. Same label is weak evidence; same bounded reference must be shown through traceable continuity.

#### C22-INHERIT-01 — composition lineage, inherited compression, and inherited Loss

Every source composite enters Chapter 22 with a formation history that is at least partly selective. Where a prior `COMPOSE` Record exists, Chapter 22 must preserve its source selection, order, constitutive relations, exclusions, and Loss. Where no prior STRATA Record exists, equivalent burdens remain: the analyst must disclose what is known and unknown about how the composite boundary and constituent set were formed.

The source-side declaration may be expressed as:

```yaml
inherited_composite_loss:
  preserved:
    - documented constituent lineage for admission, approval, and routing structures
    - recorded closure and reconsideration sequence
  compressed:
    - local variations within individual review exchanges
    - differences among communication carriers
  excluded:
    - unrelated policy processes outside the declared review cycle
  uncertain:
    - informal coordination preceding documented objection entry
  irrecoverable:
    - unrecorded alternatives considered before the surviving agenda was fixed
```

This is a source-side view of the canonical five-part Loss structure, not a second canonical Loss grammar. The present `DECOMPOSE` occurrence must later record its own additional preservation, compression, exclusion, uncertainty, and irrecoverability.

```text
inherited composite Loss
≠ new DECOMPOSE Loss

DECOMPOSE(COMPOSE(X))
≠ X
```

A finer map may reveal distinctions hidden by the composite's current compression. It cannot recover sources that were never retained, recreate excluded alternatives as facts, or infer a complete formation sequence from the current organization. Non-invertibility is not a defect to be repaired by imaginative detail. It is a constraint on the claim.

<a id="22-2-internal-composition-map"></a>

### 22.2 Internal Composition Map

The central positive object of Chapter 22 is the internal composition map. The map identifies which components belong to the same source composite, how they are related, and how those relations bear on the bounded composite function under test. It is not required to reproduce every local detail. It is required to retain enough relation structure that the composite remains recognizable and challengeable at the finer granularity.

At minimum, a useful map declares:

- component identity and source pointer;
- component role for the current claim;
- component-to-component relations;
- temporal ordering where relevant;
- institutional, spatial, or role distribution where relevant;
- dependence, inhibition, routing, feedback, repair, substitution, compensation, or conflict where supported;
- uncertainty and rival maps;
- relation to the coarser composite function.

#### C22-MAP-01 — internal composition map rather than parts list

A list such as “agenda, chair, approver, follow-up channel, reconsideration route” is not yet a decomposition. It does not establish why those elements belong to the same composite, which relations are constitutive, how the bounded function is carried, or what would change if one relation failed.

A minimal relational map for R-17C can be stated more adequately:

```text
agenda-and-admission boundary
→ channels eligible objections
→ approval-allocation gate
→ authorizes or blocks closure revision
→ closure declaration
→ routes residual objections into bounded reconsideration
→ recurrent follow-up pattern
→ stabilizes post-closure continuation
```

Counterrelations must remain visible:

```text
independent reconsideration route
→ can reopen access after local closure

non-reopening after the bounded window
→ can stabilize closure while preserving residual unresolved objection
```

The map is not a causal proof. It is a source-supported relational reconstruction for the declared composite claim. Directional arrows may indicate routing, sequence, authorization, constraint, or dependence only where the source and wording support that relation. They must not be read automatically as sufficient causes.

#### Formation map, maintenance map, and proposed finer reconstruction

A composite may no longer be maintained through the same arrangement by which it was formed. An original informal review practice may later be stabilized through formal records. A person-dependent communication route may become a standardized portal. A compensatory reconsideration process may be added after repeated local closure failures.

Chapter 22 must therefore distinguish:

```text
formation map
≠ current maintenance map
≠ proposed finer reconstruction
```

The formation map concerns how the composite came to be assembled or stabilized. The maintenance map concerns how the current composite function is reproduced now. The proposed finer reconstruction is the current analytical map produced by this `DECOMPOSE` occurrence. Where formation history is incomplete, the current map may still be warranted if the present reference, constituent lineage, constitutive relations, and bounded function remain traceable. Missing origin information must remain missing; it is not permission to turn the current map into an origin story.

#### C22-TRACE-01 — source composite remains reconstructible through the finer map

A finer analysis fails its Chapter-22 purpose if the analyst can no longer show how the selected components and relations constitute the same source composite. Traceability requires a return route from each claim-bearing map element to:

1. the source pointer;
2. the declared composite boundary;
3. the bounded function under test;
4. the coarser source claim;
5. the relevant inherited and new Loss.

For R-17C, the finer map must still explain why admission, approval, routing, and reconsideration belong to one bounded review-governance arrangement. If the analysis drifts into organization-wide communication culture, general managerial hierarchy, or unrelated appeal procedures, it has enlarged or replaced the source object. That enlargement may become a new `COMPOSE` claim, but it cannot remain hidden inside the current `DECOMPOSE` occurrence.

```text
more internal detail
+ no reconstructible composite relation
→ fragmentation or reference drift

same composite reference
+ finer relation map
→ valid Chapter-22 route candidate
```

#### Rival maps and bounded underdetermination

Sources may support more than one internal map. One reconstruction may treat the reconsideration route as part of the same composite. Another may treat it as an adjacent repair structure that interacts with, but does not belong to, the core review-governance arrangement. Chapter 22 need not force a single map where the boundary evidence remains underdetermined.

The competing maps must declare what changes:

- composite boundary;
- component membership;
- constitutive relation;
- function under test;
- source support;
- expected praxeological consequence.

A rival map is not automatically a rival truth. It is a separately testable reconstruction. The current operation may remain provisional, partially admissible, stopped, or non-capturing depending on later audits; WP1 does not assign that result.

<a id="22-3-component-hierarchy"></a>

### 22.3 Component Hierarchy

Chapter 22 uses a local hierarchy of component roles to distinguish how source-supported elements bear on the current composite claim. The hierarchy is analytical and claim-bound. It does not identify universal layers of reality, permanent component essences, or a fixed taxonomy of all composites.

Five roles are required for the Chapter-22 architecture:

```text
constitutive component
modulating component
replaceable component
compensatory component
incidental detail
```

A single source element may occupy different roles under different claims, Frames, temporal scopes, or granularities. The communication channel carrying a reconsideration notice may be replaceable for the composite's basic review-access function but modulating for speed, accessibility, and burden distribution. An independent appeal route may be compensatory for local closure failure yet constitutive for a broader claim about system-level review availability.

#### C22-HIER-01 — five roles without ontology

For the bounded R-17C claim, a provisional role map can be stated as follows:

| Source-supported element or relation | Local role for the bounded claim | Claim boundary |
|---|---|---|
| relation between admission boundary and approval gate | constitutive | without this relation, the declared select-and-authorize function materially changes |
| notice timing and access to the reconsideration window | modulating | alters accessibility and temporal efficacy without alone defining composite identity |
| communication carrier used for follow-up notice | replaceable | another carrier may preserve routing while changing cost, speed, reliability, or exclusion |
| independent reconsideration route | compensatory | may preserve review access after local closure failure or unequal approval allocation |
| room format or document layout | incidental | source-supported but not claim-relevant unless it changes admission, routing, authorization, or access |

These assignments are provisional analytical roles. They must not be converted into object essences.

```text
constitutive
≠ ontologically fundamental
≠ causally sufficient
≠ normatively superior

replaceable
≠ irrelevant
≠ incidental

incidental under this claim
≠ meaningless under every claim
```

#### Constitutive components and constitutive relations

A constitutive component participates in the relation structure without which the stated composite identity or function would materially change. The constitutive burden is counterfactually sensitive but not omnipotent: removal or material alteration must change the bounded composite claim, not necessarily eliminate every local practice associated with the source object.

In R-17C, an agenda boundary without any relation to approval allocation may still organize discussion, but it would not support the declared composite function of selecting and authorizing review decisions. Likewise, approval authority without a relation to admitted objections may exist elsewhere in the institution, but it would not constitute this bounded review arrangement.

This demonstrates why the hierarchy is relational. Components become constitutive through their role in a supported relation map, not through intrinsic importance.

#### Modulating components

A modulating component changes accessibility, cost, threshold, direction, temporal efficacy, intensity, reliability, or stabilization burden. The analyst must state the changed relation or difference. Descriptions such as “important,” “influential,” or “central” are insufficient without a declared dimension.

For example, shortening the reconsideration window may not alter the existence of a review route but may materially reduce practical accessibility. Delayed notice may preserve formal availability while increasing the burden of activation. A modulating claim therefore concerns how the composite function is carried, not merely whether a component is present.

#### Replaceable components

Replaceability concerns the carrier of a function, not the absence of consequences. Substituting an email notice with a portal notification may preserve follow-up routing while changing:

- speed;
- accessibility;
- auditability;
- burden on participants;
- failure modes;
- exclusion risk;
- residual Loss.

A replaceable component can still be locally necessary until a substitute is installed. It can also remain politically, legally, or practically consequential without Chapter 22 deciding legitimacy or policy preference.

#### Compensatory components

A compensatory component offsets weakness, delay, conflict, unequal load, or local failure elsewhere. Compensation can preserve the macrofunction while concealing where costs are transferred.

```text
macrofunction preserved by compensation
≠ local failure absent
≠ equal burden
≠ legitimacy established
```

In the anchor architecture, an independent reconsideration route may preserve some access after asymmetric local closure. That compensation does not erase the closure asymmetry. It may reduce its practical effect for some claims while introducing new timing, knowledge, documentation, or resource burdens.

#### Incidental detail

An incidental detail is source-supported but does not alter the current composite identity or function. The category protects the analysis against indiscriminate detail accumulation. It does not degrade the detail or deny relevance under another question.

Room arrangement, document formatting, or incidental wording may remain outside the current map unless they change admission, access, authority, routing, timing, or stabilization. If a later claim shows that document layout systematically obscures the reconsideration route, the same detail may become modulating. That would be a new claim-bound role assignment, not a contradiction.

#### Local hierarchy without total ranking

Terms such as dominant, peripheral, upstream, downstream, carrying, disturbing, or residual remain relative to the tested composite function. Chapter 22 does not produce one total hierarchy of components. A relation may be upstream in a procedural sequence but peripheral to authorization; dominant in temporal stabilization but weak in access allocation; carrying under normal conditions but replaceable under repair conditions.

```text
local component role
≠ permanent rank
≠ universal causal order
≠ person rank
```

<a id="22-4-dominant-operator-weighting"></a>

### 22.4 Dominant Operator Weighting

A composite may contain several operator-typed occurrences whose relative load differs by function, dimension, and time. Chapter 22 may describe that relative load as operator weighting. Weighting remains a source-side relational claim about an already bounded composite. It does not revise operator definitions, add a new operator, reorder Δ–Ψ, or assign a contextual target function.

A weighting claim must identify:

1. the bounded composite;
2. the specific composite function under test;
3. the operator-typed occurrence carriers;
4. the weighting dimension;
5. the temporal scope;
6. the source support;
7. counterpressure and residual occurrence load;
8. the expected practical difference if the weighting changed.

Without these declarations, “dominant” is rhetorical emphasis rather than controlled analysis.

#### C22-WEIGHT-01 — bounded operator weighting

For R-17C, one bounded claim may state that the admission-and-approval phase is `Ω/□`-dominant with respect to **decision access and authorization**, while the post-closure continuation is `Α/Θ`-dominant with respect to **recurrent routing and temporal stabilization**.

This does not produce a single total profile. It identifies different relative loads for different functions and phases:

```yaml
operator_weighting:
  composite: review-governance arrangement R-17C
  function_dimension_1: decision access and authorization
  temporal_scope_1: objection admission through closure
  dominant_load_1:
    - Asymmetry-typed approval allocation
    - Frame-typed admission boundary
  counterpressure_1:
    - independent reconsideration route
  function_dimension_2: post-closure continuation and stabilization
  temporal_scope_2: closure through bounded reconsideration window
  dominant_load_2:
    - Attractor-typed recurrent routing
    - Temporality-typed window and expiry structure
  residual_load_2:
    - unresolved objection persistence
```

The claim remains qualitative because the sources support relational load but not a universal numerical strength measure. “Dominant” means that the named occurrence relations carry a large share of the declared function under the stated time and sources. It does not mean exclusive control, maximal causal power, or ontological priority.

#### C22-DEPEND-01 — weighting cannot alter Δ–Ψ order or dependencies

A composite description such as `Ω/Θ`-dominant does not fuse Asymmetry and Temporality into a new operator. It does not place `Ω` above `Θ`, revise dependency structure, rename either operator, or change the PMS Base.

```text
composite weighting description
≠ operator grammar
≠ dependency graph
≠ new primitive
```

The slash is descriptive shorthand for the present bounded composite claim. It must not be read as a formal operator equation, a causal coefficient, or a new ordered pair in PMS ontology. Where precision requires it, the prose should name the actual occurrence relations instead of relying on shorthand.

#### Non-numeric default

Chapter 22 neither requires nor authorizes an additive operator score. Available sources may support ordinal or qualitative descriptions such as:

- carrying most of the access restriction;
- strongly shaping the closure phase;
- peripheral under normal conditions;
- countervailing during reconsideration;
- increasing after the window expires.

These descriptions remain valid only within their declared dimension and temporal scope. They do not combine into a universal composite score, person score, institutional maturity score, or prediction engine.

```text
operator weighting
≠ additive operator score
≠ universal strength rank
≠ authority rank
```

#### C22-DOMINANCE-01 — dominance does not erase countervailing or residual occurrences

A dominant weighting must preserve effective counterpressure. In R-17C, asymmetric closure authority may dominate the immediate approval phase while an independent reconsideration route remains countervailing. Recurrent routing may stabilize closure while residual unresolved objection persists. A dominant Frame may regulate admissibility while informal channels still carry excluded issues outside the bounded procedure.

If the analysis removes these structures merely to preserve a clean dominance claim, it overstates the composite and destroys Counterfactual Sensitivity.

```text
dominant load
≠ exclusive load

countervailing occurrence
≠ noise automatically

residual structure
≠ analytically erased
```

The practical meaning of dominance may change across time. Before closure, approval Asymmetry may carry the largest load. During reconsideration, access to records and deadlines may become more decisive. After expiry, repeated Non-Reopening and established routing expectations may carry the stabilizing burden. Time-sensitive weighting is not inconsistency; it is required where the source composite changes its internal load distribution while preserving the same bounded reference.

#### WP1 completion boundary

Sections 22.1–22.4 establish the source-entry and mapping architecture for composite decomposition:

```text
independently warranted composite source
→ composition trace and inherited Loss
→ internal composition map
→ claim-bound component roles
→ dimension- and time-bound operator weighting
```

They do not yet complete:

- source-side modulating profiles;
- distributed function;
- redundancy and bounded removal pressure;
- substitution costs and thresholds;
- internal conflict outcomes;
- composite stability;
- decomposition without fragmentation;
- Failure, Mandatory Stop, or Non-Capture routing;
- the eighteen-field composite Record view;
- final source-function effects or Output Class mapping;
- Chapter-22 provisional lock.

```text
WP1 complete
≠ Chapter 22 complete
≠ composite function confirmed
≠ final component hierarchy fixed
≠ operator profile created
≠ DECOMPOSE result selected
```

The local Chapter-22 status remains:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

The next controlled Chapter-22 step is WP2, Sections 22.5–22.8: Modulating Profiles, Distributed Function, Redundant and Substitutable Components, and Internal Conflict.

<a id="22-5-modulating-profiles"></a>

### 22.5 Modulating Profiles

Chapter 22 uses a modulating profile to describe how already identified relations inside one source composite differ in accessibility, activation, delay, persistence, exposure, coordination cost, repair burden, or stabilization load. The profile does not introduce a new component class. It does not name a new operator. It is a bounded source-side description of how the same composite function is carried under declared conditions.

A profile is admissible only after the source composite, its constitutive relations, and the tested function remain independently reconstructible. It cannot substitute for the internal composition map established in Section 22.2. The map identifies components and relations. The profile describes supported variation in how those relations operate.

```text
internal composition map
→ what is related and how

modulating profile
→ how the supported relations differ along a declared dimension
```

For the review-governance arrangement R-17C, the same reconsideration route may be formally present across several phases while its practical accessibility changes. Notice timing, record availability, activation burden, and the proximity of the expiry boundary may alter how the route can be used without changing its formal existence. A profile claim must state the specific dimension and source route rather than compressing these differences into a general judgment that the arrangement is “open,” “closed,” “strong,” or “weak.”

#### C22-PROFILE-01 — profile anti-type and source-side boundary

A modulating profile remains local to the declared composite, function, Frame, temporal scope, and evidence. It is not an operator type, composite type, person type, organizational character, or contextual target function.

```text
modulating profile
≠ operator type
≠ composite type
≠ person type
≠ contextual target function
```

This boundary matters because profile language can otherwise become an unlicensed route to RETYPE. A source-side finding that access becomes more difficult near an expiry threshold does not establish that the composite functions as an Asymmetry, Frame, or Attractor in a new target context. Such a claim would require a separate `PROJECT_AS` occurrence with declared origin type, target function, context, limits, and Loss.

The same boundary prohibits person inference. A high activation burden borne by one role-position does not establish that the role-holder is passive, incapable, resistant, dependent, or morally disadvantaged. Chapter 22 reconstructs the distribution and modulation of composite relations. It does not convert those relations into stable properties of participants.

A controlled profile declaration identifies at least:

1. the same source composite;
2. the bounded function under test;
3. the profile dimension;
4. the components or relations carrying the difference;
5. the temporal and contextual scope;
6. the source support;
7. counterpressure or offset;
8. the practical difference expected if the profile changed.

For R-17C, a bounded profile may be written as follows:

```yaml
modulating_profile:
  source_composite: review-governance arrangement R-17C
  function_under_test: bounded reconsideration after closure
  dimensions:
    access:
      supported_difference: access declines when notice is delayed relative to the revisit window
      source_route: timestamped closure notice and reconsideration procedure
      counterpressure: independent assistance channel remains available
    temporal_efficacy:
      supported_difference: activation capacity narrows as the expiry boundary approaches
      source_route: documented opening and expiry dates
      counterpressure: no supported extension mechanism in the current record
    repair_burden:
      supported_difference: missing records shift reconstruction work to the objecting role-position
      source_route: follow-up correspondence and incomplete meeting record
      uncertainty: burden magnitude is not numerically established
```

The dimensions remain separate claims. Strong evidence for the expiry structure cannot compensate for weak evidence about repair burden. Evidence that a route persists over time cannot establish who can access it. Evidence of unequal access cannot establish how long the difference persists. No average profile score is permitted.

```text
profile multiplicity
≠ synthetic total score

support for dimension A
≠ support for dimension B
```

Profiles can also change across phases without implying reference change. During admission, access may be broadly distributed while authorization remains concentrated. During closure, authorization may dominate. During reconsideration, temporal efficacy and record access may become decisive. The composite remains the same only where its bounded reference and constitutive relation trace remain reconstructible through those changes.

A profile that cannot be traced to a supported relation must remain absent or uncertain. Descriptive richness does not authorize interpolation. “High coordination cost,” “low accessibility,” or “heavy stabilization load” must be connected to source-visible differences, not inferred from the analyst's expectation of how such arrangements usually operate.

<a id="22-6-distributed-function"></a>

### 22.6 Distributed Function

A composite function may be carried across spatially separated units, institutionally separated roles, temporally alternating carriers, parallel channels, layered decision and repair structures, or distributed records and expectations. Separation does not prevent composite function. It increases the burden of demonstrating how the separated elements belong to the same bounded composite and jointly carry the same tested function.

Distribution is therefore relational. It is not a synonym for multiplicity, dispersion, co-presence, or organizational scale.

#### C22-DISTR-01 — coordination and common-function burden

A distributed-function claim requires all of the following:

1. identified distributed elements;
2. a supported relation of each element to the same source composite;
3. a coordination or dependency mechanism;
4. a common function under test;
5. a declared temporal and institutional scope;
6. the effects of partial change, delay, or failure;
7. source support and remaining uncertainty;
8. continued reference traceability at the composite level.

For R-17C, reconsideration may be distributed across the original review chair, an administrative record office, and an independent reconsideration role. These units are not treated as one function merely because all participate in the broader institution. The function becomes distributed only if the sources support a relation such as:

```text
closure notice
→ record availability
→ request routing
→ independent authorization
→ return or confirmation of revised decision status
```

The chain may include parallel routes, delayed handoffs, or alternative carriers. What matters is that the declared components jointly bear on the same bounded reconsideration function.

A source-supported representation may state:

```yaml
distributed_function:
  source_composite: review-governance arrangement R-17C
  function_under_test: bounded reconsideration after closure
  distributed_elements:
    - closure-notice carrier
    - record-access carrier
    - request-routing carrier
    - independent reconsideration carrier
  coordination_mechanism:
    - shared case identifier
    - declared routing rule
    - bounded temporal window
    - return of decision status to the review record
  partial_failure_pressure:
    record_access_delay: function may persist but activation burden and temporal efficacy change
    routing_failure: independent reconsideration cannot be reached through the documented route
    status_return_failure: reconsideration may occur without integration into the bounded review record
  uncertainty:
    - informal assistance routes are only partially documented
```

This representation distinguishes persistence of some local practices from persistence of the full distributed function. A reconsideration role may exist, but the bounded composite function may fail if no supported route connects an admitted request to that role within the declared interval. Conversely, one carrier may fail while the function persists through a supported redundant or substitutable route.

#### C22-AGGREGATE-01 — distribution is not aggregation or co-presence

```text
distribution
≠ aggregation
≠ co-presence
≠ same institution automatically
```

Several offices storing related records do not thereby form one distributed record function. Several roles discussing the same issue do not thereby form one composite decision process. Similar practices across sites do not become one macrostructure merely because they share vocabulary or outcomes.

The analyst must identify the common reference object and the mechanism through which local elements jointly carry the function. Where the analysis selects previously separate structures and newly asserts that they form one macro-composite, it has crossed into `COMPOSE`. The new relation requires its own source selection, ordering, constitutive relation, Loss, and Record.

Partial failure must be described without assuming equivalence among carriers. If one unit takes over a delayed routing function, the macrofunction may persist while cost, access, speed, auditability, or burden distribution changes. Persistence therefore does not show that nothing important changed.

```text
function persists
≠ carriers equivalent
≠ distribution unchanged
≠ Loss absent
```

Distributed function also does not authorize authority inheritance. Participation in one segment of a composite route does not grant authority over the whole composite, over other participants, or over future uses of the reconstructed model. Chapter 22 identifies function-bearing relations; it does not create governance rights.

<a id="22-7-redundant-and-substitutable-components"></a>

### 22.7 Redundant and Substitutable Components

Redundancy and substitution test whether the bounded composite function depends on one carrier, several overlapping carriers, or a relation that can survive changes in implementation. The analysis remains claim-specific. A component may be redundant for one function, unique for another, and incidental for a third.

Redundancy exists where more than one source-supported component can carry the same or overlapping claim-relevant function. It can increase persistence, but it can also increase cost, hide unequal burden, produce inconsistent routing, or preserve a macrofunction through local failure.

#### C22-REDUNDANCY-01 — bounded removal pressure

The local removal pressure asks:

> If this component were removed, disabled, delayed, or rendered unavailable while the remaining supported relation map stayed fixed as far as responsibly possible, what composite function, load, relation, or identity claim would change?

This is a bounded analytic pressure, not an empirical intervention and not the complete Counterfactual Component Test owned by Chapter 27. It must identify the held-constant conditions, the source basis for the alternative carrier, and the uncertainty introduced by the hypothetical change.

For R-17C, two reconsideration entry routes may be documented: a direct request to the independent review role and an administrative forwarding route. Their overlap supports a limited redundancy claim only for **request delivery**. It does not establish redundancy for record access, authorization, deadline extension, or integration of the result into the review record.

```text
redundant for delivery
≠ redundant for authorization
≠ redundant for record access
≠ redundant for final integration
```

Removal of the direct route may leave delivery possible through administrative forwarding while increasing delay and dependence on another role. Removal of the administrative route may preserve delivery for participants who know the direct route while reducing accessibility for those who do not. The macrofunction can therefore persist with changed burdens and exclusions.

A component is not declared necessary merely because removal would matter. The removal pressure may reveal modulation, load transfer, partial function loss, or a change in composite identity. It does not by itself establish universal causality or causal priority.

#### C22-SUBSTITUTE-01 — substitution without equivalence

Substitution asks whether another supported carrier can assume the relevant role. A controlled substitution claim identifies:

- the current carrier;
- the substitute carrier;
- the bounded function preserved;
- transition conditions;
- changes in cost, timing, burden, access, reliability, and auditability;
- new uncertainty and Loss;
- the source support for both the current and substitute routes.

```text
substitutable for function F
≠ structurally identical
≠ lossless replacement
≠ irrelevant current carrier
```

For example, replacing an email-based notice with a portal notification may preserve the function of delivering closure information. It may alter access for participants without portal credentials, change timestamp precision, reduce or increase auditability, and shift the repair burden when notification fails. The current carrier remains analytically relevant because the substitution changes how the composite function is carried.

A substitution can preserve the bounded reference object only if the constitutive relations and function remain traceable. Replacing a communication channel does not necessarily create a new composite. Replacing the admission boundary, approval relation, and routing logic together may materially alter composite identity and require a new source-object determination.

Substitution claims must also retain inherited and new Loss. A replacement may preserve present function while obscuring formation history or eliminating evidence previously available through the former carrier. Functional persistence does not reverse that loss.

#### C22-THRESHOLD-01 — qualitative change thresholds

A composite may tolerate several local failures or substitutions before the bounded function or identity changes qualitatively. Chapter 22 may identify a source- and claim-bound threshold question. It may not invent a universal percentage, component count, resilience score, or operator-weighted cutoff.

A qualitative threshold declaration states:

1. the function or identity claim at risk;
2. the relevant relation set;
3. the supported sequence or combination of changes;
4. the observable difference that would alter the claim;
5. the source ceiling and uncertainty.

In R-17C, the loss of one notice channel may not eliminate bounded reconsideration if another supported route remains timely and accessible. Loss of both notice and routing, while the deadline remains unchanged, may leave the formal reconsideration role in existence but remove the supported practical relation by which an admitted objection reaches it. The threshold concerns relation continuity, not a numerical count of surviving components.

```text
one component removed
≠ threshold crossed automatically

several components present
≠ function preserved automatically
```

Thresholds may be path-sensitive. The same components can produce different outcomes depending on order, delay, transition support, and whether compensation becomes available before the relevant window closes. Chapter 22 may retain that bounded temporal dependence without converting the composite into a new Path claim.

<a id="22-8-internal-conflict"></a>

### 22.8 Internal Conflict

Composite structures can contain components or relations whose local functions, temporal demands, access distributions, rules, or continuation tendencies conflict. Internal conflict is not external noise by default. It may be constitutive, modulating, residual, suppressible, repairable, or destabilizing relative to the bounded composite claim.

The analysis must identify:

- the conflicting components or relations;
- the specific incompatibility;
- the common source composite;
- the temporal and functional scope;
- the mechanism through which conflict affects the composite;
- source support and rival interpretations;
- what remains unresolved.

Examples include formal rule versus practical routine, closure finality versus reconsideration access, rapid authorization versus record completeness, distributed repair versus centralized control, or stabilization of routing versus persistence of unresolved objection.

#### C22-CONFLICT-01 — open conflict outcomes

Chapter 22 keeps at least five local outcomes open:

```text
conflict destabilizes the composite function
conflict is functionally integrated
conflict is suppressed through asymmetry
conflict persists as residue
conflict generates competing subpaths
```

These are descriptive possibilities, not new Output Classes, a closed schema enum, or a developmental sequence.

Internal conflict does not automatically destroy a composite. A reconsideration route can be integrated into a closure-oriented arrangement as a bounded repair relation. Conflict can persist as residue when unresolved objection remains documented but cannot alter the decision. It can be suppressed where one role-position controls admission, timing, or authoritative closure. It can generate competing subpaths when the same issue continues through formal and informal routes.

```text
internal conflict
≠ composite failure automatically
≠ healthy integration automatically
≠ legitimacy judgment
```

Persistence of the composite cannot decide which outcome applies. The same visible continuation may arise from repair, redundancy, compensation, suppression, unequal burden, or loss of effective alternatives. Source-supported relations must distinguish them.

For R-17C, closure finality and bounded reconsideration create a structured tension. The tension may be functionally integrated if the reconsideration route is accessible, source-supported, temporally viable, and capable of returning a revised status to the same review record. It may remain residual if objections persist but cannot activate the route. It may be suppressed through Asymmetry if access or authorization is selectively controlled. Several outcomes may remain partially supported across different phases, but they must not be collapsed into one total judgment.

The finer reconstruction may later confirm, refine, internally differentiate, partially preserve, reject, or leave underdetermined the bounded composite function. WP2 does not yet select the final Source-Function Effect. It prepares the conflict evidence that the full Chapter-22 result must test.

#### C22-OPBOUND-01 — operation-boundary control

Internal conflict can tempt two silent operation changes.

First, the analyst may select formal and informal continuations that were not already established as one source composite and call them “competing subpaths of the composite.” If the claim creates a new macro-object by relating previously separate structures, it requires `COMPOSE`.

Second, the analyst may claim that the source composite functions as a higher-order conflict-management Frame, institutional Attractor, or governance Asymmetry. That is a contextual target-function claim and requires `PROJECT_AS`.

```text
opening conflict inside the same composite
→ DECOMPOSE

forming a new object from previously separate conflict routes
→ COMPOSE

assigning a bounded contextual target function
→ PROJECT_AS
```

The operation boundary also applies to profiles, distributed function, redundancy, and substitution. Chapter 22 may reconstruct these structures only while the same source composite remains traceable. Reference enlargement, target-function assignment, or new source selection creates a new testable claim and a separate Record.

A changed Frame, granularity, component map, or operation does not erase an earlier failed claim. If the original composite identity cannot be preserved, the analyst must reduce, stop, fail, or leave the reconstruction uncaptured according to the later Chapter-22 completion tests. It may not preserve the old label solely to continue the analysis.

#### WP2 completion boundary

Sections 22.5–22.8 establish the middle relational architecture of composite decomposition:

```text
internal composition map
→ source-side modulating profiles
→ distributed function with coordination burden
→ redundancy and substitution under bounded removal pressure
→ qualitative threshold pressure
→ open internal-conflict outcomes
→ explicit operation boundaries
```

They do not yet complete:

- Composite Stability;
- decomposition without fragmentation;
- the final Source-Function Effect;
- the Local Operation Result;
- Prior Source-Claim Disposition;
- canonical Output Class mapping;
- Failure, Mandatory Stop, or Non-Capture routing;
- the eighteen-field composite Record view;
- the Chapter-23 handoff;
- Chapter-22 provisional lock.

```text
WP2 complete
≠ Chapter 22 complete
≠ distributed function confirmed globally
≠ substitution lossless
≠ conflict resolved
≠ composite stability established
≠ DECOMPOSE result selected
```

The local Chapter-22 status remains:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

The next controlled Chapter-22 step is WP3, Sections 22.9–22.11: Composite Stability, Decomposition without Fragmentation, Failed Composite Decomposition, the Composite Decomposition Record View, Completion Boundary, and Chapter-23 Handoff.

<a id="22-9-composite-stability"></a>

### 22.9 Composite Stability

Composite stability is a claim about the persistence of a bounded composite reference, relation set, or function across a declared interval. It is not a synonym for unchanged parts. It is not established by a stable label, repeated output, institutional age, or visible continuation alone.

A stability claim must state at least:

1. **what remains stable** — reference, bounded function, constitutive relation, role structure, access pattern, burden distribution, output, or label;
2. **over which interval** the claim applies;
3. **which local carriers change** during that interval;
4. **which relations preserve continuity** despite those changes;
5. **which repair, redundancy, substitution, compensation, suppression, or binding mechanisms** support persistence;
6. **which counterpressure and Loss remain visible**;
7. **what observable change would defeat or materially reduce the stability claim**.

These objects of stability are not interchangeable. Stable output does not establish stable access. Stable reference does not establish stable burden distribution. Stable role names do not establish stable role performance. Stable documentation does not establish stable practical continuation.

```text
stable output
≠ stable internal relations

stable label
≠ stable composite identity

stable composite function
≠ stable local carriers
```

#### C22-STABILITY-01 — macro-stability without local homogeneity

A composite may retain a bounded macrofunction while local components enter, leave, fail, change carrier, or alter load. The finer reconstruction must therefore distinguish **continuity of the composite claim** from **continuity of every local implementation**.

In R-17C, the review-governance arrangement may continue to select, route, authorize, and stabilize review decisions even if the channel used to deliver notices changes, one administrative role is replaced, or the reconsideration route is temporarily carried by another office. Such churn does not automatically destroy the source composite. Continuity depends on whether the admission boundary, approval relation, routing connection, and bounded repair route remain reconstructible as relations of the same reference object.

The opposite pressure remains equally important. A stable institutional name and repeated closure output do not preserve the composite claim if the constitutive relation between admitted objection, approval gate, and effective reconsideration disappears. The label may persist while the practical composite changes materially.

```text
local churn
≠ macro-instability automatically

macro-persistence
≠ local stability automatically

same institutional label
≠ same composite automatically
```

No anti-local or anti-macro privilege follows. Finer evidence can show that a stable macrofunction is genuinely carried through changing local structures. It can also show that apparent macro-stability is only label persistence, selective observation, or inaccessible burden transfer. The result depends on source-supported relations, not on preferred scale.

#### C22-COMPENSATE-01 — mechanisms and purchase of stability

Stability may be produced through several materially different mechanisms:

- repeated repair after local failure;
- redundant carriers;
- substitution of one carrier for another;
- compensatory routes that restore a threatened function;
- institutional binding;
- temporal alternation among carriers;
- retained expectations and repeated Non-Events;
- suppression of conflict;
- concentration of burden in one role or location;
- restricted access that prevents destabilizing inputs from reaching the composite.

The analysis must identify which mechanism is supported. It may not treat all persistence as resilience, integration, or successful repair.

For R-17C, an independent reconsideration route may compensate for a locally closed objection path. If that route is timely, accessible, connected to the same record, and capable of modifying the authoritative status, the bounded review function may remain partially stable despite local closure failure. If the route exists formally but is inaccessible to the affected participants, the same visible persistence may instead depend on exclusion or unequal load.

```text
macrofunction preserved through compensation
≠ local failure absent
≠ equal access
≠ equal burden
≠ legitimacy established
```

A stability mechanism is claim-bound. A notice substitution may preserve delivery while reducing auditability. Redundancy may preserve routing while increasing coordination cost. Suppression may preserve output while leaving unresolved structure. Repair may restore the function only after a delay that exceeds the relevant decision window. Each mechanism therefore requires its own source support, temporal scope, changed burden, counterpressure, and Loss.

The Chapter-20 result axes remain separate. A supported stability mechanism does not by itself decide the final operation result, Source-Function Effect, prior source-claim disposition, or canonical Output Class.

#### Stability result for the bounded anchor

The R-17C reconstruction supports a limited stability result. The composite function is not internally homogeneous. It persists through a mixture of constitutive relations, recurrent routing, bounded reconsideration, carrier substitution, and unequal access to repair. The coarser claim is therefore not simply confirmed or rejected as a whole. It is internally differentiated.

This conclusion remains bounded to the documented review cycle and source route. It does not establish general institutional resilience, governance quality, legitimacy, or a permanent organizational property.

<a id="22-10-decomposition-of-a-composite-without-fragmentation"></a>

### 22.10 Decomposition of a Composite without Fragmentation

A valid composite decomposition opens internal structure while preserving the reconstructibility of the source composite. It does not win by producing the largest possible number of details. It succeeds only where the finer distinctions retain a traceable relation to the same reference object and to the bounded composite function under test.

The positive standard is conjunctive. A non-fragmenting decomposition:

- keeps the source composite reference visible;
- retains the composite boundary or explicitly declares its bounded revision;
- reconstructs components and relations together;
- distinguishes component roles without turning them into ontological kinds;
- preserves composition trace and inherited Loss;
- tests operator weighting without revising PMS operators;
- keeps profile dimensions separate;
- requires coordination for distributed function;
- records redundancy, substitution, conflict, repair, compensation, and stability where relevant;
- permits rival maps and underdetermination;
- retains the coarser function as a test target;
- states new operation-level Loss;
- stops where further detail falls below the Praxeological Relevance Floor or exceeds the Traceability Ceiling.

#### C22-FRAGMENT-01 — component relation and macrofunction retention

A fragment is not defined by small size. It is a selected detail whose inclusion no longer carries a reconstructible relation to the source composite and the tested claim. A component can be small, distributed, delayed, or non-contiguous if its source-supported role and relational placement remain traceable. A large subsystem can still be a fragment if it is imported because it is interesting rather than because it bears on the bounded composite question.

```text
small detail
≠ fragment automatically

large subsystem
≠ component automatically

detail volume
≠ praxeological purchase
```

The macrofunction must remain testable throughout the finer reconstruction. It may be:

- confirmed;
- refined;
- internally differentiated;
- partially preserved;
- rejected;
- left underdetermined.

It may not disappear silently while the analysis celebrates local complexity. Where the source composite is no longer reconstructible, the operation has either fragmented, changed reference, or begun a new composition.

For R-17C, local facts about document layout, room arrangement, individual communication style, or unrelated disputes are not components merely because they occurred inside the same institution. They become relevant only if a supported relation shows that they alter admission, authorization, routing, reconsideration, burden, repair, or another declared part of the bounded composite function.

Conversely, a formally small Non-Event such as failure to reopen an objection after a documented reconsideration trigger can be component-relevant. Its relevance follows from the relation between trigger, authority, temporal window, and continued closure—not from its observational size.

#### Four result axes

Composite decomposition retains the Chapter-20 separation:

```text
local operation result
≠ source-function effect
≠ prior source-claim disposition
≠ canonical Output Class
```

For the bounded R-17C anchor, the controlled result can be represented as:

```yaml
composite_result:
  local_operation_result: supported_relational_composite_decomposition
  source_function_effect: internally_differentiated
  prior_source_claim_disposition: reduced
  canonical_output_class: admissible_with_bounded_claim
```

The local operation result states that a source-supported relational map was reconstructed. The Source-Function Effect states that the coarse review-governance function survives only in differentiated form. The prior claim disposition reduces any stronger claim of homogeneous access, integrated reconsideration, or equal burden. The canonical class applies to the bounded current decomposition claim. It does not validate every inherited description of the composite.

Other mappings remain possible under different evidence. A valid finer map with no claim-relevant gain can produce `resolution_neutral`. A materially useful but unresolved map can remain `admissible_but_provisional`. Separable supported and unsupported segments can require `partially_admissible`. An unreduced overclaim can require `claim_reduction_required`. These classes are assigned only after the relevant Chapter-20 and Chapter-22 burdens are met; local labels such as “stable,” “redundant,” or “integrated conflict” do not route automatically.

<a id="22-11-failed-composite-decomposition"></a>

### 22.11 Failed Composite Decomposition

Composite decomposition fails when the operation no longer reconstructs a source-supported relational organization of the same bounded composite. Failure does not require total absence of useful observations. The attempt may reveal local details, missing sources, rival maps, or a failed prior composite claim while still failing as the proposed transformation.

#### C22-FAIL-01 — failure pressures

Failure pressure is present where the attempted reconstruction:

- produces an atomistic parts list;
- loses constitutive relations;
- overprivileges local detail;
- treats correlation, proximity, or common membership as constitutive function;
- assigns unsupported component roles;
- invents hierarchy, operator weighting, profile, coordination, redundancy, substitution, conflict outcome, or stability mechanism;
- erases composition lineage, inherited compression, or inherited Loss;
- lets the source composite disappear;
- treats a source bundle as an established composite;
- silently forms a new macro-object;
- silently assigns a contextual target function;
- treats operator weighting as operator revision;
- converts profile or burden descriptions into person types.

A failed composite decomposition maps to `failed_transformation` only when the claim being classified is the failed operation claim. Distinct bounded findings must remain separately recordable. For example, the failure to establish one stable internal composition map does not erase a supported finding that the previous source-composite label was overbroad or that decisive relation evidence is missing.

```text
failed transformation
≠ no information learned
≠ prior composite claim rescued
```

#### C22-STOP-01 — Mandatory Stop

Mandatory Stop is required when responsible continuation would require:

- inventing components or relations beyond the source ceiling;
- treating a source bundle as a warranted composite;
- recovering irrecoverable formation history as fact;
- preserving a composite label after reference or constitutive-relation loss;
- changing Δ–Ψ order, dependencies, names, or operator content;
- creating a new `COMPOSE` or `PROJECT_AS` claim without a separate Record;
- inferring person traits, moral rank, legitimacy, sanction, diagnosis, or action authority;
- continuing below the Praxeological Relevance Floor;
- continuing above the Praxeological Traceability Ceiling.

Stop is a positive result. It preserves method integrity and prevents a partially supported map from expanding into unsupported structure. A record can therefore end with `mandatory_stop` even when some component and relation claims remain admissible in separately bounded segments.

Optional Stop remains appropriate where further detail is possible but unnecessary for the current claim. More resolution is not mandatory merely because sources exist.

#### C22-NC-01 — bounded Non-Capture

`non_capture` remains available where the source composite and decomposition question are legitimate, but no stable finer map can responsibly be established. This can occur where:

- several incompatible relation maps remain similarly supported;
- distributed coordination is inaccessible;
- component roles vary across the relevant interval without a warrantable common mapping;
- inherited Loss removes decisive formation or relation evidence;
- the composite remains recognizable at a coarse level while its finer organization cannot be stabilized;
- every candidate map requires unsupported hierarchy, weighting, or profile assumptions.

```text
non_capture
≠ coarse composite claim confirmed
≠ all rival maps equally true
≠ permission to invent a median map
≠ failed claim protected from reduction
```

Non-Capture does not immunize the prior source claim. The coarse composite function may remain reduced, rejected, or underdetermined. The operation record must state what remains supported, what failed to stabilize, and why further continuation would exceed the method boundary.

#### Non-compensation

Record completeness, detail quantity, elegant relation diagrams, source volume, symmetric operator language, or a populated extension payload cannot compensate for missing reference continuity, constitutive relations, source support, PraxisPurchase, Type Integrity, Functional Continuity, or Counterfactual Sensitivity.

```text
complete composite record
≠ admissible composite decomposition

formal map
≠ empirical relation proof
```

#### C22-RECORD-01 — Composite Decomposition Record View

The operation-specific Chapter-22 view contains exactly eighteen fields:

```yaml
composite_decomposition:
  source_composite:
  composite_boundary:
  source_composite_function:
  composition_trace:
  inherited_loss:
  target_granularity:
  decomposition_question:
  internal_composition_map:
  component_roles:
  operator_weighting:
  modulating_profiles:
  distributed_function:
  redundancy_and_substitution:
  internal_conflict:
  stability_mechanism:
  unresolved_structure:
  loss:
  admissibility_result:
```

The view does not create a parallel schema, a component-role enum, a profile taxonomy, a weighting score, a conflict class, or a stability class. It organizes the Chapter-22 burden inside the Shared Transformation Record.

The mapping is controlled as follows:

- `source.reference_object` and `source.object_typing` carry the source composite;
- `source.constitutive_source_trace` carries composition lineage and source pointers;
- `source.known_gaps` and `source.source_ceiling` carry inherited formation limits;
- `operation.kind` remains exactly `DECOMPOSE`;
- `operation.expected_praxeological_difference` carries the claim-relevant finer difference;
- `operation.details` carries source and target granularity, decomposition question, components, relations, internal temporality, support, Source-Function Effect, and unresolved structure;
- `target.reference_object` retains the same bounded composite reference;
- `loss` retains exactly `preserved`, `compressed`, `excluded`, `uncertain`, and `irrecoverable`;
- `result` keeps operation-specific result, prior-claim disposition, routing, and canonical Output Class distinct;
- controlled `extensions` may carry composition trace, inherited Loss, internal map, role assignments, weighting, profiles, distributed function, redundancy and substitution, conflict, and stability where no dedicated required field exists.

Every extension must declare its owner or source, control pointer, purpose, payload, and non-replacement status. Schema-validity proves only machine-readable consistency. It does not establish the composite, its components, or its relations.

<a id="chapter-22-completion-boundary"></a>

#### Chapter 22 completion boundary

Sections 22.1–22.11 now provide the complete generic architecture for decomposing composite structures:

```text
warranted source composite
→ composition trace and inherited Loss
→ internal composition map
→ claim-bound component roles
→ bounded operator weighting and source-side profiles
→ distributed function
→ redundancy and substitution
→ internal conflict
→ composite stability
→ non-fragmenting result discipline
→ Failure, Stop, Non-Capture, Record, and canonical Output mapping
```

The integrated WP4 audit confirms the Chapter-22 Contract, all twenty-one Preparation-Audit controls, all twenty-four Pressure Duties, composite-source identity, inherited/new Loss separation, relational-map integrity, role and profile anti-type boundaries, operation boundaries, result-axis separation, and package consistency. The Chapter-22 result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

Chapter 22 is therefore **provisionally locked**. The class names the chapter-level methodological result under the still-open SUB Part and later corpus-wide audits. It does not capture every composite, validate every role assignment or stability claim, restore a pre-composition information state, authorize a component ontology or scoring system, complete Chapter-23 temporal theory, or increase claim, application, or governance authority.

All twenty-four Chapter-22 Pressure Duties occur exactly once in the canonical prose. No standalone case, Transformation Record, Smoke Record, appendix expansion, schema artifact, new operation, Rule, Output Class, audit stage, component-role enum, profile type, operator score, person type, or authority mechanism has been produced.

```text
Chapter 22 provisionally locked
≠ every composite captured
≠ COMPOSE inverted
≠ Chapter 23 event theory complete
≠ Chapter 24 Path/Trajectory theory complete
≠ Chapter 25 resolution taxonomy complete
≠ SUB provisionally locked
```

Reopening requires a concrete conflict involving PMS Base, Structure or Contract ownership, source-composite identity, composite boundary or reference continuity, composition lineage or inherited Loss, component/relation mapping, role claims, operator weighting, source-side profiles, distributed function, redundancy or substitution, internal conflict, stability, non-fragmentation, result axes, Failure/Stop/Non-Capture, a Pressure Duty, a Reference or Formal-Model binding, or a later integrated dependency. Preference for more components, stronger causal language, universal scores, a closed role/profile taxonomy, automatic function inference, person typing, or premature event/Path/RETYPE theory is insufficient.

#### C22-HANDOFF-01 — controlled Chapter-23 handoff

The controlled continuation is:

```text
Chapter 22 provisional lock
→ Chapter 23 Preparation Gate
→ Chapter 23 — Event and Non-Event Decomposition
```

Chapter 22 hands Chapter 23 only a bounded composite result where internal temporality, event structure, delay, repeated non-decision, or Non-Event organization requires separate treatment.

The handoff may include:

- the same source reference and composite boundary;
- the internal composition map;
- component and relation claims;
- identified temporal carriers, windows, delays, transitions, and unresolved temporal questions;
- inherited and new Loss;
- source ceilings and rival maps;
- the bounded Source-Function Effect and canonical class of the Chapter-22 claim.

Chapter 23 retains ownership over:

- event decomposition;
- extended-event boundaries;
- event clusters;
- event inflation;
- Non-Event decomposition and preservation of Non-Event character;
- delay structures;
- repeated non-decision;
- internal temporal phases and completion conditions.

Chapter 24 retains Path and Trajectory decomposition. Chapter 25 retains the complete resolution taxonomy. Chapter 27 retains the full SUB limits and Counterfactual Component Test. RETYPE Chapters 34–35 retain contextual target-function profiles. No handoff inherits authority, truth status, or application legitimacy.

```text
Chapter 22 composite map
→ possible Chapter 23 temporal question

possible Chapter 23 temporal question
≠ event decomposition already completed
≠ Path or Trajectory already established
≠ target function assigned
```

A new temporal Frame, new event object, new Path, new composite, or new target function creates a new testable claim and requires the appropriate operation and Record. It does not repair an earlier failed composite claim merely by changing analytical coordinates.

<a id="chapter-23-decomposing-events-non-events-and-internal-temporal-structures"></a>

## 23. Decomposing Events, Non-Events, and Internal Temporal Structures

Chapter 23 applies the generic `DECOMPOSE` procedure to source objects whose identity depends on temporal organization. It does not define Event, Non-Event, Transition, Sequence, Path, or Trajectory again. It asks how an already bounded temporal source object can be opened under finer resolution while its reference, category, boundary, completion conditions, and praxis-relevant function remain testable.

The chapter therefore begins from a source claim, not from a stream of timestamps. An analyst may possess minutes, messages, system logs, document versions, recordings, and role records. None of these materials establishes an Event merely by being temporally ordered. They become relevant to Chapter 23 only where they support a bounded claim that something praxis-relevant changed, failed to change, remained incomplete, or was distributed across an internally structured temporal object.

Sections 23.1–23.4 establish Event-like source entry, explicit and revisable Event boundaries, Extended Events, Event Clusters, category counterpressure, and Event-Inflation control. Sections 23.5–23.8 extend the same source-bound analysis through conjunctive Non-Event entry, independent expectation testing, preservation of the higher-level `Λ` relation through positive sub-events, missing-information separation, Delay Structure, intention/person boundaries, repeated non-decision, and occurrence-specific absent binding. Sections 23.9–23.11 complete the architecture through relational internal temporal order, partial order, overlap, interruptions, thresholds, multiple clocks, temporal granularity drift, Event/Non-Event confusion control, four result axes, Failure, Mandatory Stop, Non-Capture, the seventeen-field Record view, and the Chapter-24 handoff.

The integrated Chapter-23 result is therefore a complete **temporal `DECOMPOSE` procedure for bounded Event-like and Non-Event source objects**. It preserves the source reference and makes Event category, boundary, completion, expectation, non-realization, phases, delay, multiple clocks, and category revision testable. It does not multiply Events from observation density, dissolve `Λ` into positive sub-events, convert missing information into non-occurrence, infer intention or person properties from delay, silently form a Path or Trajectory, assign a contextual target function, or increase claim or application authority.

```text
warranted temporal source object
+
declared and revisable category and boundary
+
source-supported phases, sub-events, expectations, non-realization, delay, and temporal relations
+
preservation or warranted revision of the source category
→
temporal DECOMPOSE candidate
```

<a id="23-1-event-decomposition"></a>

### 23.1 Event Decomposition

An Event-like source object is a bounded, Frame-relevant change whose occurrence makes a supported difference to the praxis reconstruction. The source may initially appear punctual because a coarse account names only a declaration, vote, signature, transfer, closure, or threshold crossing. Finer sources may show that this apparent point depended on preparation, admission conditions, partial decisions, parallel actions, role handoffs, delay, and a completion procedure.

`DECOMPOSE` does not begin by assigning every observable change a place inside the Event. It begins by declaring what the source Event claim is and why the existing compression is insufficient. The Chapter-20 requirements remain in force: the same reference object, a declared source and target granularity, a precise decomposition question, expected additional PraxisPurchase, sufficient sources, and a criterion by which the coarse Event function may be confirmed, refined, internally differentiated, partially preserved, rejected, or left underdetermined.

#### C23-EVENT-01 — independently warranted Event-like source entry

A temporal source may enter Event Decomposition only where the coarse Event claim is independently warranted before finer component selection. At minimum, the source declaration must identify:

- the reference object;
- the Event-like source category under test;
- the governing Frame;
- the coarse temporal boundary;
- the praxis-relevant change claimed to have occurred;
- the source basis for occurrence and relevance;
- the existing compression;
- the reason finer reconstruction may alter a warranted claim.

A useful source declaration can be stated as:

```yaml
event_decomposition_entry:
  reference: bounded review-decision episode E-23
  source_category: apparently punctual Event
  frame: documented review procedure
  coarse_boundary: final agenda entry through communicated closure
  coarse_change_under_test: an admitted objection became a binding review disposition
  source_basis:
    - agenda record
    - objection submission
    - quorum record
    - decision entry
    - closure communication
  current_compression: one decision Event
  decomposition_question: which phases and relations made the disposition complete?
```

This declaration does not prove that E-23 was one Event. It makes the coarse category, reference, and function available for testing. The source may later remain one Event, become an Extended Event, be reconstructed as an Event Cluster, split into several unrelated Events, or fail as a coherent Event object.

The source-entry requirement blocks several false starts:

```text
timestamp present
≠ Event established

document changed
≠ praxis-relevant Event established

utterance isolated
≠ independent Event established

several records share a date
≠ one Event established
```

A log entry may be evidence of a phase, a carrier, or a recording act rather than the praxis change under analysis. A document version may duplicate a decision already recorded elsewhere. A spoken sentence may belong to one continuing deliberative act. Event status depends on the declared Frame, claim, transition relevance, source support, and boundary—not on the smallest available unit of observation.

#### C23-BOUNDARY-01 — beginning, completion, margins, and rival boundaries

Every Event Decomposition must declare a source boundary that includes more than a timestamp. The boundary must state:

- what marks the Event's beginning;
- what marks completion;
- which contextual predecessors are necessary but remain outside the Event;
- which aftermath is needed to determine completion without becoming part of the Event automatically;
- which interruptions, resumptions, or delays occur within the candidate boundary;
- which rival boundary constructions remain supported.

For E-23, three boundary candidates may initially remain open:

```text
Boundary A:
formal vote
→ decision entry

Boundary B:
objection admission
→ deliberation
→ vote
→ decision entry

Boundary C:
objection admission
→ deliberation
→ vote
→ decision entry
→ communicated closure
```

Boundary A treats the vote as the decisive change and the record as immediate completion. Boundary B treats admission and deliberation as constitutive phases of the same decision Event. Boundary C additionally treats communication as necessary because the disposition does not become practically operative until the affected roles receive the closure. None of these boundaries follows from duration alone. Each must be tested against the source Event function.

The Event must not absorb every relevant predecessor and consequence. The earlier policy that created the review procedure may be a precondition without belonging to E-23. A later implementation dispute may be an aftermath without remaining part of the decision Event. Contextual margins preserve traceability while preventing the Event from expanding until it becomes a Path, Trajectory, institutional era, or total scene.

Reference continuity is therefore relational. The finer components must remain related to the same bounded praxis change. If the proposed opening shifts from the disposition of one admitted objection to the whole organization's governance history, the source object has changed. A broader temporal object may be legitimate, but it requires a new source claim and potentially another operation.

#### Event components are claim-bearing temporal relations

Potential Event components include:

- preparatory acts that make the bounded change possible;
- activation or admission conditions;
- partial decisions;
- role handoffs;
- parallel actions;
- threshold crossings;
- interruption and resumption;
- closure acts;
- communication necessary for practical completion.

Their inclusion remains claim-bound. A preparatory document is not automatically part of the Event merely because it came first. A communication is not automatically part of completion merely because it came later. Each component must alter the warranted reconstruction of the source Event's formation, operation, completion, role structure, access, cost, alternative space, uncertainty, or Loss.

A component can also pressure the coarse category negatively. If the purported decision entry merely copied an earlier binding disposition, the later entry may not be the Event at all. If the supposed vote lacked the required quorum, the claimed completion may fail. If communication was necessary to activate the decision but never occurred, the source may remain incomplete or may require later Non-Event analysis. WP1 keeps these possibilities open without completing the Non-Event architecture.

<a id="23-2-extended-event"></a>

### 23.2 Extended Event
