#### C24-TURN-01 — component transitions and historically traceable effects

Every turning-point claim must declare:

- the source configuration before the candidate;
- the transition or transition cluster constituting the candidate;
- the resulting configuration;
- the alternatives, costs, asymmetries, bindings, or action corridors that changed;
- the temporal window in which the change became operative;
- the evidence supporting persistence beyond the local moment;
- rival candidates and rival boundary constructions;
- the condition under which the turning-point claim would be reduced or rejected.

For R-24, concentration of approval authority may qualify only if it changes more than the formal chart. The claim requires evidence that decision access, review capacity, burden distribution, or practical continuation conditions changed. If the same offices retained the same practical authority before and after the nominal transfer, the candidate may be administratively visible but praxeologically weak.

A turning point can be dimension-specific. One transition may alter authority while access remains stable. Another may alter timing while formal roles remain unchanged. Chapter 24 therefore does not require one universal turning point for the whole trajectory.

```text
turning point for authority
≠ turning point for access automatically

turning point for formal closure
≠ turning point for practical consequence automatically
```

#### C24-ALT-01 — changed alternatives and load without retrospective inflation

A turning point must be tested through what became newly possible, harder, costlier, delayed, unavailable, or differently bound after it. The analysis may examine:

- alternatives that remained formally present but became practically inaccessible;
- alternatives that acquired higher temporal, informational, institutional, or relational cost;
- roles whose exposure or responsibility changed;
- bindings that became harder to revise;
- action corridors that narrowed, widened, or changed sequence;
- Non-Events whose later accumulation depended on the transition.

The test is not a free counterfactual. The alternative must have been historically available or sufficiently prepared within the relevant source window. Later knowledge cannot retroactively create an earlier branch.

For example, the later importance of approval concentration does not prove that it was historically experienced as decisive at the moment it occurred. Evidence may nevertheless show that after concentration, objections required a new access route, reconsideration costs increased, and local decision alternatives disappeared. Those effects can warrant a turning-point claim even without contemporaneous narrative emphasis.

```text
later narrative importance
≠ historically operative turning point

historically operative effect
≠ contemporaneous recognition required in every case
```

The decomposition must also retain counterpressure. A candidate may look decisive because later stages depend on it, yet a rival map may show that the decisive change occurred earlier through repeated deferral or later through operative closure. Where the evidence does not discriminate, the turning-point claim remains bounded or provisional.

<a id="24-6-branch-reconstruction"></a>

### 24.6 Branch Reconstruction

A branch is a historically available continuation relative to a bounded branch point. Chapter 24 reconstructs branches inside the same source Path or Trajectory; it does not invent alternative histories and does not convert every imaginable option into a past possibility.

The existing PATH statuses remain available:

```text
realized
rejected
blocked
aborted
deferred
lost
```

These statuses describe the relation between an available continuation and the realized Path. They do not establish motive, rationality, blame, legitimacy, or inevitability.

#### C24-BRANCH-01 — source, window, mechanism, and status support

Every branch claim must identify:

- the branch point;
- the continuation under consideration;
- the source supporting its availability;
- the temporal window in which it was available;
- the roles and conditions required for realization;
- the mechanism of realization, rejection, blockage, abortion, deferral, or loss;
- later reachability and residual effect;
- uncertainty and rival status assignments.

For R-24, a bounded reconsideration route may have been:

- **realized** if an operative reopening occurred;
- **rejected** if the authorized decision explicitly declined it;
- **blocked** if access or authority conditions prevented entry;
- **aborted** if reopening began but was terminated;
- **deferred** if the route remained available beyond the current window;
- **lost** if a deadline or irreversible closure removed later access.

The same label cannot be assigned without its mechanism. A branch that was never institutionally or practically available is not “blocked”; it may be merely imagined. A route that remains formally open but cannot be accessed by the relevant role may be blocked rather than realized. A route postponed beyond the relevant window may be deferred locally and lost later.

Branch status can also be dimension-sensitive. A continuation may remain legally available while becoming practically inaccessible. Chapter 24 records that divergence rather than forcing one total status.

#### C24-COUNTERFACT-01 — source-bounded unrealized alternatives

An unrealized alternative is admissible only where the source supports its historical availability. Counterfactual reconstruction may ask:

> If this supported branch had been taken within its actual window, which later Path relations would likely have differed?

But the claim must remain bounded by:

- then-available roles and capacities;
- then-existing Frames and rules;
- accessible information;
- temporal windows;
- known costs and constraints;
- source-supported mechanisms;
- explicit uncertainty.

The analysis may not import later institutions, later knowledge, or idealized options into the earlier branch space. It may not claim that the alternative would certainly have succeeded. It may not use the counterfactual to assign guilt or prescribe action.

```text
imaginable continuation
≠ historically available branch

historically available branch
≠ successful alternative guaranteed

counterfactual sensitivity
≠ prediction or verdict
```

A branch reconstruction can revise the coarse Path in several ways. It may show that a supposedly linear development contained a viable rejected route. It may show that an alleged alternative was never available. It may reveal that repeated deferral changed the branch from accessible to costly and eventually lost. It may also leave rival branch maps unresolved.

The branch map remains part of the same source object only while the alternatives relate to the same historical reference, boundary, and formation lineage. Constructing an entirely new counterfactual Path from selected alternatives would require a distinct claim and potentially a separate `COMPOSE` record.

<a id="24-7-internal-frame-changes"></a>

### 24.7 Internal Frame Changes

A Path or Trajectory may pass through several Frames while retaining one historical reference. Internal Frame change occurs where the contextual boundary of relevance, access, role, or decision changes within the source development. It is not automatically source replacement and not automatically `PROJECT_AS`.

R-24 may contain at least three candidate Frames:

```text
Frame F1 — objection admission
relevant question: may the objection enter review?

Frame F2 — decision authorization
relevant question: who may transform admitted material into a binding disposition?

Frame F3 — closure and reconsideration
relevant question: when is the disposition operative, and under what conditions may it reopen?
```

These Frames can be sequential, overlapping, nested, or partially competing. The source Path may continue across them if roles, records, obligations, unresolved residues, and practical consequences preserve historical continuity.

#### C24-FRAME-01 — reconstruct Frame change without source replacement or projection

Every internal Frame-change claim must declare:

- the earlier Frame;
- the later Frame;
- the transition, Event, Non-Event, or rule change connecting them;
- what became newly relevant or irrelevant;
- which roles, accesses, expectations, and alternatives changed;
- which structures persisted across the change;
- whether the source Path boundary remains stable;
- whether a separate operation is required.

A Frame change can occur through a formal procedural transfer, a practical shift in what counts as admissible material, a change in decision venue, a new closure condition, or a recontextualization of an unresolved objection. None of these changes automatically means that the earlier Path ended.

```text
internal Frame change
≠ new source object automatically

internal Frame change
≠ target function automatically

recontextualization occurrence inside Path
≠ PROJECT_AS automatically
```

`PROJECT_AS` becomes relevant only if the completed Path or Trajectory is assigned a bounded contextual function in another target context—for example, as an organizational Frame-function or Attractor-function. Chapter 24 instead asks how Frame changes occurred within the same historical development.

#### C24-CONTINUITY-01 — reference and functional continuity across Frame change

Continuity must be tested rather than inherited from the label. Indicators may include:

- the same dispute, decision object, or institutional process remains identifiable;
- earlier commitments or exclusions remain operative;
- roles or records carry forward;
- unresolved Non-Events and residues persist;
- later closure answers or displaces earlier questions;
- the coarse Path function remains reconstructible through the Frame change.

Continuity is pressured where:

- the object of review materially changes;
- earlier roles and obligations no longer matter;
- the new Frame begins from an independent source configuration;
- the historical relation survives only as analogy or label;
- the new selection rule excludes the earlier Path's constitutive structure.

For R-24, a move from objection admission to approval authorization may preserve the same source development if the admitted objection remains the object being processed and the authorization stage determines its disposition. If the later process addresses a different issue under a new mandate, the shared organizational label may not preserve Same-Reference identity.

Functional continuity need not mean stable local function. The Path may change from opening access to narrowing authority to stabilizing closure. What must remain reconstructible is how those changing local functions belong to one bounded development.

```text
same institution
≠ same Path

changed local function
≠ source continuity lost automatically
```

Where continuity remains underdetermined, Chapter 24 preserves the rival constructions for WP3 rather than selecting one by narrative convenience.

<a id="24-8-competing-continuations"></a>

### 24.8 Competing Continuations

An open Path or Trajectory may contain several supported future continuations. Chapter 24 reconstructs their current accessibility and relation to the source development. It does not predict which continuation will occur and does not recommend one.

For R-24, competing continuations after operative closure may include:

```text
continuation A
closure remains stable and reconsideration expires

continuation B
bounded reconsideration reopens the decision

continuation C
local repair preserves closure while modifying access or burden

continuation D
parallel dispute produces a rival review Path
```

These continuations may be unequally accessible because of:

- different authorization requirements;
- time windows;
- information access;
- costs and burdens;
- exposure and institutional support;
- binding commitments;
- unresolved Non-Events;
- prior branch loss.

#### C24-CONTINUE-01 — preserve plurality and unequal accessibility without prediction

Every competing-continuation claim must specify:

- the current source configuration;
- each supported continuation;
- the conditions required for entry;
- current availability or blockage;
- unequal costs, risks, access, and binding load;
- the relation to earlier turning points and branches;
- the temporal horizon of the claim;
- uncertainty and conditions of revision.

The claim may state that one continuation is institutionally easier or less costly than another if the source supports that difference. It may not convert accessibility into probability without an independently warranted probabilistic model. It may not treat the most accessible continuation as desirable, legitimate, or inevitable.

```text
more accessible
≠ more likely automatically
≠ more legitimate
≠ recommended

competing continuation
≠ prediction
```

The continuation map is especially important where the coarse Trajectory appears directional. A strong direction claim can survive only if counter-trends, repair routes, reversals, and blocked alternatives remain visible.

#### C24-NONLINEAR-01 — reversals, interruptions, repair, and counter-trends

A finer Path map must preserve supported non-linearity, including:

- local reversals;
- interrupted and resumed subpaths;
- parallel developments at different speeds;
- repair that changes one dimension but not another;
- counter-trends inside a broader direction;
- temporary reopening after closure;
- persistence of residue after formal completion.

For R-24, approval authority may become concentrated while access protections improve. Operative closure may stabilize while a reconsideration route opens. A local repair may reduce burden without reversing the broader trajectory. These structures do not automatically refute the coarse direction claim, but they prevent direction from becoming teleology or homogeneous progress.

The analyst must state whether the coarse direction:

- remains supported across dimensions;
- is supported only in one dimension;
- contains materially opposed subpaths;
- requires qualification;
- or becomes underdetermined.

WP2 does not select the final source-function effect or Output Class. It exposes the continuation and non-linearity structure that WP3 must integrate with inherited Loss, Path-Dependence load, rival PATH construction, and the four result axes.

#### WP2 completion boundary

WP2 establishes turning-point burden, changed alternatives and loads, branch statuses, source-bounded counterfactuals, internal Frame changes, reference and functional continuity, competing continuations, unequal accessibility, and preservation of non-linearity.

It does not yet establish:

- the final inherited-versus-new Loss account;
- irrecoverable compression or compression debt as the integrated result;
- decomposition of Path-Dependence load;
- final Same-Path versus rival-PATH adjudication;
- the four result axes;
- Failure, Mandatory Stop, or Non-Capture as the completed Chapter-24 result;
- the nineteen-field Record view;
- Chapter-25 handoff;
- Chapter-24 provisional lock.

```text
WP2 complete
≠ Chapter 24 complete
≠ one universal turning point established
≠ counterfactual success proven
≠ internal Frame change preserves Same-Path automatically
≠ continuation predicted or recommended
≠ Path Dependence confirmed
≠ final Output Class selected
≠ Chapter 24 provisionally locked
≠ SUB provisionally locked
```

<a id="24-9-irrecoverable-compression"></a>

### 24.9 Irrecoverable Compression

A Path or Trajectory enters Chapter 24 only after an earlier `COMPOSE` occurrence has selected configurations, ordered or partially ordered transitions, preserved some alternatives, compressed others, and excluded still others. Finer reconstruction can reopen this formed object. It cannot restore a complete pre-compositional history that was never retained.

The controlling relation is:

```text
SUB(PATH(X))
≠ X
```

This inequality does not mean that later decomposition is futile. It means that the later map has a different evidential and analytical history from the materials that were available before or during the original composition. It may recover details that the coarse Record omitted, integrate newly available sources, distinguish previously merged subpaths, or show that a former turning point was too coarse. None of those gains converts `DECOMPOSE` into an inverse operation.

#### Four temporal-loss positions

Every Path/Trajectory decomposition must separate at least four positions:

1. **inherited `COMPOSE` Loss** — structure already preserved, compressed, excluded, uncertain, or irrecoverable when the source Path was formed;
2. **recovered detail** — source-supported structure that existed within the original reference but was compressed rather than irrecoverably lost;
3. **newly sourced detail** — evidence obtained after the original composition, which may enrich or challenge the source Path without proving what the original analysis could have known;
4. **new `DECOMPOSE` Loss** — selection, compression, exclusion, uncertainty, and irrecoverability introduced by the present finer reconstruction.

The same datum cannot be silently moved between these positions merely to strengthen the result. A later archive discovery may supply new evidence about an earlier branch. It does not erase that the branch was excluded or unknown in the original Path formation. Likewise, a detail marked irrecoverable cannot later be described as recovered unless new evidence independently supports it and the change in source basis is declared.

#### C24-LOSS-01 — separate inherited and current Loss

A valid Chapter-24 Record must disclose:

- which coarse structures remain preserved;
- which original distinctions were compressed;
- which alternatives or local relations were excluded;
- which relations remain uncertain;
- which historical distinctions remain irrecoverable;
- which details were recovered from already available materials;
- which details depend on later or newly accessed sources;
- which Loss is introduced by the current `DECOMPOSE` occurrence.

The canonical Loss fields remain exactly:

```yaml
loss:
  preserved:
  compressed:
  excluded:
  uncertain:
  irrecoverable:
```

Chapter 24 may distinguish inherited and current instances of this structure inside the operation-specific view. It may not add a sixth Loss category.

For R-24, the coarse trajectory preserved objection admission, repeated deferral, concentration of approval authority, operative closure, and bounded reconsideration. It may have compressed individual responsibility transfers, parallel access work, and local repair. It may have excluded unsuccessful informal objections and detailed branch timing. Some private deliberations may remain irrecoverable. The finer map may recover portal logs and meeting sequences, but it cannot reconstruct undocumented reasons or complete the earlier alternative space by narrative interpolation.

#### Compression debt

`Compression debt` names the unresolved traceability burden carried by a coarse Path claim when important constitutive distinctions remain compressed or irrecoverable. It is not a quantity and not an accusation against the original composition.

Compression debt can concern:

- uncertain intermediate configurations;
- unresolved relation among parallel subpaths;
- unlocatable turning-point effects;
- unknown branch availability;
- collapsed cost or asymmetry distributions;
- missing distinction between local repair and trajectory reversal;
- inherited uncertainty about periodization.

It may be reduced by new evidence or a better relational map. It may also remain. A low-detail source Path can remain useful under a bounded claim while openly carrying compression debt. Conversely, an elegant detailed map can still fail if it hides inherited Loss.

```text
more recovered detail
≠ original complete history restored

compression debt
≠ score
≠ Output Class
≠ new PMS primitive
```

#### C24-NONINVERSE-01 — prohibit complete-history recovery

No Chapter-24 result may state or imply that finer reconstruction has recovered `X` in full. The non-inverse rule survives even where all currently known records have been reviewed because:

- original selection already structured visibility;
- unknown or destroyed materials may remain;
- some alternatives were never recorded;
- later evidence is conditioned by later Frames;
- current decomposition applies a new selection rule;
- irrecoverability is not cancelled by analytical confidence.

The strongest permissible claim is therefore bounded: the current decomposition reconstructs a source-supported finer map of the same candidate Path or Trajectory under declared Loss. It is never the complete history itself.

<a id="24-10-decomposition-of-path-dependence"></a>

### 24.10 Decomposition of Path Dependence

Path Dependence is a property claim about historical indispensability. It is not a hidden substance inside a Trajectory and not a synonym for long duration, recurrence, or narrative richness. Chapter 24 opens the load-bearing historical structures through which the present configuration may depend on the prior Path.

The decomposition begins with the existing Chapter-12 question:

> Would the present configuration, meaning, cost structure, or continuation space be materially different under a relevantly different supported history while current conditions are held as comparable as the claim permits?

Chapter 24 does not rerun the complete Chapter-12 property test from first principles. It maps the internal carriers of an already proposed Path-Dependence claim and determines whether the coarse claim remains reconstructible.

#### Path-Dependence load

A Path-Dependence load is a claim-bound set of historical structures that carry dependence in a named dimension. Candidate profiles include:

```text
Α + Θ
→ recurrence, stabilization, and lowered local friction

Ω + Θ
→ accumulated cost, exposure, access, and exit asymmetry

Ψ + Θ
→ commitment, obligation, and binding load

Λ + Θ
→ repeated non-decision, residue, delay, and lost windows
```

These expressions describe occurrence-level profiles. They do not fuse operators, revise Δ–Ψ dependencies, or create new compound operators. No profile is automatically necessary or sufficient for every Path-Dependence claim.

For R-24, the finer reconstruction may show:

- recurrent deferral lowered the friction of further deferral (`Α + Θ`);
- approval concentration accumulated unequal access and reconsideration costs (`Ω + Θ`);
- formal closure and reliance on the decision increased binding load (`Ψ + Θ`);
- unresolved objections and expired windows persisted as residue (`Λ + Θ`).

The profiles can support different dimensions and different intervals. Strong access dependence may coexist with weak dependence in the communication carrier. A later repair can reduce one load without erasing another.

#### C24-PDLOAD-01 — decompose without reification or score

Every Path-Dependence-load claim must identify:

- the present analytical cut;
- the tested dependence dimension;
- the historical carrier or relation;
- the relevant interval or sequence;
- the present structure allegedly carried by that history;
- a current-condition sufficiency challenge;
- relevant counterevidence or repair;
- uncertainty and Loss;
- the bounded effect on the coarse Path-Dependence claim.

The analysis may conclude that the proposed dependence is:

- supported in a bounded dimension;
- distributed across several historical carriers;
- weaker than the coarse claim suggested;
- reducible to order dependence;
- explainable largely by current conditions;
- or underdetermined.

It may not compute a universal Path-Dependence score, rank persons or institutions, infer inevitability, or treat the presence of multiple profiles as cumulative proof.

```text
many dependence-bearing profiles
≠ stronger claim automatically

historical load
≠ determinism

Path Dependence decomposed
≠ Path Dependence confirmed automatically
```

A coarse Trajectory may remain useful even when the stronger Path-Dependence claim is reduced. The disposition of the property claim must therefore remain separate from the continued usability of the Path object.

<a id="24-11-decomposition-versus-alternative-path-construction"></a>

### 24.11 Decomposition versus Alternative PATH Construction

A finer temporal account can remain a `DECOMPOSE` occurrence only while it opens the same formed PATH object. Where the analysis materially changes source selection, periodization, formation relations, or historical referent, it constructs a rival PATH object and requires a separate `COMPOSE` occurrence.

The distinction cannot be decided by whether both accounts use the same institutional name, dates, endpoint, or source archive. It depends on object identity and formation lineage.

#### Same-Path indicators

A candidate finer map remains pressure-tested as the same Path where it preserves or explicitly revises within one reference:

- the historical process under analysis;
- source and endpoint boundaries;
- the governing Frame or the traceable sequence of internal Frame changes;
- the original relevance and selection rule;
- constitutive transitions and coarse function;
- central Non-Events, alternatives, and Loss;
- the relation between finer subpaths and the original source object.

A decomposition may move a turning point, reveal a parallel subpath, or reduce a Path-Dependence claim while still opening the same Path. Revision does not itself create a new object.

#### Rival-PATH indicators

Rival construction pressure arises where the proposed map:

- chooses a materially different beginning or endpoint;
- selects a different set of source configurations as constitutive;
- applies a different relevance or formation rule;
- turns formerly external or excluded processes into the central object;
- constructs a new macro-Path from previously independent paths;
- changes the historical referent rather than opening its internal structure;
- uses the same materials to answer a materially different PATH question.

R-24 illustrates the problem through three periodizations:

```text
process-centered construction
→ begins with first objection admission

 authority-centered construction
→ begins with concentration of approval authority

consequence-centered construction
→ begins with operative closure and tracks later burden
```

The authority-centered account may be a finer phase map of the process-centered Path if it remains an internal subpath and preserves the larger reference. It becomes a rival PATH construction if it discards admission and closure relations as non-constitutive and forms a different development around authority concentration. The consequence-centered account likewise may open a later phase or form a distinct Path, depending on its source boundary and formation rule.

#### C24-RIVALPATH-01 — require separate records for separate objects

When a candidate map crosses the Same-Path boundary, the analysis must:

1. stop treating it as continuation of the current `DECOMPOSE` occurrence;
2. preserve the current source claim and its disposition;
3. declare the rival source selection, boundary, ordering, and formation rule;
4. execute a separate `COMPOSE` record;
5. declare the rival composition's own Loss;
6. compare the two objects without merging them into a compromise history.

```text
rival PATH construction
≠ failed DECOMPOSE automatically

admissible rival COMPOSE
≠ original Path disproven automatically

new transformation
= new testable claim
```

A useful rival Path can expose the limits of the original periodization. It does not inherit its Output Class, support, or authority.

#### Four result axes

Chapter 24 preserves the established result architecture:

```text
local operation result
≠ Path/Trajectory source-function effect
≠ prior source-claim disposition
≠ canonical Output Class
```

For the bounded R-24 illustration, a permissible local mapping is:

```yaml
path_trajectory_result_R24:
  local_operation_result: supported_relational_path_decomposition
  source_function_effect: internally_differentiated
  prior_source_claim_disposition: reduced
  canonical_output_class: admissible_with_bounded_claim
```

This result means that the source Path remains reconstructible through a finer relational map, while its earlier linearity and broad Path-Dependence language require reduction. It does not establish one final periodization, complete historical recovery, causal sufficiency, inevitability, legitimacy, or recommendation.

#### C24-RESULT-01 — keep the axes non-compensatory

A supported local map cannot compensate for a failed Same-Reference claim. A preserved coarse function cannot compensate for invented branches. A reduced prior claim cannot be concealed by selecting an admissible Output Class. The canonical class applies only to the current bounded transformation claim.

<a id="24-12-failed-trajectory-decomposition"></a>

### 24.12 Failed Trajectory Decomposition

A Path/Trajectory decomposition fails where finer detail no longer performs a source-preserving relational reconstruction. Failure is not limited to factual error. It also includes operation confusion, identity loss, unsupported internal structure, and decomposition that destroys the very Path function it claims to explain.

Typical failure forms include:

- additional chronology without transition or subpath gain;
- arbitrary temporal fragments presented as subpaths;
- unsupported intermediate configurations or Transition Clusters;
- retrospective turning points without operative historical effects;
- imagined alternatives presented as historical branches;
- forced linearity across parallel or competing subpaths;
- Frame changes treated as self-evident continuity;
- Path Dependence treated as substance, score, or determinism;
- inherited Loss hidden behind newly available detail;
- complete-history recovery claims;
- rival periodization hidden as the same `DECOMPOSE` occurrence;
- target-function assignment hidden inside temporal description;
- decomposition to the point that the source reference and coarse function become unrecognizable.

#### C24-STOP-01 — Failure and Mandatory Stop

`failed_transformation` is appropriate where the completed attempt does not satisfy the Chapter-24 requirements but the failure itself can be stated without further prohibited inference. Examples include an unsupported subpath map, a turning-point claim defeated by finer evidence, or a decomposition that cannot reconnect its components to the source Path.

`mandatory_stop` applies before continuation where proceeding would require:

- inventing transitions, subpaths, turning points, branch availability, or Frame continuity;
- reconstructing irrecoverable history as fact;
- preserving Same-Path identity after a reference break;
- hiding a new periodization or formation rule inside `DECOMPOSE`;
- inferring intention, guilt, person type, legitimacy, prediction, or recommendation;
- revising Δ–Ψ or fusing operators;
- crossing the Source or Traceability Ceiling;
- using finer detail to rescue a previously failed coarse claim without a new test.

Stop is a positive methodological result. It preserves what remains supported and prevents analytical recursion from becoming immunity.

#### C24-NC-01 — Non-Capture without coarse-claim rescue

`non_capture` remains available where the Path question is legitimate but no responsible finer map can be selected. This may occur when:

- rival periodizations remain equally supported;
- intermediate transition relations are unavailable;
- turning-point candidates cannot be discriminated;
- historical branch availability is irrecoverable;
- several clock or Frame relations cannot be joined;
- inherited compression prevents a responsible Path-Dependence map;
- every candidate decomposition loses Source Identity.

```text
non_capture
≠ coarse Path confirmed
≠ coarse Trajectory confirmed
≠ rival histories merged
≠ irrecoverable detail restored
```

Non-Capture does not protect the earlier broad Path claim. That claim must retain its own supported, reduced, rejected, or underdetermined disposition.

#### Path/Trajectory Decomposition Record View

The operation-specific view contains exactly nineteen fields:

```yaml
path_trajectory_decomposition:
  source_path_object:
  source_category:
  source_reference_and_boundary:
  source_path_or_trajectory_function:
  original_selection_and_formation:
  inherited_compose_loss:
  target_granularity:
  decomposition_question:
  subpaths:
  transition_clusters:
  turning_points:
  branch_reconstruction:
  internal_frame_changes:
  competing_continuations:
  path_dependence_load:
  rival_path_status:
  unresolved_structure:
  loss:
  admissibility_result:
```

The view maps into the Shared Transformation Record plus controlled `extensions`. It creates no parallel schema, closed Path or branch enum, turning-point score, branch probability, Path-Dependence score, or automatic Same-Path decision.

The `admissibility_result` must preserve the four result axes and identify any separate rival `COMPOSE` record. The `loss` field retains the canonical five-part Loss structure. `inherited_compose_loss` records the source Path's prior Loss without pretending that it was generated by the current operation.

#### C24-HANDOFF-01 — bounded Chapter-25 handoff

The controlled continuation is:

```text
Chapter 24 provisional lock
→ Chapter 25 Preparation Gate
→ Chapter 25 — Resolution Gain, Neutrality, Drift, and Escape
```

Chapter 24 hands Chapter 25 only the unresolved resolution questions produced by the completed finer map, including:

- whether the additional distinctions generate Resolution Gain;
- whether correct additional detail remains Resolution Neutral;
- whether complexity has become Resolution Drift;
- whether finer analysis is being used as Resolution Escape;
- whether source coherence persists despite detail growth;
- which claims require reduction, Stop, or Non-Capture.

Chapter 25 owns the complete taxonomy and tests for Resolution Gain, Neutrality, Drift, and Escape. Chapter 24 does not preselect those outcomes merely because a finer Path map exists.

Protected later ownership remains:

```text
Chapter 26
→ complete operation-boundary adjudication

Chapter 27
→ complete SUB Limits and Counterfactual Component Test

Chapter 28
→ integrated cases, local audit, and Part output mapping

RETYPE
→ contextual target functions of completed Paths and Trajectories
```

<a id="chapter-24-completion-boundary"></a>

#### Chapter 24 completion boundary

The integrated WP4 audit confirms the Chapter-24 Contract, all twenty-four Preparation-Audit controls, all twenty-four Pressure Duties, PATH-source and formation-lineage integrity, Same-Path/rival-PATH separation, subpath and transition-cluster coherence, turning-point and branch source burdens, internal-Frame and continuation boundaries, inherited/current Loss separation, non-invertibility, bounded Path-Dependence load, result-axis separation, Failure, Mandatory Stop, Non-Capture, and package consistency. The Chapter-24 result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

Chapter 24 is therefore **provisionally locked**. The class names the chapter-level methodological result under the still-open SUB Part and later corpus-wide audits. It does not recover complete history, select one universally privileged periodization, establish Path Dependence in every dimension, decide counterfactual truth, predict or recommend a continuation, reject all rival PATH constructions, complete the Chapter-25 Resolution Taxonomy, or increase claim, application, governance, or person-level authority.

All twenty-four Chapter-24 Pressure Duties occur exactly once in canonical prose. No standalone Case, Transformation Record, Smoke Record, appendix expansion, schema artifact, new operation, Rule, Output Class, audit stage, Path/branch/periodization enum, turning-point or Path-Dependence score, probability model, prediction engine, person type, or authority mechanism has been produced.

```text
Chapter 24 provisionally locked
≠ original history recovered
≠ one periodization universally privileged
≠ Path Dependence automatically confirmed
≠ rival PATH automatically rejected
≠ prediction or recommendation authorized
≠ Chapter 25 completed
≠ SUB provisionally locked
```

Reopening requires a concrete conflict involving PMS Base, Structure or Contract ownership, PATH-source identity, original selection or formation lineage, inherited Loss, Same-Path continuity, subpath or transition-cluster relations, turning-point or branch support, internal Frame continuity, competing-continuation bounds, irrecoverable compression, Path-Dependence load, result axes, Failure/Stop/Non-Capture, a Pressure Duty, a Reference or Formal-Model binding, or a later integrated dependency. Preference for denser chronology, stronger directionality, one total periodization, complete-history language, closed branch classes, prediction, person typing, or premature Chapter-25/RETYPE claims is insufficient.

<a id="chapter-25-resolution-gain-neutrality-drift-and-escape"></a>

## 25. Resolution Gain, Neutrality, Drift, and Escape

Chapter 25 evaluates what a finer reconstruction has actually accomplished. Chapters 18–24 establish source entry, granularity relations, the generic `DECOMPOSE` procedure, and object-family-specific ways of opening occurrences, composites, temporal structures, Paths, and Trajectories. Chapter 25 does not repeat those procedures. It compares the warranted coarse reconstruction with the warranted finer reconstruction and classifies the resolution result.

The governing question is not how many details were added. It is:

> Which warranted statement about the reconstructed praxis must now be expressed differently, and what follows if no such statement changes?

The comparison is claim-bound. A finer map may contain many accurate distinctions and still leave the tested claim unchanged. It may instead expose a constitutive relation, alter a boundary, redistribute a cost or asymmetry, reduce a Path claim, reveal hidden Loss, or defeat the source category itself. Conversely, an apparently sophisticated finer map may add complexity without relational performance, or may shift to another level merely to preserve a claim already burdened by counterpressure.

Sections 25.1–25.4 establish four principal local resolution results:

```text
Resolution Gain
→ the warranted reconstruction changes

Resolution Neutrality
→ the finer reconstruction is supported, but the tested claim does not change

Resolution Drift
→ complexity and inferential load grow faster than discrimination, relation support, or source coherence

Resolution Escape
→ granularity changes while the unresolved burden on the prior claim is displaced rather than answered
```

These are local resolution results. They are not four new operations, not four new source categories, and not substitutes for the canonical Output Classes. The same finer reconstruction may have a local resolution result, a source-function effect, a prior-claim disposition, and a canonical Output Class; those axes remain separate.

Sections 25.1–25.4 establish bounded coarse/finer comparison on the same tested claim, Resolution Gain through an explicit changed warranted reconstruction, Resolution Neutrality as supported no-change, Resolution Drift as complexity outrunning discrimination, relation support, or source coherence, Resolution Escape as displaced counterpressure, warranted revision with preserved prior disposition, and the four-attempt R-25 anchor. Sections 25.5–25.8 establish Detail without Purchase, the bounded removal test, separate component/relation/claim support, coarser-function anchoring, graph-neutral relation support, Source Overreach, non-compensation, Calibration Loss, open-threshold discipline, and bounded monitoring-kernel support without a universal halting oracle. Sections 25.9–25.11 complete the architecture through Decomposition Fatigue, six local resolution families, four result axes, unsupported-refinement handling, Failure, Mandatory Stop, Non-Capture, materially bounded re-entry, the nineteen-field Resolution Assessment Record View, and the Chapter-26 handoff.

The integrated Chapter-25 result is therefore a complete **claim-bound resolution-assessment and bounded continuation procedure for `DECOMPOSE`**. It determines whether finer reconstruction changes the warranted claim, validly leaves it unchanged, drifts beyond discrimination and support, or escapes unresolved counterpressure; it keeps source-function effect, prior claim disposition, local result, and canonical Output Class separate; and it treats Stop as a positive method result with traceable re-entry conditions. It does not solve universal halting, convert graph density or formal completeness into warrant, automate semantic truth or source adequacy, erase prior failure through reframing, mix `DECOMPOSE` with `PROJECT_AS`, diagnose persons, or increase application, governance, or authority.

<a id="25-1-resolution-gain"></a>

### 25.1 Resolution Gain

Resolution Gain occurs where finer resolution changes a warranted praxis reconstruction. The change may strengthen a claim, but strengthening is not required. Gain may also narrow, differentiate, reduce, or reject a coarse claim. What matters is that at least one warranted statement must now be formulated differently because the finer reconstruction reveals a relevant distinction or relation that the coarse account did not adequately carry.

#### C25-ENTRY-01 — bounded coarse/finer comparison on the same tested claim

Resolution classification requires an explicit comparison entry. At minimum, the analyst must declare:

- the source Transformation Record or bounded source object;
- the tested claim;
- the coarse reconstruction;
- the finer reconstruction;
- the comparison basis;
- the source and relation support available to both descriptions;
- the praxis dimensions under test;
- the condition under which the claim would count as changed or unchanged.

A bounded R-25 comparison may begin as follows:

```yaml
resolution_comparison_entry:
  source_object: review-governance Trajectory R-24
  tested_claim: approval concentration was the single turning point governing access and binding closure
  coarse_reconstruction:
    turning_point: formal concentration of approval authority
    function: explains the transition from open review to binding closure
  finer_reconstruction_question: did access restriction and authority concentration form one transition or distinct historically operative transitions?
  comparison_basis:
    - timing and order of access restriction
    - formal authority transfer
    - threshold for objection entry
    - binding effect on closure
  same_claim_required: true
  new_claim_if_scope_changes: separately_declared
```

The comparison does not require identical wording at both resolutions. It requires continuity of the tested burden. If the finer reconstruction changes the source object, Frame, periodization, target function, or claim question materially, that change must be declared and may require a new Transformation Record. A new question can be legitimate; it cannot be presented as though it had answered the old one.

The entry rule blocks several shortcuts:

```text
coarse and fine descriptions concern the same topic
≠ same tested claim established

more detailed record
≠ valid comparison established

new source object or function
≠ refinement of the old claim automatically
```

#### C25-GAIN-01 — a warranted reconstruction must change

A Resolution Gain candidate must identify the exact warranted difference. Gain may consist in:

- correcting the source category;
- altering an Event, Non-Event, Path, branch, phase, or turning-point boundary;
- revealing a constitutive relation hidden by the coarse map;
- distinguishing sequential from parallel or partially ordered structures;
- redistributing access, cost, exposure, responsibility, authority, or binding load;
- changing which alternatives were historically available;
- changing an expectation, delay, or Non-Event claim;
- identifying inherited or newly produced Loss;
- showing that the coarse source function is only partially preserved;
- establishing a warranted Stop or Non-Capture condition concealed by the coarse reconstruction.

For R-25 Attempt A, finer evidence distinguishes two transitions:

```text
access restriction
→ fewer objections can reach the operative threshold

formal authority concentration
→ fewer roles can produce a binding disposition
```

The coarse claim treated approval concentration as the single turning point governing both access and binding closure. The finer reconstruction shows that the access corridor had already narrowed before the formal authority transition. The warranted reconstruction must therefore change: the turning-point function is distributed across a transition cluster, and the coarse claim is too strong in its original form.

This is Resolution Gain even though the original claim is reduced. Finer resolution has improved the warrant structure by showing which transition carried which function.

```text
Gain
≠ stronger claim automatically

Gain may produce
→ confirmation
→ refinement
→ differentiation
→ reduction
→ rejection
```

#### C25-CLAIMEFFECT-01 — name the changed or unchanged claim

Every gain classification must state the claim effect in propositional form. It is insufficient to report that the graph is richer, the chronology is denser, or more components are visible. The record must make clear what can now be said, what can no longer be said, and what remains unresolved.

A bounded claim-effect statement for R-25 Attempt A is:

```yaml
claim_effect:
  prior_claim: formal authority concentration was the single turning point governing both access and binding closure
  revised_claim: access restriction and formal authority concentration were distinct but related transitions with different functions in the coarse Trajectory
  prior_claim_disposition: reduced
  retained_uncertainty:
    - whether both transitions belong to one transition cluster
    - whether the earlier access restriction was reversible during the tested window
```

The claim effect is not the canonical Output Class. It is also not a declaration of empirical finality. It records the local consequence of the finer reconstruction for the tested claim.

<a id="25-2-resolution-neutrality"></a>

### 25.2 Resolution Neutrality

Resolution Neutrality occurs where a finer reconstruction is valid, source-supported, and correctly related to the coarse object, yet leaves the tested warranted reconstruction unchanged. Neutrality is therefore a substantive comparison result, not a synonym for analytical failure.

#### C25-NEUTRAL-01 — supported no-change remains distinct from failure

A neutral result requires:

- an admissible coarse/finer comparison;
- adequate source support for the added detail;
- adequate relation support where relations are claimed;
- preserved source identity or an explicitly controlled translation;
- no changed warranted statement within the tested dimensions;
- no hidden new Loss that would alter the claim;
- no unresolved burden shifted to another level.

R-25 Attempt B adds minute-level timestamps for messages already known to occur within the same bounded review phase. The added times may be correct. They do not change:

- the order of relevant transitions;
- the roles involved;
- the access or authority threshold;
- the costs or alternatives;
- the expectation window;
- the source function;
- the Loss relevant to the tested claim.

The appropriate local result is Resolution Neutrality.

```text
accurate added detail
+
valid comparison
+
no changed warranted reconstruction
→
Resolution Neutrality candidate
```

Neutrality must remain distinct from several neighboring results:

```text
Resolution Neutrality
≠ unsupported refinement
≠ missing information
≠ failed transformation
≠ claim reduction automatically
≠ non_capture
```

A neutral result may justify Optional Stop because the current claim needs no further opening. It does not by itself require Mandatory Stop. Mandatory Stop belongs to cases where continuation would cross source, purchase, coherence, calibration, anti-immunization, or traceability boundaries. Those complete rules are owned by WP2 and WP3.

Neutrality can also protect the coarse function. If finer distinctions leave the tested reconstruction unchanged, the analyst may state that the coarse account was adequate **for that bounded claim**. This is not permission to treat the coarse account as exhaustive, universally sufficient, or immune to another question.

```text
neutral for tested claim Q
≠ neutral for every possible claim

coarse function sufficient here
≠ coarse object complete
```

<a id="25-3-resolution-drift"></a>

### 25.3 Resolution Drift

Resolution Drift occurs where analytical complexity, fragmentation, or inferential burden grows faster than the reconstruction's relational and discriminative performance. Drift is not simply “too much detail.” A dense reconstruction may be admissible where each distinction carries source-supported load. Drift begins where added structure no longer improves, and may actively weaken, the ability to reconstruct the source object and test the claim.

#### C25-DRIFT-01 — complexity must be tested against discrimination and relation support

Drift pressure is present where one or more of the following occur:

- components multiply while their relations remain unspecified or unsupported;
- timestamps become denser without changing a temporal claim;
- alternative maps proliferate without stable comparison conditions;
- semantic precision increases while source precision decreases;
- the coarser source function becomes less reconstructible;
- the source object disappears into a list of local details;
- every counterexample produces another layer of analysis rather than a bounded revision;
- the analysis can be stabilized only by adding still more analysis;
- formal or visual completeness is treated as substantive purchase.

R-25 Attempt C converts hundreds of record-level actions into graph nodes. The resulting graph may be technically valid as a representation of records. Yet the relations that would distinguish constitutive transitions from incidental activity remain incomplete. Node density increases, while the review-governance Trajectory becomes harder to reconstruct and the tested turning-point claim remains unchanged.

```text
more nodes
+
more edges proposed
+
less relation support
+
weaker source-object reconstruction
→
Resolution Drift pressure
```

The graph itself does not cause drift. A two-dimensional, three-dimensional, interactive, or non-visual representation may all support admissible analysis. Drift concerns the relation between complexity and warrant, not the display medium.

```text
spatial richness
≠ relational support

interactive exploration
≠ changed claim

graph density
≠ Resolution Gain
```

Drift also differs from honest uncertainty. An analyst may state that several relations remain unknown and stop with a bounded provisional map. Drift arises where unsupported complexity is continued, promoted, or used as if it had increased discriminative power.

WP1 establishes the concept and its characteristic pressure. WP2 owns the complete Source Overreach, Calibration Loss, coarser-function, relation-support, and non-compensation tests. WP3 owns final classification, Stop, Failure, Non-Capture, and re-entry.

<a id="25-4-resolution-escape"></a>

### 25.4 Resolution Escape

Resolution Escape occurs where counterpressure burdens a claim, the analysis changes granularity or relative level, and the resulting local detail is presented as if it had answered the original burden when it has not. Escape is therefore an anti-immunization failure.

The characteristic pattern is:

```text
counterexample burdens claim C
→ analysis moves to finer level L2
→ local distinctions D are produced
→ burden on C remains unanswered
→ D is presented as confirmation of C
```

The problem is not the move to finer resolution. Such a move may be exactly what a warranted revision requires. The problem is preserving the old claim's authority while changing the object of proof.

#### C25-ESCAPE-01 — granularity change cannot rescue an unanswered claim

R-25 Attempt D begins with the coarse claim:

```text
formal closure ended reconsideration
```

A counterexample shows documented reconsideration activity after formal closure. The coarse claim is therefore burdened. The analysis then shifts to post-closure micro-interactions and argues that their existence demonstrates how closure continued to organize the process. That new local claim may be worth testing, but it does not establish that reconsideration ended.

```text
closure continued to structure later interactions
≠ reconsideration ended
```

Using the finer claim to preserve the original one is Resolution Escape. The earlier claim disposition must remain visible. At minimum, the analysis must stop before the local detail is promoted as confirmation of the defeated claim.

Escape can occur through changes in:

- granularity;
- relative level;
- Frame;
- temporal boundary;
- source object;
- composition;
- target function.

The general anti-immunization rule remains:

```text
new transformation
= new testable claim
```

A changed analytical coordinate may open a legitimate new inquiry. It cannot erase the failed condition attached to the prior claim.

#### C25-REVISION-01 — warranted revision preserves the prior disposition

A warranted revision differs from Escape by declaring the break explicitly. It must:

1. retain the original tested claim;
2. record the counterpressure or failed condition;
3. assign the prior claim its warranted disposition;
4. formulate the new finer claim separately;
5. declare the new comparison basis and Source Scope;
6. test the new claim without inheriting authority from the old one.

For R-25 Attempt D, a warranted revision could state:

```yaml
warranted_revision:
  original_claim: formal closure ended reconsideration
  counterpressure: documented reconsideration activity occurred after formal closure
  original_claim_disposition: rejected
  new_finer_claim: formal closure altered the route, cost, and authority conditions of later reconsideration activity
  inherited_authority: prohibited
  new_test_required: true
```

The new claim may later be supported, reduced, rejected, or left underdetermined. Its success would not retroactively make the original claim true.

#### C25-FOURTEST-01 — one source object receives all four attempts

The R-25 anchor applies the four result concepts to one bounded source object so that classification differences cannot be attributed merely to different domains or object types:

| Attempt | Finer-resolution move | Required local reading |
|---|---|---|
| A | access restriction distinguished from formal authority concentration | Gain candidate because the turning-point claim changes |
| B | minute-level timestamps added within an already bounded phase | Neutral candidate because no tested praxis dimension changes |
| C | hundreds of record actions converted into nodes without adequate relation support | Drift candidate because complexity outruns discrimination and source coherence |
| D | post-closure micro-interactions used to preserve the defeated claim that closure ended reconsideration | Escape candidate and Mandatory Stop pressure |

The four attempts demonstrate that the same quantity of added detail could receive different classifications depending on what it changes, what supports it, and how it treats prior counterpressure.

```text
resolution result
≠ detail count
≠ graph size
≠ analytical effort
```

Attempt D must not proceed as confirmation of the original claim. Attempt C may also require Stop if relation support and source coherence cannot be restored. The complete Stop, Failure, Non-Capture, and re-entry decisions remain deferred to WP3; WP1 establishes only the result-specific pressure and the anti-immunization boundary.

#### WP1 completion boundary

WP1 establishes:

- bounded comparison entry on the same tested claim;
- Resolution Gain through changed warranted reconstruction;
- Resolution Neutrality as supported no-change rather than failure;
- Resolution Drift as complexity outrunning discrimination, relation support, or source coherence;
- Resolution Escape as unresolved counterpressure displaced by coordinate change;
- explicit claim-effect statements;
- warranted revision with preserved prior-claim disposition;
- the four-attempt R-25 anchor and mandatory Stop pressure for Escape.

WP1 does not establish:

- the full Detail-without-Purchase test;
- Components without Coarser Function;
- Source Overreach;
- Calibration Loss and open-threshold discipline;
- Decomposition Fatigue;
- the complete six-family local classification;
- final four-axis result mapping;
- complete Failure, Mandatory Stop, Non-Capture, or re-entry rules;
- the nineteen-field Resolution Assessment Record View;
- the Chapter-26 handoff;
- the provisional Chapter-25 or SUB lock.

```text
WP1 complete
≠ Chapter 25 complete
≠ every added detail classified automatically
≠ neutral result means failure
≠ graph complexity means gain
≠ finer claim repairs prior claim
≠ final Output Class selected
≠ Chapter 25 provisionally locked
≠ SUB provisionally locked
```

<a id="25-5-detail-without-purchase"></a>

### 25.5 Detail without Purchase

A finer reconstruction may be correct, internally consistent, and richly documented while still failing to carry additional praxeological purchase for the tested claim. Chapter 25 therefore separates the validity of a distinction from the work that distinction performs in reconstruction.

A distinction has purchase only where it changes or protects at least one warranted element of the tested praxis account. Relevant elements include:

- source identity or boundary;
- component or relation structure;
- temporal order, overlap, threshold, or completion;
- role, access, cost, exposure, asymmetry, or binding;
- historically available alternatives;
- Event, Non-Event, Path, or Trajectory classification;
- source-function preservation or revision;
- Loss, uncertainty, Stop, or Non-Capture conditions;
- the disposition of the tested claim.

The list is not a score and does not require that every finer distinction affect every dimension. One supported distinction may have decisive purchase for one bounded claim and none for another.

#### C25-PURCHASE-01 — detail without purchase remains below the Relevance Floor

A finer distinction remains below the Praxeological Relevance Floor for the tested claim where removing it would leave the warranted reconstruction, claim disposition, source-function status, Loss, and Stop conditions unchanged.

A bounded counterfactual test is:

```text
remove distinction d from the finer reconstruction
→ no warranted statement changes
→ no relevant uncertainty or Loss changes
→ no source or relation burden changes
→ d carries no additional purchase for tested claim Q
```

This does not mean that `d` is false, trivial in every context, or permanently irrelevant. It means that the present transformation cannot use `d` to justify a stronger, different, or more complete result.

R-25 Attempt B provides the local anchor. Minute-level timestamps are added to messages already known to fall within the same review phase. The times may be accurately sourced. Yet they do not alter transition order, access, authority, expectation, cost, alternative availability, source function, or Loss. The detail is valid but carries no additional purchase for the tested turning-point claim.

```text
valid distinction
≠ praxeologically purchasing distinction automatically

no purchase for Q
≠ no possible purchase for Q2
```

The appropriate local pressure is Resolution Neutrality where the comparison and support conditions are satisfied. If the added distinctions are unsupported, the result is not neutrality. If the source question changes, the analysis must declare a new claim rather than retroactively assigning purchase to the old comparison.

#### C25-SUPPORT-01 — unsupported refinement remains distinct from neutrality

Resolution Neutrality presupposes a valid finer reconstruction. Unsupported refinement fails before the no-change result can be established.

The distinction is:

```text
supported detail + valid comparison + no changed warranted claim
→ Resolution Neutrality candidate

unsupported detail or relation
→ unsupported refinement pressure
→ not Resolution Neutrality
```

An unsupported refinement may still reveal a useful question, candidate relation, or source request. It may be retained as an explicitly provisional hypothesis. It may not enter the warranted reconstruction as though its failure to change the claim demonstrated neutrality.

The support test separates at least three burdens:

1. **component support** — is the finer component or occurrence evidenced?
2. **relation support** — is the asserted sequence, dependence, substitution, threshold, or constitutive relation evidenced?
3. **claim support** — does the supported component and relation justify the stated effect on the tested claim?

Passing one burden does not pass the others. A record may establish that two activities occurred without establishing their order, dependence, or role in a transition. A graph may faithfully contain both nodes while the edge between them remains hypothetical.

WP3 owns the final routing of unsupported refinement through claim reduction, Failure, Stop, provisional handling, or Non-Capture. WP2 establishes only that unsupported structure cannot be misclassified as supported no-change.

<a id="25-6-components-without-coarser-function"></a>

### 25.6 Components without Coarser Function

A successful `DECOMPOSE` result must keep the finer components anchored to the coarser reference object and the source function under test. The operation does not succeed merely because plausible or source-supported parts have been identified.

The anchoring question is:

> How do these finer components and relations reconstruct, revise, limit, or defeat the coarser object's tested function?

#### C25-COARSE-01 — components require reconstructive anchoring to the coarser function

Components without a reconstructible relation to the coarser function produce a parts inventory, not a completed resolution assessment. The relation may show that the coarser function is:

- preserved across the components;
- distributed among them;
- carried by only some components;
- substituted through changing local carriers;
- interrupted, delayed, or conditionally realized;
- only partially preserved;
- rejected by the finer reconstruction;
- underdetermined because the linking relations remain unsupported.

For R-25 Attempt C, hundreds of record actions can be represented as nodes. The graph remains analytically incomplete if it cannot show which nodes and relations carry the review-governance Trajectory, which are incidental records, and how the alleged turning point connects source and target configurations.

```text
components present
≠ coarser function reconstructed

coarser function not reconstructed
≠ components false automatically
```

The analyst must resist two opposite errors. The first is **coarse immunization**, where every component is forced to serve the inherited coarse claim. The second is **component autonomy**, where the finer inventory is treated as successful despite losing the source object and tested function. Source preservation permits revision; it does not require confirmation.

A valid finer result may therefore conclude that the coarser function was wrongly attributed. Such a rejection can carry Resolution Gain because the finer reconstruction has clarified why the coarse account fails. What remains prohibited is abandoning the coarse comparison while continuing to claim that `DECOMPOSE` has succeeded on that source object.

#### C25-RELATION-01 — detail or graph density cannot substitute for supported relations

Relations are claim-bearing structures. They cannot be inferred from visual proximity, temporal adjacency, common labels, shared storage, or graph topology alone.

At minimum, a claimed relation must declare:

- the relata;
- the relation type or bounded descriptive form;
- the source basis;
- the Frame and temporal scope;
- whether the relation is observed, reconstructed, inferred, or unresolved;
- the claim consequence if the relation is retained or removed;
- rival relations or counterpressure where relevant.

```yaml
relation_support_check:
  relata:
    - access restriction
    - authority concentration
  proposed_relation: earlier access restriction constrained the later authority transition
  component_support: supported
  relation_support: partial
  observed_order: supported
  constitutive_dependence: unresolved
  claim_consequence: turning-point function may be distributed, but causal sufficiency is not established
```

A three-dimensional viewer may display multiple relation candidates, uncertainties, Loss states, and rival maps simultaneously. That capacity can materially improve exploration and auditability. It does not convert candidate edges into supported relations. Spatial distance, clustering, centrality, animation, user selection, or visual salience remain display operations unless separately connected to warranted source claims.

```text
visual relation
≠ historical relation automatically

navigable edge
≠ supported edge automatically
```

The Reader or a graph application may expose the support state of each relation and permit bounded analytic interventions. Any intervention that changes source selection, relation typing, periodization, or claim structure creates a new testable transformation state; it does not silently edit the authority of the prior record.

<a id="25-7-source-overreach"></a>

### 25.7 Source Overreach

Source Overreach occurs where the precision, completeness, or semantic load of the finer reconstruction exceeds what the available sources can warrant. It is an upper-bound failure: the analysis presents more determinate structure than its evidence can traceably carry.

A source limitation is not itself Source Overreach. The analyst may responsibly declare that order, motive, dependency, or boundary remains unknown. Overreach begins when that limitation is crossed, hidden, or converted into apparently established structure.

#### C25-OVERREACH-01 — semantic precision must not exceed source precision

The controlling relation is:

```text
semantic and structural precision
≤ source-supported precision
```

Typical forms of Source Overreach include:

- deriving exact phases from records that support only a broad interval;
- asserting dependence where sources establish only coexistence or order;
- treating database absence as a Non-Event without an expectation basis;
- turning model-generated candidate relations into observed relations;
- assigning intention, strategy, responsibility, blame, or person properties from structural delay;
- reconstructing an irrecoverable branch as though its availability were known;
- converting uncertainty into a precise score, category, or timeline;
- using later completeness to erase inherited Loss;
- treating visual coherence as historical coherence.

A finer model may legitimately be more explicit than any single source because reconstruction can integrate multiple sources. The limit concerns the combined support relation, not literal copying. Integration must still identify which sources support which component and relation, how conflicts were handled, and what remains excluded, uncertain, or irrecoverable.

For R-25 Attempt C, the graph may include a candidate edge between access restriction and authority concentration. If the archive supports order but not dependence, the graph must preserve that distinction. Rendering the edge as a confirmed constitutive relation would exceed the Source Ceiling.

```text
source supports A before B
≠ source supports A caused or constituted B
```

Source Overreach can coexist with genuine local insight. Supported components remain preserved. The overreaching claim must be reduced, separated, or stopped; it may not borrow authority from the supported remainder.

#### C25-NOCOMP-01 — formal completeness cannot compensate for absent purchase or support

No combination of formal virtues compensates for missing praxeological or evidential conditions. The following cannot substitute for purchase, relation support, source coherence, or calibration:

- schema completeness;
- syntactic validity;
- exhaustive fields;
- numerical precision;
- visual elegance;
- graph connectivity;
- model consistency;
- repeated agreement among derived artifacts;
- computational expense;
- analyst effort.

```text
machine-readable completeness
≠ empirical completeness

formal consistency
≠ semantic warrant

more structure
≠ more authority
```

This non-compensation rule is especially important for executable or parallel kernels. A monitoring kernel may reliably detect repeated states, resource growth, unchanged claim effects, unsupported relation promotion, threshold mutation, or escape patterns. Those detections can trigger bounded Stop pressure or a request for human/source review. The kernel cannot convert the absence of substantive support into support merely because every required field is populated.

The formal model may verify that source-overreach status, support status, unresolved structure, and reasons are declared. It may not decide semantic truth, causal sufficiency, normative legitimacy, or person responsibility.

<a id="25-8-calibration-loss"></a>

### 25.8 Calibration Loss

Resolution comparison requires stable enough conditions to distinguish a supported revision from a moving target. Calibration Loss occurs where the thresholds, comparison coordinates, or revision conditions change in response to results so that the preferred claim can no longer be meaningfully weakened.

Calibration is not fixed certainty. A threshold may be provisional, source-sensitive, and revisable. What must remain visible is why it was chosen, what evidence could change it, and whether it changed before or after the relevant counterpressure appeared.

#### C25-CALIBRATE-01 — rival reconstructions require stable comparison and revision conditions

Calibration Loss pressure arises where:

- the tested claim changes after each counterexample without recording the prior disposition;
- the required purchase threshold is raised or lowered only to preserve the preferred result;
- rival finer maps are judged under different criteria without justification;
- every anomaly receives a new exception;
- no possible source finding could reduce or reject the claim;
- the comparison basis becomes too fluid to distinguish Gain, Neutrality, Drift, or Escape;
- the analyst recursively adds detail to stabilize unsupported prior detail;
- Stop and re-entry conditions are altered after they are met.

For R-25 Attempt A, the initial change condition asks whether access restriction and authority concentration carried distinct historically operative functions. If the evidence shows distinct timing and effects, the analyst may not redefine “single turning point” to mean merely “one retrospectively important theme” and then declare the original claim confirmed. That would change the calibration after counterpressure.

A controlled revision instead records:

```yaml
calibration_revision:
  prior_test: one transition carried both access and binding functions
  counterpressure: access narrowed before formal authority concentration
  prior_claim_disposition: reduced
  revised_test: whether the two transitions formed one bounded transition cluster
  reason_for_revision: finer order and role evidence
  inherited_authority: prohibited
```

The new test is legitimate because the old result remains visible.

#### C25-THRESHOLD-01 — an open threshold remains distinct from Calibration Loss

An open or provisional threshold is admissible where uncertainty is genuine and explicitly controlled. It must declare:

- the tested dimension;
- the current threshold or comparison condition;
- the source basis;
- the uncertainty range;
- rival thresholds or maps;
- what evidence would move the threshold;
- whether movement would create a new claim;
- the Stop condition if discrimination cannot be achieved.

```text
threshold provisional and revisable under declared conditions
≠ Calibration Loss

threshold changed only after adverse result, without prior disposition
→ Calibration Loss pressure
```

The distinction protects iterative inquiry. STRATA does not require false precision before analysis can begin. It requires that iteration remain traceable and counterfactually sensitive.

A deep or parallel monitoring kernel could operationalize the declaration layer by tracking:

- which threshold version governed each run;
- whether a result changed the threshold;
- whether the prior claim disposition was preserved;
- whether repeated iterations altered any warranted praxis dimension;
- whether relation support increased or merely the graph expanded;
- whether a declared Stop condition was crossed;
- what materially new basis would permit re-entry.

Such a kernel would support the treatment of practical halting conditions for bounded PMS processes. It would not decide the universal halting problem, prove empirical truth, or authorize semantic and normative conclusions. Its output would remain a traceable process record and a bounded Stop recommendation or enforcement under declared invariants.

#### WP2 completion boundary

WP2 establishes:

- Detail without Purchase relative to the tested claim;
- the bounded removal test at the Praxeological Relevance Floor;
- unsupported refinement as distinct from Resolution Neutrality;
- separate component, relation, and claim support burdens;
- reconstructive anchoring of components to the coarser source function;
- relation support that cannot be replaced by detail or graph density;
- Source Overreach where semantic precision exceeds source precision;
- the non-compensation of absent purchase, support, coherence, or calibration by formal completeness;
- Calibration Loss through unstable comparison or revision conditions;
- open and provisional thresholds under explicit revision discipline;
- bounded monitoring-kernel support without automatic semantic or universal halting decisions.

WP2 does not establish:

- Decomposition Fatigue;
- the complete six-family resolution classification;
- the final four-axis mapping;
- complete unsupported-refinement routing;
- final Failure, Mandatory Stop, Non-Capture, or re-entry rules;
- the nineteen-field Resolution Assessment Record View;
- the Chapter-26 handoff;
- the provisional Chapter-25 or SUB lock.

```text
WP2 complete
≠ Chapter 25 complete
≠ supported component means supported relation
≠ graph completeness means coarser function preserved
≠ open threshold means Calibration Loss
≠ monitoring kernel solves universal halting
≠ final Stop or Output Class selected
≠ Chapter 25 provisionally locked
≠ SUB provisionally locked
```

<a id="25-9-decomposition-fatigue"></a>

### 25.9 Decomposition Fatigue

A source object can remain decomposable in principle after the current claim has ceased to benefit from further opening. Chapter 25 calls this condition **Decomposition Fatigue**. The term names a methodological relation between a bounded question, the available sources, and the performance of repeated finer reconstruction. It does not describe the analyst, the software, or the source object as psychologically or intrinsically exhausted.

Decomposition Fatigue becomes relevant only after a valid comparison has already been made. It cannot be invoked merely because a reconstruction is difficult, expensive, slow, or technically complex. At minimum, the analyst must be able to show that continued differentiation has repeatedly failed to change any warranted praxis dimension, improve relation support, repair source coherence, answer counterpressure, or reduce a material uncertainty carried by the tested claim.

#### C25-FATIGUE-01 — Decomposition Fatigue is a methodological Stop marker, not analyst psychology

A bounded fatigue declaration must state:

- the tested claim and source object;
- the last resolution-bearing distinction;
- the subsequent distinctions tested;
- whether each later distinction produced Gain, valid Neutrality, Drift, Escape, unsupported refinement, or Non-Capture pressure;
- the source and relation support still available;
- the reason further opening is unnecessary or inadmissible for the present question;
- the Stop status;
- the condition under which re-entry would become warranted.

```yaml
decomposition_fatigue_example:
  source_object: review-governance Trajectory R-24
  tested_claim: formal closure ended all practically available reconsideration
  last_resolution_bearing_distinction: formal closure separated from later bounded reconsideration route
  subsequent_attempts:
    - minute-level ordering within an already established phase
    - duplicate record-channel separation
    - additional graph-node subdivision without new relation support
  observed_result:
    - resolution_neutral
    - resolution_neutral
    - resolution_drift
  analyst_state: not_assessed
  source_object_intrinsically_exhausted: not_claimed
  stop_pressure: optional_stop_then_mandatory_if_drift_continues
  reentry_condition: new source, repaired relation map, revised bounded claim, or new counterexample
```

A declaration of fatigue does not mean that no future question could profit from finer resolution. The same source object may later support another bounded decomposition under a different claim, Frame, source basis, or comparison condition. What has ended is the warrant for continuing the current line of opening under the current burden.

```text
further distinction possible
≠ further distinction warranted for this claim

Decomposition Fatigue
≠ analyst fatigue
≠ source simplicity
≠ permanent undecomposability
≠ canonical Output Class
```

Fatigue may support Optional Stop after a completed neutral result. It becomes part of Mandatory Stop pressure when continuation merely repeats no-gain findings, crosses source support, loses the coarser function, increases Calibration Loss, or becomes Resolution Drift or Resolution Escape. The marker therefore helps distinguish disciplined termination from arbitrary abandonment.

<a id="25-10-resolution-classification"></a>

### 25.10 Resolution Classification

Chapter 25 uses six local resolution families:

```text
gain
neutral
drift
escape
unsupported
non_capture
```

They classify the performance of a bounded coarse/finer comparison. They do not form a second list of canonical Output Classes, and they do not replace the four result axes already established for `DECOMPOSE`.

#### C25-CLASSIFY-01 — the six local resolution families remain distinct and map through existing formal fields

The six families are used as follows:

- **gain** — a supported finer reconstruction changes at least one warranted claim;
- **neutral** — a supported comparison leaves the tested claim unchanged;
- **drift** — added complexity outruns discrimination, relation support, or source coherence;
- **escape** — coordinate change displaces an unresolved burden on the prior claim;
- **unsupported** — the proposed finer distinction or relation lacks sufficient source support for the asserted reconstruction;
