- **non_capture** — the question is legitimate, but the available evidence and comparison conditions do not responsibly discriminate among the remaining alternatives.

Only the first four map directly to the existing `resolution_test_result` values for `DECOMPOSE`. Unsupported refinement is represented through existing support status, claim disposition, Failure or Stop routing, and bounded reasons. Non-Capture is represented through the existing capture statement and the canonical `non_capture` Output Class where that class is selected.

```text
unsupported
≠ neutral

non_capture
≠ compromise between gain and drift

local resolution family
≠ canonical Output Class
```

#### C25-AXES-01 — local result, source-function effect, prior claim disposition, and canonical Output Class remain separate

Every completed assessment keeps four axes visible:

```text
1. local resolution result
2. source-function effect
3. prior source-claim disposition
4. canonical Output Class
```

A valid Gain may produce `internally_differentiated` while reducing the prior claim and routing to `admissible_with_bounded_claim`. A valid neutral result may preserve the source function and route to `resolution_neutral`. Unsupported refinement may leave the source function underdetermined, require claim reduction, and route to `failed_transformation`, `claim_reduction_required`, `mandatory_stop`, or `admissible_but_provisional` depending on the remaining supported structure. No axis mechanically determines the others.

```yaml
resolution_axis_examples:
  gain_case:
    local_resolution_result: resolution_gain
    source_function_effect: internally_differentiated
    prior_claim_disposition: reduced
    canonical_output_class: admissible_with_bounded_claim
  neutral_case:
    local_resolution_result: resolution_neutral
    source_function_effect: confirmed
    prior_claim_disposition: preserved
    canonical_output_class: resolution_neutral
  unsupported_case:
    local_resolution_result: unsupported
    source_function_effect: underdetermined
    prior_claim_disposition: reduced
    canonical_output_class: claim_reduction_required
  non_capture_case:
    local_resolution_result: non_capture
    source_function_effect: underdetermined
    prior_claim_disposition: unresolved
    canonical_output_class: non_capture
```

These examples are mappings, not automatic routing rules. The canonical class remains claim-specific and must satisfy the Admissibility Band, Type Integrity, continuity, Source Ceiling, Loss, Stop, and Non-Capture requirements.

#### C25-UNSUPPORTED-01 — unsupported refinement receives bounded Failure, reduction, Stop, or provisional handling rather than neutrality

Unsupported refinement occurs where a proposed finer distinction may be plausible but cannot be carried at the asserted precision by the available sources. Its treatment depends on what remains supported:

- if the unsupported detail is removable and the coarser claim remains independently warranted, remove or bracket it;
- if the unsupported detail was necessary for the stronger claim, reduce that claim;
- if the finer reconstruction has lost the source object or depends on fabricated relations, route to Failure;
- if continuation would require further unsupported distinctions, apply Mandatory Stop;
- if a materially new source or bounded question could repair the deficit, retain a provisional re-entry condition.

Unsupported refinement is not classified as Neutrality because the required supported comparison has not been completed. It is not automatically `non_capture` because the problem may be a specific support failure rather than an irresolvable classification question.

#### C25-NC-01 — Non-Capture remains available without rescuing the coarse or finer claim

Non-Capture is appropriate where the resolution question remains legitimate but the evidence cannot responsibly distinguish among supported rival maps or result families. Examples include:

- two finer reconstructions with equal source support and materially different claim effects;
- incomplete relation evidence that prevents deciding between valid Neutrality and bounded Gain;
- incompatible but individually defensible Frames without a warranted translation;
- unresolved source identity after finer opening;
- calibration conditions too weak to discriminate without false precision.

```text
non_capture
≠ coarse claim confirmed
≠ finer claim confirmed
≠ neutrality by default
≠ averaged classification
```

The result preserves the unresolved structure and the Loss that prevents capture. It does not authorize the analyst to retain whichever claim is more useful, intuitive, or visually coherent.

<a id="25-11-stop-reentry-and-completion"></a>

### 25.11 Mandatory Stop, Re-entry, and Completion

Stop in Chapter 25 is not an external interruption appended after analysis. It is an internally warranted result of the same resolution assessment. The question is not whether every possible continuation will terminate. It is whether continuation of this bounded transformation remains admissible under its declared source, claim, comparison, and invariant conditions.

#### C25-STOP-01 — Mandatory Stop follows when continuation lacks purchase or support, loses the source object, or shifts the unresolved burden

Mandatory Stop applies before further decomposition where continuation would require one or more of the following:

- adding distinctions below the Relevance Floor after a completed no-gain finding;
- asserting components or relations beyond source support;
- preserving a parts map after the source object or coarser function has been lost;
- filling source gaps with model defaults or visual interpolation;
- moving thresholds after adverse findings to preserve a preferred claim;
- changing granularity, Frame, level, composition, or target function without preserving the prior claim disposition;
- recursively supporting one unsupported detail with another unsupported detail;
- continuing after Decomposition Fatigue has become Drift or Escape;
- crossing the Source or Traceability Ceiling;
- deriving person, motive, legitimacy, prediction, sanction, or authority claims.

A practical PMS runtime may monitor declared signals for these conditions and enforce a bounded Stop under explicit invariants. Such enforcement treats the current process; it does not decide universal halting, empirical truth, or semantic validity.

Stop preserves what has already been supported. A valid neutral finding remains valid after Stop. A reduced claim remains reduced. A failed claim remains failed. No later transformation may erase these dispositions.

#### C25-REENTRY-01 — re-entry requires a new source, claim, Frame, comparison basis, or bounded question

Re-entry is admissible only when a materially new basis changes the testable situation. Qualifying bases include:

- newly available sources;
- a repaired relation map;
- a revised and explicitly bounded claim;
- a different declared Frame;
- a new counterexample;
- a different source object;
- a new comparison basis;
- a previously unavailable calibration threshold;
- a changed runtime invariant relevant to the bounded process.

Re-entry requires a new Transformation Record or a separately segmented claim within an authorized chain. It must cite the earlier Stop, preserve the earlier claim disposition, declare the new basis, and repeat the relevant source, Loss, and admissibility tests.

```text
re-entry
≠ resume as though Stop never occurred

new basis
→ new testable claim
→ new record or explicit claim segment
```

A mere desire for more detail, a larger graph, additional compute, or a differently rendered interface is not a re-entry basis unless it changes source access, relation support, calibration, or the bounded question.

#### Resolution Assessment Record View

The Chapter-25 operation-specific view contains exactly nineteen fields:

```yaml
resolution_assessment:
  source_transformation_record:
  tested_claim:
  coarse_reconstruction:
  finer_reconstruction:
  comparison_basis:
  source_support:
  relation_support:
  changed_praxis_dimensions:
  claim_effect:
  coarser_function_status:
  source_overreach_status:
  calibration_status:
  decomposition_fatigue_status:
  resolution_result:
  stop_status:
  reentry_condition:
  unresolved_structure:
  loss:
  admissibility_result:
```

The view maps into the Shared Transformation Record plus controlled `extensions`. It creates no parallel schema, automatic classifier, complexity score, purchase ratio, fatigue score, or universal stopping oracle. The Formal Model may require that the fields are present, values are drawn from controlled inventories where applicable, mappings are complete, and reasons are recorded. It may not decide substantive PraxisPurchase, source adequacy, semantic relation support, calibration quality, or the correct Stop and re-entry judgment.

#### C25-HANDOFF-01 — Chapter 26 receives only unresolved operation-boundary questions, not a mixed operation

The controlled continuation is:

```text
Chapter 25 provisional lock
→ Chapter 26 Preparation Gate
→ Chapter 26 — The Boundary between SUB and RETYPE
```

Chapter 25 hands Chapter 26 only questions that remain after the resolution assessment, especially whether an attempted finer analysis has ceased to reconstruct the internal constitution of the source object and has begun assigning a bounded contextual target function.

The handoff may include:

- the source Transformation Record;
- the tested coarse and finer claims;
- the resolution result;
- the preserved prior claim disposition;
- the coarser-function status;
- unresolved Frame or target-context changes;
- Stop and re-entry status;
- Loss and Non-Capture conditions.

Chapter 26 receives no implicit mixture of `DECOMPOSE` and `PROJECT_AS`. Where both are required, they must remain separate operation occurrences with separate claims, records, Loss declarations, and admissibility results.

```text
Chapter 25
→ what did finer resolution accomplish?

Chapter 26
→ did the analysis remain SUB,
  or did it cross into RETYPE / PROJECT_AS?
```

Chapter 27 retains complete SUB Limits. Chapter 28 retains integrated cases, Local Audit, and Part output mapping. RETYPE retains contextual target functions. LIMITS retains system-wide Source Ceiling, Anti-Immunization, Stop, Non-Capture, and authority constraints.

<a id="chapter-25-completion-boundary"></a>

#### Chapter 25 completion boundary

The integrated WP4 audit confirms the Chapter-25 Contract, all twenty-four Preparation-Audit controls, all twenty-four Pressure Duties, coarse/finer comparison integrity, Gain/Neutrality/Drift/Escape separation, purchase and coarser-function tests, component/relation/claim support separation, Source Overreach and Calibration Loss boundaries, anti-immunization, Decomposition Fatigue, six-family classification, four result axes, Failure, Mandatory Stop, Non-Capture, re-entry, the nineteen-field Record view, Chapter-26 ownership, and package consistency. The Chapter-25 result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

Chapter 25 is therefore **provisionally locked**. The class names the chapter-level methodological result under the still-open SUB Part and later corpus-wide audits. It does not decide universal halting, guarantee termination of an arbitrary process, automatically classify semantic purchase or source adequacy, turn correct detail into Gain, treat Neutrality as universal sufficiency, convert graph or formal complexity into warrant, erase a failed claim through finer resolution, automate Stop or re-entry, complete the SUB/RETYPE boundary, or increase claim, application, governance, or person-level authority.

All twenty-four Chapter-25 Pressure Duties occur exactly once in canonical prose. No standalone Case, Transformation Record, Smoke Record, appendix expansion, schema artifact, new operation, Rule, Output Class, audit stage, resolution enum, purchase/calibration/fatigue score, universal-halting decider, runtime kernel, graph application, Reader implementation, person type, or authority mechanism has been produced.

```text
Chapter 25 provisionally locked
≠ universal halting decided
≠ every resolution question captured
≠ supported detail means Gain
≠ Neutrality means universal sufficiency
≠ Stop erases supported findings or prior failure
≠ re-entry resumes as though Stop never occurred
≠ Chapter 26 completed
≠ SUB provisionally locked
```

Reopening requires a concrete conflict involving PMS Base, Structure or Contract ownership, comparison identity, claim effect, source or relation support, coarser-function continuity, Source Overreach, calibration, anti-immunization, Decomposition Fatigue, local-family classification, result-axis separation, Failure/Stop/Non-Capture, re-entry, a Pressure Duty, a Reference or Formal-Model binding, or a later integrated dependency. Preference for more detail, denser graphs, additional compute, a different rendering, stronger automation, a universal stopping claim, or premature SUB/RETYPE/RETYPE conclusions is insufficient.

<a id="chapter-26-the-boundary-between-sub-and-retype"></a>

## 26. The Boundary between SUB and RETYPE

Chapter 26 controls the point at which an analysis stops merely opening the internal constitution of a source object and begins asserting that the same origin-typed object performs a bounded function in another declared context. Chapters 18–25 establish source entry, granularity relations, `DECOMPOSE`, occurrence-, composite-, temporal-, and Path-specific opening, resolution assessment, bounded Stop, and re-entry. Chapter 26 does not repeat those procedures and does not pre-write RETYPE. It classifies the operation claim that the analysis is actually making.

The governing question is:

> Is the analysis reconstructing what internally constitutes, reproduces, maintains, interrupts, or destabilizes the source object, or is it preserving that object while assigning a bounded contextual target function?

The boundary is difficult because both analyses may use the same source materials, introduce new distinctions, change the descriptive vocabulary, and place the object under a different Frame. Their linguistic surfaces may therefore look similar. The operation is determined instead by the relation among source object, analytical question, granularity, context, explanatory target, and claimed function.

Sections 26.1–26.4 establish the bounded operation entry, the internal-constitution question, the contextual-target-function question, origin-type preservation, source-function/target-function separation, and the primary distinction between `DECOMPOSE` and `PROJECT_AS`. Sections 26.5–26.9 test the same Trajectory and Attractor-typed occurrence under separate operation claims, require bounded target-context and target-function support, guard subtle and reverse misclassification, preserve changed-Frame discipline, and keep recontextualization available where neither transformation claim is present. Sections 26.10–26.12 complete the claim-segment-specific decision test, dual-operation chains without a fourth operation, separate claims/Records/Loss/results, explicit chain order, invalid-collapse routing, four result axes, Failure, Mandatory Stop, Non-Capture, the nineteen-field Operation-Boundary Assessment View, and the Chapter-27 handoff.

The integrated Chapter-26 result is therefore a complete **SUB/RETYPE operation-boundary procedure**. It classifies what transformation claim an analysis actually makes without inferring operation identity from vocabulary, heading, interface role, Graph geometry, usefulness, or changed perspective; it permits recontextualization as a positive non-operation result; and it requires separate operation occurrences whenever both `DECOMPOSE` and `PROJECT_AS` are needed. It does not execute a complete RETYPE family analysis, replace origin type with target function, merge two operations or Records, repair a failed source claim through contextual usefulness, automate semantic classification, pre-empt Chapter 27, or increase claim, application, governance, or person-level authority.

The initial Chapter-26 architecture is:

```text
source object with declared origin type
+
source function or explanatory target
+
analysis question, Frame, granularity relation, and proposed context
+
internal-constitution test
+
target-function test
→
bounded operation candidate
```

No new operation is introduced. The only relevant operation candidates remain `DECOMPOSE` and `PROJECT_AS`, with recontextualization still possible where neither transformation claim has yet been made.

<a id="26-1-why-the-boundary-is-difficult"></a>

### 26.1 Why the Boundary Is Difficult

SUB and RETYPE can both make a familiar object appear differently structured. A decomposition may introduce a new heading for an internal component map. A projection may rely on detailed source features to justify a contextual target function. A later analyst may also change Frame, audience, or representational medium without performing either operation. Surface form therefore underdetermines operation identity.

#### C26-ENTRY-01 — the operation-boundary comparison requires a bounded common entry

Before the operation is classified, the analyst must declare at least:

- the source object;
- its origin type;
- the source function or explanatory target currently under test;
- the source Frame;
- the current and proposed granularity;
- the analysis question;
- any proposed target context;
- any proposed target function;
- whether the source object remains the unit of analysis;
- whether internal structure is being opened;
- whether the claim changes only description, changes granularity, or assigns a contextual function;
- unresolved uncertainty, Loss, and possible Stop pressure.

A bounded R-26 entry may use the review-governance Trajectory developed in Chapters 24–25:

```yaml
operation_boundary_entry:
  source_object: review-governance Trajectory R-24
  source_origin_type: Trajectory
  source_function_under_test: organizes the historical relation among objection admission, deferral, authority concentration, closure, and reconsideration
  source_frame: review-governance formation
  current_granularity: bounded Trajectory with transition clusters and subpaths
  proposed_questions:
    sub_question: which subpaths, transition clusters, Non-Events, and residual bindings constitute the Trajectory?
    retype_question: does the retained Trajectory perform a bounded calibration function in a declared audit-training context?
  proposed_target_context: audit-training context
  proposed_target_function: calibration case for distinguishing formal closure from finality
  classification_status: unresolved_before_boundary_test
```

The two questions concern the same source object but do not make the same claim. The SUB question treats the Trajectory as the explanatory target and asks how its internal structure is formed. The RETYPE question treats the retained Trajectory as an origin-typed unit and asks what bounded function it performs in another context.

The shared source does not merge the operations:

```text
same source object
≠ same transformation claim

same evidence base
≠ same operation occurrence

new description
≠ operation identity established
```

The boundary can also remain unresolved. If the analyst cannot yet state whether a phrase names internal source structure, a target-context function, or only a new descriptive perspective, operation classification must remain provisional rather than being inferred from wording.

<a id="26-2-sub-question"></a>

### 26.2 SUB Question

The SUB question is:

> What finer structures constitute, reproduce, maintain, interrupt, destabilize, or revise this source object?

The question keeps the source object as the explanatory target. It changes the distinction set through which that object is reconstructed. The object may later be confirmed, internally differentiated, reduced, rejected, or left underdetermined, but the analysis remains directed toward its internal constitution.

#### C26-INTERNAL-01 — SUB opens internal constitution without decomposing the operator type

A candidate remains within `DECOMPOSE` where it reconstructs source-supported internal structures such as:

- components and component roles;
- internal relations and dependencies;
- phases, sub-events, Non-Events, delays, and thresholds;
- transition clusters, subpaths, branches, or residual structures;
- internal Frame changes;
- differentiated reproduction, repair, substitution, or destabilization work;
- inherited and new Loss;
- finer evidence that confirms, revises, or rejects the source function.

The source object remains the object being explained. Where the source is an operator-typed occurrence, the occurrence may be opened; the PMS operator type itself is never decomposed. Where the source is a composite, Event, Non-Event, Path, or Trajectory, the finer map must remain traceable to that same bounded reference object or explicitly record the failure of source continuity.

For R-26, a SUB claim may ask how three source-related courses jointly carry the Trajectory:

```text
access and objection-admission subpath
+
decision-authority and repeated-non-decision subpath
+
closure and reconsideration subpath
→
internal constitution of the same review-governance Trajectory
```

This claim may expose different temporal rates, overlaps, interruptions, or residual bindings. None of those findings by itself assigns the Trajectory a function in an audit-training context.

#### C26-GRANULARITY-01 — changed granularity remains distinct from changed target context

`DECOMPOSE` primarily changes granularity or the internal distinction set. A proposed lower granularity must specify what becomes distinguishable that was compressed at the source level. The analysis may also use a different Frame to make those distinctions visible, but the operative claim still concerns the internal source structure.

```text
finer internal map
+
source object remains explanatory target
→ DECOMPOSE pressure

new audience or display context
+
no contextual function claim
→ operation not established
```

A change of granularity can occur without a target-context change. Conversely, a target-context change can occur without any finer decomposition. If both change, the case carries dual-operation pressure and must later be segmented rather than collapsed.

#### C26-SOURCEFUNC-01 — source function remains the explanation target within SUB

The source function is the bounded function, role, or formation claim that the decomposition is trying to reconstruct internally. It is not the later target function of `PROJECT_AS`.

For the R-26 Trajectory, the source-function question may be whether the historical relation among admission, deferral, authority concentration, closure, and reconsideration forms one bounded trajectory. The decomposition may show that this source function is:

- confirmed;
- refined;
- internally differentiated;
- partially preserved;
- rejected;
- or underdetermined.

These are source-function effects. They remain internal to the `DECOMPOSE` claim.

```text
source function as explanation target
≠ target function as contextual claim
```

A decomposition that rejects the original source function has not thereby become RETYPE. It may simply be a failed or claim-reducing `DECOMPOSE` occurrence. Operation identity depends on the claim being attempted, not on whether the source object survives unchanged.

<a id="26-3-retype-question"></a>

### 26.3 RETYPE Question

The RETYPE question is:

> What bounded function does this origin-typed source object perform within a declared target context?

The question retains the source object as a unit and treats its origin type as preserved. It then asserts a new contextual function whose warrant depends on source features and target-context conditions. This is `PROJECT_AS` pressure.

#### C26-TARGET-01 — a target-function claim requires a declared context and a source-traceable function

A bounded target-function candidate must declare:

- the origin-typed source object;
- the target context;
- the proposed target function;
- the source features on which the function depends;
- the contextual conditions under which the function holds;
- rival functions and counterpressure;
- excluded dimensions and Loss;
- the conditions under which the projection would fail or require Stop.

For R-26, the Trajectory may be proposed as a calibration case in an audit-training context because it preserves the difference among formal closure, practical continuation, residual binding, and later reconsideration. That proposition is not established merely because the Trajectory contains those features. The analysis must show how those origin-typed features perform the proposed function in the declared context.

```yaml
project_as_candidate:
  source_object: review-governance Trajectory R-24
  origin_type_preserved: Trajectory
  target_context: audit-training context
  proposed_target_function: calibration case for distinguishing formal closure from finality
  source_features_required:
    - formal closure
    - post-closure reconsideration activity
    - residual binding
    - preserved prior claim disposition
  target_function_status: proposed_not_yet_established
```

This bounded candidate does not pre-empt RETYPE. It identifies what must later be tested if `PROJECT_AS` is executed.

#### C26-ORIGINTYPE-01 — origin type remains preserved under PROJECT_AS pressure

`PROJECT_AS` does not convert the Trajectory into a new PMS primitive, operator type, or ontological kind. The source remains a Trajectory. “Calibration case” is a contextual target function, not its origin type.

```text
origin type: Trajectory
+
target function: calibration case
≠
new origin type: Calibration-Trajectory
```

The distinction is necessary because target functions are context-bound and defeasible. The same origin-typed object may support a different bounded function in another context, no function in a third context, or `non_capture` where contextual support is insufficient. None of these outcomes changes the PMS Base or authorizes authority inheritance.

The origin type may itself remain contested from an earlier `DECOMPOSE` result. A projection cannot repair that contest by simply using the source object. The target-function claim must preserve the prior source-claim disposition and state whether its use depends on a confirmed, reduced, or provisional origin-typed source claim.

#### C26-LABEL-01 — label, analogy, interface role, and visual position do not prove a target function

A new label may summarize internal components without creating a target function. An analogy may suggest a possible relation without establishing projection. An interface may assign a node a display role without making a substantive contextual claim.

For the planned Reader or 3D graph viewer:

```text
node grouped under “calibration”
≠ calibration function established

central visual position
≠ structural centrality established

interactive use by a reader
≠ target function warranted

spatial proximity
≠ source relation or projection proof
```

The interface can expose a proposed operation boundary, allow users to compare source and target claims, and show separate Records. It cannot perform the semantic work merely by rendering the object in another spatial or functional arrangement.

Likewise, a descriptive phrase such as “closure architecture,” “learning scaffold,” or “attractor-like pattern” does not determine whether the analysis is SUB, RETYPE, analogy, or recontextualization. The claim structure and declared context decide.

<a id="26-4-decomposition-versus-functional-projection"></a>

### 26.4 Decomposition versus Functional Projection

The primary distinction can now be stated directly:

```text
DECOMPOSE
→ opens internal source structure
→ changes granularity or internal distinctions
→ keeps the source object as explanatory target

PROJECT_AS
→ preserves the origin-typed source object
→ declares a bounded target context
→ asserts a contextual target function
```

#### C26-QUESTION-01 — classify the operation by the question and claim, not by vocabulary

A first bounded decision test asks:

1. Is the analysis opening internal structure?
2. Is the primary transformation a change of granularity or distinction set?
3. Does the source object remain the explanatory target?
4. Is the source function being confirmed, differentiated, reduced, rejected, or left underdetermined?

Where these conditions dominate, the candidate is `DECOMPOSE`.

A second bounded decision test asks:

1. Does the origin-typed source object remain a unit?
2. Is another target context declared?
3. Is a bounded function in that context asserted?
4. Are origin type and target function kept distinct?
5. Does the function depend on source-traceable features and contextual conditions?

Where these conditions dominate, the candidate is `PROJECT_AS`.

A compact comparison is:

| Test coordinate | DECOMPOSE pressure | PROJECT_AS pressure |
|---|---|---|
| explanatory target | internal constitution of the source object | contextual function of the retained source object |
| primary change | granularity or internal distinction set | functional relation to a declared target context |
| source object | opened into finer structures | retained as origin-typed unit |
| function under test | source function | target function |
| typical failure | fragments, unsupported relations, lost source coherence | unbounded context, label substitution, origin-type replacement, unsupported function |
| record consequence | one `DECOMPOSE` occurrence | one `PROJECT_AS` occurrence |

The table is a decision aid, not an automatic classifier. Ambiguous prose may need analyst judgment, separate claim segmentation, or `non_capture`. A case that genuinely contains both operations must later be represented as an ordered chain with separate occurrences rather than one mixed Record.

The WP1 boundary therefore preserves four possible readings:

```text
internal constitution claim
→ DECOMPOSE candidate

bounded contextual function claim
→ PROJECT_AS candidate

changed perspective without either claim
→ possible recontextualization

both claims present
→ dual-operation pressure; separation required
```

WP1 establishes the operation-entry and primary distinction. It does not yet decide subtle trajectory or Attractor cases, fully classify recontextualization, execute a dual-operation chain, map invalid collapse, select final result axes or Output Classes, complete Failure/Stop/Non-Capture, instantiate the nineteen-field boundary view, hand Chapter 27 its limit questions, or lock Chapter 26 or SUB.

```text
WP1 complete
≠ Chapter 26 complete
≠ target function established
≠ recontextualization fully classified
≠ dual operation may share one Record
≠ final Output Class selected
≠ Chapter 26 provisionally locked
≠ SUB provisionally locked
```

<a id="26-5-trajectory-decomposition-and-projection"></a>

### 26.5 Trajectory Decomposition and Projection

The same Trajectory may enter both operations without becoming the same operation occurrence. The decisive difference lies in what the analysis asks the Trajectory to support.

For the bounded review-governance Trajectory `R-26`, the two claims are:

```text
DECOMPOSE claim
→ Which subpaths, transition clusters, Non-Events,
  authority changes, and residual bindings constitute
  or destabilize this Trajectory?

PROJECT_AS claim
→ What bounded function does the retained Trajectory
  perform in a declared audit-training context?
```

The first claim opens the Trajectory as a source object. The second retains the Trajectory as an origin-typed unit and tests a contextual function. Their evidence may overlap, but their warrants, Loss accounts, and possible failures do not.

#### C26-TRAJECTORY-01 — one Trajectory may support separate operation occurrences without operation identity collapse

The trajectory comparison must produce two separately intelligible claim packets.

The `DECOMPOSE` packet may reconstruct:

- an access and objection-admission subpath;
- a decision-authority and repeated-non-decision subpath;
- a closure and reconsideration subpath;
- transition clusters connecting those courses;
- residual bindings after formal closure;
- inherited and current Loss;
- a source-function effect such as internal differentiation or reduction.

The `PROJECT_AS` packet retains the origin type `Trajectory` and proposes a bounded target function, for example:

```yaml
trajectory_projection_boundary_packet:
  source_object: R-26
  source_origin_type: Trajectory
  target_context: bounded audit-training exercise
  proposed_target_function: calibration case for distinguishing formal closure from practical finality
  required_source_features:
    - formal closure occurrence
    - later reconsideration activity
    - residual binding after closure
    - preserved prior claim disposition
  boundary_status: project_as_candidate
```

This packet identifies a candidate function and its burden. It does not establish the complete RETYPE theory or authorize the target function merely because the source features are present.

The outputs remain separate:

```text
DECOMPOSE output
→ finer source reconstruction and source-function effect

PROJECT_AS output
→ bounded target-function assessment in a declared context
```

A successful decomposition may provide source features needed by the projection. That dependency does not make the projection a component of the decomposition. Conversely, a useful projection cannot retroactively validate an unsupported subpath, transition relation, or Trajectory claim.

#### C26-CONTEXT-01 — a target context must be declared as a bounded functional environment

A target context is not merely a later date, a new audience, another screen, or a different vocabulary. It is the bounded environment in which the retained source object is claimed to perform the proposed function.

A sufficient declaration must state:

- who or what uses the function;
- for which bounded task or comparison;
- under which conditions;
- which source dimensions are relevant;
- which dimensions are excluded;
- when the function would no longer hold.

For `R-26`, “audit-training exercise” is more specific than “learning,” “organization,” or “Reader.” It identifies a task in which analysts must distinguish formal closure, practical continuation, residual binding, and reconsideration. The same Trajectory displayed in a general Reader has not thereby acquired that function.

```text
object shown in a context
≠ object performs a function in that context
```

#### C26-FUNCTION-01 — the proposed target function requires source-traceable and context-specific support

A target function must depend on identifiable source features and on the target-context conditions that make those features functionally relevant. The claim cannot be supported by usefulness, resemblance, or analyst intention alone.

For the calibration-case candidate, the analyst must show why the retained relation among closure, continuation, residual binding, and reconsideration enables discrimination within the training task. If the Trajectory lacks that relation, if the exercise does not test it, or if a simpler object performs the same function without the asserted structure, the target-function claim is weakened or fails.

```text
source features present
+ target context declared
≠ target function established automatically

source-traceable relation
+ contextual functional difference
→ PROJECT_AS warrant pressure
```

The source features may be supplied by a prior admissible `DECOMPOSE` Record, but their target-context role remains a new testable claim.

The comparison therefore admits four non-compensatory result patterns:

| Source reconstruction | Target-function candidate | Boundary consequence |
|---|---|---|
| supported | supported | two separately admissible occurrences may later be linked |
| supported | unsupported | decomposition result is preserved; projection is reduced, stopped, or failed |
| unsupported | supported | contextual use does not repair the unsupported internal map |
| unsupported | unsupported | no mixed compromise claim is available |

The third pattern requires particular care. A bounded contextual function may sometimes be supported by coarse, independently established source features even where one proposed finer decomposition fails. The projection then depends on the retained coarse source claim and must state that dependence explicitly. It may not cite the failed finer map as though it had become valid through use.

Likewise, a detailed and admissible decomposition may yield no defensible target function in the proposed context. This is not a defect in `DECOMPOSE`; it is an independent result of the `PROJECT_AS` burden. The boundary architecture thus preserves useful negative findings rather than forcing one operation to inherit the other’s success.

<a id="26-6-attractor-occurrence"></a>

### 26.6 Attractor Occurrence

An Attractor-typed occurrence creates a particularly difficult boundary because “Attractor” may name an occurrence typing, an internal recurrence pattern, or a higher-level contextual function. These uses must not be merged.

#### C26-ATTRACTOR-01 — Attractor occurrence, internal recurrence structure, and Attractor-function remain distinct

A bounded Attractor occurrence may enter `DECOMPOSE` as the source object. The analysis can open it into:

- repeated Frames;
- recurrence relations;
- blocked or progressively costly alternatives;
- Non-Events and lost windows;
- maintenance practices;
- accumulated friction;
- local repair and destabilization;
- source-function effects.

This remains occurrence-level reconstruction. It does not decompose the PMS Attractor operator type and does not automatically create a higher-level Attractor-function.

A later `PROJECT_AS` occurrence may retain the origin-typed source occurrence or a separately formed Trajectory and ask whether it performs an Attractor-function in a declared organizational, historical, instructional, or comparative context. That claim requires a bounded target context and must show what is drawn, stabilized, narrowed, or recurrently returned within that context.

```text
Attractor-typed occurrence
≠ PMS Attractor operator type

recurrence inside the occurrence
≠ contextual Attractor-function automatically

same term “Attractor”
≠ same claim or operation occurrence
```

The target-function claim may fail even where the occurrence decomposition is admissible. For example, repeated return within one bounded procedure may support the occurrence typing but may not perform a larger organizational Attractor-function. The reverse is also possible: a contextual Attractor-function may be supportable from a coarse retained object while a proposed finer decomposition remains unsupported. Neither result repairs the other.

<a id="26-7-subtle-misclassification"></a>

### 26.7 Subtle Misclassification: SUB-Looking Analysis that Assigns a Target Function

Misclassification is subtle where an analysis uses decompositional vocabulary, lists internal details, or appears beneath a SUB heading while its operative claim assigns the retained object a function in another context.

#### C26-SUBMIS-01 — decompositional form does not keep a target-function claim inside SUB

Consider the statement:

> Decomposing the review Trajectory reveals a warning architecture for live governance monitoring.

The first clause may refer to internal source reconstruction. The second clause assigns the retained Trajectory a warning function in a monitoring context. If the analysis asks how the Trajectory should operate as a warning signal, comparison object, audit trigger, or coordination interface, it has crossed into `PROJECT_AS` pressure.

The following features do not keep it within SUB:

- a list of internal components;
- a lower-level diagram;
- the word “decomposition”;
- placement in the SUB Part;
- use of a source-derived label;
- a graph in which the function appears as a node property.

The claim must be segmented:

```text
Claim A
→ these source-supported components constitute the Trajectory
→ DECOMPOSE

Claim B
→ the retained Trajectory performs a warning function
  in live governance monitoring
→ PROJECT_AS
```

If Claim B has no declared target context, no source-traceable functional relation, or no contextual failure condition, it cannot hide inside Claim A. The correct response is not to relabel the entire passage RETYPE automatically, but to separate the claims and preserve independent results.

A 3D graph viewer may make the misclassification easier to notice by displaying source-component edges separately from target-function edges. It cannot resolve the semantic boundary from geometry alone.

<a id="26-8-reverse-misclassification"></a>

### 26.8 Reverse Misclassification: RETYPE-Looking Language that Remains SUB

The reverse error occurs where a new noun, role-like phrase, or functional vocabulary appears to assign a target function but only summarizes internal source structure.

#### C26-RETYPESIM-01 — a functional-sounding summary remains SUB where no external contextual function is asserted

Suppose the internal reconstruction of `R-26` is called a “closure architecture,” “coordination structure,” or “decision bottleneck.” These phrases may summarize how subpaths, thresholds, roles, and Non-Events relate within the same Trajectory. Where the source object remains the explanatory target and no different target context or contextual function is asserted, the analysis remains within `DECOMPOSE` pressure.

```text
functional-sounding label
+ same source object as explanation target
+ no declared target context
+ no new contextual function
→ may remain SUB
```

The phrase “decision bottleneck,” for example, may describe an internal relation among information access, authority concentration, repeated non-decision, and binding thresholds. It becomes `PROJECT_AS` only when the retained source object is asserted to function as a bottleneck, warning, interface, model, or calibration device in another declared context.

The distinction protects SUB from unnecessary routing into RETYPE and protects RETYPE from becoming a catalogue of descriptive metaphors. A label may still be misleading or unsupported, but that is a source-typing or claim-calibration problem, not automatic evidence of operation change.

Reverse misclassification also occurs in interfaces. A Reader panel titled “Audit Function” may merely group source-level findings for navigation. Unless the corpus or analyst record asserts a contextual target function, the panel title does not execute `PROJECT_AS`.

<a id="26-9-sub-versus-recontextualization"></a>

### 26.9 SUB versus Recontextualization

A source object may become differently legible without being decomposed further and without receiving a target function. This middle possibility must remain open.

#### C26-RECONTEXT-01 — changed legibility without a transformation claim remains recontextualization

Recontextualization may include:

- reading the same records from a later historical vantage;
- switching between participant, institutional, archival, or audit perspectives;
- changing the question used to navigate the material;
- translating terminology for another audience;
- presenting the same object in a Reader or graph environment;
- comparing the object with another bounded context without assigning a function.

These changes can alter salience, visibility, or interpretation. They do not automatically change the source granularity or assert a contextual target function.

For `R-26`, a later audit Frame may make residual binding after formal closure more visible. If the analyst only notes that the same Trajectory now appears differently, no operation has yet been established. If the analyst opens the residual binding into finer relations, `DECOMPOSE` pressure begins. If the analyst asserts that the retained Trajectory functions as a calibration case in audit training, `PROJECT_AS` pressure begins.

```text
changed legibility
≠ finer internal reconstruction automatically
≠ contextual target function automatically
```

#### C26-FRAME-01 — a changed Frame alone is not PROJECT_AS

A Frame determines what is relevant, visible, expected, or comparable within an analysis. A target context determines where a retained source object is claimed to perform a bounded function. These coordinates can change together, but they are not equivalent.

```text
new Frame
→ may change the analytical view

new target context + bounded target function
→ PROJECT_AS pressure
```

A later Frame may expose a prior counterexample, revise the source function, or motivate a new decomposition. None of those effects by itself establishes a target function. Conversely, a `PROJECT_AS` claim may preserve the same analytical Frame while introducing a different bounded target context.

For the Reader and 3D graph application, rotation, filtering, layer selection, temporal scrubbing, or switching between source and audit views is ordinarily representational recontextualization. An operation begins only when the interaction is captured as a declared claim with the relevant source, granularity, context, function, support, Loss, and failure burden.

WP2 therefore establishes the trajectory and Attractor comparisons, the two-direction misclassification guard, and the positive recontextualization boundary. It does not yet complete the decision procedure, execute or validate a dual-operation chain, define separate-record routing in full, adjudicate invalid collapse, select final result axes or Output Classes, complete Failure/Mandatory Stop/Non-Capture, instantiate the nineteen-field Record view, hand Chapter 27 its complete limit questions, or lock Chapter 26 or SUB.

```text
WP2 complete
≠ Chapter 26 complete
≠ target function automatically established
≠ changed Frame is PROJECT_AS
≠ functional language is RETYPE automatically
≠ dual operation may share one Record
≠ final Output Class selected
≠ Chapter 26 provisionally locked
≠ SUB provisionally locked
```
<a id="26-10-sub-retype-decision-test"></a>

### 26.10 SUB / RETYPE Decision Test

The operation-boundary decision is not a vocabulary test and not a single yes/no question. It is a bounded sequence that preserves recontextualization, dual-operation pressure, and Non-Capture where the declared features do not support a responsible classification.

#### C26-DECISION-01 — complete the decision test through source, question, granularity, context, and function

The analyst must test the following positions in order:

1. **Source entry.** Is there one bounded source object with a declared origin type, source Frame, source function under test, source support, and Loss?
2. **Internal-opening question.** Does the claim identify finer components, relations, phases, subpaths, thresholds, or carrying structures of that same source object?
3. **Granularity relation.** Does the distinction set become finer while the source object remains the explanatory target?
4. **Unit status.** Does the source object remain the unit to which the internal reconstruction refers, even if its coarse typing is later refined, reduced, rejected, or left underdetermined?
5. **Target-context question.** Is a different bounded context declared with task, conditions, relevant and excluded dimensions, and a failure boundary?
6. **Target-function question.** Is the retained origin-typed source object claimed to perform a source-traceable function within that target context?
7. **Recontextualization check.** Has only the Frame, viewpoint, vocabulary, navigation path, or representation changed without an internal-constitution or target-function claim?
8. **Dual-pressure check.** Are both an internal-opening claim and a contextual target-function claim present?
9. **Record-separation check.** Where both claims are present, can they be segmented into separate operation occurrences with independent support, Loss, result, and failure possibility?
10. **Uncertainty check.** If the answers remain entangled or source support cannot discriminate them, must the operation boundary remain underdetermined or `non_capture`?

The resulting local findings may be described as:

```text
decompose_candidate
project_as_candidate
recontextualization_only
dual_operation_required
operation_boundary_underdetermined
invalid_collapse_detected
```

These are local findings rather than new canonical Output Classes. They do not replace the declared operation in the Shared Transformation Record and do not authorize automatic routing from prose, graph geometry, labels, or interface use.

The minimum boundary logic is:

```text
internal structure opened
+ granularity primarily changed
+ source object remains explanatory target
→ DECOMPOSE candidate

origin-typed source object retained
+ bounded target context declared
+ source-traceable target function asserted
→ PROJECT_AS candidate

changed legibility only
+ neither transformation claim established
→ recontextualization only

both claim structures present
→ dual operation required
```

A missing answer does not automatically favor either operation. For example, a detailed account with no stable source object may fail as `DECOMPOSE`; a clearly named target context with no supported function may fail as `PROJECT_AS`. Where both candidate claims remain materially ambiguous, the method must preserve the ambiguity rather than manufacture a compromise operation.

The decision test is also claim-segment specific. One paragraph, diagram, or graph scene may contain multiple claims. Classification follows each claim segment, not the container in which it appears. A chapter heading, file location, visual layer, node color, or interaction mode cannot classify the operation.

<a id="26-11-dual-operation"></a>

### 26.11 Dual Operation

A case may require both `DECOMPOSE` and `PROJECT_AS`, but it never requires a mixed fourth operation. Dual operation means a chain of distinct occurrences whose order, source handoff, and independent burdens are declared.

#### C26-DUAL-01 — dual-operation cases retain distinct occurrence identity

For the R-26 Trajectory, a valid chain may take the form:

```text
DECOMPOSE(R-24)
→ reconstruct subpaths, transition clusters,
  Non-Events, authority shifts, and residual binding

PROJECT_AS(R-24)
→ test the retained origin-typed Trajectory
  as a bounded calibration case
  in an audit-training context
```

The second operation may use findings produced by the first, but the source of the projection must be declared precisely. It may project:

- the original coarse Trajectory whose support remains independently sufficient;
- the admissibly decomposed Trajectory together with selected, source-supported internal findings;
- or a reduced source claim whose earlier disposition is preserved.

It may not silently project a failed finer map as though the projection had validated it.

#### C26-RECORD-01 — separate claims, Records, Loss, and results are mandatory

Each operation occurrence requires its own:

- operation identifier;
- source and target declaration;
- bounded claim;
- source support and relation support;
- Frame and granularity declaration;
- selection and Loss account;
- local operation result;
- source-function or target-function effect;
- prior claim disposition;
- canonical Output Class;
- Failure, Stop, and Non-Capture possibility.

A chain reference may link the Records, but it does not merge them. The first Record may state which source features are handed to the second. The second must state which of those features are necessary for the contextual target function and what would falsify or reduce that function.

```text
shared source history
≠ shared Record

shared evidence
≠ shared Loss account

successful first link
≠ successful second link
```

The two Loss structures may overlap but remain occurrence-specific. `DECOMPOSE` records what was preserved, compressed, excluded, uncertain, or irrecoverable in opening the source object. `PROJECT_AS` records what source features are preserved or excluded when the retained object is assigned a bounded target function. A single undifferentiated Loss paragraph would conceal which claim carried which omission.

#### C26-CHAIN-01 — link order and independent failure remain explicit

The order of a dual-operation chain matters. `DECOMPOSE → PROJECT_AS` means that a finer source reconstruction precedes the contextual projection. `PROJECT_AS → DECOMPOSE` means that an already projected object or function claim becomes the bounded source of a later internal reconstruction. These chains are not interchangeable and are not presumed reversible.

```text
DECOMPOSE → PROJECT_AS
≠ PROJECT_AS → DECOMPOSE
```

Every link remains a new testable claim. Failure at one link does not erase supported findings from another link, but it may alter what can be handed forward. If the first link fails to establish the proposed internal structure, the second may proceed only from an independently warranted coarse source object and must not cite the failed detail. If the projection fails, the prior decomposition remains intact within its own claim ceiling.

A stopped chain may re-enter only through a new explicit occurrence with a materially new basis. Re-entry does not reopen earlier Records as though their result had never occurred.

The formal model may verify separate record identifiers, operation order, source/target references, Loss fields, and independent result declarations. It may not determine from form alone whether the internal reconstruction or target function is substantively warranted.

<a id="26-12-invalid-collapse"></a>

### 26.12 Invalid Collapse

Invalid collapse occurs where the analysis removes the boundary between internal constitution and contextual target function, or where it uses support for one operation as automatic support for the other.

#### C26-COLLAPSE-01 — invalid collapse is detected and routed without inventing a mixed operation

Typical collapse patterns include:

- treating a component map as proof of a target function;
- treating a useful target function as proof that the proposed internal map is correct;
- replacing the origin type with the target function;
- placing two operation claims in one Record without segmentation;
- assigning one Loss statement and one result to both occurrences;
- using a new label, analogy, interface role, or graph position as function proof;
- treating a changed Frame as `PROJECT_AS` without a target function;
- rescuing a failed `DECOMPOSE` claim by projecting the object under a more useful label;
- hiding an unsupported target function inside greater detail;
- inheriting authority from the source object, target context, software layer, or analyst role.

The proper response is claim segmentation where possible. If the claims can be separated, each receives its own occurrence and Record. If separation would require invented components, invented context, invented functional relations, or retroactive repair of a failed claim, the mixed analysis must fail or stop. No fourth operation is introduced to absorb the ambiguity.

```text
DECOMPOSE
≠ PROJECT_AS

combined prose
≠ combined operation

invalid collapse detected
≠ compromise operation authorized
```

#### C26-AXES-01 — local result, function effect, prior disposition, and Output Class remain separate

The operation-boundary assessment preserves four axes:

```text
1. local boundary result
2. source-function or target-function effect
3. prior source-claim disposition
4. canonical Output Class
```

A bounded example may be:

```yaml
operation_boundary_result_R26:
  local_boundary_result: dual_operation_required
  decompose_source_function_effect: internally_differentiated
  project_as_target_function_effect: underdetermined
  prior_source_claim_disposition: reduced
  canonical_output_classes:
    decompose_record: admissible_with_bounded_claim
    project_as_record: non_capture
```

The example uses two canonical classes because it contains two Records. It does not assign two classes to one Record. A single occurrence still maps to exactly one canonical Output Class after all relevant gates.

Local findings such as `dual_operation_required`, `recontextualization_only`, or `invalid_collapse_detected` are not Output Classes. They guide claim segmentation and routing. They cannot compensate for absent support, Type Integrity, origin-type preservation, Contextual Boundedness, or Loss.

#### C26-STOPNC-01 — Failure, Mandatory Stop, and Non-Capture preserve the operation boundary

A boundary transformation fails where:

- the source object or origin type cannot be identified;
- internal structure is asserted without source-supported granularity or relations;
- a target function is asserted without a bounded context or functional relation;
- the origin type is replaced;
- a dual-operation case remains in one Record;
- one operation is used to immunize the other;
- graph structure, formal completeness, or interface use substitutes for semantic warrant.

Mandatory Stop applies before continuation would require inventing internal structure, context, function, origin-type continuity, operation order, or record separation; concealing an operation switch; erasing a prior failure through projection; converting analogy into function; or inferring person properties, legitimacy, recommendation, sanction, or authority.

`non_capture` remains available where the question is legitimate but the evidence does not responsibly discriminate among `DECOMPOSE`, `PROJECT_AS`, recontextualization, or a separable dual-operation chain. It does not default the case to either operation and does not authorize a mixed record.

```text
non_capture
≠ default DECOMPOSE
≠ default PROJECT_AS
≠ mixed-operation compromise
≠ permission to replace origin type
```

#### Operation-Boundary Assessment Record View

The Chapter-26 view is:

```yaml
operation_boundary_assessment:
  source_object:
  source_origin_type:
  source_function_under_test:
  source_frame:
  proposed_target_context:
  proposed_target_function:
  analysis_question:
  granularity_change:
  internal_structure_opened:
  source_object_unit_status:
  frame_or_context_change:
  recontextualization_status:
  operation_candidate:
  operation_boundary_reasons:
  dual_operation_status:
  separate_record_requirements:
  unresolved_structure:
  loss:
  admissibility_result:
```

The view contains exactly nineteen fields and maps into the Shared Transformation Record plus controlled `extensions`. It creates no parallel schema, operation score, automatic classifier, target-function catalogue, origin-type enum, or authority route.

#### C26-HANDOFF-01 — Chapter 27 receives bounded SUB-limit questions only

The controlled continuation is:

```text
Chapter 26 provisional lock
→ Chapter 27 Preparation Gate
→ Chapter 27 — SUB Boundary Conditions
```

Chapter 26 hands Chapter 27 only questions that remain inside `DECOMPOSE` after the operation boundary has been classified:

- whether finer distinctions retain PraxisPurchase;
- whether source support reaches the claimed internal structure;
- whether the source object and coarser function remain traceable;
- whether the lower or upper SUB boundary has been crossed;
- whether component counterfactuals support constitutive relevance;
- whether Claim Reduction, Stop, or Non-Capture is required.

Chapter 27 does not inherit unresolved target-function claims. Those claims require separate `PROJECT_AS` occurrences and belong to RETYPE. Chapter 28 retains cases and Local Audit; RETYPE Chapters 29–40 retain complete contextual-function theory; LIMITS retains system-wide Source, Stop, Non-Capture, anti-immunization, and authority consolidation.

<a id="chapter-26-completion-boundary"></a>

#### Chapter 26 completion boundary

The integrated WP4 audit confirms the Chapter-26 Contract, all twenty-four Preparation-Audit controls, all twenty-four Pressure Duties, origin-type preservation, source-function/target-function separation, internal-constitution/contextual-function distinction, granularity/target-context separation, changed-Frame and recontextualization boundaries, Trajectory and Attractor counterpressure, subtle and reverse misclassification guards, target-context and function support, dual-operation occurrence identity, separate Records/Loss/results, chain order, invalid-collapse routing, result-axis separation, Failure, Mandatory Stop, Non-Capture, the nineteen-field Record view, and package consistency. The Chapter-26 result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

Chapter 26 is therefore **provisionally locked**. The class names the chapter-level methodological result under the still-open SUB Part and later corpus-wide audits. It does not automatically classify ambiguous prose, prove a contextual target function, convert recontextualization into an operation, permit shared Records or Loss for two operations, create a fourth operation, replace origin type, complete RETYPE, decide Chapter-27 limits, lock SUB, or increase claim, application, governance, or person-level authority.

All twenty-four Chapter-26 Pressure Duties occur exactly once in canonical prose. No standalone Case, Transformation Record, Smoke Record, appendix expansion, schema artifact, new operation, Rule, Output Class, audit stage, target-function or origin-type enum, operation score, semantic classifier, graph application, Reader implementation, person type, or authority mechanism has been produced.

```text
Chapter 26 provisionally locked
≠ every ambiguous passage classified automatically
≠ target function established by decomposition
≠ changed Frame is PROJECT_AS automatically
≠ recontextualization is an operation automatically
≠ dual operation collapsed or recorded once
≠ Chapter 27 completed
≠ SUB provisionally locked
```

Reopening requires a concrete conflict involving PMS Base, Structure or Contract ownership, source-object identity, origin type, source or target function, target context, granularity relation, recontextualization status, dual-operation segmentation, operation order, separate Records or Loss, invalid-collapse routing, result axes, Failure/Stop/Non-Capture, a Pressure Duty, a Reference or Formal-Model binding, or a later integrated dependency. Preference for a simpler label, one mixed narrative, denser graphs, stronger automation, automatic target-function inference, origin-type replacement, or premature Chapter-27/RETYPE conclusions is insufficient.

<a id="chapter-27-sub-boundary-conditions"></a>

## 27. SUB Boundary Conditions

Chapter 27 applies the already established Admissibility Band locally to `DECOMPOSE`. Chapters 18–26 define eligible source objects, granularity relations, the generic operation, occurrence- and object-family applications, resolution outcomes, bounded Stop and re-entry, and the boundary between internal reconstruction and contextual functional projection. Chapter 27 does not redefine those controls. It specifies where finer reconstruction ceases to carry the tested praxis claim or ceases to remain reconstructibly anchored to its source object.

The governing question is:

> Where does a finer reconstruction fall below praxeological relevance, and where does it exceed the point at which its components and relations remain traceable to the source object and its tested function?

The two boundaries are not mirror images. The lower boundary concerns distinctions that may be correct but do no additional work for the tested claim. The upper boundary concerns a finer map whose parts, relations, precision, or object boundaries no longer support a reconstruction of the source object. Between them lies the admissible local SUB range.

Sections 27.1–27.4 establish the source-bound entry, Lower and Upper SUB Boundaries, Resolution without Purchase, Source-Reference continuity, relation burden, and Fragmentation without Reconstruction. Sections 27.5–27.8 establish the local Source Ceiling, claim-specific support descriptions, the bounded Counterfactual Component Test, local Component Sensitivity, Coarser-Function Traceability, and Type Preservation with explicit source-type revision. Sections 27.9–27.12 complete No Privilege of Fine Resolution, Optional and Mandatory Stop, Claim Reduction, Non-Capture, the conjunctive and non-compensatory SUB Admissibility Test, four result axes, Granularity-Escape and re-entry controls, the twenty-field SUB Boundary Assessment View, and the Chapter-28 handoff.

The integrated Chapter-27 result is therefore a complete **local SUB boundary procedure**. It identifies the claim-relative lower boundary at which finer distinction ceases to add warranted praxis discrimination and the upper boundary at which components, relations, precision, reference, function, or type no longer sustain reconstruction of the source object. It preserves coarse-claim revisability, rejects automatic priority for fine resolution, separates Source Ceiling from Non-Capture, and treats Stop as a positive bounded result rather than evidence that no finer structure exists. It does not create a new system-wide LIMITS layer, universal component ontology, causal engine, granularity scale, purchase or support score, automatic Source-Ceiling or Output-Class classifier, case corpus, Local Audit, Chapter-28 result, SUB Part lock, person judgment, or authority increase.

The initial Chapter-27 architecture is:

```text
eligible DECOMPOSE source object
+
declared source claim and granularity relation
+
expected additional praxis difference
+
component, relation, reference, and coarser-function pressure
→
lower-boundary, admissible-range, or upper-boundary candidate
```

No numerical purchase score, fragmentation score, component ontology, or automatic boundary classifier is introduced.

<a id="27-1-lower-sub-boundary"></a>

### 27.1 Lower SUB Boundary

The Lower SUB Boundary is the point below which a proposed finer distinction does not change a warranted praxis reconstruction for the tested claim. It is not the smallest physically observable event, the shortest timestamp interval, the most detailed record, or the lowest technically available representation. It is a claim-relative boundary.

#### C27-ENTRY-01 — the SUB boundary test begins with a source-bound claim entry

Before a lower or upper boundary is assessed, the analyst must declare at least:

- the source transformation record or bounded source object;
- the tested source claim and source function;
- the source and target granularity;
- the expected additional praxis difference;
- the dimensions that would have to change for the finer reconstruction to matter;
- the source reference that must remain identifiable;
- the coarser function that must remain traceable or be explicitly revised;
- known support limits, uncertainty, Loss, and Stop pressure.

A bounded R-27 entry may continue the review-governance Trajectory from Chapters 24–26:

```yaml
sub_boundary_entry_R27:
  source_object: review-governance Trajectory R-24
  tested_source_claim: formal closure and later reconsideration form one bounded trajectory with residual binding
  source_function_under_test: reconstructs how closure, residual obligation, and reopening remain historically connected
  source_granularity: trajectory with bounded subpaths and transition clusters
  target_granularity: closure and reconsideration components with declared relations
  expected_additional_praxis_difference: distinguish whether post-closure activity changes the meaning of finality or only records implementation detail
  source_reference_required: same bounded review-governance trajectory
  coarser_function_pressure: retained from entry and subject to confirmation, revision, reduction, rejection, or underdetermination
  boundary_status: unresolved_before_test
```

The expected difference prevents detail from becoming its own justification. The finer reconstruction must specify in advance what could change: a role, temporal relation, threshold, access condition, cost, binding, alternative, expectation, source-function claim, uncertainty, or Loss. A generic promise to reveal “more structure” is insufficient.

#### C27-LOWER-01 — the lower boundary is crossed where added distinction has no PraxisPurchase for the tested claim

Lower-boundary pressure arises where a finer distinction is source-supported or at least technically possible but its removal leaves the warranted reconstruction unchanged. Typical cases include:

- additional timestamps that do not alter sequence, overlap, delay, threshold, or completion;
- more component names that do not change roles or relations;
- finer document versions that do not alter access, responsibility, commitment, cost, alternative, or source function;
- repeated neutral refinements without a new comparison basis;
- differences visible only at sensor, logging, rendering, or interface granularity;
- graph subdivisions whose removal changes no supported relation or claim disposition.

```text
additional distinction
+
no supported change to a praxis-relevant claim dimension
→
lower-boundary pressure
```

The result does not imply falsehood. A detail may be correct and still be unable to carry the transformation claim. The relevant question is not whether the distinction exists, but whether it changes what can responsibly be said about the source object under the declared Frame and claim.

The lower boundary is also not permanent. A distinction that lacks purchase for one claim may matter under another explicit claim, after new sources, or under a materially changed granularity relation. Such use requires a new testable claim; it does not retroactively turn the earlier neutral result into Gain.

<a id="27-2-resolution-without-purchase"></a>

### 27.2 Resolution without Purchase

Resolution without Purchase occurs where the finer reconstruction is valid and supported but does not alter the tested warranted claim. It is the local Chapter-27 expression of Resolution Neutrality, not a synonym for unsupported refinement or failed transformation.

#### C27-PURCHASE-01 — supported no-purchase remains distinct from unsupported detail

The distinction is conjunctive:

```text
supported finer detail
+
valid coarse/fine comparison
+
no changed warranted praxis claim
→
Resolution Neutrality candidate

unsupported detail
→
source-support, relation-support, reduction, failure, or Stop pressure
```

Neutrality therefore requires more discipline than simply observing that the conclusion did not change. The finer map must itself be supported, comparable to the coarser map, and connected to the same tested claim. If its components or relations are speculative, the result is not neutral; the refinement is unsupported or underdetermined.

For R-27, minute-level ordering inside a formally recorded closure may add accuracy while leaving all of the following unchanged:

- the relevant authority configuration;
- the closure threshold;
- the residual binding;
- the later reconsideration window;
- the available alternatives;
- the coarser trajectory function;
- the prior claim disposition and Loss.

That detail may remain useful for archival navigation or presentation. It does not strengthen the `DECOMPOSE` claim merely because it increases node count, timestamps, precision, or interactive affordances.

```text
representation density
≠ PraxisPurchase

technical precision
≠ stronger warrant automatically

interactive inspection
≠ changed claim
```

A positive Optional Stop may follow when the coarser reconstruction already carries the tested function and further opening only repeats neutrality. The complete Stop classification remains Chapter 27 WP3. At WP1, the requirement is narrower: no-purchase must be recorded as such rather than rhetorically converted into Gain, depth, or superior truth.

Resolution without Purchase also protects the coarser map from unnecessary displacement. The coarser reconstruction may remain preferable where it preserves the relevant function with less Loss, clearer comparison, lower source burden, or better communicability. This preference is local and claim-bound; it does not immunize the coarser claim against a future supported difference.

<a id="27-3-upper-sub-boundary"></a>

### 27.3 Upper SUB Boundary

The Upper SUB Boundary is crossed where finer reconstruction no longer remains reconstructibly anchored to the source object and the function under test. The problem is not that the map contains too much detail in the abstract. The problem is that the detail ceases to form a supportable reconstruction of the same bounded object.

#### C27-UPPER-01 — upper-boundary pressure begins where detail outruns reconstructive anchoring

Typical indicators include:

- the source object disappears into an inventory of parts;
- components are listed but their relations are absent or merely assumed;
- relation precision exceeds the sources;
- one source object silently becomes several objects without a new operation claim;
- the coarser function can no longer be confirmed, revised, reduced, rejected, or left explicitly underdetermined;
- the source type survives only as a heading while the finer map no longer supports it;
- model completion fills missing phases, edges, causes, or intentions;
- microdescription grows while source reference and claim translation weaken.

```text
more components or finer relations
+
loss of source reference or coarser-function traceability
→
upper-boundary pressure
```

The upper boundary is a local SUB application of traceability discipline. It is not the complete system-wide Praxeological Traceability Ceiling owned by LIMITS. Chapter 27 asks whether this `DECOMPOSE` occurrence can still return its findings to the source object and tested function.

#### C27-REFERENCE-01 — source reference must remain identifiable or be explicitly revised

A finer map need not preserve every coarse description. It must preserve the bounded historical or structural reference object, or explicitly state that the original reference claim has failed, split, or become underdetermined.

Source-reference continuity may be supported through:

- a stable bounded object across finer phases or components;
- explicit relations linking components to the same event, occurrence, composite, Path, or Trajectory;
- declared transition or dependency relations;
- preserved temporal, role, or formation boundaries;
- a documented revision explaining why the coarse object must be divided or rejected.

What is prohibited is nominal preservation:

```text
same label
+
unrelated fragments
≠ same source object preserved
```

If the finer evidence shows that the coarse source actually contains several independently formed objects, the analyst may need a separate `COMPOSE` or rival-source claim. Chapter 27 does not conceal that operation change by continuing to call the result a finer decomposition of one object.

Source reference and source-function confirmation remain distinct. The same object may remain identifiable while its proposed function is reduced or rejected. Conversely, a familiar functional label cannot preserve reference after the object boundary has broken.

<a id="27-4-fragmentation-without-reconstruction"></a>

### 27.4 Fragmentation without Reconstruction

Fragmentation without Reconstruction is the characteristic upper-bound failure mode of SUB. It occurs where analysis produces parts but cannot responsibly reconstruct how those parts relate to the source object or to one another.

#### C27-FRAGMENT-01 — a component inventory is not yet a decomposition result

Fragmentation can appear as:

- named components with no constitutive, temporal, dependency, substitution, conflict, or maintenance relation;
- multiple local maps with no shared source reference;
- events or Non-Events detached from the event, Path, Trajectory, occurrence, or composite function they supposedly carry;
- graph nodes whose edges encode layout, similarity, proximity, or software grouping rather than source-supported relations;
- parts treated as self-sufficient explanations where their effect depends on the relation map;
- repeated splitting that removes the distinctions necessary to recognize the original source object.

A valid decomposition must do more than enumerate. It must state what the components are components **of**, how they are connected, which relations are supported, and what remains uncertain or irrecoverable.

#### C27-RELATION-01 — component support and relation support are independent burdens

A source may establish that two components existed without establishing how they were connected. Relation support must therefore be declared separately from component support.

```text
component A supported
+
component B supported
≠
relation A → B supported
```

The relevant relation may be sequential, partially ordered, overlapping, substitutive, enabling, blocking, maintaining, conflicting, or underdetermined. A visually convenient edge, temporal adjacency, common label, or shared repository location is not enough. Full Source-Ceiling and support-status treatment remains WP2, but WP1 fixes the non-substitution: parts cannot compensate for missing relations, and formal graph completeness cannot compensate for absent source warrant.

#### C27-COARSEPRESSURE-01 — coarser-function pressure remains visible from entry through result

Every component and relation claim must remain answerable to the coarser function under test. The finer reconstruction must show whether it:

- confirms the coarser function;
- refines or internally differentiates it;
- preserves it only partially;
- reduces or rejects it;
- or leaves it underdetermined.

The coarser function is not a conclusion that must survive. It is the translation pressure that prevents the decomposition from becoming an autonomous parts catalogue.

For R-27, identifying closure records, residual obligations, and reconsideration activities is not enough. The analysis must state how their supported relations affect the tested claim that one bounded trajectory connects formal closure with later reopening. If that relation cannot be reconstructed, a weaker component-level finding may survive, but the stronger trajectory-decomposition claim cannot be retained unchanged.

Fragmentation may therefore lead to different later outcomes:

- Claim Reduction where supported component-level findings remain;
- `failed_transformation` where the proposed decomposition collapses;
- `non_capture` where rival relation maps remain equally supported;
- Mandatory Stop where further relation claims would exceed source support or require invented structure.

The complete routing belongs to WP3. WP1 establishes the boundary logic: additional distinctions remain admissible only while they carry a praxis-relevant difference and continue to form a source-traceable reconstruction rather than a denser but disconnected inventory.

#### WP1 completion boundary

WP1 establishes the source-bound entry, Lower SUB Boundary, Resolution without Purchase, Upper SUB Boundary, source-reference requirement, component/relation separation, Fragmentation without Reconstruction, and continuous coarser-function pressure. It does not yet classify the complete Source Ceiling, execute component sensitivity counterfactuals, decide type preservation and revision, establish no privilege of fine resolution in full, select final Stop/Claim-Reduction/Failure/Non-Capture outcomes, instantiate the twenty-field Record view, produce Chapter-28 cases, or lock Chapter 27 or SUB.

```text
WP1 complete
≠ Chapter 27 complete
≠ supported detail always has purchase
≠ component presence proves relation
≠ source reference proves source function
```
