```text
≠ upper-bound pressure automatically means non_capture
≠ final Output Class selected
≠ Chapter 27 provisionally locked
≠ SUB provisionally locked
```

The local WP1 result remains:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

<a id="27-5-source-ceiling"></a>

### 27.5 Source Ceiling

The Source Ceiling is the highest level of component, relation, temporal, and functional precision that the available source basis can responsibly carry for the tested `DECOMPOSE` claim. It is local to the source object, claim, Frame, granularity relation, and relation type. It is not a universal data-quality rank and does not become more permissive because a model can generate a coherent finer map.

#### C27-SOURCECEIL-01 — the local Source Ceiling bounds every component and relation claim independently

A decomposition may remain below the Source Ceiling for one claim and exceed it for another. A source may support that a component existed while leaving its order, role, dependency, or functional effect unresolved. The ceiling must therefore be applied separately to at least:

- component existence and bounded identity;
- temporal order, overlap, delay, and threshold;
- dependency, enabling, blocking, substitution, maintenance, or conflict relations;
- event and Non-Event claims;
- source-function effects;
- counterfactual component claims;
- type-preservation or type-revision claims.

```text
source supports component presence
≠ source supports exact relation
≠ source supports causal load
≠ source supports source-function effect
```

For R-27, the records may support that a formal closure record, residual obligations, and later reconsideration activities occurred. They may support that the obligations remained binding after closure. They may not support the exact sequence of every informal communication, the intention of every participant, or a unique causal route from residual obligation to reopening. A decomposition that inserts those missing relations because they make the trajectory narratively complete exceeds its Source Ceiling.

The Source Ceiling is reached positively when the finest responsible claim has been stated and its remaining uncertainty has been preserved. Reaching it is not itself Failure or `non_capture`. It becomes upper-boundary pressure only when the transformation continues by inventing components, relations, precision, or functions that the source basis cannot carry.

#### C27-SUPPORTSTATUS-01 — local support statuses remain claim-specific and non-canonical

Chapter 27 uses five bounded support descriptions:

```text
supported
partially_supported
underdetermined
speculative
unavailable
```

They are not Output Classes, scores, probabilities, or a universal evidence ladder. Each status answers a specific question about a specific component, relation, or functional effect.

- `supported` means the bounded claim remains within the available source load.
- `partially_supported` means a weaker or narrower version is carried while the stronger version is not.
- `underdetermined` means the source basis supports more than one materially different reconstruction.
- `speculative` means the claim depends on assumptions not carried by the current sources.
- `unavailable` means the required source access or record is absent, lost, or irrecoverable for the current test.

`unavailable` does not prove a Non-Event. `underdetermined` does not authorize averaging rival relations. `partially_supported` does not permit the unsupported remainder to be retained without Claim Reduction. `speculative` may remain visible as a hypothesis, but not as a completed decomposition result.

A graph or Reader may display these statuses. It may not infer them from node count, edge density, visual confidence, source-file presence, or formal schema completion.

<a id="27-6-counterfactual-component-test"></a>

### 27.6 Counterfactual Component Test

The Counterfactual Component Test asks whether a bounded change to one identified component would alter the reconstructed source function under the declared relation map. It tests local praxeological load; it does not establish universal causality, metaphysical necessity, or an exhaustive ontology of parts.

#### C27-COMPONENTTEST-01 — component load is tested through bounded removal, delay, deactivation, replacement, or material alteration

For each candidate component, the analyst declares:

1. the component and its supported boundaries;
2. the source-supported relations connecting it to other components;
3. the coarser function under test;
4. the bounded counterfactual variation;
5. the expected function effect;
6. assumptions, alternatives, uncertainty, and Loss.

The admissible variations are:

```text
remove
 delay
 deactivate
 replace
 materially alter
```

The selected variation must be meaningful for the component. “Removing” a Non-Event, replacing an irrecoverable phase with an invented phase, or deactivating an operator type is not a valid test. The test applies to bounded occurrences, components, relations, or composite constituents—not to operator types as such.

For the R-27 trajectory, the residual-obligation component can be tested by asking whether the reconstructed connection between formal closure and later reopening would remain the same if the obligation had been absent, discharged earlier, delayed, or replaced by a non-binding recommendation. The answer must stay within supported history and declared alternatives. It cannot infer what every actor would have intended or done in an unrestricted possible world.

#### C27-SENSITIVITY-01 — component sensitivity distinguishes local load without creating a new ontology

The bounded result descriptions are:

```text
constitutive
strongly_modulating
weakly_modulating
replaceable
incidental
underdetermined
```

- `constitutive` means the tested coarser function cannot be reconstructed in its current form without that component under the declared relation map.
- `strongly_modulating` means the function remains identifiable but its threshold, direction, binding, cost, availability, or temporal form changes materially.
- `weakly_modulating` means a supported difference remains, but the core function and claim disposition remain stable.
- `replaceable` means another supported component or relation can carry the relevant load without preserving every local feature.
- `incidental` means the component is present but its bounded alteration changes no warranted aspect of the tested source function.
- `underdetermined` means the available sources or rival relation maps do not permit responsible classification.

These descriptions are local findings, not PMS primitives, person types, moral ranks, universal causal categories, or Output Classes. Several components may jointly carry a function even where none is individually constitutive. Conversely, a component may appear central in a graph while remaining replaceable or incidental to the tested function.

A bounded R-27 finding may therefore be recorded as:

```yaml
component_sensitivity_R27:
  component: residual obligation after formal closure
  bounded_variation: replace with non-binding recommendation
  source_function_under_test: historically connects closure to later reopening through continuing binding
  component_support: supported
  relation_support: partially_supported
  sensitivity_result: strongly_modulating
  warranted_effect: trajectory remains identifiable but the claim of continuing binding must be reduced
  causal_scope: local_to_declared_source_object_relation_map_and_claim
  universal_causality_claimed: false
  uncertainty: informal reopening route remains underdetermined
```

#### C27-COUNTERFACT-01 — bounded sensitivity does not become universal causal explanation

The Counterfactual Component Test is constrained by the observed source object and a declared comparison basis. It does not answer what would happen in every possible context, whether a component is generally necessary for all objects of the same type, or which person caused the outcome.

```text
local function changes under bounded variation
≠ universal causal law
≠ intention established
≠ person responsibility assigned
≠ component necessary in every comparable case
```

Where the variation requires invented actors, motives, institutions, sources, or relations, the test must be reduced, remain underdetermined, or stop. Formal simulation can expose dependency on assumptions; it cannot convert those assumptions into empirical support.

<a id="27-7-coarser-function-traceability"></a>

### 27.7 Coarser-Function Traceability

Coarser-Function Traceability is the ability to return every material finer finding to the source function under test and state how that function is affected. It is the positive reconstruction condition corresponding to the upper boundary. It does not require preservation of the original function claim.

#### C27-COARSETRACE-01 — each material component and relation must translate back to the coarser-function claim

A finer reconstruction remains traceable when it can state, with source support, whether the coarser function is:

```text
confirmed
refined
internally_differentiated
partially_preserved
reduced
rejected
underdetermined
```

The translation must identify which components and relations carry the result, which remain incidental or replaceable, and what Loss is introduced by the finer map. A list of detailed findings without an explicit function effect remains analytically incomplete even if every listed component is individually supported.

For R-27, the relation among formal closure, residual obligation, and later reconsideration may support internal differentiation: closure ended one formal decision phase, while residual obligation preserved a bounded route through which finality remained revisable. If the connection between obligation and reopening is only partially supported, the function claim must be reduced accordingly. The source object may remain one trajectory while the stronger claim of a uniquely determined reopening route is rejected.

```text
source object preserved
≠ source function confirmed

source function revised or rejected
≠ source object lost automatically
```

Traceability can therefore produce a stronger, weaker, differently bounded, or underdetermined function claim. Its requirement is translation, not conservation.

Coarser-Function Traceability also constrains component sensitivity. A component classification that cannot state its effect on the coarser function is not yet a completed sensitivity finding. Likewise, a function effect that cannot be traced to supported components and relations is only a coarse restatement, not a successful decomposition.

<a id="27-8-type-preservation"></a>

### 27.8 Type Preservation

Type Preservation keeps operator type, operator occurrence, source-object type, and the tested source-function claim distinct during finer reconstruction. `DECOMPOSE` opens occurrences and composites. It does not open or rewrite the operator type itself.

#### C27-TYPEPRESERVE-01 — finer reconstruction preserves type integrity by keeping type level and occurrence level distinct

The governing separations are:

```text
operator type
≠ operator occurrence
≠ source-object type claim
≠ coarser-function claim
```

An Attractor-typed occurrence may be decomposed into recurrence, blocked alternatives, maintenance, repair, cost, and residual binding. The Attractor operator type is not decomposed. A Trajectory may be opened into subpaths and transition clusters without transforming `Trajectory` into a new primitive. A Frame-typed occurrence may be reconstructed through internal distinctions without decomposing the Frame operator type.

Type integrity is preserved when:

- the exact occurrence or composite being opened remains identified;
- the governing operator type is not treated as a component inventory;
- component findings remain occurrence-level or object-level claims;
- type labels are not retained merely because the chapter began with them;
- revision of the source-type claim is explicit and source-supported.

#### C27-TYPEREVISE-01 — the source-type claim may be confirmed, restricted, rejected, or left underdetermined

Type Preservation does not immunize the initial typing. The finer reconstruction may show that the occurrence:

```text
confirms the initial source type
supports only a restricted type claim
requires a rival source-object description
rejects the initial type claim
leaves the type claim underdetermined
```

For example, an occurrence entered as one Trajectory may decompose into several independently formed sequences lacking the relations required for one bounded Trajectory. The correct result may be type-claim rejection or a separate `COMPOSE` proposal, not nominal preservation of `Trajectory`. Conversely, a source type may remain supported while its proposed function is reduced or rejected.

Type revision is a new testable claim. It must preserve the prior claim disposition and Loss. A change of granularity does not erase that the original typing failed or remained underdetermined.

WP2 therefore closes the middle support architecture of Chapter 27: the Source Ceiling bounds what can be claimed; the Counterfactual Component Test estimates local component load; Coarser-Function Traceability returns finer findings to the tested function; and Type Preservation keeps type levels intact while permitting explicit source-type revision. WP3 now completes the local boundary routing through no privilege of fine resolution, Optional and Mandatory Stop, Claim Reduction, Non-Capture, the conjunctive SUB Admissibility Test, separated result axes, anti-escape re-entry, the twenty-field Record view, and the controlled Chapter-28 handoff.

#### WP2 completion boundary

```text
WP2 complete
≠ Chapter 27 complete
≠ Source Ceiling is a universal evidence score
≠ component sensitivity proves universal causality
≠ constitutive means necessary in every comparable case
≠ source reference confirms source function
≠ type preservation immunizes the initial type claim
≠ final Stop or Output Class selected
≠ Chapter 27 provisionally locked
≠ SUB provisionally locked
```

The local WP2 result remains:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

<a id="27-9-no-privilege-of-fine-resolution"></a>

### 27.9 No Privilege of Fine Resolution

Finer resolution has no automatic epistemic, praxeological, or methodological priority. It may expose a relation that the coarse reconstruction compressed; it may also add no relevant difference, exceed source support, break source reference, or weaken comparison. The appropriate resolution is the one that carries the tested claim with the strongest bounded combination of PraxisPurchase, Source Support, relation support, source-reference continuity, coarser-function traceability, calibrated Loss, and explicit uncertainty.

#### C27-NOPRIV-01 — finer resolution is compared rather than privileged

For the tested claim, a finer reconstruction may be:

```text
better
co-equal
worse
incomparable
```

- `better` means it changes a warranted praxis-relevant claim while remaining within the local Source Ceiling and reconstructive burdens.
- `co-equal` means it is supported but changes no warranted result relevant to the current claim.
- `worse` means it introduces greater unsupported relation load, fragmentation, calibration loss, or Loss than the coarse reconstruction.
- `incomparable` means the coarse and fine reconstructions answer materially different bounded questions or cannot be translated without inventing a common measure.

These are local comparison descriptions, not Output Classes or numerical ranks. The comparison must state which claim dimension changes: temporal order, access, cost, binding, alternatives, source function, type claim, uncertainty, Loss, or Stop condition. A finer map does not become preferable because it contains more nodes, longer provenance, higher precision, a denser graph, or more interactive controls.

```text
finer
≠ truer automatically
≠ more useful automatically
≠ more authoritative automatically
```

For R-27, a relational reconstruction that distinguishes formal closure, residual obligation, and later reconsideration may be better for testing continuing binding. A minute-level event list may be co-equal if it changes no relation. A graph that inserts inferred links may be worse. A participant-experience account and a governance-record reconstruction may be incomparable where neither can be translated into the other's claim without exceeding its sources.

No-privilege does not protect coarse claims. Where finer supported evidence rejects the coarse source-function or type claim, the coarse claim must be reduced or rejected. The rule prevents only automatic victory by resolution depth.

<a id="27-10-sub-stop-conditions"></a>

### 27.10 SUB Stop Conditions

SUB Stop is the positive determination that the current `DECOMPOSE` occurrence has reached, or must not cross, its bounded continuation limit. It concerns this source object, claim, granularity relation, source basis, and relation map. It is not a claim that no finer structure exists.

#### C27-STOP-01 — Optional and Mandatory SUB Stop remain distinct

Optional Stop is warranted when a sufficient bounded result is available and further opening would add only supported neutrality, duplicate distinctions, or costs disproportionate to the tested claim. It preserves the current result and records why continuation is unnecessary.

Mandatory Stop is required before continuation would demand any of the following:

- distinctions below the Praxeological Relevance Floor for the tested claim;
- components, temporal order, relations, Non-Events, functions, or counterfactual effects beyond the local Source Ceiling;
- continuation after source reference has broken without an explicit rival-object or new-operation claim;
- a parts inventory that cannot return to the coarser function;
- model coherence, simulation, graph topology, or formal completion as substitute evidence;
- granularity change used to rescue a previously failed claim without a new testable claim;
- decomposition of an operator type rather than a bounded occurrence or composite;
- person, motive, legitimacy, responsibility, diagnosis, sanction, recommendation, or authority inference.

```text
Optional Stop
→ further continuation unnecessary

Mandatory Stop
→ further continuation inadmissible
```

A Stop preserves all supported findings, uncertainty, Loss, and prior dispositions. It does not convert Neutrality into Gain, underdetermination into confirmation, or Failure into success. A runtime or audit kernel may enforce declared thresholds and invariants, but it does not thereby decide empirical truth or the universal halting problem.

#### C27-CLAIMREDUCE-01 — Claim Reduction preserves the strongest surviving bounded finding

Claim Reduction is required when the original decomposition claim is too strong but a narrower source-supported finding survives. Examples include:

- components are supported, but one or more connecting relations are not;
- the source object remains identifiable, but the proposed coarser function is only partially preserved;
- a component is strongly modulating rather than constitutive;
- temporal coexistence is supported, but exact order or causality is not;
- the source type remains viable only in a restricted form;
- the finer map supports internal differentiation but not the stronger claim of a unique pathway.

Claim Reduction changes the claim disposition; it is not a cosmetic confidence label. The unsupported remainder is excluded or marked uncertain, and canonical Loss records what was compressed, excluded, uncertain, or irrecoverable.

```text
weaker supported finding survives
≠ original stronger claim survives
```

For R-27, the decomposition may support that residual obligation materially modulated the continuity between closure and later reopening while leaving the exact informal route underdetermined. The warranted result preserves the modulating relation and reduces the stronger claim that residual obligation uniquely caused reopening.

<a id="27-11-sub-non-capture"></a>

### 27.11 SUB Non-Capture

SUB Non-Capture is appropriate where the decomposition question is legitimate, the source object remains bounded, and relevant structure is visible, but no available granularity and relation map can responsibly carry a determinate reconstruction. It protects the method from invented closure, not the source claim from failure.

#### C27-NC-01 — Non-Capture remains available without coarse-claim rescue

Non-Capture may be required when:

- rival component boundaries remain equally source-supported;
- competing relation maps carry materially different coarser-function effects without a responsible discriminator;
- the finest source-supported detail still cannot connect parts to the source function;
- source identity itself remains divided among rival bounded objects;
- all available granularities either lack PraxisPurchase or exceed the Source Ceiling;
- irrecoverable structure prevents responsible type or function disposition.

```text
non_capture
≠ coarse claim confirmed
≠ finer claim confirmed
≠ rival maps averaged
≠ missing edges invented
```

Supported local findings remain recordable under Non-Capture. The analyst may preserve component presence, known relations, excluded alternatives, source-access limits, and Loss while declining the stronger reconstruction. Non-Capture therefore differs from total ignorance and from `failed_transformation`: the former may retain a bounded unresolved field; the latter states that the attempted transformation claim did not satisfy its required gates.

Non-Capture must not become a shield for a weak coarse claim. Where the coarse claim has already failed, the inability to produce a complete finer replacement does not restore it. The disposition remains failed or reduced, while the finer reconstruction may separately receive `non_capture`.

<a id="27-12-sub-admissibility-test"></a>

### 27.12 SUB Admissibility Test

The complete Chapter-27 test consolidates the local boundaries without replacing the Shared Transformation Record, the sixteen canonical Rules, or the later system-wide LIMITS consolidation.

#### C27-ADMITTEST-01 — the complete SUB test is conjunctive and non-compensatory

```text
Admissible(DECOMPOSE, X, C)
iff

AdditionalPraxisDifference
and SourceSupport
and SourceReferencePreserved
and ComponentRelationsReconstructible
and CoarserFunctionTraceableOrExplicitlyRevised
and TypeIntegrityMaintainedOrExplicitlyRevised
and GranularityDeclared
and StopConditionDefined
```

Every conjunct is independently necessary for an admissible positive decomposition claim. More detail cannot compensate for absent PraxisPurchase. Component support cannot compensate for unsupported relations. Source-object continuity cannot compensate for an untraceable function claim. Formal elegance cannot compensate for Source-Ceiling overreach. A declared Stop cannot repair an already invalid claim; it only prevents further invalid continuation.

The test may route to any canonical class warranted by the complete record, including `admissible`, `admissible_with_bounded_claim`, `admissible_but_provisional`, `resolution_neutral`, `partially_admissible`, `claim_reduction_required`, `mandatory_stop`, `failed_transformation`, or `non_capture`. `analogy_only` remains available only where the output is explicitly analogical rather than a completed source reconstruction. No local status mechanically selects a class.

#### C27-AXES-01 — boundary result, function effect, prior disposition, and Output Class remain separate

The integrated result preserves four axes:

```text
1. local SUB-boundary result
2. coarser-function or source-type effect
3. prior source-claim disposition
4. canonical Output Class
```

A local boundary result may report, for example:

```text
within_bounded_sub_range
lower_boundary_no_purchase
upper_boundary_fragmentation
source_ceiling_reached
claim_reduction_required
sub_boundary_underdetermined
```

These descriptions are not Output Classes. A source function may be `reduced` while the canonical class is `admissible_with_bounded_claim`; a supported no-purchase result may map to `resolution_neutral`; source-ceiling continuation may require `mandatory_stop`; and an unresolved rival reconstruction may map to `non_capture`. The prior coarse claim may remain rejected even where a new finer claim is admissible.

#### C27-ESCAPE-01 — granularity change and re-entry preserve prior failure

Changing granularity, component boundaries, relation vocabulary, graph layout, source Frame, or analytical interface creates a new testable claim where it materially changes the transformation. It does not erase the disposition of the prior claim.

```text
new granularity
→ new testable claim
≠ failed prior claim repaired automatically
```

Re-entry after Stop requires a materially new basis, such as new sources, a repaired relation map, a revised bounded claim, a different declared source object, a newly available threshold, or a relevant change in runtime invariants. More compute, additional generated detail, higher-dimensional visualization, or a relabelled component set is insufficient by itself.

A re-entered Record must reference the earlier Record, preserve its result and Loss, declare the new basis, and expose what would now count as success, reduction, Failure, Stop, or Non-Capture. This prevents Granularity Escape from turning recursive decomposition into claim immunization.

#### SUB Boundary Assessment Record View

The complete operation-specific view remains inside the Shared Transformation Record and controlled `extensions`:

```yaml
sub_boundary_assessment:
  source_transformation_record:
  tested_source_claim:
  source_object_and_type:
  source_function_under_test:
  source_granularity:
  target_granularity:
  expected_additional_praxis_difference:
  actual_changed_praxis_dimensions:
  lower_boundary_status:
  upper_boundary_status:
  source_ceiling_status:
  component_sensitivity_findings:
  component_relation_status:
  source_reference_status:
  coarser_function_status:
  type_integrity_status:
  stop_or_reentry_status:
  unresolved_structure:
  loss:
  admissibility_result:
```

The twenty fields do not create a parallel schema, purchase score, support score, sensitivity score, component ontology, automatic Source-Ceiling detector, or automatic Output-Class mapper. The Formal Model may verify presence, closed inventories, field shape, exact counts, and prohibited inferences. It may not determine actual components, relations, constitutiveness, source sufficiency, correct granularity, semantic adequacy, person properties, legitimacy, or authority.

#### C27-HANDOFF-01 — Chapter 28 receives tested boundary findings without inheriting an unearned result

The controlled continuation is:

```text
Chapter 27 provisional lock
→ Chapter 28 Preparation Gate
→ Chapter 28 — SUB Cases, Local Audit, and Part Output
```

Chapter 28 receives:

- the complete Chapter-27 boundary procedure;
- the R-27 comparison obligations;
- source, component, relation, function, and type findings;
- lower- and upper-boundary results;
- Stop, Claim Reduction, Failure, Non-Capture, and re-entry conditions;
- all unresolved structure and canonical Loss;
- the twenty-field view and exact Duty inventory.

Chapter 28 owns the positive, negative, confusion, Stop, and Non-Capture cases; chain tests where relevant; Local Audit; canonical output mapping; chapter lock; and SUB Part lock. Chapter 27 does not perform those cases or lock the Part in advance. LIMITS retains the system-wide consolidation of Relevance Floor, Traceability Ceiling, Source and Claim Ceiling, Stop, Non-Capture, anti-immunization, and authority boundaries.

<a id="chapter-27-completion-boundary"></a>

#### Chapter 27 completion boundary

The integrated WP4 audit confirms the Chapter-27 Contract, all twenty-four Preparation-Audit controls, all twenty-four Pressure Duties, the non-symmetric Lower and Upper SUB Boundaries, supported Neutrality without PraxisPurchase, Source Ceiling, independent Component and Relation Support, Source Reference, Coarser-Function Traceability, bounded Component Counterfactuals, local Sensitivity, Type Preservation and Revision, No Privilege of Fine Resolution, Optional and Mandatory Stop, Claim Reduction, Failure, Non-Capture, anti-immunization across granularity change, materially bounded re-entry, four result axes, the twenty-field Record view, protected Chapter-28/LIMITS ownership, and package consistency. The Chapter-27 result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

Chapter 27 is therefore **provisionally locked**. The class names the chapter-level methodological result under the still-open SUB Part and later corpus-wide audits. It does not establish that every source boundary is empirically decidable, that fine resolution is preferable, that Stop proves no finer structure exists, that Source Ceiling entails Non-Capture, that Claim Reduction preserves a stronger failed claim, that type integrity immunizes initial typing, that re-entry erases prior Failure or Stop, that Chapter-28 cases or Local Audit have been completed, that SUB is locked, or that any person, legitimacy, application, governance, or authority judgment follows.

All twenty-four Chapter-27 Pressure Duties occur exactly once in canonical prose. No standalone Case, Transformation Record, Smoke Record, appendix expansion, schema artifact, new operation, Rule, Output Class, audit stage, Source-Ceiling enum, Component ontology, Sensitivity/Purchase/Support score, automatic boundary or Output-Class classifier, graph application, Reader implementation, person type, recommendation, sanction, or authority mechanism has been produced.

```text
Chapter 27 provisionally locked
≠ finer resolution automatically privileged
≠ supported detail automatically has PraxisPurchase
≠ component presence proves relation or function
≠ Source Ceiling automatically means Non-Capture
≠ Stop proves no finer structure exists
≠ Claim Reduction preserves the stronger claim
≠ re-entry erases prior Failure, Stop, or Loss
≠ Chapter 28 completed
≠ SUB provisionally locked
```

Reopening requires a concrete conflict involving PMS Base, Structure or Contract ownership, source-object identity, tested source claim, source or target granularity, expected praxis difference, Source Ceiling, Component or Relation Support, Source Reference, Coarser Function, Type Integrity, bounded counterfactual load, no-fine-privilege, Stop/Reduction/Failure/Non-Capture routing, anti-immunization, re-entry, result-axis separation, a Pressure Duty, a Reference or Formal-Model binding, or a later integrated dependency. Preference for more detail, a more complete graph, a stronger causal reading, one automatic score, inferred missing edges, preservation of an initial type, or premature Chapter-28/LIMITS conclusions is insufficient.

---

<a id="28-sub-cases-countercases-and-local-audit"></a>

## 28. SUB Cases, Countercases, and Local Audit

Chapter 28 tests the completed SUB method against explicit artifacts. It does not derive a second theory from examples. Chapters 18–27 remain the definition and procedure sources; Chapter 28 demonstrates whether their distinctions survive positive cases, countercases, confusion cases, record validation, Local Audit, output mapping, and Part-lock pressure.

```text
case artifact
≠ theory source

formal completeness
≠ substantive admissibility

one positive case
≠ DECOMPOSE validated generally
```

Sections 28.1–28.7 establish the case wrapper, four-layer artifact contract, and six positive cases. Sections 28.8–28.15 establish six countercases and the first two operation-confusion cases. Sections 28.16–28.20 complete the analogy and modulation confusion cases, the twenty-four-question SUB Local Audit, canonical output mapping, complete Loss and alternatives review, chain discipline, and closing lock decision. Every actual case remains bounded by its own source object, claim, Frame, granularities, components, relations, source-function effect, resolution result, Loss, alternatives, Stop/Non-Capture status, and governance ceiling.

The integrated Chapter-28 result is a complete **case-tested local SUB closure procedure**. Sixteen Chapter-28 targets exist as linked Markdown/YAML/audit/mapping sets, all three lock-critical sets and the additionally mandatory operator-error and fragmentation sets are complete, and the Part-specific audit passes without creating a second theory, new operation, new operator, new Rule, new Output Class, new audit stage, schema replacement, automatic semantic classifier, person judgment, application authority, or authority inheritance.

<a id="28-1-case-architecture"></a>

### 28.1 Case Architecture

#### C28-ARCH-01 — the SUB case wrapper exposes the test without creating a parallel schema

A Chapter-28 case has three possible classes: positive case, countercase, or confusion case. The class names the test role, not the canonical Output Class. A positive case demonstrates one bounded reconstruction that survives the relevant gates. A countercase identifies the precise gate at which a plausible decomposition fails, reduces, stops, or becomes non-capturable. A confusion case holds neighboring interpretations open until operation, source object, relation map, and claim burden can be separated.

The explanatory case view records:

- case identity, class, and governing claim;
- Claim Scope and Claim Ceiling;
- source Record, object, origin type, Frame, and granularities;
- decomposition question and expected additional Praxis difference;
- source basis, components, and component relations;
- source-reference, function, type, and resolution findings;
- operation-boundary and counterfactual status;
- complete Loss, alternatives, local audit, canonical mapping, Stop/Non-Capture, and governance boundary.

This view does not replace `Transformation_Record.schema.json`. Case-specific explanation belongs in the Markdown reconstruction and existing extension carriers; operation, source, target, admissibility, Loss, result, and governance remain controlled by the Shared Transformation Record.

#### C28-ARTIFACT-01 — a case is complete only through four linked and verified layers

Every instantiated case requires:

```text
Markdown reconstruction
+
schema-valid YAML Transformation Record
+
case-local audit
+
exactly one canonical Output-Class mapping
```

`Case_Index.md` and `Case_Index.yaml` register identity, operation, local result, mapping, status, links, lock relevance, and SHA-256 hashes. An artifact is not counted because a filename or narrative exists. The Markdown and YAML files must both exist; the Record must validate; the twelve audit stages and case-specific SUB checks must be complete; links and hashes must resolve; and exactly one of the ten canonical classes must be selected.

The following six positive cases instantiate that contract. Each executes one `DECOMPOSE` occurrence. None decomposes a PMS operator type, constructs a new PATH object, assigns a target function, or generates authority.

<a id="28-2-frame-typed-occurrence"></a>

### 28.2 Positive Case: Frame-Typed Occurrence

#### C28-FRAME-01 — a Frame-typed occurrence is opened through its production and maintenance relations

[Frame-Typed Occurrence case](../03_cases/Case_Index.md) opens one intake-review Frame occurrence into an intake gate, role-routing practice, threshold check, and closure-and-repair routine. Eligibility conditions access; assigned access carries the threshold; threshold outcome routes closure and residual repair. The source function is therefore internally differentiated without treating the Frame operator type as a material aggregate.

Removing role routing preserves the legibility of requests but breaks actionable review access. Replacing it with open access changes the occurrence-level Frame function. The counterfactual supports a bounded component relation; it does not type persons, prove institutional legitimacy, or define Frame generally.

The homogeneous-Frame claim is superseded without erasure. The narrower relational occurrence claim maps to `admissible_with_bounded_claim`.

<a id="28-3-attractor-typed-occurrence"></a>

### 28.3 Positive Case: Attractor-Typed Occurrence

#### C28-ATTR-01 — an Attractor occurrence is reconstructed through recurrence-maintaining relations

[Attractor-Typed Occurrence case](../03_cases/Case_Index.md) opens one recurring workaround configuration into a default route, switching cost, maintenance action, and delayed repair path. Delay raises the cost of switching; the higher cost redirects practice to the default; maintenance restores that route after attempted deviation.

The reconstruction refines the recurrence claim because an available low-cost repair alternative weakens return to the workaround. It does not decompose the Attractor operator type or establish a universal dynamic law. It also does not perform `PROJECT_AS`: no external Attractor-function is assigned.

The bounded recurrence claim maps to `admissible_with_bounded_claim`.

<a id="28-4-distributed-asymmetry"></a>

### 28.4 Positive Case: Distributed Asymmetry

#### C28-ASYM-01 — Asymmetry may be distributed across relations without a single central carrier

[Distributed Asymmetry case](../03_cases/Case_Index.md) reconstructs unequal practical capacity across initiation access, delay burden, exit cost, and repair capacity. Limited initiation access concentrates delay; accumulated delay raises exit cost; limited repair capacity prolongs the unequal burden.

Equalizing initiation access reduces one dimension but does not remove exit-cost or repair-capacity asymmetry. The occurrence is therefore internally distributed. No single component or person is promoted into the complete source, and no moral or legitimacy ranking follows.

The broader single-center dominance claim is withdrawn in favor of a bounded distributed relation claim mapped to `admissible_with_bounded_claim`.

<a id="28-5-structured-non-event"></a>

### 28.5 Positive Case: Structured Non-Event

#### C28-NONEVENT-01 — a Non-Event is reconstructed through expectation, window, positive sub-events, blockage, and later carryover

[Structured Non-Event case](../03_cases/Case_Index.md) begins from an expected review within a declared window. Agenda preparation, scheduling, and evidence assembly occur, but a source-supported blockage prevents completion; the workaround persists beyond the window.

The positive sub-events do not erase the non-realization, and missing information does not create it. The `Λ` claim depends on the declared expectation, bounded window, supported non-completion, and later relevance. If the review had occurred inside the window, the later workaround would lose its status as carryover from that missed transition.

The result refines the structured Non-Event and maps to `admissible_with_bounded_claim` without Event Inflation, intention inference, or blame.

<a id="28-6-trajectory-decomposition"></a>

### 28.6 Positive and Lock-Critical Case: Trajectory Decomposition

#### C28-TRAJECTORY-01 — one warranted Trajectory is opened into subpaths and relations without creating a new PATH

[Admissible Trajectory Decomposition case](../03_cases/Case_Index.md) is the WP1 lock-critical artifact. Its source is one previously warranted review-governance Trajectory. `DECOMPOSE` opens it into an intake subpath, review cluster, formal closure, residual obligation, and later reopening subpath.

The load-bearing relation is not mere chronology. Review culminates in closure; closure leaves a declared obligation unresolved; continuing binding provides the source-traceable connection to reopening. Replacing the residual obligation with a non-binding recommendation preserves formal closure but weakens the continuing-binding relation. The Trajectory remains identifiable, while its finer source-function claim changes.

This record does not compose a new Trajectory and does not project an audit-training or warning function. Source reference, subpaths, relations, Loss, uncertainty, and operation boundary are complete. The result maps to `admissible`.

<a id="28-7-resolution-gain"></a>

### 28.7 Positive Case: Resolution Gain

#### C28-GAIN-01 — finer reconstruction gains warrant by changing the claim, not by adding detail alone

[Resolution Gain case](../03_cases/Case_Index.md) tests a coarse occurrence previously summarized as authority concentration. The finer source packet distinguishes access restriction, sequencing control, residual binding, and repair capacity.

Removing access restriction changes participation but not residual binding. Removing residual binding changes repair pressure but not initial sequencing. The coarse unitary claim therefore divides into separate supported relations. This is Resolution Gain because the warranted reconstruction changes; it is not Gain because the graph is denser, the vocabulary is more precise, or the record is longer.

The earlier total authority-concentration claim remains superseded without erasure. The retained bounded dimensions map to `admissible_with_bounded_claim`. No causal monopoly, legitimacy judgment, person attribution, or authority inheritance is produced.

[`C28-OVERFINE-01`](../03_cases/markdown/C28-OVERFINE-01_Overfine_Analysis_below_the_Relevance_Floor.md) begins from an already adequate review-occurrence reconstruction. Second-level timestamps, cursor actions, display states, and token subdivisions are all synthetically supported. Removing them changes no warranted claim about access, threshold, closure, repair, cost, binding, alternatives, or source function.

The finer packet is therefore not false and not unsupported. It is `resolution_neutral` for the tested claim. Continued opening would cross the lower SUB boundary by accumulating distinctions without PraxisPurchase. The prior adequate reconstruction and the factual microdetail remain preserved, while the continuation claim maps to `mandatory_stop`.

```text
supported detail
+
no changed warranted claim
→ Stop pressure
≠ detail false
```

<a id="28-9-unsupported-internal-structure"></a>

### 28.9 Countercase: Unsupported Internal Structure

[`C28-UNSUPPORTED-01`](../03_cases/markdown/C28-UNSUPPORTED-01_Unsupported_Internal_Structure.md) contains directly recorded entry, review, and closure events. A hidden routing role and causal chain would make the occurrence narratively coherent, but neither appears in the source packet. Component plausibility cannot supply relation warrant.

The stronger internal-routing claim is excluded. The recorded event field and coarse order survive as a weaker formulation. This is not Resolution Neutrality, because the unsupported refinement never qualified as a valid coarse/fine comparison. The case maps to `claim_reduction_required` and preserves the hidden route as unavailable rather than converting absence into a Non-Event or intention claim.

[`C28-OPTYPE-01`](../03_cases/markdown/C28-OPTYPE-01_Operator_Decomposition_Error.md) attempts to treat the Frame operator type Δ–Ψ as a material aggregate of role, threshold, and memory parts. The proposed components are category artifacts, not occurrence-level source structures.

```text
operator type
≠ operator occurrence
≠ composite
```

The attempted operation fails before any downstream causal, person, legitimacy, or authority inference. Δ–Ψ remains unchanged and occurrence-level Frame cases remain separately testable. Their co-presence, temporal proximity, shared storage, and graph adjacency do not establish routing, dependency, or contribution to the claimed service function.

The isolated findings survive. The stronger same-reference decomposition fails because component relations and coarser-function traceability remain underdetermined. More nodes would intensify fragmentation rather than repair it. The case maps to `failed_transformation`.

<a id="28-12-resolution-escape"></a>

### 28.12 Countercase: Resolution Escape

#### C28-ESCAPE-01 — new granularity cannot erase an earlier failed claim

[`C28-ESCAPE-01`](../03_cases/markdown/C28-ESCAPE-01_Resolution_Escape.md) starts from a closure-finality claim already defeated by post-closure review activity. Successive moves to wording fragments, interface states, and micro-interactions leave that counterexample unanswered while repeatedly redescribing what counts as the relevant claim.

The result is `resolution_escape`, not Gain or Neutrality. Formal closure and later review remain preserved; the claim that closure ended reconsideration remains failed. Continued escape receives `mandatory_stop`. Re-entry requires a materially new claim that directly addresses the counterexample and preserves the earlier failure.

The macro-Asymmetry source object is therefore not established by decomposition. Local burdens survive, while the coordinated macroclaim is reduced. A future macro object would require a separate `COMPOSE` occurrence with Selection Rule, constitutive relations, Loss, and result. The current record maps to `claim_reduction_required`.

<a id="28-14-sub-or-retype"></a>

### 28.14 Confusion Case: SUB or RETYPE?

#### C28-SUBRETYPE-01 — internal reconstruction and contextual target function require separate occurrences

[`C28-SUBRETYPE-01`](../03_cases/markdown/C28-SUBRETYPE-01_SUB_or_RETYPE.md) internally opens a Trajectory into formal closure, residual binding, and later reopening. The same prose also calls the Trajectory a calibration case for audit training.

The first segment is a `DECOMPOSE` claim. The second is a proposed `PROJECT_AS` claim because it assigns the preserved origin-typed Trajectory a function in a target context. SUB success cannot establish that function. The primary record therefore retains the internal reconstruction, separates the target-function claim and future Record, and maps the undifferentiated whole to `partially_admissible`.

```text
DECOMPOSE success
≠ PROJECT_AS success

shared source
≠ shared Record, Loss, or result
```

The artifact is lock-critical, but final lock-critical closure remains dependent on the WP3 chain and integrated Local-Audit checks.

<a id="28-15-sub-or-new-path"></a>

### 28.15 Confusion Case: SUB or New PATH?

#### C28-SUBPATH-01 — finer source material may expose a rival PATH rather than another component

[`C28-SUBPATH-01`](../03_cases/markdown/C28-SUBPATH-01_SUB_or_New_PATH.md) preserves one internal subpath and transition cluster of a previously composed Trajectory. A second independently formed sequence shares material with the source but lacks a supported constitutive relation to it.

The source subpath remains a `DECOMPOSE` finding. The independent sequence is not absorbed as another component merely because it appears in the finer packet. It requires a separate `COMPOSE` claim to test whether a rival PATH object exists. The undifferentiated whole maps to `partially_admissible`, with same-source findings retained and the candidate PATH segment separated.

<a id="28-19-output-mapping-and-loss"></a>

### 28.19 Output Mapping, Loss, Alternatives, and Chain Discipline

#### C28-MAPPING-01 — local findings map to the closed ten-class inventory without automation

The sixteen Chapter-28 target cases now span positive reconstruction, bounded admissibility, neutral continuation, reduction, analogy, partial admissibility, Stop, and Failure. Source-function effect, resolution result, prior disposition, and Output Class remain separate axes. The case index and individual Records carry the exact mappings; no local label becomes an eleventh class.

| --- | --- |
| Frame, Attractor, Asymmetry, Non-Event, Resolution Gain | `admissible_with_bounded_claim` |
| Admissible Trajectory Decomposition | `admissible` |

`resolution_neutral` and `non_capture` remain available canonical routes even though no Chapter-28 standalone target selects them as its final class. The case corpus is a discrimination set, not a requirement that every class receive a specimen.

#### C28-LOSSALT-01 — every case preserves complete Loss, alternatives, Claim Ceiling, and surviving findings

All sixteen Artifact Sets contain the five canonical Loss fields. They identify stronger excluded claims, surviving weaker findings, unresolved structure, materially relevant alternatives, re-entry conditions, and Claim Ceilings. Empty fields do not prove losslessness or absence of alternatives. Case hashes and cross-links bind the artifacts without increasing their authority.

#### C28-STOPNC-01 — Stop, reduction, Failure, and Non-Capture remain non-equivalent

```text
mandatory_stop
≠ claim_reduction_required
≠ failed_transformation
≠ non_capture
```

Stop governs prohibited continuation; reduction retains a weaker supported claim; Failure records unmet constitutive conditions; Non-Capture remains the responsible result where supported rivals cannot be discriminated without false precision. None restores a prior failed claim.

#### C28-CHAIN-01 — operation-chain pressure requires separate claims, Records, Loss, and results

The lock-critical SUB/RETYPE artifact now passes the chain-separation test. The internal Trajectory reconstruction remains in one `DECOMPOSE` Record. The proposed audit-training target function remains an unexecuted future `PROJECT_AS` claim. The SUB/new-PATH artifact likewise leaves the candidate PATH to a future `COMPOSE` Record.

```text
shared source history
≠ shared claim
≠ shared Record
≠ shared Loss
≠ shared result
```

No operation link inherits success, Failure, Stop, Non-Capture, or authority from another.

### 28.20 Closing Statement and Provisional Lock

#### C28-LOCK-01 — the integrated audit provisionally locks Chapter 28 and Part II without extending authority

All sixteen specified Chapter-28 targets are represented in canonical prose and as standalone Markdown/YAML/audit/mapping sets. The three lock-critical sets are complete; Operator Decomposition Error and Fragmentation without Source Function satisfy their additional complete-artifact burdens; all indexed Transformation Records validate; each actual Record selects exactly one canonical Output Class; the twenty-four-question Local Audit passes; all twenty-four Chapter-28 Pressure Duties occur exactly once; and indexes, paths, hashes, References, Formal Model, package, CRC, and byte-roundtrip controls agree.

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

The class records methodological completion under the still-open RETYPE, LIMITS, integrated-case, corpus, model-finalization, derivative, Reader, Graph, and release audits. It is not a thirtieth case Record, a universal validation of `DECOMPOSE`, empirical confirmation of any synthetic case, or an increase in application authority.

Chapter 28 is therefore **provisionally locked**, and Part II — SUB is **provisionally locked** through Chapters 18–28.

The lock confirms:

1. only bounded occurrences and composites are decomposed; Δ–Ψ operator types remain untouched;
2. every valid `DECOMPOSE` occurrence declares source object, source and target granularity, decomposition question, components, relations, source-function effect, Loss, alternatives, result, and governance boundary;
3. Source Reference and Coarser-Function Traceability are retained or explicitly revised rather than silently discarded;
4. fine resolution has no automatic epistemic, causal, practical, or authority privilege;
5. Resolution Gain, Neutrality, Drift, and Escape remain distinct;
6. Source Ceiling, calibration limits, lower and upper SUB boundaries, Optional Stop, Mandatory Stop, Claim Reduction, Failure, and Non-Capture remain available and non-equivalent;
7. `DECOMPOSE` remains distinct from description, analogy, recontextualization, rival `COMPOSE`, and `PROJECT_AS`;
8. operation chains require separate claims, Records, Loss, results, and canonical mappings;
9. a change of Frame, granularity, level, composition, label, interface, or target context does not erase a prior failed or reduced claim;
10. case artifacts and formal validation test controlled structure but do not determine empirical truth, causality, semantic or normative validity, person properties, legitimacy, recommendation, sanction, or authority.

The sixteen Chapter-28 target mappings remain bounded and descriptive:

```text
admissible:                         1
admissible_with_bounded_claim:      5
analogy_only:                       1
partially_admissible:               2
claim_reduction_required:           3
mandatory_stop:                     2
failed_transformation:              2
```

`admissible_but_provisional`, `resolution_neutral`, and `non_capture` remain available canonical classes. Their absence as final mappings among the sixteen Chapter-28 standalone targets is not a defect, quota failure, or routing rule. `resolution_neutral` appears as a local result within the Overfine case before Mandatory Stop, and `non_capture` remains explicitly open wherever supported rivals cannot be responsibly discriminated.

The integrated WP4 audit confirms the Chapter-28 Contract, the Part-II Block Contract, all twenty-four Preparation-Gate controls, all twenty-four Chapter-28 Pressure Duties, all sixteen standalone Chapter-28 Artifact Sets, all twenty-nine indexed case Records, the twenty-four-question SUB Local Audit, the closed operation/Rule/Output-Class/audit-stage inventories, the Chapters-18–28 cross-chapter dependency chain, protected Chapter-29/RETYPE and LIMITS ownership, Reference and Formal-Model synchronization, case-index paths and hashes, relative links, fingerprints, package integrity, ZIP CRC, and byte-identical roundtrip.

```text
+ case/schema/index/hash/link/package controls passed
→ Part II — SUB provisionally locked
```

```text
Part II — SUB provisionally locked
≠ final PMS-STRATA lock
≠ universal decomposability
≠ empirical or causal validation
≠ fine resolution privileged
≠ every ambiguity captured
≠ PROJECT_AS executed
≠ RETYPE completed
≠ LIMITS consolidated

```

Reopening requires a concrete conflict involving PMS Base, Structure or Contract ownership, source-object eligibility, operator-type integrity, granularity, component or relation support, Source Reference, Coarser Function, Source Ceiling, resolution classification, operation boundary, Loss, alternatives, Stop/Reduction/Failure/Non-Capture routing, anti-immunization, case artifact integrity, schema or mapping validity, Reference or Formal-Model binding, package integrity, or a later RETYPE/LIMITS/integrated-audit dependency. Preference for more detail, more cases, a denser graph, a stronger class, fewer visible losses, one automatic score, or a more decisive narrative is insufficient.

The controlled handoff is:

```text
Part II — SUB provisional lock
→ Part III — RETYPE
→ Chapter 29 Preparation Gate
→ Functional Projection without Origin-Type Replacement
```

RETYPE receives tested finer source traces, origin types, source-function effects, operation-boundary findings, Loss, alternatives, Failure, Stop, Non-Capture, and prior claim dispositions. It inherits no target function, no successful `PROJECT_AS` result, no authority, and no permission to replace the origin type.
